# CubbyLLM Hypothesis Document — Things to Validate

Status: draft, 2026-07-23, revised same day. This is a pre-registration of falsifiable hypotheses, not a design doc. Nothing here is a commitment to build anything — it's the accumulated research and concept-survey material from the CubbyLLM planning phase, organized so each claim can be checked before engineering time gets spent on it.

**Revision note (2026-07-23):** the first draft of this document implicitly treated cubby-lm's frozen trunk shape as a constraint CubbyLLM would build on top of. That premise was wrong and has been corrected throughout: CubbyLLM's architecture is an entirely different structure, built from scratch, including the vocabulary — which will be significantly larger than cubby-lm's, specifically enabled by removing the full-vocabulary softmax step. See Section 1 and the rewritten Group C.

**Validation-campaign note (2026-07-23, later the same day):** a full validation pass was executed over every group — see **`VALIDATION_REPORT.md`** for results and `validation/` for the runnable experiments + captured logs behind every number. Hypotheses below now carry a **Validation result** line where the campaign touched them; several statuses changed, and one premise (H-B3's `UNBIND_ROLE` location) was corrected. The report also lists what would change each conclusion.

## 0. How to use this document

Every hypothesis below follows the same shape: a claim, why we currently believe it (which source it traces back to), a concrete way to check it, and what result would kill it. A hypothesis with no kill criterion isn't really a hypothesis — it's an opinion — so every entry has one even where it's approximate. Status is one of **VERIFIED** (independently reproduced this session, not just asserted by a source document), **PLAUSIBLE** (a real source claims it, mechanism makes sense, but nobody has measured it against Cubby's actual code), or **SPECULATIVE** (asserted in a vision/blueprint document with no implementation behind it at all).

Hypotheses are grouped by the two structural problems driving the redesign (memory/forgetting, VSA binding head), then by the two halves of the one-line goal (fast/profitable, automatic specialization), then backbone and hardware, then a small set of organizational hypotheses, then training-data findings. Section 10 gives a suggested validation order — cheapest and most falsifiable first, hardware last.

## 1. Context

**Goal (one-liner):** Fast and profitable LLM gen 2 hybrid — general tasks plus specialization, automatic.

CubbyLLM is the redesigned successor to `cubby-lm`, drawing on lessons from `cubby-lm` and its environment sibling `cubemind`. Two concrete problems motivate the redesign, both already scoped by the user:

**Problem 1 — VSA binding head.** Cubby's current `VSABindingHead` operates over a frozen D=10240 codebook. **Corrected 2026-07-23 (validation campaign):** the dict-lookup `UNBIND_ROLE` this paragraph previously attributed to the binding head actually lives in **cubemind's VM** (`reasoning/vm.py:602-606`) — `vsa_binding_head.py` contains no unbind mechanism at all; it is a clean cosine-readout output head. The real-unbind gap is real but sits on the VM/reasoning side; the binding-head work for CubbyLLM is a from-scratch build either way (see H-B3, H-B5).

**Problem 2 — Hebbian memory layer.** The current Hebbian/Oja-style memory update is detrimental to memory: it drifts and forgets catastrophically under sequential learning. This needs fixing using the research summarized in Section 3.

**Structure — two different axes, kept deliberately separate.** CubbyLLM's *package/directory layout* will likely mirror cubemind's — meaning cubemind's intended, documented structure, not its accumulated sprawl (see Group F). CubbyLLM's *model architecture* is a different question entirely: **CubbyLLM is an entirely different structure than cubby-lm, built from scratch, including the vocabulary** (correction, 2026-07-23). cubby-lm's trunk is prior art — informative about what worked, what didn't, and what to avoid repeating — not a frozen substrate CubbyLLM has to sit on top of. Nothing below should be read as "patch cubby-lm's trunk"; where a hypothesis references cubby-lm's actual code, it's diagnosing the predecessor to inform a fresh design, not scoping an in-place edit.

**New, explicit requirement (2026-07-23): a much larger vocabulary than cubby-lm's 32k, unlocked specifically by removing the full-vocabulary softmax step.** The causal order matters and is worth stating precisely: vocab size is being treated as a variable that gets freed up once the output/embedding mechanism stops paying an O(vocab) cost per step — not a number chosen first that then needs optimizing around. This promotes what was previously framed as a set of possible efficiency optimizations (old Group C) into a load-bearing design requirement; see the rewritten Group C below, including two new hypotheses (H-C6, H-C7) this requirement surfaces.

cubby-lm's own shape, kept here for reference and lessons-learned, not as a CubbyLLM constraint: d_model=2560, n_layers=22, d_ffn=6848, tokenizer `grillcheese_spm32k_v2` (32k SPM, frozen in cubby-lm), MinGRU recurrence per `HybridBlock` with optional MoE / sliding-window attention / `EpisodicMemory` / SwiGLU FFN. `SegmentMemory`, `MTP`, and `CubeLangHead` exist as code but are not wired into the default forward pass — `CubeLangHead` is self-labeled "SCAFFOLD." Worth carrying forward deliberately rather than repeating by accident: the value of gating each new capability behind an explicit version flag, and the risk of building attachments (MTP, CubeLangHead, SegmentMemory) that never get wired into the actual forward path.

**Training data (added 2026-07-23):** a real, substantial corpus (~210GB) exists at `$CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA` — the user's own framing was that it can be used both for testing and as raw material for "a better dataset." Full findings are in the new Group G below, but the single most consequential discovery deserves a mention here: a training-data-generation script in this folder revealed an already-existing, already-in-production VSA/hyperdimensional-computing implementation (`grilly.experimental.vsa.ops`) running at the exact same D=10,240 dimensionality as both cubby-lm's binding head and cubemind's world-arena block-codes. That's not yet confirmed to be the same substrate, but it's a strong enough lead that it now sits at the top of Group B's validation order (H-B5), ahead of the two candidates the earlier survey found.

## 2. The central bet (H0)

**Claim.** Context-conditioned, hypernetwork-style parameter generation — making a model's active weights a function of context, θ=f(c), rather than a fixed state that gets overwritten in place — is simultaneously the fix for catastrophic forgetting *and* the mechanism for automatic specialization. If true, the two halves of CubbyLLM's one-line goal ("general tasks" and "specialization automatic") collapse into one underlying mechanism rather than two separate subsystems.

**Why we believe it.** Three independent sources converge on this same shape, which is a stronger signal than any one of them alone:

The Gradient Context Encoder (GCE) work (Ran Cheng, "Context Channel Capacity") argues catastrophic forgetting is mathematically forced whenever a state-based learner runs at C_ctx=0 — i.e., whenever weight updates don't structurally depend on context — and that the fix is making θ an unbypassable function of context, f_GCE(c), verified by Wrong-Context Probing (P5) so the context signal can't be optimized away in training (the "Structural Bypass Defect"). This closed a CIFAR-10 oracle gap from 23.3pp to 0.7pp in the source material.

Sylph, independently, replaces global softmax classifiers with sigmoid-decomposed binary classifiers whose parameters are generated by a few-shot hypernetwork conditioned on task embeddings — explicitly to avoid global gradient interference and prevent forgetting during sequential task adaptation. Same shape (hypernetwork generates params from context), different framing (classifier heads, not general weight rectification).

Dragon Hatchling (BDH) arrives at a related idea from the architecture side: its `bdh-cl` continual-learning extension pairs fast local Hebbian plasticity with mechanisms to keep it from drifting, and the broader "trillion-parameter blueprint" research (Section 6) explicitly proposes a GCE-style controller as the piece that prevents BDH's in-place Test-Time-Training fast weights from drifting.

**What's notable:** none of the four surveyed sibling folders (cubby-concepts, AURA_GENESIS, GrillCheese, Novel_GNN_Arch) implement this pattern anywhere. The closest analog is AURA_GENESIS's Liquid-MoE `tau(x)`-conditioned continuous-time gating cell (`aura/core/liquid_moe.py` — real, working, NumPy-only), which is *routing* (selecting among fixed experts) rather than *generating* continuous parameters, but is a plausible template for the small controller network that would emit the context embedding driving a hypernetwork. This means H0 is genuinely new work for CubbyLLM, not something to import — everything downstream in Groups A–D is really this one bet applied to different parts of the model.

**A fourth independent instance, added 2026-07-23:** generating token embeddings from context instead of storing them in a static lookup table (Group C's H-C1/H-C3, now driven by the much-larger-vocabulary requirement in Section 1) is itself a specific case of H0's mechanism — θ_embedding = f(token/context) rather than a fixed embedding matrix. The vocab requirement and the forgetting fix aren't two unrelated bets that happen to share a document; they're two applications of the same underlying idea, this time one driven by a hard product requirement rather than a research paper. That's a reason for more confidence in H0, not less.

**How to validate.** Build the smallest possible GCE-style controller (a context embedding feeding a rectification to one small weight block, not the frozen trunk) and run the P5 Wrong-Context Probing protocol against it, plus a toy sequential-task retention benchmark, compared against a frozen-weight baseline and the current Hebbian layer.

**Kill criterion.** If P5 probing shows the context signal gets optimized away even after architectural hardening (the Structural Bypass Defect resists the standard countermeasures), or if the added hypernetwork forward/backward pass erases the "fast" half of the goal at the scale CubbyLLM actually needs, the central bet needs revision — not abandonment, but a scaled-down or partial version (e.g., gating only specific memory slots rather than general weight generation).

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): SUPPORTED at toy scale, with one load-bearing caveat.** The smallest θ=f(c) prototype (frozen backbone per this section's own protocol, hypernetwork-generated head, 6 sequential tasks, no replay) shows near-zero forgetting **only with architectural hardening** (von-Oswald-style output regularization — literally the hypernetwork output regularizer of von Oswald, Henning, Sacramento & Grewe, *Continual learning with hypernetworks*, ICLR 2020, arXiv:1906.00695; citation pinned here 2026-08-28 after the oracle-competition scoring in `docs/research/2026-08-28-oracle-competition-scored.md` noticed it lived only in the agenda's Q6 note): task-0 MSE 0.062→0.060 across all tasks, P5 right-ctx 0.06 vs wrong-ctx 1.87 (context load-bearing, no bypass). The **naive** hypernetwork without hardening ends *worse than the fixed-weight baseline* (2.734 vs 1.778 final MSE) — the hypernetwork's own shared weights are themselves a C_ctx=0 learner and interfere internally, and its P5 probe flags bypass. So H0 is only ever "generation + anti-drift countermeasure," never naive generation. Cost: 4.1× baseline per step at toy scale, growing with number of protected contexts — the "fast"-half kill clause stays open at scale. `VALIDATION_REPORT.md` / `validation/exp_h0_gce.py`.



**Stand-in pointer (2026-09-03, `standin/README.md` § "Two adapters on one base — v8"; a `[stand-in]` fact, not a trunk result).** One fine-tuned 2.6B carrying both the program-emission and the talk data was measured to interfere across rounds (identity fell in v5, forge decision 1.00 → 0.17 in v6, arithmetic 0.825 → 0.750 → 0.675 over v5–v7 as the chat volume grew; each repaired by replaying the hurt family ×3/×4). The serve stack now speaks the θ=f(c) interface — `Emitter.emit(…, context=)` with the route role and the hormonal state as c — and resolves it through a K=2 tag-indexed adapter bank (`ContextualEmitter`, the frozen-router shape of H-C4). The v8 A/B (program-only vs talk-only adapters, same base, same 40/task VM eval) is the cheapest measurement of whether context-selected parameters remove that interference; its kill line is recorded there. It says nothing about the trunk's hypernetwork itself.

**A WORKING theta=f(c) IMPLEMENTATION ALREADY EXISTS — found 2026-08-02 in cubemind, port it rather than rebuild.** `cubemind/execution/mindforge.py` (452 lines) is real, runs, and has full analytic gradients: `MindForge.forge(context, layer_id) -> (A, B)` at L302, `forge_with_cache` L309, `backward(d_A, d_B, layer_id, cache)` L358. Its parameterization is the one CubbyLLM should adopt: a block-code hypervector context is projected `W_proj (d_hidden, k*l)`, LayerNormed, concatenated with a per-layer embedding `layer_embeddings (n_layers, d_hidden)`, pushed through an MLP to `W_coeff (n_basis, d_hidden)`, and the generated adapter is a **linear combination of learned low-rank bases**: `A = sum_i coeffs[i]*A_basis[i]`, `B = sum_i coeffs[i]*B_basis[i]`. Verified by execution — gradients populate all 10 parameters, and it is genuinely conditioned on both inputs (layer sensitivity |A(ctx,L0)-A(ctx,L1)| = 2.30; context sensitivity |A(c0,L0)-A(c1,L0)| = 67.15). It is correctly wired into `cubemind/training/vsa_lm.py::VSALayer` (L877 forge, L921 the analytic backward). **This matters because CubbyLLM's own `HyperGenerator` generates a full d*d matrix (ctx_dim=32 -> 4.19M outputs, ~134M params) which will not scale**; the basis-coefficient form is roughly 40x smaller and generates *per-layer* adapters. Two caveats: the GPU path is dead (`_mindforge_gpu_ok()` checks for `mindforge_basis_mix`/`mindforge_bwd_coeff`/`mindforge_bwd_basis` in grilly_core, which do not exist, so everything runs numpy `tensordot`), and there is a stale duplicate `mindforge.patch.py` encoding a removed-softmax design that `harrier_pretrain.py` was written against — that trainer hand-rolls approximate gradients, never calls `forge.backward()`, and has produced no checkpoint. Take `mindforge.py`, not either of those.

**The torch port that exists is DEGENERATE — this is the live gap between the bet and the code.** `cubemind/model/cubby/heads.py::MindForgeLoRAHead` (L17) claims to be a "PyTorch port of cubemind.execution.mindforge" but drops the two things that make it theta=f(c): there is **no layer embedding** (it is per-head, not per-layer) and **no VSA input** (context is a plain dense vector). Worse, it is self-conditioned in every call site — `MinGRUMultiTask.forward` L272 calls `head(pooled)` with `context=None`, and L86-87 then assigns `context = x`, with the default head spec declaring `"task_code_source": "self"`. So in practice it computes **theta = f(h)**, conditioned on the model's own pooled hidden state; no external task/context signal crosses into it at all. The `context=` argument is plumbed and nothing supplies it. Also `basis_B` is zero-initialised there (L80) while the numpy version uses small-random (L243) with the explicit comment "Zero init kills gradients through the LoRA path" — the two implementations contradict each other on their own core design point, so the torch LoRA path may not be training.

## 3. Group A — Memory & forgetting fix

**H-A1 — Diagnosis: the Hebbian layer fails because it runs at C_ctx≈0.** The Impossibility Triangle (zero forgetting + online learning + finite parameters can't all hold for a state-based learner) predicts this. Status: **VERIFIED (2026-07-23 campaign, both halves)** — structurally, the real `HebbianGrowthLayer` update is dW = f(x, xWᵀ, W) with no context input existing anywhere in its signature or update path, so C_ctx = 0 *by construction* (no estimation needed); empirically, after learning a task to EV 0.97 the shared basis retains EV 0.06 (**forgot 94%**) after three subsequent tasks; neurogenesis mitigates to 72% forgotten but doesn't rescue. Two incidental findings: the layer's default `nonlinear` (y³) mode never tracks subspace structure under a reconstruction metric (only `sanger` mode learns), and its residual metric becomes unreliable (EV>1) once grown bases decorrelate. See `VALIDATION_REPORT.md` / `validation/exp_a1_drift.py`.

**H-A2 — GCE-style generation removes catastrophic forgetting in CubbyLLM's memory layer.** Status: **toy-scale VERIFIED in the hardened form** (2026-07-23 campaign — see H0's validation result above for the numbers; the toy experiment *is* this hypothesis's specified side-experiment). Kill criterion checked and not triggered: bypass did not survive hardening. Still open at scale: the hardening cost curve and whether the mechanism stays scoped to the memory pathway in a real model — the second kill clause is untested until there is a real network to scope it in. `validation/exp_h0_gce.py`.

**H-A3 — A Sparse Distributed Memory substrate is more forgetting-resistant than dense Hebbian updates, purely by construction.** Source: `cubby-concepts/vsa/sdm.py` — real code, independently verified this session (staged the files, ran `pytest` directly: 61/61 tests passed, 81.6s wall time). Verified recall: 100% accuracy (1000/1000, cosine similarity 0.9957) at M=D=10000 with 1000 stored associations. Each write spreads across roughly 40 of 10,000 hard locations (calibrated Hamming radius) instead of overwriting one dense weight matrix — the standard SDM graceful-degradation argument versus Hopfield-style catastrophic collapse. Status: **the mechanism is VERIFIED; the forgetting-resistance property itself is PLAUSIBLE, not proven** — the cubby-concepts repo has no test that pushes the SDM toward/past capacity or compares its degradation curve against a Hebbian baseline. The repo's own CLAUDE.md names a "Zero-Forgetting Stability Benchmark" but it doesn't exist as code anywhere — file, function, or test. Also note: the repo proves the CLAUDE.md-specified Hamming radius of 4500 bits is actually broken (activates ~0 locations at full dimension) and ships a regression test for that fixed bug — a good sign for the code's honesty, a reminder to use the calibrated radius, not the originally-specified one. **Validate** by building that benchmark (it doesn't exist yet) and running it against SDM and the current Hebbian layer side by side.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): the benchmark now exists (`validation/exp_a_forgetting.py`) and the answer is split.** At matched state (~D² numbers each, D=2048, SDM M=D, online single-pass): SDM is the **only order-agnostic candidate** — at N=1024 its recall is ~15% uniformly across write order, while every dense method is recency-biased or totally collapsed (pure Hebbian: 0% for old AND new — overload destroys everything). That's the "graceful by construction" half, CONFIRMED. The capacity half is REFUTED at parameter parity: SDM's curve (99%→77%→15% at N=128/512/1024) sits below NLMS (100%→100%→51%) — the famous 100%@1000 cubby-concepts result used M=D=10000, ~5× the state per stored pair. SDM buys *fairness*, not *capacity*. Status: mechanism VERIFIED, blanket forgetting-resistance claim now has honest boundaries.

**H-A4 — NLMS is a cheaper drop-in replacement than a full hypernetwork rewrite.** Source: `AURA_GENESIS/aura/core/nlms.py` — real, complete, ~14KB, textbook Normalized LMS (`w ← (1−l2)·w + mu·(e·x)/(‖x‖²+ε)`), extended with group-sliced per-feature learning rates, a prosody-style gate, and dead-zone guards that skip updates when the prediction is already close enough. NLMS is error-corrective and self-normalizing by input energy, which classical adaptive-filter theory uses to bound weight drift — a real structural advantage over raw Hebbian/Oja. Status: PLAUSIBLE, with an important caveat surfaced by the AURA_GENESIS survey: AURA's actual anti-forgetting strategy is not "a smarter shared update rule," it's hard parameter isolation — one private linear head per domain/realm (88 domain files + ~150 realm files under `svc_nlms_weights/`), created on demand and never shared across concepts. That sidesteps interference by construction, not through NLMS's math, and doesn't obviously transfer to a shared dense memory layer where isolation isn't an option. **Validate** by swapping Oja for NLMS in one memory update path and rerunning the same forgetting benchmark as H-A3, in a setting where weights are actually shared (not per-concept-isolated) — that's the setting AURA_GENESIS never tested.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): SUPPORTED in exactly that never-tested shared-weight setting, with its failure mode now named.** In the H-A3 benchmark, matrix NLMS posts the best capacity curve of every candidate (100% recall through N=512, 51% at N=1024, graceful decline, no catastrophic cliff) — clearly better than pure Hebbian (cliff to 0% at 1024) and *vastly* better than the flat-decay Hebbian production form (65.6% already at N=128, worst curve measured). The cost: strongest recency bias past capacity (write-order quartiles 0/11/93/100 oldest→newest) — NLMS forgets oldest-first, LRU-like. As a cheap upgrade over Oja: yes. As zero-forgetting: no. `validation/exp_a_forgetting.py`.

**H-A5 — An external episodic store can complement, not replace, a fixed weight-based memory layer.** Source: GrillCheese's `capsulememoryintegration.md` (833 lines, full read) — a hippocampus-inspired design: Dentate-Gyrus-style sparse expansion (32D→128D, ~2% top-k winner-take-all) feeding CA3-style FAISS pattern completion, with importance-scored eviction (`score = consolidation_priority · stability · (recency + log(access_count))`, top 80% kept, protected items never evicted). STATUS.md corroborates a running instance (97 memories stored). This is architecturally a complementary-learning-systems split (fast episodic store outside the weights + slow weight-based consolidation), the same philosophy as Memorizing Transformer/MemGPT — it never touches synaptic plasticity at all, so it's orthogonal to, not competing with, the Hebbian fix. Status: PLAUSIBLE as a complementary mechanism, not a fix for the underlying problem. One documented weakness: the residual-stream injection hook (tiling a 32D vector across a 3072D residual stream with no learned up-projection) is methodologically weak; the text-context injection half is more credible. **Validate** by prototyping the DG-style sparse-expand + pattern-completion step as a side channel and measuring whether it reduces pressure on the weight-based memory — explicitly not a substitute for fixing that memory.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): mechanism VERIFIED at toy scale.** DG-style expansion (8× up-projection, 2% WTA) over a Hebbian store, with a *size-matched dense control*: at N=512 associations both dense variants collapse to 0% recall while the sparse code holds 100% — pattern separation, not parameter count, buys ~2× capacity (its own cliff arrives at N=1024). Also observed: dense Hebbian fails as a cliff (100%→0% between N=256 and 512), not a graceful curve. Complement framing confirmed. `validation/exp_a5_sparse.py`.

**H-A6 — Negative-result flag: no sibling folder has a validated fix for this problem.** All four surveys converge on the same finding. GrillCheese's own production Hebbian layer (`grillcheese/backend/brain/gpu_brain.py::hebbian_update`) implements `ΔW = η·⟨pre⊗post⟩ − λ·W` — a flat decay term, not true Oja normalization (no `post²·W` term, no norm-convergence guarantee) — the same failure class CubbyLLM is trying to fix, with a documented live shape-mismatch bug in the same function. Its own `forreview/capsules/DECISION.md` lists "Add EWC for continual learning" as still-open future work. Grepping GrillCheese's entire `learning/` package turns up no EWC, no orthogonal projection, no replay-based consolidation — only a crude activation-threshold "neuronal growth" heuristic, loosely adjacent to SPHeRe's structural-projection idea but untested against it. Novel_GNN_Arch's closest analog, `apply_outcome_update()` (importance-gated plasticity — a per-memory-slot effective learning rate driven by a plasticity trait and a simulated reward-prediction-error signal), is a well-shaped design sketch conceptually close to Elastic Weight Consolidation applied at memory-slot granularity, but it's ~250 lines of hand-tuned, duplicated-across-two-files demo code, tested only on 2–10 hardcoded synthetic records with randomly sampled (not task-derived) outcomes — zero quantitative forgetting-benchmark evidence either way. **Conclusion, not really a hypothesis: CubbyLLM cannot shortcut this problem by importing a sibling's solution. Budget real R&D time and benchmark every "reuse" candidate rather than adopting any of them on faith.**

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): every sub-claim fact-checked by direct file reads and CONFIRMED**, with three precision fixes: GrillCheese's flat-decay update is at `gpu_brain.py:376` exactly as described, but the documented shape-mismatch bug is filed against its *caller* (`unified_brain.py::_update_hebbian_weights`, per `gcheese-faiss/CRITICAL_ISSUES.md:104-110`), not `hebbian_update` itself; `apply_outcome_update()` is ~38 lines duplicated across two files (not ~250 total); and the nuclear-test demo uses fixed ±0.8 outcomes — only the extended demo randomizes. Zero grep hits for EWC/elastic/orthogonal/replay/consolidat/fisher across GrillCheese's `learning/` package, and `DECISION.md:241` lists EWC as future work, both confirmed verbatim. The negative result stands at full strength.

**H-A7 — The learning gate: weight updates from live data enter the trunk only through a nightly, pre-registered promotion rule composed from harnesses that already exist — and that rule can tell a forgetting update from a clean one. Added 2026-08-28.** Source: the oracle-competition scoring (`docs/research/2026-08-28-oracle-competition-scored.md`), whose blind round produced the industry-default learning loop and a prior-art list none of which was in this repo. Context: `docs/ARCHITECTURE_VISION.md` lists the learning-gate as `[far]` ("vetted sources, anomaly detection, rollback") with no hypothesis entry, no kill criterion and no experiment — the one piece of the continual-learning story with no measurement path. H0 says *how* weights may depend on context; this entry says *when* a candidate update is allowed in at all. Prior art to build from (all ≤ 2025, IDs verified by the competition's own search passes, not re-verified here): **KTO** (Ethayarajh et al. 2024, arXiv:2402.01306 — learns from unpaired binary 👍/👎, the feedback a product actually produces); **O-LoRA** (Wang et al. 2023, 2310.14152) and **Online-LoRA** (Wei et al. 2024, 2411.05663) — orthogonal-subspace / task-free forgetting control for adapter-shaped deltas; **experience replay** (Rolnick et al. 2019, 1811.11682); the **ripple-effect test** for knowledge edits (Cohen et al. 2023, 2307.12976) as the regression suite an edit must pass; **TracIn** (Pruthi et al. 2020, 2002.08484) as per-update influence tracing — `git blame` for a promoted delta; **conformal LM** (Quach et al. 2023, 2306.10193) for the abstention contract; **sleep-time compute** (Lin et al. 2025, 2504.13171) for the always-on consolidation slot. Claim, concrete — promote candidate a_{t+1} iff every term holds: Δ general-capability regression ≥ 0 (H-G4's `socratic_test_text.jsonl` harness); Δ dated-key forgetting ≥ 0 against the NLMS curve (H-G2's NYT harness); in-window needle recall ≥ 95% (H-D4's `exp_needle_recall.py`); binding-channel retrieval ≥ 70% (H-B6's offline probe); claimed-answer precision ≥ 0.99 (the CoT pipeline eval); routing precision ≥ 0.90 (the margin gate). Every term is a measured artifact today; every candidate run writes a manifest (data hashes, seeds, base-checkpoint hash, delta hash) and is rollbackable by construction because deltas are never merged destructively. Two measured reasons the gate is not paranoia: H-B6 (a self-grading objective Goodharts while its loss looks like progress) and H0 part 2 (online-trained routers collapse) — so nothing the model scores itself ever enters, only execution-verified or human-thumbed signal. **Validate** (cheap, 151M checkpoint, CPU-tolerable): build the gate as one script over the existing harnesses and feed it three known deltas on `hd5_mem21.pt` — (a) a deliberately-forgetting update (plain SFT on one narrow slice, no replay: the H-A1 failure shape), (b) a matched update with replay + hardening, (c) the all-zero delta as the null; record which gate fires, at what margin, and the wall-clock. **Kill:** if the composed gate cannot separate (a) from (b) at any threshold the individual harnesses support — passes the forgetting arm or fails the clean one — then the harnesses measure the wrong things for update admission and the learning gate needs its own benchmark before any nightly path is built; second kill: if a full gate pass exceeds a nightly budget (~1 GPU-hour at 151M) it cannot be nightly and must be tiered (cheap gates every night, expensive weekly). Status: **RAN 2026-08-30 (Colab A100-40G) — the gate SEPARATES: at the nightly LR where a 3.3M-token replay update is CE-neutral on every source (3e-5, and 1e-5), the code-only update is still rejected with the mechanism named. Kill did not fire. Nightly LR = 3e-5. Full ladder below.** `validation/exp_a7_learning_gate.py` (+ `test_a7_gate.py`, 9 pins on the pure decision logic) implements the experiment with two pre-registered corrections found while building it: (1) **three of the six proposed terms cannot see a trunk delta** — claimed-answer precision and routing precision are model-free surfaces (word table + VM), and the H-G2 NYT-vs-NLMS curve benchmarks memory *methods*; the trunk's forgetting signal is per-source held-out CE instead; (2) every delta is scored against the base on *identical* held-out windows, so thresholds are max(min ε, 3 × **paired** SE) per metric — the unpaired seed-to-seed noise (0.14 nats on the mix at 16 batches) is the wrong noise for a paired comparison and would hide real forgetting; retrieval, which has no pairing, uses the seed-to-seed ε with a 0.50 collapse floor. CPU shape: ~9 s/step, ~50 min end to end — a session teardown killed it twice at step ~100/120 (partial base numbers, for orientation only: ce_mix 3.21 / 3.34 on the two streams, GSM8K text 3.71, retrieval 0.70 / 0.74, code-only fine-tune loss ~1.9 flat). The recorded run is the Colab one: **`notebooks/a7_learning_gate.ipynb`** (A100, B32 × 200 steps = 3.3M tokens per delta, minutes). **Result** (`validation/logs/exp_a7_learning_gate.{log,json}`, copied from Drive; `notebooks/a7_learning_gate.ipynb`; base `hd5_mem21.pt` step 92k, deltas 200 steps × B32 × S512 = 3.28M tokens at LR 1e-4, fresh Adam, 10-step warmup; eval 32 mix / 8 per source / 8 GSM8K batches = 1,024 / 256 / 256 paired windows; base ce_mix 3.2146 (seed 779: 3.1998), GSM8K text 3.733, retrieval 0.738 / 0.744): **C (null) PROMOTE** — every paired difference exactly 0. **A (code-only, no replay) REJECT, and the rejection names the mechanism:** mix +0.201 (thr 0.024), GSM8K +0.087 (thr 0.020), **every one of the 12 unseen sources +0.116 to +0.185 nats** (thr 0.030) and `pretrain_ext` **+1.09** (thr 0.091) — the smallest source (0.9M tokens, base CE 0.25 = memorized) is the most fragile, exactly the forgetting-theory prediction — while the trained source itself sat at **−0.003** and its train loss was flat (1.87 → 1.86; code was already learned). Retrieval *rose* to 0.777. **B (full mixture = replay) REJECT** — but not for forgetting: mix +0.046 (thr 0.010) and **all 13 sources +0.035 to +0.066, *including* the sources it was trained on**, GSM8K −0.005 (passes), retrieval 0.801. A uniform rise across every source, code included, is something forgetting cannot produce; the train loss confirms it — **B's training loss went *up*, 3.05 → 3.27, over its 200 steps**: a fresh Adam at 1e-4 on a converged checkpoint (which ended its cosine near 3e-5) perturbs the whole model. The gate rejected a genuinely bad update. **Reading:** by the letter the first kill fires (B rejected); by the evidence the *clean arm was not clean* — the failure is in the arm's construction (optimizer state + LR), not in the gate's ability to discriminate, and the gate lacks only a *naming* term: it labels a uniform rise "forgetting_source:*" on 13 sources when the honest label is "uniform regression (LR/optimizer)". Two consequences, both pre-registered here: (1) **decider — the LR ladder**: rerun B alone at LR 3e-5 and 1e-5, and once with the checkpoint's Adam state restored (`CB_A7_ARMS=B`, `CB_A7_RESUME_OPT=1` in the script; ladder cell in the notebook); the gate separates iff some nightly LR exists at which a replay update is CE-neutral on every source while the code-only update at the *same* LR still fires — if no such LR exists at 3.3M tokens, the kill stands and the nightly path needs a different update rule (KTO on thumbs, not SFT); (2) **add the pattern term**: a uniform rise (all sources fire with |spread| ≪ mean) is reported as `uniform_regression`, a selective rise (unseen sources fire, trained source improves) as `forgetting` — same decision, correct name. Budget: one full gate evaluation = **48 s** on an A100 (nightly cost of *checking*); each 3.3M-token proposal = 72–76 s. **Run 2 (same day, user-run; `validation/logs/exp_a7_learning_gate_run2_src0.1.{log,json}` — the Drive log is an append log holding the first run, two aborted starts at 3e-4 and 8e-5, and this one): LR 8e-5, ε_src raised to 0.10.** Same shape: A rejected and named (mix +0.162, GSM8K +0.065, 10/12 unseen sources above even the 0.10 ε, `pretrain_ext` +0.83, trained source −0.009); C passes; **B rejected on mix alone** (+0.033 vs 0.010) with the per-source column silenced by construction (+0.026…+0.040 on all 13) — and **B's train loss still rises, 3.051 → 3.255**. Two things this pins: the uniform drift scales with LR (mix +0.046 → +0.033 for 1e-4 → 8e-5) and so does A's signal (+0.201 → +0.162), so the ladder's real question is whether an LR exists at which B is CE-neutral *and* A still fires — or whether restoring the optimizer state (`CB_A7_RESUME_OPT=1`) removes the drift at the same LR, which would leave A's signal intact. Raising ε_src is not a fix (it is fitting the threshold to the arm); the mix term, which cannot be loosened without changing the claim, still rejects. **The LR ladder (same day, user-run, B alone, fresh Adam unless noted; `validation/logs/exp_a7_learning_gate_{lr3e-5,lr1e-5,lr1e-4_resume}.*`): B at 3e-5 → PROMOTE** (mix +0.005 vs thr 0.010; worst source `kbtxt` +0.009 vs 0.030; GSM8K −0.014; retrieval 0.759); **B at 1e-5 → PROMOTE** (mix −0.002, every source −0.001…−0.012, GSM8K −0.011); **B at 1e-4 with the checkpoint's Adam state restored → REJECT, *worse* than the fresh run** (mix +0.055, all sources +0.038…+0.052, `pretrain_ext` +0.19) — the drift is the learning rate, not the optimizer state. **Correction to the reading above:** the "train loss rose 3.05 → 3.23" was not evidence of perturbation — the curve has the identical shape at 1e-5, 3e-5, 8e-5 and 1e-4 (same data seed 11 ⇒ same batch order; the first ten batches are simply easier); the held-out CE is the only evidence and it was sufficient. **State of the kill:** a nightly LR at which a 3.3M-token replay update is CE-neutral on every source exists (≤ 3e-5); the separation verdict now waits on **A at 3e-5 and 1e-5** (the notebook's ladder cell runs both): the gate separates iff the code-only update still fires there. Prediction on record, from the 1e-4/8e-5 scaling (mix +0.20 → +0.16, sources +0.12–0.19 → +0.09–0.16): at 3e-5 A fires on mix and most sources; at 1e-5 A fires on mix and `pretrain_ext` but most per-source rises fall under 0.03 — if that is what happens, the per-source term needs its ε derived from the paired SE at the nightly LR rather than the 0.03 floor, and the mix + memorized-source terms carry the decision. **A ran (same day; `exp_a7_learning_gate_A_{lr3e-5,lr1e-5}.*`) — VERDICT: the gate separates.** At **3e-5**: B PROMOTE (above) vs **A REJECT** — mix **+0.059** (paired SE 0.0018), **12/12 unseen sources +0.032…+0.074** (all above the 0.03 floor), `pretrain_ext` **+0.208**, GSM8K +0.012, trained source **−0.019**. At **1e-5**: B PROMOTE vs **A REJECT** — mix **+0.017** (SE 0.0006), `pretrain_ext` **+0.053**, the other sources +0.010…+0.024 under the floor, trained source −0.017 — exactly the prediction on record. Null promotes in every run. **Decisions:** (1) the composed gate — paired mix CE, per-source CE, GSM8K-text CE, retrieval health — *is* a gate for trunk updates at this scale; H-A7's first kill does not fire, and neither does the second (a gate evaluation is 37–50 s on an A100, a 3.3M-token proposal 65–77 s). (2) **Nightly LR for the 151M trunk = 3e-5** — the largest A/B margin with B neutral (A's mix margin 0.059 vs B's 0.005). (3) Two refinements, cheap and queued: the per-source ε should be max(floor, 3 × paired SE) with the floor lowered toward the SE scale (at 1e-5 the 0.03 floor hid 11/12 sources whose rises were 10–40× their SE); and the naming term (uniform rise on every source incl. the trained one ⇒ `uniform_regression`; selective rise with the trained source improving ⇒ `forgetting`) so the reason on a rejection is the true one. (4) Optimizer-state restore is not a lever (worse at the same LR); LR is. Not part of the pretraining gate.

**H-A8 — Capping the episodic store at 16384 beyond-window tokens costs nothing measurable, and buys a decode step that can be recorded once and replayed. Added 2026-09-23.** Source: the capture work on `HybridBackbone.step`. The store was three tensors grown by `torch.cat` and read through a host `len()`. Both are fatal to a recorded step — a growing tensor is a new graph signature every token, so a recording is taken and thrown away every token and nothing ever replays; a host `len()` is a value the replay cannot reproduce, because a replay re-issues commands and never runs the Python that computed it. Fixing both means a fixed-capacity store written in place at a slot chosen on the device, and a fixed capacity means that past `capacity` aged-out tokens the oldest entry is overwritten. **Claim:** at `capacity = 16384` (16x the training context, and 32x the 512-token window that gates what reaches the store at all) that eviction is unreachable in any context this model is evaluated at, so the behaviour change is nominal; and the recording it enables is worth more than any recall it could cost. **Why we believe it.** Measured on an RX 6750 XT with `hd5_mem21` (d512/L8, V=127996, `mem_every=2`, window 512), 700 generated tokens: the capture-safe step produces **token-for-token identical** output to the old growing store on real torch and on grilly2, and graph replay takes the token from 61.6 to 102.7 tok/s (1.67x); with the head quantized to int8, 118.4. Eviction never fires in that run — the store holds 193 entries of 16384 — which is the point: at any context under 16896 tokens the fixed store and the growing one hold exactly the same set. **The cost, which is real and is not recall.** The read is sized by capacity rather than by fill, so it scans 16384 rows to find 8 among 193. Measured per episodic read, 4 memory layers: **3.32 ms/token at 16384 against 1.75 ms at 512** — and only 2x for a 64x longer scan, because the cost is dispatch count, not bytes. That is why graph replay at capacity 16384 (102.7 tok/s) is still below the old *eager* step (118.8), while at capacity 1024 it is 125.8. **How to validate.** Run `validation/exp_needle_recall.py` at needle distances past 16384 with `mem_capacity` at 16384 and at a capacity larger than the context, and compare recall; separately, sweep `mem_capacity` over 512...16384 and record tok/s, which is the curve that says what the default should be. **Kill criterion.** Either half kills the default: (a) recall at a distance inside 16384 differs at all between the capped and uncapped stores — that would mean the causal accounting is wrong, not that the capacity is too small, and the bug is in `_memory_step`, not the number; or (b) recall past 16384 matters for a real evaluation, in which case the capacity is a config knob (`HybridBackbone(mem_capacity=)`) and the default moves. A third, softer kill on the *default value* rather than the mechanism: if the scan cost above is not removed by a real top-k selection (grilly2's `topk` is currently sort-then-take), the honest default is the smallest capacity that covers the evaluated context — on the measurements above that is 1024, not 16384.

**H-A9 — Evolution strategies on the program adapter's LoRA factors, scored by the cubelang VM, buys held-out verify-to-gold that supervised fine-tuning has stopped buying — and the gain survives the H-A7 gate. Added 2026-09-23.** Source: the v9 job, and TODO.md's RLVR rung, which already names ES as the gradient-free alternative to GRPO. Two facts set it up. First, **SFT has run out of signal on at least one family**: v14e_nochain is 600/600 on the gen-3 held split (exp_r17, 2026-09-15), so there is nothing there for any optimizer to win. Second, **something did regress and may not have come back**: arithmetic fell 0.825 -> 0.750 -> 0.675 across the v5-v7 rounds as chat volume grew (the two-adapter interference recorded under H0), and each repair was a replay, not a fix. ES is the shape that fits what is left: the reward is the VM's verdict, which is not differentiable, the base is frozen, and only the adapter's low-rank factors move, so a forward-only optimizer on a 12 GB card is enough. **Claim, in two parts.** (a) *Headroom exists*: at least one program family scores below 0.95 verify-to-gold on the held-out split under v14e_nochain Q4. (b) *ES closes some of it*: perturbing only the LoRA factors with rank-1 antithetic noise and scoring each member by the shaped ladder of cubby-lm's TRUNK_VM_EMISSION_CONTRACT §3a (0.1*parses + 0.2*compiles + 0.3*executes + 1.0*(result == gold)) raises that family's **held-out** verify-to-gold over v14e by at least two standard errors, within a fixed budget, while holding every other family at v14e (40/task) and holding the output format. **Why we believe it.** ES needs no gradient through the VM, which is the whole reason the RLVR rung was blocked; EGGROLL-style low-rank perturbation makes a population of 32 a batched forward rather than 32 weight copies, which is what makes it fit beside a 2.6B int8 base; and the thing being tuned is 64-rank factors, not 2.6B parameters, so the search space is the size ES is good at. **What would make the result a lie, and the guards against each.** A program that hardcodes the gold answer scores 1.0 on the ladder and is worthless, so the correctness term counts **only if the same program also passes the counterfactual planter's perturbed numbers and entities** — a hardcoder caps at 0.6. Fitness noise can look like learning, so a **null control** runs the same budget with fitness shuffled across members and must not improve held-out. Train-split leakage can look like learning, so held-out is read every K iterations and never used to select. **How to validate.** Step 0, before anything is built: `standin/eval_emitter_vm.py --gguf <v14e_nochain Q4> --per-task 40` per family on the held-out split, covering arithmetic, kernel, chain, role binding and forge decision/compare. Step 1: ES on the family with the most headroom — population 32 as 16 antithetic pairs, greedy decoding, a fresh 16-32 prompt minibatch from that family's TRAIN split each iteration, held-out every K. Step 2: the candidate goes to the H-A7 gate at its measured thresholds and, if it passes, serves beside v14e for an A/B on the live self-test before anything changes. **Kill criterion, three ways.** (i) *Step 0 kills (a)*: if every family is already >= 0.95, there is nothing to tune and ES would need a new family — a different decision, and the job stops there. (ii) *The budget kills (b)*: if held-out verify-to-gold has not beaten v14e by >= 2 SE within the budget (proposed: 200 iterations), stop and record it; ES at this population and rank is not the tool. (iii) *A regression kills it outright*: a gain on the target family bought with any loss on another family or on output format is a fail, not a trade, because the serving stack runs all of them. **Step 0 result (2026-09-23, RX 6750 XT, v14e_nochain Q4_K_M through
llama.cpp Vulkan, 40 val records per family, 160 total, 3m26s;
`standin/data/out/eval_emitter_vm_v9_step0.json`): claim (a) HOLDS — the
first kill does not fire.** Scored against the val split of
`emitter_sft_v14e_nochain.jsonl`, the file v14e was actually trained on:

| family | n | executes | verify-to-gold | SE | +2 SE |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| arithmetic | 40 | 1.000 | **0.775** | 0.066 | 0.907 |
| kernel | 40 | 0.950 | **0.850** | 0.056 | 0.963 |
| plan | 40 | 1.000 | no gold | — | — |
| role_binding | 40 | 1.000 | no gold | — | — |

Within kernel the game sub-families are already solved — `game:compare`
1.000 (n=6), `game:count` 1.000 (n=10), `game:decision` 1.000 (n=9) — and
the residual sits in bare `kernel`, 0.583 at n=12, too few to target.
`plan` and `role_binding` carry no gold by construction (role binding
returns a bound symbol; there is nothing to match), so verify-to-gold is
not defined for them and they are held at `executes`, both 1.000.

**Two corrections to this entry's own framing, from running it.** First,
the families are not the six listed above: **`chain` does not exist in this
dataset** — v14e's work order WO-1.3 dropped it — which is also why
exp_r17 reports 600/600 on the gen-3 held split, and `forge
decision/compare` are not top-level families but the `kernel/game:*`
sub-groups, both already at 1.000. Second, **`kernel`'s headline 0.850 is
higher than the 0.583 the eval prints for its bare sub-row**, because that
row excludes the game sub-families; by family, arithmetic is the weakest.

**Target: arithmetic.** It is the weakest family, it has by far the largest
held-out pool (329 val records against kernel's 95), and it is the one with
the documented regression history (0.825 -> 0.750 -> 0.675 over v5-v7).
Its 9 misses in 40 are **wrong answers, not malformed programs**: every one
executed, and the VM returned a value that was not the gold (28 vs 40.5,
100 vs 200, 72 vs 1152, 24 vs 6 — ratios and a non-integer, so wrong
operator or wrong operand order rather than a format failure). That is the
shape ES can move; a parse or compile failure would not have been.

**The kill criterion's bar, fixed here rather than left to step 1.** "Beats
v14e by >= 2 SE" is only meaningful once the SE is pinned to a stated
sample. At n=40 the bar is **0.907** — a 0.13 jump, almost certainly out of
reach, which would make the criterion fail by construction rather than on
the merits. So **the held-out read is the whole arithmetic val split, 329
records**, giving SE 0.023 and a bar of **0.821** — a 0.046 jump over
v14e's 0.775. That is the number ES has to clear; 40-record reads are for
the every-K-iterations progress check only and never for the decision.

**Settings, corrected against the EGGROLL paper (2026-09-23).** The ES
implementation was built from a shader audit plus standard ES, not from
the paper (Sarkar et al., *Evolution Strategies at the Hyperscale*,
arXiv:2511.16652). Reading it changed three things before any iteration
ran:

- **The step size was wrong by ~2000x.** The paper absorbs `1/sigma` into
  its learning rate (Eq. 6), so `alpha = lr/sigma` is what sets the step,
  and it uses `alpha = 0.001` across populations from 256 to 65,536
  (Tables 3, 4). This job carried `sigma=0.01, lr=0.02`, i.e.
  `alpha = 2.0` — a **measured per-element step of 7.4 sigma**, seven
  times the radius at which the fitness was measured. It would have
  diverged on iteration 1 and read as "ES does not help here". Now
  `lr=1e-5`.
- **Fitness shaping is now EGGROLL §6.3**, which is what the paper uses
  for its reasoning fine-tunes: linear in the score rather than in the
  rank, so a member that solved the batch outright counts for more than
  one that edged out a tie. `fitness()` therefore returns one ladder
  score **per prompt** and the optimizer takes the (member, prompt)
  matrix. `shaped_sd` is logged per iteration, because unlike centred
  ranks its spread is not fixed.
- **What ES moves stays the LoRA factors**, which is the paper's Table 4
  / §M setup (persistent rank-4 LoRA on all projections, base frozen,
  sigma 0.01) rather than its headline formulation, where the mean
  parameter is the weight matrix and only the *perturbation* is low-rank.
  The difference does not bind here: at population 32 the per-step update
  is rank <= 32 either way, far under the rank-64 adapter's ceiling.

**sigma, measured rather than copied (2026-09-24).** The paper's 0.01 is
meaningless without the scale of what it perturbs, and this adapter's
factors are not the paper's: `std(lora_A) = 1.33e-2` but
`std(lora_B) = 1.72e-3` across all 122 of each, so 0.01 perturbs every
`lora_B` by **5.8x its own standard deviation**. A member that far out is
not a neighbour of v14e, it is a different model — and a different model
does not emit `<|im_end|>`. Since grilly's decode compaction can only drop
rows that *finish*, one non-terminating member holds the whole batch open
to `--max-new-tokens`. Measured, 32 members on one prompt, RX 6750 XT:

| sigma | sigma/std(B) | terminated | decode steps | seconds | distinct outputs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.010 | 5.83 | **31/32** | **768** | **150.7** | 32 |
| 0.006 | 3.50 | 32/32 | 177 | 34.4 | 28 |
| 0.004 | 2.33 | 32/32 | 160 | 31.4 | 26 |
| **0.003** | 1.75 | 32/32 | 99 | **20.0** | 22 |
| 0.002 | 1.17 | 32/32 | 95 | 19.2 | 16 |
| 0.001 | 0.58 | 32/32 | 93 | 18.8 | 5 |
| 0.0005 | 0.29 | 32/32 | 88 | 17.9 | **1** |

Both ends fail and neither raises. Above the knee one hung member costs
the batch **7.5x**; below it every member decodes identical tokens, the
fitness is the same for all 32, and ES takes no step — which is why the
shaped spread is now logged per iteration. **sigma is 0.003** (lr 3e-6, so
alpha stays 0.001). It is the knee on both axes and also maximises
distinct outputs per second.

Two things this surfaced. First, `_centred_ranks` gave a *tied* population
a full spread — a flat objective produced a full-size step in whatever
direction the noise generator happened to order the members. Ties now
share their average rank, so a flat population takes no step (fixed in
grilly, with tests). Second, grilly inherits `repetition_penalty = 1.1`
from LFM2's `generation_config.json` while llama.cpp's default is 1.0, so
it is **another unaccounted difference in the open 0.625-vs-0.800 baseline
comparison**, alongside the BOS token. It costs no measurable time (46.49
vs 46.53 s) — this is a correctness note, not a speed one.

**The run was executed, and claim (b) is in trouble before the budget is
(2026-09-24).** Two iterations at the real shape — population 32,
minibatch 16, sigma 0.003, prompts-per-batch 1, RX 6750 XT:

| | iter 1 | iter 2 |
|---|---|---|
| train fitness mean | 1.1234 | 1.2426 |
| shaped_sd | 0.216 | 0.211 |
| **member effect** | **0.0000** | **0.0000** |
| split-half correlation of member means | **-0.377** | -0.086 |
| seconds | 1057 | 995 |

The member effect is the fraction of score variance that belongs to the
*member* rather than the prompt, calibrated against simulation: pure
scatter reads 0.01-0.02, a real effect reads 0.3-0.9. It reads **zero**,
and `shaped_sd` sits *below* the 0.246 that m=16 noise alone produces.
A member's score on the first eight prompts does not predict its score on
the other eight. Members do differ — they diverge on 12 of 16 prompts —
but they differ in *which* prompts they happen to get right, not in how
good they are. **ES is ranking scatter.**

The arithmetic says why, and it is not fixable by budget:

- **The ladder is binary in practice.** Only two rungs ever occur, 0.6 and
  1.6. v14e always emits a program that parses, compiles and executes, so
  `0.1*parses + 0.2*compiles + 0.3*executes` is a constant and the whole
  reward is the correctness bit. The ladder was put there precisely
  because "a binary right/wrong reward is flat almost everywhere"; on this
  model it *is* binary.
- **One bit times sixteen prompts cannot resolve the effect.** At p=0.523
  and m=16, sampling noise on a member's mean is sd 0.125, while the
  measured spread of member means is 0.054 — the observed spread is *less*
  than chance. Resolving a true 2% quality difference needs m > 624
  prompts per member, i.e. ~20,000 generations an iteration against the
  current 512.

Caveat on power: with 32 members the statistic cannot see an effect below
roughly 5% of total variance, so this shows the effect is small, not that
it is exactly zero. It is small enough that 200 iterations of this design
would rank noise.

**And the starting point is wrong anyway.** Held-out read 0.250 on 8
prompts (SE 0.153) where v14e measures 0.775. That is the same unresolved
grilly2-vs-llama.cpp gap as the 0.625-vs-0.800 arithmetic read — the BOS
fix is applied but unmeasured, and `repetition_penalty = 1.1` was found
on 2026-09-24. **The bar of 0.821 was set from llama.cpp's baseline; it
cannot be chased from a backend scoring 0.15-0.5 below where that baseline
was measured.** Close the baseline gap before spending any ES budget.

Cost, for whenever it is spent: **~1,000 s an iteration** at minibatch 16
(prompts-per-batch 1, which measured 1.24-2.0x faster and far steadier
than 4: 289/284 s vs 360/572 s at minibatch 4, because a batch costs its
longest row and max-of-32 is shallower in the length tail than
max-of-128). 200 iterations is **~56 hours**.

The one gap left is **population size**. Every LLM run in the paper uses
256 to 8192, and its central pretraining finding is that large populations
are what make ES work; 32 is what fits this card serially. If (b) fails on
the budget, read it as a statement about population 32, not about ES.

Status: **step 0 DONE, claim (a) supported; claim (b) PARKED 2026-09-24**
(Nick) before any real iteration. What stands:

- **The engine is built.** grilly2 has `grilly.learning.es`, LFM2, an
  unmerged LoRA with per-row population noise, and (24 Sep) left-padded
  `generate` that drops rows as they finish. `exp_v9_es.py` batches
  members x prompts (`--prompts-per-batch`) and the held-out read
  (`--held-out-batch`, 16 prompts at once: 40/40 texts identical to the
  serial read, 120 s against 357 s).
- **Why parked: the tuned model is not the served one.** On the same 40
  held-out prompts, the emitter through grilly2 (int8, 768 tokens) reads
  26/40 where llama.cpp step 0 read 31/40. ES would climb the grilly
  version; whether a gain carries to what Cubby serves is unknown, so the
  gap comes first if this is picked up again.
- **Throughput ceiling on 12 GB.** 32 members x 1 prompt: 63 s a prompt
  at 768 tokens (serial 122 s). 2 prompts per batch spilled past the card
  because the padded KV cache sits in grilly's pooled (power-of-two,
  retained) memory; at 128 tokens, where it fit, it was 1.5x faster a
  prompt. Fix before reuse: exact-size persistent buffers for
  `PaddedKVCache`. Realistic gain 2-3x, not 10x.

Logs at `validation/logs/exp_v9_es_*.{log,json}`.

**H-A10 — (added 2026-09-24) A nightly sleep cycle turns the day's refusals into the night's curriculum: over successive nights on a fixed battery the refusal share falls while spoken defects stay at zero.** Source: the self-evolution brainstorm (Nick, 24 Sep) under invariants 1 and 6 -- the model proposes, the host disposes, only a gate promotes, every change on a ledger. **Built (v1):** `cubbyllm/reasoning/sleep.py` (replay certified chains into hippocampal episodes; make gate-accepted facts from trusted, non-latent sources durable, loaded at boot by `AskLoop.load_learned`; route every refusal by reason to the queue for the level that must change; audit spoken answers against gold or the asker and deliver every outcome to the striatum; a ledger with the git revision and the input hash; idempotent per night), `AskLoop(history_path=)` and `serve_api --history/--learned`. Pinned by `tests/reasoning/test_sleep.py` and `standin/tests/test_sleep_loop.py` (day 1 fetches, the night consolidates, day 2 answers with the source emptied). **First run** on the SimpleQA search-and-learn bench replayed as three nights (`docs/research/2026-09-24-sleep-cycle.md`): the report shows refusals MOVING (lev9: not-covered 464 -> 338, unknown-relation 56 -> 130, so the next lever is the lexicon), surfaced the one recorded defect (Kafr al-Awamid, a subdistrict spoken for a district) and made 134 accepted facts durable; the ledger is unchanged by re-running all three nights. **Kill:** after five nights on a fixed battery the refusal share has not fallen, or any defect is spoken, or the queues grow while the battery stands still (the loop collecting, not learning). Status: **OPEN -- v1 built and run on recorded days; the queues are not yet acted on (v2) and the ask loop does not yet read the episodes.**

**H-A11 — (added 2026-09-24) Graph-of-thought at an ambiguous hop: following every candidate to the end of the chain and certifying each branch in the VM speaks where the branches agree and names the split where they do not, without a single wrong answer; and in an open world, one pick by the asker recovers what the refusal withheld.** Source: Nick, 24 Sep ("graph of thought with branching also needs some love"), on exp_r31, which branched OUTSIDE the walk. **Built:** `pipeline._explore` / `_converged` (every candidate object a branch, nested splits branch again, bound `branch=12`, each branch through the same `_check_chain` as the greedy walk; speak only if every branch finished, verified and names ONE answer; `closed_world=True` lets a branch the store cannot finish count as refuted, never the default); `answer(choose=)` narrows a hop to a candidate the asker picked and can never add one; `learn` fetches the entity a branch stalled on; `standin/ask.branch_clarify` offers a MID-chain split as choices and never a last-hop one (a pick there would be the answer coming back 'verified' -- the poisoned-capital case); `graph.py` keeps every ask's run as typed nodes and edges (supports, verifies, alternative_of, agrees_with, contradicts, learned_into, retries) in the ask history; the panel draws branches, green where they agree and red where they contradict. **Measured** (`validation/exp_r32_branching_inpipe.py`, the 65 CLUTRR fork records r31 used, real cubelang VM): refuse-on-sight 12/65 spoken; closed world 44/65 (r31 exactly: 12 + 32 recovered), 0 wrong; open world 12/65 (32 held by a branch that could not finish, 18 divergent last-hop sets, 3 VM rejections), 0 wrong; open + one asker pick 44/65, 0 wrong (34 splits offered, all resolved in one round, 2 then VM-rejected at 5-6 hops). Cost: 9x the VM calls on an ambiguous question (439 vs 48 over the 65), none on the rest. **Kill:** any wrong answer spoken through a converged branch set or a choice; or live ambiguous_hop refusals whose splits are mid-chain are rarely resolved by the offered pick (the asker's choice list is the wrong question). Status: **OPEN -- supported on CLUTRR (0 wrong in every arm); not yet measured on live askers.**

**H-A12 — (added 2026-09-24) The web as the source of last resort turns refusals the store cannot fill into verified answers without a single wrong one, because a web triple is only a witness: it is held until two independent sites state it, and the VM still certifies the chain.** Source: Nick, 24 Sep ("need to add websearch to it too to find answers"); the don't-know offer design of 2026-09-04. **Built:** `standin/web_source.py` (Brave / SearXNG; Wikipedia→Wikidata handoff when one item carries the relation, schema.org JSON-LD minus page metadata, entity-opening sentence frames, the local LFM as a grounded, kind-checked reader; sites = registrable domains with the Wikipedia family folded and archives excluded; a persistent tally), `learn_and_answer(fallbacks=)` (the web only after the primary source has nothing more; per-fact provenance and hold), `serve_api --web`, the sleep cycle keeping only two-site facts. Pinned by `standin/tests/test_web_source.py` (16). **Live smoke on real pages** (no search key yet): the reader, unguarded, wrote '112 languages' as a birthplace and '1815-1852' as a birth date; with prose-only passages, grounding and the kind check every value kept was right, and every fact stayed held at one site each -- the rule working. **Kill:** any wrong answer spoken on web facts; or, on a live battery of `retrieval_exhausted` questions with a search key, fewer than one in ten reach two independent sites (the rule holds everything and the web buys nothing). **Measured live** (`validation/exp_r33_web_heldout.py`, 60 withheld wiki-world facts, Brave, real VM, reader off): v1 spoke 12 with 1 WRONG (Agha Hashar Kashmiri's death date, four sites repeating an old Wikipedia lead); v3 -- spellings merged, finer values vouching for coarser ones, one value per relation, copied text counted as one witness, the Wikipedia family speaking through its live article -- spoke 15/60 (11 correct, 4 near, **0 wrong**), held 14 (11 right, 3 wrong held back), split 1, found nothing for 30 (place of death 14/15). Scoring: a containing place answers a PLACE-of-birth question (Nick). Status: **OPEN -- survives the kill clause on 60; the reader arm and a bigger battery are next.**

**H-A13 — (added 2026-09-24) A skill library of composition rules, learned overnight from episodes whose relation gold or an asker states and adopted only with zero counterexamples across the whole record, answers "how is B related to A?" at depths no episode reaches, without a single wrong answer.** Source: Nick, 24 Sep (self-evolution level 3; choices: rules first then CubeLang helpers, zero-counterexample gate, every application through the VM). **Built:** `cubbyllm/reasoning/skills.py` (`Library` replayed from an append-only ledger, retire never delete; `mine` with closure -- a premise is read off every split of an episode the library already composes -- and a gate that re-derives EVERY past episode before a rule gets in; `derive` over every bracketing, a split refused; `relate` over every path between the two, speaking only when the composing paths agree, each certified in the VM with its facts through `pipeline._check_chain`), `TripleIndex.paths`, the ask loop's `relation_ask` / `AskLoop.relation`, the sleep cycle's `skills` phase and four new routes, `serve_api --skills`. Pinned by `tests/reasoning/test_skills.py` (17) and `standin/tests/test_ask_relation.py` (5: day 1 refuses, the night learns, day 2 answers a four-hop question no episode was about). **Measured on CLUTRR's own question** (`validation/exp_r34_clutrr_relations.py`, gen_train23_test2to10, real cubelang VM; train as one day of refused asks with gold, one night, test as day 2): 109 rules adopted in 3 rounds; **827/1,146 correct, 319 refused, 0 wrong; at 4-10 hops, depths no training record reaches, 746/1,003, 0 wrong**; the null control (edges into B relabelled) 0/1,146 spoken; the shuffled library 29 wrong in 150 (the instrument sees wrongs). The whole-record gate rejected 8 rules the 2-hop records alone support -- (son, grandfather) is father 156 times and father-in-law 16, a real ambiguity only 3-hop records show; the 2-hop-only arm adopts them and says nothing wrong on this split by luck, not construction. 206 of the 319 refusals are true bindings recovered at 0.447-0.473 against the served two-binding threshold 0.4736 (exp_r11's lowest observed true binding; the control floor there is ~0.03, exp_r28): a diagnostic at 0.2 reads 1,033/1,146 and 950/1,003 at >=4, still 0 wrong -- recalibrating is Nick's decision, not taken here. Write-up: `docs/research/2026-09-24-skill-library.md`. **Kill:** any wrong relation spoken; or a rule adopted by the gate that a later clean episode contradicts more than once in five nights (the gate is admitting non-functions); or on a live battery the relation asks are refused `no_rule` night after night while their episodes accumulate (the library collecting, not learning). **Second split** (gen_train234_test2to10, train 2-4 hops; `exp_r34_clutrr_relations_gen_train234_test2to10.log`): the gated arm 552/1,048 correct, **0 wrong** (469/903 at >=4); the 2-hop-only ablation 727 correct and **3 WRONG** (*mother* for *mother-in-law*, through a (husband, brother) -> brother rule the whole-record gate keeps out) -- the harness's verdict line counts both arms and reads KILLED; what it killed is the ablation, and the criterion is left as written. The cost of zero counterexamples shows there: 16 premises kept out, some real ambiguities, some apparent label noise ((husband, father): father-in-law 114, father 2), and 395 of the 496 refusals are `no_rule`; the night now lists contested premises for the host (`skills_contested.jsonl`), most one-sided first, and `python -m cubbyllm.reasoning.skills adopt` is the host's word on one (the minority discounted on the ledger; refused if any other episode would derive wrongly). A diagnostic of that word (`..._gen_train234_host_diag.log`): adopting the four premises >=95% one-sided ((husband|wife, father|mother) -> father/mother-in-law) reads 560 correct and **10 wrong** against 552 and 0 -- every one CLUTRR's gold calling a wife's father *father* in a story with a two-hop shortcut beside the proof chain. Scored against gold, the strict gate is what kept the record clean; the host should not adopt these. The robustness splits cannot be measured from this pull (noise edges carry no relation types). **Live teaching:** `POST /ask/feedback` -- an asker's stated relation makes a relation ask an episode; support counts distinct askers, so one asker cannot put a rule in. **Real families (`validation/exp_r35_wikidata_kinship.py`, Wikidata's own P1038+P1039 statements as gold, the family graph gendered with Wikidata's declared inverses, the P279 hierarchy as term grain, 2,050 train / 486 test pairs by subject): KILLED.** 255 rules and 109 inverse rules adopted; the gated arm spoke 70 and got **8 wrong**, the reversed questions 34 with 6 wrong, the inverse arm (store leads only B to A, `relate_back`) 22 with 4 wrong; null 0 breaches, shuffled 18 wrong. Most wrongs are two TRUE relations against one gold (a son-in-law who is also an adopted son; a brother who is also a twin -- the hierarchy says what entails what, not what excludes what) or gold that contradicts the graph; one is a real induction failure (the inverse of *niece* for a woman learned as *father's sister* from brothers' daughters only). Fixed on the way: the null arm had fabricated facts from direct edges (10 false breaches), and `inverse_of` had borrowed coarser terms' inverses through the hierarchy (*ancestor* for a grand-nephew). Status: **CLUTRR: survives (0 wrong, both splits). Real families: KILLED as built -- the next step is a kinship model of CONTRADICTION (generation, line, sex per term), not more rules.**

**H-A14 — (added 2026-09-24) CubeLang helpers mined from the emitter's own verified programs -- two binary steps whose first feeds only the second, as one VM-verified function -- halve the programs the emitter has to write without changing a single result, and an emitter taught to call them gets arithmetic right at least as often as v14e.** Source: Nick, 24 Sep (the skill library's second kind, emitter sub-programs first). **Built:** `cubbyllm/reasoning/helpers.py` (parse the step programs, mine the recurring two-op shapes, bake in a leaf that is mostly one number, rewrite, and gate: every program on record that a helper would rewrite must run in the VM to exactly its own result, then all helpers jointly; retire, never delete, on the skill ledger as `kind: helper`); `eval_emitter_vm.run_vm` supplies the definitions a program calls (`with_helpers`). Pinned by `tests/reasoning/test_helpers.py` (6). **Measured** (`validation/exp_r36_emitter_helpers.py`, v14e_nochain arithmetic, real VM): 32 helpers adopted on train; 5,551/5,819 train and 315/329 val programs rewritten, **0 results moved**, `solve` statements -54.6% / -55.5%; 60/60 val programs written with the calls alone ran through `run_vm`; the 16 helpers with swapped sub/div operands all rejected by the same gate. **Kill:** any rewritten program whose result moves; or, after an adapter round on the rewritten set (`exp_r36/emitter_sft_arithmetic_helpers.jsonl`), held-out verify-to-gold below v14e's 0.775 on the same split (the shorter program bought with wrong answers is a fail). Status: **OPEN -- the rewrite half supported (0 moved); the training half not run.**

## 4. Group B — VSA binding head fix

**H-B1 — cubby-concepts' `vsa/core.py` + `vsa/resonator.py` is usable as CubbyLLM's binding-head algebra, conditionally.** Status: **VERIFIED as working code** (61/61 tests independently reproduced), **PLAUSIBLE as a fit for Cubby specifically**. The four-layer pipeline — `core.py` (bind = elementwise product, bundle = elementwise sum + sign threshold, permute = cyclic shift, all on D=10000 bipolar {-1,+1} int8 vectors), `sdm.py` (Kanerva associative storage), `graph.py` (`SequentialHyperGraph`, chains simple binds for combinatorial reach), `resonator.py` (`ResonatorNetwork`, iterative factorization via continuous-softmax cleanup, RMS renormalization, verified stochastic restarts) — is real, vectorized, honestly documented (it proves and tests a negative capacity result rather than hiding it). F=3 factorization holds 100% accuracy up to codebook size ≈80; F=2 (the plain binding-head case) is trivial to codebook size 1000+. The condition: this only drops in cleanly if CubbyLLM's binding head can operate in bipolar ±1 space and doesn't need to be trainable/differentiable — there are no learned parameters anywhere in this code, `bind`/`bundle`/`permute` are fixed algebra and the resonator's codebooks are pre-generated, not trained. **Validate** by checking whether Cubby's current `VSABindingHead` representations are already bipolar-compatible, or would need a binarization/quantization step (or a low-risk torch port of what are conceptually simple elementwise ops) first.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): condition RESOLVED — binarization is ~free in the operating regime.** Test suite re-reproduced again (61/61, 55.1s). The head's continuous query keeps cos 0.798 with its sign() (the theoretical √(2/π)), and in the regime that matters (query cos≈0.55 to its target codeword) binarized readout keeps **100.0%** top-1 target retention at D=10240, V=4096. (Zero-signal worst case: 17.6% argmax agreement — reported for honesty; not the operating point.) H-B1's bipolar algebra is compatible with the head via a cheap sign(). `validation/exp_b_algebra.py`.

**H-B2 — GrillCheese's `holographic_relation_test.py` + `brain_resonator_test.py` is a second, independently-useful reference implementation.** Flagged by its own survey as "the single most relevant find for CubbyLLM's VSA/HDC binding-head work." Real, dependency-free (NumPy+FFT), tested, runnable code: Holographic Reduced Representation binding (`bind(a,b) = real(ifft(fft(a)*fft(b)))`, circular convolution — a *different* algebra than cubby-concepts' elementwise bipolar bind), unbind via correlation, a genuine resonator network doing the classic Frady/Kent/Olshausen/Sommer iterative decode loop, plus a binary/bipolar "Vulkan-ready" variant (>95% classification accuracy in testing) closer to Binary Spatter Code. Status: PLAUSIBLE, VERIFIED as real runnable code but not benchmarked at CubbyLLM's scale. **Validate** by comparing HRR/circular-convolution against Binary Spatter Code/elementwise-bind directly against Cubby's actual requirements before choosing — they are genuinely different algebras (HRR handles real-valued/continuous vectors more naturally; BSC is simpler and cheaper in hardware) and the choice has real downstream consequences, not just a naming difference.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): head-to-head run at D=10240.** Role-filler unbind from bundled superpositions (100-filler codebook): both algebras hold ≥97% recall out to **n=320 bound pairs** — far beyond any realistic binding-head load; first separation only at n=320 (HRR 100%, BSC 97%). Capacity does not decide this choice; representation compatibility does — HRR unbinds the head's continuous query natively, BSC needs the sign() step H-B1's result just showed is ~free. Note H-B5's confirmation adds a third family (grilly's NVSA block codes) whose docstring targets exactly this continuous-vs-bipolar tension. `validation/exp_b_algebra.py`.

**H-B3 — The current binding head's unbind is a structural placeholder, so this is new-build, not patch work.** Status: **re-check DONE (2026-07-23 campaign) — the claim as written was MISATTRIBUTED, the conclusion survives.** `vsa_binding_head.py` was reread in full: it contains **no `UNBIND_ROLE` and no unbind mechanism of any kind** — it is a clean, well-engineered cosine-readout head (continuous query projection → cosine vs fixed bipolar codebook → τ·softmax; the only `UNBIND_ROLE` in cubby-lm is a tokenizer special-token string). The dict-lookup `UNBIND_ROLE` is **cubemind VM code**: `reasoning/vm.py:602-606`, `_unbind_role` returns `self._role_bindings[reg].get(role)` — confirmed never touching `bc.unbind`. So: real unbind must indeed be built from scratch (candidates H-B1/H-B2/H-B5), but it is VM/reasoning-side work, and the LM head is NOT broken — arguably the strongest piece of predecessor code reviewed.

**Re-confirmed unchanged 2026-08-02, with a detail that makes it worse than "placeholder".** `cubemind/reasoning/vm.py` L602-606 is still `return self._role_bindings[register_name].get(role_name)` — a dict lookup. But the *write* side does real algebra and then discards it: `_bind_role` (L575-600) computes `bound = self.bc.bind(role_vec, filler_vec)` and bundles it into the register (L591-593), then at L598-600 shadows the same association into a plain Python dict — **and the dict is the source of truth**. So the VSA is computed and thrown away. All four `tests/test_vm.py` assertions (L462-486) read through the dict, giving the algebra zero test coverage. Real `bc.unbind` exists (`cubemind/ops/block_codes.py` L157, numpy fallback L411) and six other opcodes use it (`_diff` L610, `_detect_pattern` L630, `_recall_position` L770, `_learn_transformation` L813, `_discover_sequence` L839, `MindForge.verify_duality` L283) — so this is one targeted placeholder, not a repo-wide gap. **Nothing to port for the hard part:** grep for `resonator` across cubemind's Python returns a single hit and it is a comment (`vm.py` L75). No resonator, no iterative factorization, no SDM. `CleanupMemory` (`vm.py` L68) is a brute-force linear scan over a dict, and cubemind's own `vm.md` L213 concedes it "needs C++ with grilly's hamming_topk".

**Update (2026-08-05, reasoning-bridge slice) — real unbind EXISTS and is now Python-reachable; the "no real implementation anywhere" framing is superseded.** The placeholder above is cubemind's *Python* VM. Separately, **cubelang's Rust VM has real VSA unbind** — `op::UNBIND` + cosine cleanup, reachable from a CubeLang program as `use vsa; recover(reg, role)` (built in the cubelang foundation cycle). The reasoning-bridge slice exposed it to Python over a **symbolic boundary** (roles/fillers/program in, a recovered symbol out; no raw hypervector crosses — the trunk's grilly algebra and cubelang's Rust VSA are non-interoperable families): plant a `(role→filler)` binding, recover it through the real unbind+cleanup over **JSON (M1)** and **protobuf (M2)**, against wrong-role (recovers a *different* filler) and absent-role (recovers *nothing*) controls (`cubbyllm/bridges/`, `tests/bridges/`). M2 also surfaces the cleanup **confidence** (the recover cosine as an additive `RunResult.similarity` side-channel — ~0.5 for a clean 2-pair-bundle recovery, `None` when nothing binds) and gates the path with **verify-before-execute** (the reasoning source is `compile_strict`-checked before it runs; a before/after worktree proof confirmed the from-source path was previously unverified). So real unbind is no longer hypothetical or unbuilt: it works in cubelang's VM, Python-callable with confidence + verification. What remains genuinely unbuilt: cubemind's *own* VM unbind (still the dict lookup), and the resonator / iterative-factorization / SDM cleanup for the hard many-item case (grilly's `hamming_topk` is the intended substrate there).

**H-F2 first step (2026-08-05).** This reasoning bridge is the first concrete, tested step toward the Cubby↔CubeMind bridge (H-F2): a controlled Python↔VM reasoning roundtrip over a symbolic boundary. The full bidirectional bridge H-F2 calls for — carrying a context/task embedding in both directions plus generated-parameter/specialist handles (`cubbyllm/bridges/world_model.py`'s `WorldModelBridge` Protocol) — remains the later cycle.

**H-F2 M1 built (2026-08-06) — automatic specialization across the Cubby↔MoWM bridge.** The full bidirectional bridge's first slice is built and tested end to end over the block-code boundary. CubbyLLM poses a `Challenge` (a `(k,l)` grilly block-code) through the `WorldModelBridge` Protocol; MoWM's real `CubbyBridge` adapter (`mowm/bridges/`, on the real 741-green mowm suite) answers it in three tiers: (1) **reuse** a memoized specialist for an exact-tag repeat; (2) **route** to an expert world by cosine similarity of the context to that world's **individual axioms** when the challenge resembles one; (3) **spawn** a new specialist from the nearest axioms when none matches. Proven with three controls: a novel (hash-derived) challenge spawns exactly one world and reuses it (single-spawn control); a challenge equal to a world's axiom routes to it, never spawning (reuse control); and — the load-bearing one — a **near-threshold fuzzy challenge (a real axiom perturbed to cosine ≈0.75, under a fresh tag) routes to that world without spawning**, proving genuine *similarity* routing rather than exact-match. Protocol-first coupling; dependency mowm→cubbyllm only (CubbyLLM stays torch/mowm-free at import). Structure: CubbyLLM ships `WorldModelBridge` + `FakeWorldModel` (`cubbyllm/bridges/`); MoWM ships `CubbyBridge` (`mowm/bridges/`).

**Honest scope of M1 (a load-bearing correction from the whole-branch review).** Routing must run on a world's **individual axiom vectors**, not its `axiom_vec` **signature**: the signature is a `bind`-chain, and VSA binding is quasi-orthogonal to its own inputs, so cosine against it is **inert** — it matches nothing but a hand-constructed exact copy.
