The first shipped adapter routed on the signature (and design spec §4.4, plus an earlier draft of this note, described gate-weight routing and "a spawned world enters the active pool" idempotence); **none of that was functional** — the review caught it, and routing was re-based on individual axioms (`AxiomLibrary.select`'s basis) so the similarity tier is real. Even so, that tier fires only for contexts near the library's **axiom basis**; at toy scale the tests construct such contexts synthetically, because there is no organic context-encoding pipeline yet. **Whether real trunk-inferred contexts land near axioms is a separate, unvalidated data question** — the honest M1 claim is "specialization via exact-tag memoization + spawn-on-novel + *demonstrated* (not yet organically-fed) individual-axiom similarity routing." Exact-repeat idempotence is guaranteed by the `tag → world_id` memo regardless of routing. Cross-world data-sharing, SR axiom discovery, imagination rollouts, and multimodal challenges remain later slices.

**H-F2 M2 screened (2026-08-06) — the semantic-encoding gate is CLOSED, and the mowm-side path was deliberately NOT built.** M2 took the data question M1 deferred (*do organically encoded contexts land near axioms, so the similarity tier fires?*) and ran it as a screen with a kill criterion rather than as a build: mowm's own **280 axioms across 15 domains**, exported once to `data/mowm_axioms.json`; leave-one-out over all 280; **cross-view** (seeds encoded from `formula_str`, challenges posed as `name`); routing scored exactly the way `CubbyBridge._best_match` routes — max cosine to a world's *individual* axioms, with the axiom's own formula excluded — at the decided k=80 / l=128 algebra. **Three of Stage 1's four bars FAILED** (`validation/logs/exp_f2m2_stage1.json`): bar 1 baseline sanity **PASS**; bar 2 encoder sanity (best trunk self-retrieval top-1 ≥ 0.50) **FAIL at 0.029**; bar 3a (best trunk macro ≥ 2× macro-chance, i.e. ≥ 2/15 = 0.1333…) **FAIL at 0.0962 = 1.44×**; bar 3b (best trunk ≥ 0.60 × `ref`) **FAIL at 0.269**. The decision rule's `trunk < 0.60 × REF` branch fired, recorded in the log as `"fine-tune (M3) — gap is large"`, so the design's §4.3 live confirm and its `mowm/encoding/semantic_axioms.py` **were never built**. **mowm has no semantic axiom path**: `WorldEncoder`'s hash encoding is untouched and M1's shipped bridge is unchanged. A closed gate is M2's deliverable, not an unfinished milestone.

**Premise CONFIRMED — hash encoding routes at chance, and against the matched null "at chance" is now a direct measurement rather than an inference.** The `hash` arm (BLAKE2b-seeded one-hot-per-block, M1's status quo, reimplemented in ~5 lines so the screen imports neither mowm nor cubemind) scores **macro 0.0747** and self-retrieval **0.0036**, i.e. 1 axiom in 280 (`exp_f2m2_stage1.json`). Bar 1's ceiling was `1.2 × macro-chance = 0.0800`, so the PASS is near-binding, not decorative — and it only became so after the methodology corrections below. The stronger statement comes from the measured null: a **pure-noise one-hot encoder** in exactly `hash`'s format scores **macro 0.0666 ± 0.0125** over 10 seeds (`validation/logs/exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json`), so `hash`'s 0.0747 is **+0.6 sd — statistically indistinguishable from noise**, and its self-retrieval 0.0036 matches the noise floor of 0.0029 ± 0.0033 exactly. This is the sentence M1 could not write: BLAKE2b really does destroy the locality similarity-routing needs, so the similarity tier cannot fire organically against hash-encoded axioms.

**The `hash` arm's small excess over `1/15` is an argmax tie-breaking artifact, and it is now attributed rather than left loose.** One-hot-per-block cosine takes only 81 values at K=80, so exact ties are routine: **59.6% of `hash`'s routing decisions are ties, mean tied group 3.21 candidates**, against **55.9% ± 4.1%** for the matched one-hot noise null (`exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json`). `np.argmax` breaks every tie toward the **lowest index**, and `data/mowm_axioms.json` stores its 15 domains as **contiguous runs with `atomic` first**, so the pull is systematic. It shows up in the null itself: one-hot noise inflates the two lowest-index domains (`atomic` **0.1529** against its own baseline of 0.0573, `bio` **0.1467** against 0.0502) and *depresses* the largest one (`physics` **0.1595** against 0.2975), which is exactly the shape of `hash`'s real per-domain profile (`atomic` 0.2941, `physics` 0.2262, and zero in eight domains). The excess is a property of the tie-break, not of the encoder — and its direction makes bar 1 *harder* to pass, so the PASS remains trustworthy.

**On micro, report both baselines — and the matched one is what changes the readings.** The **majority-class rate, 0.300**, is a legitimate strong baseline (what "always guess physics" scores), and only `ref` (0.450) clears it. But the **matched null for an argmax-over-279-candidates metric** is `Σ_d n_d(n_d−1) / (280·279) = 9576/78120 = 19/155 =` **0.1226**, reproduced empirically by the noise encoder at **0.1232 ± 0.0074** (`exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json`). Against *that* floor: `hash` **0.1179** sits essentially **on** it — 0.0047 below the exact value, and **+1.40 sd against its own format-matched one-hot null (0.0921 ± 0.0184)**, or −0.73 sd against the dense null (0.1232 ± 0.0074); |z| ≤ 1.4 either way, so the routes-at-chance reading holds under both — corroborating that finding on a second metric; `t-bag` **0.1107** is −1.7 sd; `ngram` **0.1036** is −2.7 sd; and **`t-ctx` 0.0786 is −6.0 sd, i.e. mean-pooled trunk states route *worse than a random encoder* on micro** — a stronger and less comfortable result than "worse than always guessing physics." Only `ref` clears both baselines (+44 sd). **Read this as a property of the representation, not a defect in the model**: pooled next-token states are not merely uninformative for routing, they are *anti*-informative on this metric — the anisotropic cone (unrelated cosine 0.756) concentrates every text near one direction, so what little cosine ordering survives is dominated by something other than domain. Noise at least has no systematic structure to mislead with; a strongly anisotropic embedding does. That is exactly why M3's answer is a separate encoder component rather than more trunk (see below), and it is also why `t-ctx`'s *macro* can sit +3.7 sd **above** its null while its micro sits 6 sd below — the two metrics genuinely disagree about this arm, and both readings are reported rather than one being chosen.

**An LM trunk is not a retrieval encoder — and M2's real contribution is quantifying how far it isn't.** State the framing before the numbers, because the earlier version of this paragraph read as the trunk *failing*, which mis-describes what was measured. `hd5_mem21.pt` is a **causal language model**. Its *training* objective is next-token cross-entropy (`cubbyllm/training/loop.py:124`; this run drives that loop, `train_colab.py:478`) plus exactly two additive terms, **neither of which is a similarity, contrastive, or retrieval objective over sentence representations**: the θ=f(c) hardener's anti-drift penalty on *generated parameters* (`loop.py:126-129`), and a VSA binding aux loss **gated by `bind_weight` and hard-defaulted to 0** (`loop.py:130-137`; `CB_BIND_W` sits behind a `LEAVE AT 0` guard at `train_colab.py:95-98`) — the one similarity-shaped loss this project ever had, **falsified and switched off** in H-B6, with this run confirmed at `bind_weight=0` two independent ways (see the long-horizon note below). So nothing in the objective ever asked the model to make two texts about the same thing land near each other. **Mean-pooling its hidden states is a convention borrowed from encoder-style models — not an interface the trunk was trained to expose.** Its measured anisotropy (unrelated-sentence cosine **0.756**, `exp_f2m2_selftest.log`) is the *expected* geometry of a next-token objective, not a defect. So the arms below measure an LM at a task it was never built for; that is a legitimate and useful measurement, and it is **not** a verdict on the model at its actual job (generation, and carrying context for θ=f(c)). Both trunk arms were built from the real 92k-step checkpoint (`$CUBBY_MODELS\hd5_mem21.pt`, blake2b-128 `6c9f6ddff5342e268cac805615d0d74a`, stamped in `exp_f2m2_stage1.log`): `t-bag` = mean of trained token-embedding rows, `t-ctx` = full forward, mean-pooled. `t-ctx` won at **macro 0.0962 (1.44× chance), self-retrieval 0.029**, against `t-bag` **0.0754 (1.13×)** and the `ref` MiniLM-L6-v2 arm's **0.3580 (5.37×), self-retrieval 0.229** — `trunk/ref = 0.269` (all `exp_f2m2_stage1.json`). The **per-domain breakdown explains the weakness rather than merely reporting it** — but only once each domain is read against **its own** baseline. **The per-domain null is not flat: it is `(n_d − 1)/279`**, ranging from **0.0323** for a 10-member domain (`identity`) to **0.2975** for `physics` (84 members), because the metric takes an argmax over the other 279 items and a domain is hit in proportion to the candidates it owns (`validation/logs/exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json` tabulates all 15, analytic and empirical). Read that way: `t-ctx` reaches **linguistics 0.500** against a 0.0394 baseline (**12.7×**) and **language 0.400** against 0.0502 (8.0×), but **physics 0.0357 against 0.2975 — 0.12×, far *below* chance for that domain**, where `ref` reaches **0.738** (2.5×). `ref`'s **linguistics 0.833** is likewise a **21×** multiple of its baseline, a much larger one than its headline physics 0.738 — the flat-baseline reading had these two backwards. **8 of 15 `t-ctx` domains score exactly 0.0**, which is a weaker signal than it looks for the small ones (a 10–12 member domain's baseline is only 0.032–0.039, so a single miss-free run is unremarkable) and a strong one for `physics`, whose 0.298 baseline makes 0.0357 a real deficit. The overall shape still holds: a general-text LM with real language signal and essentially none on symbolic notation, which is most of what this corpus is made of. Contextual pooling does beat bag-of-embeddings, so the trunk body contributes something — just nowhere near enough to serve as a routing encoder, which it was never trained to be.

**What follows for M3 is a component boundary, not a repair job.** The question is not "how do we fix the trunk" — **M2 measured nothing about generation, so none of this is evidence about the trunk's performance at its actual task, in either direction** — but that **the routing encoder is a separate component from the trunk**, and M2 has now costed the alternatives. Three candidates, in rough cost order. **(a) A small purpose-built or fine-tuned encoder**, which the `ref` arm sizes — and the size comparison has to be **like-for-like**, which the obvious "22M beat 153M" phrasing is not. MiniLM-L6-v2's **22.7M total** reaches macro 0.3580 where the trunk's **153.4M total** reaches 0.0962; but **131.1M of the trunk's total is two untied `127996 × 512` vocab tensors** (2 × 127,996 × 512 = 131,067,904 of 153,383,440), leaving a **22.3M body** — against MiniLM's own **11.0M body** (the remaining 11.7M being its word-embedding table). **The honest read cuts both ways:** the trunk is not 6.8× the size of the thing that beat it, so raw scale does not explain the loss; but its *body* is still about **2× MiniLM's body** and loses by 3.7×, so "it is merely smaller" does not explain it either. (Counts derived from the pinned checkpoint's own tensor shapes — identity stamped in `exp_f2m2_stage1.log` — and from the pinned `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2`; the design spec's §3 makes the same body-vs-total point.) **(b) The training-free lexical route**, which is cheaper than it looks — see the `ngram` result above and the mechanical note there: `ngram` and `hash` share the *same* BLAKE2b machinery, so the finding is that **whole-string** hashing destroys locality, not that BLAKE2b does, which makes upgrading `WorldEncoder._hash_to_vec` inside the existing hash architecture a near-free lever rather than a rewrite. **(c) A trained projection head *on top of* the trunk** — the only option that reuses the trunk **legitimately**, by learning the encoder interface rather than assuming mean-pooling already is one. Note that (c) is also the only one that keeps a single model serving both roles, which is what the θ=f(c) story wants; M2 says nothing about whether it works, only that it is the honest form of the question.

**Uncomfortable control: a training-free lexical sketch beats the trained trunk.** The `ngram` arm — character-trigram block codes bundled by superposition, **no model, no training, no projection**, born directly in block space — reaches **macro 0.1400 = 2.10× chance** with self-retrieval **0.100** (`exp_f2m2_stage1.json`), beating both trunk arms and being **the only non-`ref` arm to clear the 2×-chance bar that bar 3a asks of the trunk**. Its macro is **+8.2 sd above the matched noise null of 0.0599 ± 0.0098** (`validation/logs/exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json`), so the win is real and not a baseline artifact. **The qualifier this branch's own micro/macro correction demands: `ngram` is mid-pack on micro and below `hash` there** — 0.1036 against `hash`'s 0.1179, and **−2.7 sd below the matched micro null of 0.1226**. So the honest claim is narrower than "the sketch beats the trunk": it beats both trunk arms on the **macro** metric and on self-retrieval, while its **micro** sits below the random-encoder floor like every non-`ref` arm. Macro is the metric the kill criterion uses and the one that is not dominated by physics's 30% share, so the comparison stands — but it is a macro claim, not a blanket one. Two consequences, both honest ones. (a) `hash` should not be presented as the only baseline the trunk had to clear: `ngram` is a *stronger* training-free null, and the trunk loses to it. (b) M3's question is therefore not just "fine-tune an embedder" but **"why does a next-token LM carry less domain-routing signal than raw lexical overlap?"** — a question about what the objective builds, not about model quality. **(c) The mechanically important detail, easy to miss: `ngram` and `hash` share the *same* BLAKE2b machinery** — identical `blake2b(..., digest_size=8)` → `int.from_bytes(..., "little") % 2**63` → `default_rng(seed).integers(0, L, size=K)` seeding and index draw (`validation/exp_f2m2_semantic_routing.py:115-147`). They differ in exactly two ways: `hash` hashes the **whole string** and *assigns* one-hot, while `ngram` hashes **character trigrams** and *accumulates* (bundles) them. So the premise M2 confirmed is **"whole-string hashing destroys locality," not "BLAKE2b does"** — and the fix lives inside the existing hash architecture rather than replacing it, which is why this is the cheap lever in M3's option (b) below.

**Discretization is INCONCLUSIVE — and M2's earlier "quantization preserves, even improves, routing" claim is RETRACTED.** The shipped Stage-2 numbers ordered **`pq-adc` 0.4188 > `pq` 0.3719 > `dense` 0.3580 > `argmax` 0.3133** on `ref` (`exp_f2m2_stage2_ref.json`) and the same way on `t-ctx` (**0.1068 > 0.1030 > 0.0962 > 0.0736**, `exp_f2m2_stage2_t-ctx.json`), with bar 4 recorded PASS on both. Review found the cause: **the PQ codebook was fit on the very items leave-one-out exists to exclude.** At 280 axioms over `L=128` centroids that is ~2.2 points per centroid, so a candidate's reconstruction can carry a fraction of the query's own formula — a channel `dense` does not have. The tell was already in the table: `argmax`, the only discretizer with no fitted codebook, was the only variant that *lost* to dense. Two controls followed (`exp_f2m2_leakage_ref.json`, `exp_f2m2_leakage_t-ctx.json`), both logging the verdict `"gain FAILS both controls -- shipped result was leakage"`. Under the **item-disjoint control** (fit on a stratified half, score only the other half, `n_scored = 146`) **the ordering inverts and PQ collapses**: on `ref`, `argmax` **0.3281** now beats `dense` **0.3017** while `pq` falls to **0.1971** and `pq-adc` to **0.2116**; on `t-ctx`, `pq` **0.0828** and `pq-adc` **0.0778** fall below `dense` **0.1276** (`argmax` **0.1149** sits just under dense there, so the argmax-overtakes-dense inversion is `ref`-only). **What is unanimous across both arms is that `pq` and `pq-adc` drop below `dense` once the codebook cannot see the scored items.** The **source-disjoint control** (codebook fit on the `name` view) is mixed and arm-specific: on `ref`, `pq-adc` 0.3874 > dense 0.3580 but `pq` 0.3378 < dense; on `t-ctx` the two swap (`pq` 0.0976 > dense 0.0962, `pq-adc` 0.0821 < dense). **Do not read this as "PQ hurts" either.** Neither control is clean — the name-fit control confounds surface modality (it fits on natural language and scores symbolic notation), and the half-split control's codebook is fit on ~134 points across 128 centroids (~1.05 per centroid), degenerate enough that it may *understate* PQ. **The correct conclusion is that this corpus cannot settle the question**, with a concrete requirement for one that can: **n ≫ L — at least ~1280 axioms for L=128 — with a fit set disjoint from the scored items.** ADC remains architecturally sensible on its own terms (only the stored side must be one-hot to participate in `bind`/`bundle`; a routing query never needs quantizing) but it has **no evidence of improving routing**. The one dense-vs-one-hot fact that survives the disjoint control is bar 4's actual question — one-hot-per-block does not *destroy* routing relative to dense, though under control B that holds via `argmax` (0.3281 vs 0.3017 on `ref`; 0.1149 vs 0.1276, still ≥ 0.80× dense, on `t-ctx`), not via `pq`. **A note on the two exported artifacts, because they outlived the conclusion they encode:** `data/axiom_embeddings_ref.npz` and `data/axiom_embeddings_t-ctx.npz` store the Stage-2 embeddings together with `discretization="pq"`, that variant's `tau_match`, and `centroids` — *the leakage-contaminated codebook itself*. They are a **record of what the screen produced, not a configuration to load**: nothing in CubbyLLM or mowm reads them, and the only consumer they were ever written for (the mowm-side semantic path, the plan's Task 7) was gated out by the Stage-1 kill criterion and never built. Both files now carry that retraction as `note`/`retracted` string fields **inside the npz** so a future reader cannot lift `tau_match` or `centroids` out of them without seeing it.

**The ROC-AUC null on this corpus is ~0.11, not 0.5 — so the earlier "every AUC is below 0.5, therefore nothing separates" reading is WRONG, and correcting it makes the arms look better, not worse.** AUC here is the pooled, unpaired Mann-Whitney statistic over all same-domain maxima against all different-domain maxima. But `same_cos[i]` is a **max over the (n_d − 1) same-domain candidates** while `diff_cos[i]` is a **max over the (n − n_d) different-domain candidates** — roughly **14 against 265** on this corpus — and the max of more draws is stochastically larger, so this statistic's null sits **far below 0.5 by construction**. That is a property of the candidate sets, not a diagnosis of the encoders. Measured rather than argued: pushing a **pure-noise encoder** through the *shipped* `loo_domain_routing` / `recalibrate_tau` over the real corpus's domain structure, 10 seeds, gives an AUC null of **0.1133 ± 0.0072** in the dense code format and **0.1605 ± 0.0162** in the one-hot format (`validation/logs/exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json`). The control that proves it is a candidate-set-size artifact: on a **balanced** two-domain structure over the same 280 items (≈139 against 140 candidates), the same noise gives **0.5131 ± 0.0191** — 0.5, exactly as an AUC null should be when the two candidate sets are the same size.

**Against that measured null, six of the eight recorded AUCs show real separation, one is marginal, and one is genuinely below chance.** Reading each variant against the null for its own code format (`dense` and `pq-adc` compare continuous vectors → the 0.1133 null; `argmax` and `pq` compare one-hot codes → the 0.1605 null): on `ref`, `pq-adc` **0.454** (≈ +47 sd), `dense` **0.430** (≈ +44 sd), `pq` **0.427** (≈ +16 sd) and `argmax` **0.358** (≈ +12 sd) all sit far above; on `t-ctx`, `pq-adc` **0.246** (≈ +18 sd) and `dense` **0.227** (≈ +16 sd) do too; `argmax` **0.182** is **marginal** (≈ +1.3 sd, only just past the null's observed maximum of 0.180); and `pq` **0.0915** sits **below** its null (≈ −4.3 sd) — the one arm the original blanket claim actually holds for, and notably the variant whose τ of **0.850** comes from the leakage-contaminated codebook retracted above (`exp_f2m2_stage2_ref.json`, `exp_f2m2_stage2_t-ctx.json`, nulls from `exp_f2m2_null_baseline.json`). So the honest statement is **`ref` separates strongly, `t-ctx` separates weakly but really, and only `t-ctx`'s `pq` is at-or-below chance** — not "nothing separates."

**What survives, on better grounds: no M2 number is a usable `tau_match`, and M1's `0.35` is not replaced by any of them.** Separation being above its null is not the same as a transferable threshold, and four independent reasons block the transfer. (a) **The τ values live in incomparable spaces**: `t-ctx`'s `pq` τ of 0.850 is a one-hot block-code cosine (81 levels at K=80), its dense τ of 0.257 is a continuous cosine, and M1's 0.35 is a k=4/l=32 one-hot cosine (five levels). (b) The **corpus is cross-modal** (`name` against `formula_str`), so its same/different distributions are not production's. (c) The `pq` τ specifically is an artifact of the **retracted** leakage result. (d) Even the best AUC here, `ref`'s 0.454, is weak *in absolute terms* — a screen result, not a calibration. Treat every τ in `exp_f2m2_stage2_*.json` and in the exported `.npz` files as a record of what this screen measured, never as a setting to load.

**The corpus poses a harder task than production would — a calibration caveat on bar 2, not an excuse.** Even `ref` reaches only **0.229** self-retrieval (`exp_f2m2_stage1.json`), because the cross-view design pairs natural-language `name` against symbolic `formula_str`: a **cross-modal** match, harder than routing a natural-language question to a domain. Bar 2's 0.50 was calibrated for a same-modality task, so read that FAIL as diagnostic rather than decisive — while noting the caveat was written *after* the run and retroactively softens a bar that failed. The metric also has a ceiling below 1.0: two `name` values (*De Broglie Wavelength*, *Boltzmann Distribution*) each appear twice with different formulas, embed identically, and share one argmax, so self-retrieval tops out at **278/280 = 0.993** (`validation/exp_f2m2_semantic_routing.py:339-346`, `self_retrieval_top1`'s docstring). This points at a cheap first M3 experiment before concluding the embedder is the bottleneck: encode axioms from **combined** text (name + domain + formula) and pose natural-language questions against them — the production shape — rather than name-against-formula.

**Four methodology corrections, recorded because this is exactly what rots silently — and one error class caught three times.** (a) The kill criterion compared **macro** accuracy against the **majority-class rate (0.300)**, which is a *micro* baseline. Macro-averaging removes class imbalance by construction, so every constant classifier — the majority-class strategy included — and uniform-random guessing all score exactly `1/n_domains`. The category error made bar 1 trivially satisfied and set bar 3a at an unreachable macro of 0.600; against the correct macro-chance of 0.0667 the bars become 0.0800 exactly (near-binding, actual 0.0747) and 2/15 = 0.1333… (reachable, actual 0.0962). Both chance levels and both actuals are in `exp_f2m2_stage1.json` (`micro_chance`, `macro_chance`, `arms.hash.macro`, `arms.t-ctx.macro`); the two bounds are `1.2 ×` and `2 ×` macro-chance per the design's §4.2. **The measured verdict did not change** — the bars were wrong, the answer was not. (b) The design spec claimed challenges were also posed in the reverse direction; **they never were** (`run_stage1` measures name→formula only, and both directions share one symmetric cosine matrix), so the claim was dropped from the spec rather than quietly left standing. (c) **The whole-branch review found (a) recurring in three more places** — ROC-AUC read against 0.5, micro read only against the majority-class rate, and per-domain recall read against one flat number, when each of those statistics is an **argmax over ~279 candidates** whose null is set by candidate-set sizes rather than by class priors. All three are now measured rather than assumed, with a **pure-noise encoder** pushed through the *unchanged* shipped metrics over the real corpus's domain structure and over a **balanced** control structure, 10 seeds each (`validation/exp_f2m2_semantic_routing.py --null-baseline` → `validation/logs/exp_f2m2_null_baseline.log` / `.json`). The measured nulls: **AUC 0.1133 ± 0.0072** dense / **0.1605 ± 0.0162** one-hot on the corpus structure against **0.5131 ± 0.0191** on the balanced one; **micro 0.1232 ± 0.0074** (analytic 19/155 = 0.1226); **macro 0.0599 ± 0.0098** dense / **0.0666 ± 0.0125** one-hot (analytic 53/837 = 0.0633, close to but not equal to the 1/15 the bars use); **self-retrieval 0.0032 ± 0.0026**. **Two of the three corrections made findings stronger, not weaker** — `hash` routing at chance became a direct measurement, and `t-ctx`'s micro turned out to be *below* the random-encoder floor — while the third **reversed** a claim: "no arm separates same-domain from different-domain" was an artifact of the 0.5 null and is retracted above. The lesson is the one this milestone had already learned once at bar 3a: **a metric's baseline is a property of the metric's own procedure, and must be derived (or measured) from that procedure, not borrowed from a familiar number.** (d) **The same species of error at the level of the design rather than the statistic: bar 3b (`trunk ≥ 0.60 × REF`) and the decision rule's "ship the trunk" branch treated the trunk as an *embedder candidate*, which the architecture never claimed it was.** The trunk's role is generation and carrying context for θ=f(c); it has no similarity, contrastive or retrieval objective in its loss, so a bar asking it to come within 0.60× of a purpose-trained sentence encoder was measuring it against a standard that was never the right one — a *category* mismatch, exactly like (a) and (c)'s *baseline* mismatches, just one level up. **The bar's FAIL verdict stands and its consequence was correct** (do not wire a semantic path the screen showed does not route), but the right reading of the FAIL is "an LM trunk is not a retrieval encoder," not "our trunk underperformed." **Three instances of one error class in one milestone is itself the finding**: whenever this project compares a measured thing to a reference, the next question should be *is this reference the right null/standard for this exact procedure?* — asked before the run, not after it.

**Two supporting measurements, from the screen's own self-test (`validation/logs/exp_f2m2_selftest.log`).** The orthonormal projection is cosine-exact as designed: the self-test asserts `max|Δ| < 1e-5` between pre- and post-projection cosine matrices (`validation/exp_f2m2_semantic_routing.py:852-857`, inside `self_test`) and measures **8.77e-09**, ~3 orders inside the bar. Note the **assertion bound is the enforced claim**; the printed value is the measurement it passed by, not a separately guarded invariant. Separately, `t-ctx` is strongly **anisotropic** — unrelated-sentence cosine **0.756**, against `t-bag`'s **−0.019** — i.e. mean-pooled LM hidden states occupy a narrow cone. That is a *candidate explanation* for weak routing (a centering/whitening step is the standard remedy, and an M3 candidate), **not** evidence of structure, and it is corroborated independently by the logged τ 0.850 / AUC 0.0915 fingerprint above. Every figure in these paragraphs links a file in `validation/logs/`.

**H-F2 M3 fast-encoder screen (2026-08-07) — 0.86–0.95× the teacher's routing quality with no model in the challenge path.** The user set the binding constraint directly: embedders are too slow for serving, so the challenge-side encoder must be model-free (axioms are encoded offline regardless, per H0's frozen shape). Measured on the same 280-axiom LOO screen against the same matched nulls, with per-challenge latency: **(1) model-free lexical arms** — words+IDF reaches macro **0.1730** (2.73× null) at **~19 µs**, BPE token-ids+IDF **0.1520** at ~29 µs using ids the pipeline already holds, and a K-sweep 80→320 buys only +6% relative at 4× memory, so dimensionality is a mild constraint rather than the lever (`exp_f2m2_fast_arms.{log,json}`). **(2) BEAGLE distributional memory is a NEGATIVE** — accumulating each token's co-occurrence neighbors over 200M wiki_full tokens *degrades* routing (pure memory 1.0–1.3× null; self-retrieval collapses to 0.025; blending the signal back recovers only partway, 0.1204): superposition smears exactly the surface-overlap signal this task runs on. Side-finding: raw-case vs lowercased costs ~0.04 macro on its own (`exp_f2m2_beagle.{log,json}`). **(3) The result: model2vec-style static distillation** — run MiniLM **once, offline**, over the vocabulary's word strings (2.3 s here), strip the anisotropy direction (mean+PC1) from the table, project through the cosine-exact orthonormal map into (K,L) block space, and 0.5-blend each word's vector with its seeded one-hot signal code so exact surface overlap keeps contributing; serve by table lookup + IDF-weighted adds. Winner: **macro 0.3407 (5.38× null) = 0.95× the live teacher at ~25 µs/challenge** — ~350× faster than the teacher's 8.9 ms single-query — with every ingredient attributable (words-over-tokens; −PC1 +0.067; +signal +0.021; learned-over-random table values ≈ 2×) (`exp_f2m2_m2v.{log,json}`). **Honesty check passed:** refitting IDF and PC1 on external wiki_full text (20k docs; 744/848 axiom words covered) drops the winner to **0.3079 (4.86× null, 0.86× teacher)** — the in-domain statistics were worth ~0.033 and the result survives out-of-domain stats (`exp_f2m2_m2v_ood.{log,json}`). Caveats: the benchmark is the same 280-axiom cross-modal screen (its caveats above apply); latencies are unoptimized Python (the Rust VM / Vulkan path can only lower them); no τ here is a transferable operating point (the τ-caveat paragraph above applies unchanged, and route-misses fall through to spawn, so routing errors are soft). **Wired into mowm** as `mowm/encoding/semantic_words.py` — an offline `build`/teacher pass and a serve-time `FastWordEncoder` that imports only numpy.

**H-F2 M3 production screens + the centroid/margin wiring (2026-08-07).** The production table was built (`$CUBBY_MODELS\fastword_table_v1.npz`, 30,275 words = top-30k corpus DF ∪ all 901 axiom words, broad-corpus IDF + PC1 per the OOD form; axiom-corpus eval macro **0.2993 = 0.84× teacher**, self-retrieval 0.161, build log beside the artifact) and screened on real data three ways. **(1) 42-domain routing** (`exp_m3_domain_routing.{log,json}`; $CUBBY_DATASETS\domains, 34 seeded + 5 held out): macro/micro **0.5946 vs measured noise null 0.0339** at ~1.1 ms/passage, **0.75× MiniLM** (0.7973 at ~15 ms batched); first production-calibrated τ = 0.4518; the weak half quantified — open-set spawn AUC 0.624. **(2) Open-set scorer screen** (`exp_m3_open_set.{log,json}`; 7 scorers × 3 rotated hold-outs): **centroid routing beats the shipped max-over-individual-axioms rule 0.680 vs 0.589 macro at ~40× fewer comparisons** — empirically vindicating the M1 whole-branch reviewer's bundled-signature proposal (a centroid is the similarity-preserving bundle; the bind-chain stays inert) — and **margin gating** (best − second-best) yields the right operating profile: 92% precision on what routes, 87% of never-seen-domain challenges sent to spawn; per-domain z-norm calibration is a recorded negative (0.435); MiniLM ceiling 0.825, i.e. separation *confidence* is the teacher's remaining edge. **(3) Real questions** (`exp_m3_science_qa.{log,json}`; the 238-row tagged stratum inside science_qa_prep — n=60, 3 tags, small-n caveats recorded): Q→A retrieval hit@1 **0.667** (0.70× teacher, chance 0.017) and tag routing **0.900 = MiniLM parity** at 13× its batched speed — natural-question→explanation is far easier than M2's synthetic name→formula (0.16), confirming that write-up's production-shape prediction. **Wired (mowm `3c217f9`, suite 754 green):** `World.axiom_centroid` (normalized mean of individual axioms) + `CubbyBridge` tier-2 = centroid scores with an absolute floor AND a `tau_margin` gate, ambiguity falling through to spawn; the three M1/M2 kill-criterion tests pass unchanged under the new rule, and three new tests pin centroid similarity-preservation, tie→spawn, and the single-world floor. Both taus are deployment-calibrated constructor parameters — the screens are the calibration method, and no τ transfers across encoders or corpora (the M2 lesson, unchanged).

**H-F2 M3 scale + memory-pathway evals (2026-08-07) — the haystack curve and the injection/chase result.** Two follow-on evals on the production table, both on $CUBBY_DATA_ROOT/valid_scaling_law_with_facts.pq (10k multi-hop questions; every row's answer verified to live in its final supporting fact, and its designated `fact_to_inject` verified present in the chain, 10000/10000) with DBpedia abstracts as distractors. **(1) Haystack** (`exp_m3_haystack.{log,json}`; 400 questions, 636 target facts): the dense form is flat to 100k distractors (any-supporting-fact hit@1 0.978→0.973; MiniLM anchor 0.993), and the **compact 80-byte form** (argmax index per block — the qFHRR integer-phase reading, ADC-scored with no fitted codebook) degrades gracefully to **0.713 hit@1 / 0.920 hit@20 at 1M documents**, having encoded the 1M store in ~9 min (543 µs/doc) vs ~30 min for the batched teacher — implying the two-stage serve shape: compact shortlist@20 + dense re-rank. **(2) Injection accessibility + multi-hop chase** (`exp_m3_injection.{log,json}`; 800 questions): once a fact is injected into the store, the question reaches it top-5 at **0.616 = 0.95× teacher** (0.649) — but both encoders sit on the same **surface-form cliff by hop** (table 0.995/0.289/0.226 across 1/2/3 hops; teacher 1.00/0.34/0.30): intermediate-hop facts share only ~55% of their words with the question (vs ~75% at hop 1), so the cliff is the task's, not the table's. Robust at scale: +100k distractors moves inject hit@1 0.481→0.480. **The notable finding: iterative chase** — n_hop rounds of top-1 with the retrieved text appended to the query, still pure table lookups — lifts full-chain recovery 0.665→**0.733** (3-hop 0.195→**0.371**, paired ~1.9× on the same questions, on a budget of only n_hop picks vs single-shot's top-20), while **the identical protocol does nothing for MiniLM** (0.719→0.720), leaving the chased table at parity with the chased teacher. Query expansion is the natural multi-hop mechanism for a bag-of-words encoder — each retrieved fact donates the bridging entity words the question lacks — where appending text to a sentence-encoder query dilutes as much as it informs. Honest bounds: per-hop table-vs-teacher differences are within noise at n=159 (3-hop); the load-bearing claims are the paired improvement and the protocol asymmetry. One chase bug was caught by the N=100k run itself (query expansion indexed target texts only; a retrieved distractor crashed it) and fixed so expansion uses whatever row was actually retrieved — a wrong pick poisoning the expanded query is part of what the metric measures. Method + full numbers drafted as a private working paper: `docs/papers/2026-08-07-qfhrr-semantic-word-tables.md`.

**H-F2/H-B3 CoT pipeline M1 (2026-08-07/08) — built, live-VM-proven, and the kill criterion returned an INFORMATIVE PARTIAL FALSIFICATION that narrows the value claim to verified-answer precision.** The cubbyllm→cubelang→bridge→output chain (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-07-cot-pipeline-design.md`, 12-commit SDD on feat/cot-pipeline) is real: `cubbyllm/reasoning/` (planner grammar, CubeLang chain-program builder, walk/repair/verify pipeline with the claimed-answer invariant asserted at its single `verified=True` site; pure-Python, `__wiring__`-declared, torch-free), 24 tests including a live smoke through the real Rust VM (2-hop bound recoveries ~0.50, absent-role control **0.029** — the live run corrected a fake-test assumption: cosine cleanup returns a *low-similarity nearest symbol* for absent roles on populated frames, None only for empty frames; the control check follows the spec's below-τ semantics at all four sites). **The measured verdict** (`exp_m3_cot_pipeline.{log,json}`; 800 eval / 200 disjoint calibration): CoT **0.390** overall vs retrieval-only 0.486 and chase-only **0.724** — clause 1 FAILS (beats retrieval-only at 2-hop **0.457 vs 0.023**; exact tie at 3-hop 15/159 = 0.094) while clauses 2–4 PASS: **claimed-answer precision 0.994, zero sub-τ verified results, control pass 341/341**. The honest reading, pre-committed by the spec: chase-only wins raw accuracy because it never refuses (337/800 questions fall outside the M1 question grammar and CoT declines them; chase also "wins" some questions CoT correctly refuses); **when this pipeline claims, it is right — the auditable-trace property free-form CoT cannot offer, measured not asserted.** Honesty caveats recorded in the artifacts themselves: τ_vm's calibration DEGENERATED to a floor (the clean 1,241-fact store produced zero incorrect calibration hops → Youden had no negatives, AUC 0.0; the 0.994 precision is post-hoc evidence doing calibration's job — self-reported via `tau_vm_calibration_degenerate` in the json); **the spec's 100k-distractor second arm was descoped between spec and plan without a recorded reason (a process miss caught by the final whole-branch review) — now explicitly a follow-up, and the likely cure for the degenerate calibration (a distractor-laden store manufactures genuine negatives)**; the corpus is NOT pure ASCII (30/800 questions) and cubelang's lexer mojibakes non-ASCII fillers (corruption can only cause verify-*fail*, never a false claim — the honest direction) — the cross-repo lexer fix is queued. Follow-ups, in value order: (1) question-grammar coverage (337/800 unparseable is the coverage ceiling); (2) the distractor arm + real negative-example calibration; (3) cubelang UTF-8 lexer fix; (4) trunk-SFT program generation (deferred by design). **Eval wave v2 (2026-08-13) — OVERALL PASS ×2, and a calibration finding worth its own line.** Grammar v2 (337→37 unparseable) + the corrected calibration turned the M1 falsification into the campaign's first full PASS on both arms (`exp_m3_cot_pipeline_v2{,_100k}.{log,json}`, commit ab23f85, reviewer-reproduced): verified coverage **0.646**, claimed-answer precision **0.9923**, by-hop 0.865/0.566/0.220 (beats retrieval-only at 2-hop AND 3-hop), planted-fault catch **1.000** on all four classes, organic-confusable catch 0.77–0.80, control 528/528, **identical CoT numbers at 0 and 100k distractors**. The finding: **an execution engine's similarity measures binding FIDELITY, not truth** — semantic near-miss negatives cannot calibrate it (round 2 pinned τ at 1.0 and zeroed multi-hop coverage at perfect precision); the correct calibration is **frame-size-conditional floors from correct-recovery quantiles**, which landed at 1.000/0.474/0.220 ≈ the 1/n bundle-dilution the algebra predicts — threshold design and representation theory now agree. The 4 verified-but-wrong answers are **corpus-dedup collisions** (typo'd surface forms collapse distinct chains' facts into one store entry; hits baselines identically; not a verify leak). Harvest per `docs/schemas/cot-harvest-schema.md`: 800 records/run, 517 verified pairs, 1 DPO pair (low yield → repair-heuristic follow-up). **Corpus data-quality note (2026-08-13, user-caught):** the .pq's entity orthography is systematically noisy — "Huamn" for "Human" in **568 answers / 1,098 facts vs only 29/61 spelled correctly (~95% typo'd)**, "united stated" family at 387 answers (probe: `Huamn is the instance of Hierocles (author of Synecdemus)`). Typos are chain-consistent, so exact-match scoring stays internally coherent — but every accuracy figure on this corpus measures **chain-recovery fidelity against the corpus's own ground truth, not real-world spelling**, harvested SFT tuples inherit the orthography, and any user-facing answer surface needs a normalization pass in front of it. **Counterfactual-neighborhood harvest (2026-08-28) — the Q23 "regretted discard" built and run.** `exp_m3_cot_pipeline.py` now re-plants the four fault classes on every verified chain's *own* accepted triples, recovers each corrupted hop through the VM at the deployed floor, and writes the per-hop outcome into the harvest record's `counterfactuals[]` (schema section added; fake-VM unit pins `validation/test_cot_counterfactuals.py`, 6 tests). Run `_v3cf` (800/200, no distractors; `exp_m3_cot_pipeline_v3cf.{log,json}`, `cot_harvest_v3cf.jsonl`, same 0.646 / 0.9923 / OVERALL PASS as v2): **517/517 verified chains, 1,899 corrupted hops (598 wrong-entity, 233 wrong-relation, 662 inverted-direction, 406 wrong-hop-order; 189 non-ASCII hops excluded), 1,899 caught, 0 escaped**, ~262 ms/chain, 1,899 VM calls — the harvest now refills every planted-fault stratum at ~3.7 hard negatives per verified answer for free. Two honest readings: (1) **every catch was by symbol mismatch, none by the floor** — the caught recoveries' similarity is median 0.503 / q90 1.00 (a wrong filler binds and recovers *faithfully*), so on this VM the string-match check is 100% of the defense against content faults and the manufactured negatives carry no τ information; they are hard negatives for a *successor* verifier (learned / bundled-superposition cleanup), not for the current floors — fidelity ≠ truth, now quantified on 1,899 hops; (2) the first pass (`*_v3cf_run1.*`, kept) reported **3 "escapes", all a generator bug** — `wrong_relation` on 3-hop chains whose hop-0 and hop-1 objects coincide compared a recovery against itself (a no-op fault the hop-order branch already skipped); fixed in `_fault_instances` + regression test, and the rerun's 0 escapes is the number that matches the shipped code. Not built: Qwen's k-mutation ladder (block-level flips can't cross the symbolic bridge — needs a VM-side op). Origin: N4 of `docs/research/2026-08-28-oracle-competition-scored.md`.

**Update 2026-09-03 — repair budget 3 → 1, measured lossless.** The GoT challenge's replay checks on `validation/logs/cot_harvest_v3cf.jsonl` (800 questions) showed the three-repair budget binds on every failure (246/246 used all three, all with zero hops verified) while no verified chain ever needed more than one repair (514 used none, 3 used one). The re-run with `--max-repairs 1` (`validation/logs/exp_m3_cot_pipeline_rb1.{json,log}`) reproduces the M1 numbers exactly — 517 verified, 513 correct, claimed-answer precision 0.9923, absent-role control 528/528 — at 45.9 ms per question for the CoT arm vs 120.9 ms. `pipeline.answer()`'s default and the serve path are now 1. Same source: the pipeline's failures are retrieval exhaustion (246 of 283), not candidate choice (only 19.8% of hops have a second candidate above `tau_ret`) and not the verifier (all 1,899 planted counterfactual faults caught by `symbol_mismatch`); the next experiment is a lower `tau_ret` with the VM deciding per hop. Scoring doc: `docs/research/2026-09-03-got-challenge-scored.md`.

**H-F2 M3 teacher ladder + vocab scaling + published-benchmark anchor (2026-08-07, queue item 4).** The paper-prerequisite baselines, run on the exact recorded protocols (same seeds). **(1) Released model2vec/potion head-to-head** (`exp_m3_potion_baseline.{log,json}`): potion-base-8M beats the MiniLM-taught v1 table on 42-domain routing (0.7223 vs 0.5946 macro) but LOSES NYT year placement (0.0443 vs 0.0638 exact); distilling potion itself into block space through the identical recipe recovers routing to 0.6464 — the cosine-exact projection means the block target costs nothing, attributing the gap to teacher strength + vocab coverage; the 0.5 signal blend is measured optimal vs 0/0.25 on both screens (`exp_m3_potion_blend0.log`). **(2) Teacher ladder** (`exp_m3_teacher_ladder.{log,json}`, user-driven): **raw bge-m3 (GGUF Q8, llama-cpp) is a NEGATIVE teacher** — 0.566 routing, below MiniLM, despite being the far stronger sentence embedder (single words are not its input regime; `exp_m3_bgem3_block_arm.log`); its Tokenlearn-compiled form (potion-multilingual-128M, whose own teacher IS bge-m3) jumps to 0.622 — static compilation is the load-bearing step; winner **potion-retrieval-32M: routing 0.680, NYT 0.080 exact / MAE med 15, axiom screen 0.3132** — strictly dominating v1/v2 on every screen → built as `fastword_table_v3.npz` (a builder meta-stamp bug — hardcoded teacher id, ignored --signal-blend — was caught during v2 validation and fixed, mowm `2a298a2`). **(3) Vocab scaling** (same teacher; 60k/100k builds + screens): 30k→0.680, **60k→0.699 (= 97% of native potion's 0.722)**, 100k→0.705 (inside ±0.014 noise, NYT/axiom flat) — coverage was most of the residual gap and saturates at 60k; **`fastword_table_v4.npz` (60,151 words, 1.1GB, ~33µs) is the production table**; the 100k candidate is kept as the saturation datapoint. **(4) BEIR dbpedia-entity, full 4.64M corpus** (`exp_m3_beir_dbpedia.{log,json}`; graded nDCG@10, 400 test queries, all 43,515 judged docs present): **the method's measured boundary** — v1 table two-stage 0.081 (compact-alone 0.040; shortlist recall@100 0.143 is the bottleneck), released potion-8M dense 0.224, published BM25 ~0.313 (literature anchor, unverified) — open-vocabulary web ENTITY search defeats a corpus-DF word list (tail entity names all hash-fallback), and even potion loses to lexical BM25 there; caveat: the run predates v3/v4 (used v1). Recorded in the paper §5.8 + negatives; the table's validated domain is routing/temporal/known-corpus retrieval, not open-web entity search.

**H-F2 M3 temporal worlds + causal events + inter-world query (2026-08-07).** The user's design frame: the world was not the same from one era or one year to the next — each era and each year is a world (months deliberately too granular; kept as metadata only), worlds need causal events connecting them, and a world missing an answer must be able to query another world. Two screens, both with measured noise nulls and MiniLM anchors. **(1) Curated events** (`exp_m3_temporal_causal.{log,json}`; $CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA\temporal\historical\train_augmented.jsonl — 4,000 dated events, 6 named period-eras, year_start with BC negative, per-event `impact` consequence text; the HUGE_ENTITIES 5,804-book corpus was probed and deferred as the unlabeled scale-up path — extraction works but it holds misfiled content, e.g. a 1943–46 novel under AncientClassical). **The load-bearing leakage catch:** "augmented" means duplicated — 3,187/4,000 rows are paraphrase copies of another row's event (1914 holds three Franz Ferdinand assassinations), and row-level LOO left siblings in every centroid, inflating exact-year to 0.890; **event-level exclusion** (all same-(title,year) rows out of every world when that event challenges) is the honest protocol — third leakage instance caught this project, same shape as the PQ retraction. Honest numbers: era routing **0.669 macro (ref 0.713 = 0.94×; null 0.163)**; flat year routing over 239 year-worlds **0.735 exact / 0.899 ±50y / MAE median 0 = 0.98× teacher** (0.747; null: 0.005 exact, MAE med ~730y); the digit-masked arm is **identical** (0.735) — placement is content-based, not date-reading. **Hierarchy is a real trade, not a free win:** era→year routing drops to 0.542 exact (teacher identically: 0.562) at 59 vs 239 comparisons — the era gate's errors strand queries, which is exactly what inter-world query repairs. **Causal:** event→impact retrieval among 4,000 confusables (any-sibling-positive, the fair form under duplication) **0.337 hit@1 / 0.631 hit@5** (ref 0.448/0.757 = 0.75×; chance 0.00025); impacts routed to year-worlds land **later than their event 55.2% vs matched null 44.6%** in both encoders — causality measurably points forward; and the year-centroid similarity **gradient decays monotonically** with temporal distance (0.342 at Δ<10y → 0.251 at Δ>500y) — "the world was not the same" is in the representation. **Inter-world query (delegation):** worlds ranked by self-excluded centroid; the consulted world answers only if its best member clears τ, else the query passes on (a fourth leakage variant was caught here: full-centroid ranking let the challenge's own vector nudge world choice — on NYT, where year-centroids sit at 0.86–0.92 mutual cosine, that 1/100 nudge alone was worth 0.064→0.372 exact). Fixed protocol at τ=0.6: **matches exhaustive flat search's answer-found rate (0.554) at 63 vs 3,783 member comparisons (60× cheaper), 2% of queries delegated**, yr-exact 0.936 vs flat's 0.942. **(2) NYT hard mode** (`exp_m3_nyt_years.{log,json}`; $CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA\temporal\nyt_data — 294 YEAR_MONTH archive files, 1852–2024; 14,700 deduped abstracts, 100/year, 147 year-worlds; abstracts include genuinely timeless content, the real-text ceiling): **the table beats the teacher on every year metric** — exact 0.064 vs 0.046, ±10y 0.354 vs 0.338 (3× the 0.120 null), **MAE median 19y vs 21y** (null 50.7) — dating real newspaper text is a surface-vocabulary task (names, spellings, topics), the third measured instance of surface-beats-semantics after routing-parity and chase; the fine gradient is again monotone (0.918 at Δ<2y → 0.855 at 50–100y); and delegation's τ needs per-corpus calibration exactly as every τ before it (curated best-member scores run high — 2% delegate at τ=0.6; NYT scores run low — 84% delegate at τ=0.4, degenerating toward flat search for only +0.04 ±50y over trusting the routed world). Architecture reading: centroid-routed worlds + confidence-gated peer query is validated as the serve shape; the (event, impact, Δyear) pairs are the natural transition-training data for year-worlds' HYLAs (not yet wired). **Wired (mowm `ff57a5f` on feat/world-query, suite 762 green):** `World.best_member` (the "do I actually hold this?" primitive), `MoWMRouter.query_worlds`+`WorldAnswer` (worlds ranked by axiom-centroid; first world whose best member clears `tau_answer` answers; exhausted → global best seen; router mediates every hop, worlds never reference each other), and `CubbyBridge` tier (b2) (centroid winner short of `tau_answer` → redirect to the world that cleared it; route-vs-spawn untouched; `tau_answer=None` disables). 8 new tests pin the delegation geometry — a near-miss world outranks the true holder on centroids and the query still lands on the holder — plus the no-spawn guarantee.

**H-B4 — Multi-factor unbinding needs chained F=2 hops, not one large resonator solve.** This is a hard capacity ceiling, not an implementation weakness: flat F=5 factorization is mathematically proven impossible at D=10000 (P_cap ≈ 0.64·D^(1/(F-1))), and cubby-concepts ships a passing regression test (`test_resonator_quadrillion_is_beyond_capacity`) asserting exactly that. Status: VERIFIED (the capacity math and the test both exist and pass). The demonstrated workaround — `vsa/graph.py`'s `SequentialHyperGraph`, chaining simple F=2 binds through an SDM, verified 100% accuracy from depth 1 to depth 20 with zero error accumulation — is how the design reaches combinatorial reach from polynomial storage. **Validate/apply directly:** any CubbyLLM binding-head design that needs more than ~3 simultaneously-bound factors must budget for chained decomposition from the start; this isn't optional future-proofing, it's a known mathematical limit.

**Real-workload datum + a correction (2026-08-02), from cubemind's I-RAVEN crosstalk note (dated 2026-03-19).** This is the first measurement of what bundle load the VM channel actually needs, and it is a task metric rather than a probe: I-RAVEN sits at 86.1% overall, with single-entity configs (Center, L-R, U-D, O-IC) at **96-100%** but multi-entity grids (2x2, 3x3, O-IG) stuck at **67-77%**. The split is capacity — a grid panel bundles 9 object-position pairs into one vector and unbinding SNR falls below the discrimination threshold. Three things follow.

(1) **The note's proposed "expand to k=32, l=256 (d=8192)" should instead be the shape already decided in H-B5: k=80 x l=128 = d=10240.** cubemind runs I-RAVEN at k=16 x l=128 = d=2048, i.e. 5x under the standardized algebra. Moving to the decided shape keeps l=128 (block length unchanged, so codebooks and shaders stay compatible) and only adds blocks, whereas 32x256 invents a third shape and breaks the "one algebra across model + world-arena" property that was the entire point of H-B5.

(2) **The note's "exponentially more capacity" is wrong and should not be planned against.** Bundling crosstalk falls as ~1/sqrt(D) — this project already measured the law in H-C7 (max-crosstalk grows as sqrt(2 ln V / D)). So 2048 -> 8192 is 4x D for **2x** SNR, and 2048 -> 10240 is 5x D for ~2.2x. Real and worth doing, roughly linear in D — not exponential.

(3) **Hierarchical bundling is not a new fix; it is H-B4, and it only works with cleanup between levels.** Bundling objects into rows and then rows into a panel is exactly the chained F=2 decomposition described above, already VERIFIED at 100% accuracy from depth 1 to depth 20 in `cubby-concepts/vsa/graph.py`'s `SequentialHyperGraph`. The critical detail the note omits: that result holds *because* the chain passes through an **SDM cleanup at each hop**, which resets accumulated noise. Without a cleanup step between levels, hierarchical bundling merely redistributes the same total crosstalk across two hops and buys far less than it appears to. Port the SequentialHyperGraph pattern, not just the two-level idea.

Also note the regime: I-RAVEN objects share attribute vocabularies (shape/colour/size), so the fillers are **correlated**, not quasi-orthogonal. H-G2 measured that penalty directly — real keys ran 3.1x more correlated than random bipolar and cost every candidate 2-3x recall. Capacity here is therefore worse than random-vector math predicts, which is consistent with 9 items straining a space where H-B6's probe held ~97% retrieval at n=16 on quasi-orthogonal trunk features.

**A caution carried over from H-B6, aimed at the note's closing claim.** It states that a CNN -> blockSoftmax -> bind/unbind pipeline "can learn hierarchical bundling automatically via backprop" because "the VSA algebra is its own adjoint, so the gradient naturally discovers the right decomposition." The adjoint half is true and elegant: the adjoint of bind-by-b is correlation-by-b, which is unbind. The inference from it is exactly the assumption H-B6 falsified. A differentiable VSA pipeline has a degenerate solution available — collapse the encodings so everything scores well on the objective while losing discrimination — and it will take it: optimizing `binding_aux_loss` alone drove filler-filler cosine 0.006 -> 0.974 and retrieval 96% -> chance, while the loss looked like clean progress the whole way. I-RAVEN accuracy is a real task metric and would eventually catch that, unlike the proxy H-B6 used. But "did hierarchical decomposition actually emerge" needs its own discrimination check; do not infer it from the loss curve.

**H-B5 — Added 2026-07-23. `grilly.experimental.vsa.ops` may already be the production VSA substrate this whole stack shares, and should be checked before choosing between H-B1 and H-B2.** Discovered via `$CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA\regenerate_svc_targets.py` (read in full) — a real, currently-used pipeline that builds BLAKE3-hashed bipolar hypervectors at D=10,240 (80 blocks × 128 bits) via `grilly.experimental.vsa.ops.BinaryOps` and `grilly.utils.stable_hash`, using role-filler binding (subject/verb/context bound to fixed role vectors named `cubemind:role:subject/verb/context`, then bundled) to generate real training targets from actual conversation/instruction data (491,367 records; three output variants — baseline, BLAKE3, and a 4th-role emotion-augmented version — totaling roughly 33GB). D=10,240 is the exact dimensionality of cubby-lm's `VSABindingHead` codebook and matches cubemind's world-arena block-codes (k=80×l=128=10,240); the role-vector naming (`cubemind:role:...`) ties it explicitly to the cubemind lineage. This is very unlikely to be coincidental. `grilly` (the C++/Vulkan GPU backend sibling repo, at `$CUBBY_GITHUB\grilly` per earlier session findings) has not yet been surveyed in this project. Status: SPECULATIVE that this is the same substrate as the binding head (not yet confirmed by reading `grilly`'s actual source) — but unlike H-B1 (cubby-concepts) and H-B2 (GrillCheese's standalone experiments), this is the only VSA candidate found so far that's already wired into live production data generation, not just a tested-but-standalone prototype. **Validate first, before committing engineering time to H-B1 or H-B2:** read `grilly`'s `experimental/vsa/ops.py` (or equivalent) directly, and check whether it's the actual mechanism behind (or a close relative of) cubby-lm's `VSABindingHead` and cubemind's `BlockCodes`. **Kill criterion:** if it turns out to be an unrelated, narrowly-built one-off for this specific SVC-target pipeline with no real connection to the binding head, it drops back to a third reference implementation alongside H-B1/H-B2, no more or less privileged than either. If confirmed connected, it may substantially shortcut Group B's whole "which algebra do we build" question — the open work would shift to whether its role-filler binding scheme (built for subject/verb/context triples) generalizes to CubbyLLM's actual token/concept binding needs, not whether the underlying primitives work at all.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): CONFIRMED CONNECTED — status upgraded from SPECULATIVE.** cubemind's `BlockCodes` is a thin layered wrapper over grilly: `grilly.backend._bridge.blockcode_bind/unbind/similarity` (C++/Vulkan, path 1) → `grilly.experimental.vsa.block_ops.BlockCodeOps` (Python GPU, path 2) → numpy (verified in `cubemind/cubemind/ops/block_codes.py:4-6,35-45,127-187`). grilly's `experimental/vsa/` ships **all three candidate families**: `BinaryOps` (bipolar elementwise, exact bind/unbind, `hash_to_bipolar` — the BLAKE-hash lineage the binding head's own docstring names for cross-repo codebook sharing), `HolographicOps` (HRR/FFT = H-B2's algebra), and `BlockCodeOps` (IBM-NVSA sparse block codes, per-block circular convolution — whose docstring explicitly targets "the gradient explosion of flat HRR or the zero-gradient problem of bipolar sign()"), plus a resonator. Group B's question collapses to: **which grilly family does the binding head standardize on, and import-vs-port** — exactly the shortcut this hypothesis predicted. The generalization question (SVC triples → token/concept binding) remains open as stated.

**H-B6 — Added 2026-07-30. A parameter-free VSA binding auxiliary loss, applied during LM pretraining, shapes the trunk's hidden states to be VSA-bindable at no measurable language-modeling cost.** Source: `cubbyllm/model/binding/torch_ops.py` — the **MAP scheme from `cubby-concepts/vsa`** (bind = bipolar elementwise product, self-inverse; `binding_aux_loss` bundles N=16 trunk hidden states, each bound to a fixed bipolar role key, unbinds each, and minimizes the cosine reconstruction error), wired into `TrainLoop` behind `bind_weight` (`cubbyllm/training/loop.py`, computed in fp32 outside the bf16 autocast). Note the deliberate two-channel split: MAP is used **only for the differentiable aux loss** because bipolar elementwise bind is a plain Hadamard product (already differentiable, exact single-pair recovery), whereas the decided *production* VM-side algebra is `BlockCodeOps` (H-B5, parameter-free and non-differentiable). The claim: an auxiliary objective rewarding quasi-orthogonal, high-capacity representations can be trained *jointly with* next-token CE without the two objectives competing — yielding a trunk whose features are natively bindable for the VM/world-model channel, "for free." **Validate** by running the aux loss live in the real assembled `CubbyModel` and watching whether the binding loss falls (trunk becoming bindable) *while* bpc keeps tracking a no-binding baseline (binding not stealing LM capacity), specifically through the warmup→full-LR transition where a competing objective would surface. **Kill criterion:** if bpc diverges upward from a no-binding baseline once the binding gradient is at full strength, the objectives compete — dial the weight back or drop the aux loss.

**Validation result (2026-07-30, live 2B run — mechanism VERIFIED, "free" PLAUSIBLE).** In the real assembled `CubbyModel` (D=2048, L=32, MinGRU backbone, hybrid vocab, hardened memory; `bind_weight=0.42`, N=16; B=8×S=768, Colab single-GPU, bf16 + grad-checkpointing), the binding loss fell from ~0.735 (at an earlier 0.1 diagnostic weight) — 0.57 by step 500 of the 0.42 run — to a **~0.20 plateau across steps 2000–3000** (cosine recovery ~0.80, well past the N=16 random-filler crosstalk floor cos ≈ 1/√16 = 0.25, and *holding* rather than sliding to zero or bouncing back). Crucially `bpc` fell **in lockstep** (≈2.3 → 1.95 → 1.74 → 1.69 → ~1.5 at steps 500/1000/2000/2500/3000) and did **not** stall or bump through either the warmup→full-LR transition (step 2000, LR reaching 3e-4) or 1,000 further steps of sustained full LR — the exact points where a competing objective would declare itself. That co-descent also rules out the one degenerate way to game the aux loss (collapsing `h` to a low-rank state, which would *raise* bpc, not lower it). `validation/logs/binding_aux_loss_live_2b_2026-07-30.log`. **VERIFIED:** at real-run (early) scale, a zero-parameter aux loss makes the trunk VSA-bindable under the MAP scheme. **Only PLAUSIBLE (not yet VERIFIED):** that it is truly *free* — this rests on "no bpc bump through sustained full LR" (strong circumstantial); the clean A/B (bpc vs the saved no-binding `ckpt_1` baseline at matched steps) was **not captured numerically**, and the run is at ~step 2500 (~early), not a completed pass. **Open items this surfaced:** (1) confirm at full-run scale (~40B tokens) that bpc never diverges from a no-binding baseline; (2) **MAP-bindability is not proven to transfer to `BlockCodeOps`-bindability** — both families reward quasi-orthogonal high-capacity codes so it likely helps, but the production VM algebra is a different family and this is untested; (3) "bindable trunk features" has not been shown to make VM-side unbind (H-B3) actually work on real trunk `h` — aux-probe capacity ≠ usable relational readout downstream.

**KILL CRITERION TRIGGERED (2026-08-02) — H-B6 IS FALSIFIED. The aux loss was Goodharted; do not use it.** The offline probe (`validation/exp_b6_blockcode_transfer.py`, gate passed: it reproduces the run's own objective at cos 0.840 vs the logged 0.842) added the metric the training loss never had — **retrieval accuracy**, i.e. can you tell *which* filler was bound. At n=16 the trained trunk scores **11.5% (chance = 6.25%)** while the **untrained control scores 97.5%**. Training made the trunk *dramatically worse* at the only thing a binding channel is for. Mechanism, reproduced synthetically by optimizing h against `binding_aux_loss` alone with no LM objective: cos climbs 0.251→0.863 while **filler-filler cosine climbs 0.006→0.974** and retrieval falls 96.1%→4.7%. The loss rewards cosine-to-own-filler and never penalizes cosine-to-*other*-fillers, so **collapsing every hidden state in a sequence onto one direction scores ~0.86**. The trained model sits exactly on that curve. So the earlier "bind 0.735→0.158" was real as a number and meaningless as a capability: it measured collapse, not bindability. The "+0.088 BlockCode transfer" the probe reported is transfer of the collapse artifact — the verdict line said POSITIVE because it keyed on cosine delta and ignored retrieval; that heuristic is wrong and is being changed. **The deeper finding, and the reason no fix is worth building: an untrained trunk is ALREADY a near-optimal VSA substrate.** Random high-dimensional vectors are quasi-orthogonal by construction — that is the property hyperdimensional computing is founded on — so retrieval starts at 96–98% and there is nothing for a binding objective to add. A discriminative (InfoNCE-over-the-bundle) reformulation was built and tested: it *prevents* the collapse (filler-filler stays ~0.00, retrieval 96–98%) but leaves cosine pinned at the 1/sqrt(n) crosstalk floor, i.e. it buys **nothing over random init**. **CONFIRMED ON A TRAINED CONTROL (2026-08-02, `exp_b6b_ckpt_ab.py`).** `ckpt_1` — same corpus, same architecture, 7,500 steps, binding loss OFF — scores **96.9% retrieval** with filler-filler cosine **0.276**, against the binding-trained checkpoint's **11.9%** and **0.934**. Its binding cosine is 0.243, i.e. exactly the 1/sqrt(16) crosstalk floor, as expected for a trunk that never saw the objective. **This exonerates the architecture**: the theta=f(c) memory layer, the MinGRU trunk and the hybrid vocab all preserve retrieval fine — the aux loss alone causes the collapse. It also gives the healthy baseline for real (not random) features: modest within-sequence correlation ~0.28 is harmless; it is the jump to ~0.93 that destroys discrimination. The language-modelling half of the A/B is **permanently unanswerable from these artifacts** — the checkpoints are 20,000 vs 7,500 steps and the run overwrote a single checkpoint path, so no matched-step binding checkpoint exists (held-out bpc 1.0722 vs 1.3924 is dominated by the 12,500 extra steps and attributes nothing). Moot for the decision, since the loss damages the channel it was meant to build. **Action: set `bind_weight=0` for the DDP run.** Everything below this line describes the falsified configuration and is retained as the record of how it looked before the retrieval metric existed. `validation/logs/exp_b6_blockcode_transfer.log`.

**Run completed (2026-08-01).** The same run finished its full **20,000-step** cosine schedule (LR decayed to the 3e-5 floor; ~123M tokens, ~3.9h wall, peak 92.5GB). Over the complete run `bind` did **not** stop at the 0.20 plateau — it kept slowly descending to **~0.158** (cos recovery ~0.84) while `bpc` improved to **~1.1**, the two curves tracking together the entire way with no divergence at any point. So the "free" evidence now spans a *complete* schedule, not just the early steps. Two caveats keep it PLAUSIBLE rather than VERIFIED: it's a small token budget (a 2B trunk at ~123M tokens is heavily undertrained — the real test is the 40B-token DDP run), and the numeric `ckpt_1` A/B still wasn't captured. Generations at step 20000 show genuine domain acquisition — coherent multi-sentence legal prose, coding-task instruction prompts, contract-clause QA — where step 3000 was still title-stub/loop fragments. Log tail appended to the same file.

**Long-horizon confirmation (2026-08-05) — a 92k-step run with the loss OFF holds the substrate.** The H-D5 episodic-memory run (`CB_MEM_EVERY=2`, checkpoint `hd5_mem21.pt`; 151M-param Chinchilla-optimal hybrid backbone (2B is the target arch, not this run's size) + hardened θ=f(c), Colab single-GPU) ran to **step 92,000** of a planned 100k (stopped early — out of credits) at **`bind_weight=0`**, i.e. the falsified aux loss OFF, per the action above. Confirmed two independent ways: `train_colab.py:95` hard-defaults `CB_BIND_W=0` behind a `LEAVE AT 0` guard, and the step log prints no `bind` field (it only appears when `BIND_W>0`, `train_colab.py:541`) — the pasted log has none. With the loss off, the `representation_health` retrieval probe (n=16) drifted from ~98% early to a **stable ~77% (ff +0.42–0.47)** by step 92k — modest erosion under a near-complete schedule of *joint* LM+memory training, **not** the collapse the aux loss itself produced (11.5%), and ~12× above chance (6.25%); bpc settled ~0.90–0.94 (ppl ~20–24) with coherent multi-domain generations. This is the long-horizon evidence H-B6's ≤20k-step runs never had: left alone, the trunk stays a usable VSA substrate across ~92k steps of real training — no binding objective needed, exactly as the falsification concluded. **The one branch this leaves untested** (explicitly *not* a revival of the MAP aux loss): whether the *discriminative* InfoNCE-over-the-bundle reformulation — which at short scale prevented collapse but bought nothing over random init — would *hold* retrieval near its ~97% init through long training instead of letting it drift to ~77%. A 77% channel is healthy, so this is low priority, but it is the sole open question the run surfaces.

**H-B7 — Added 2026-08-02. The I-RAVEN numbers are NOT a VSA result and must not be presented as one.** Status: **VERIFIED by reading the benchmark source.** `cubemind/benchmarks/iraven.py` (1609 lines) produces the reported 86.3% baseline / 90.3% after position-aware scoring. Provenance, precisely: `evaluate_problem_dataset` (L617) parses `problem["metadata"]` XML at L644-645 and `parse_panel_entities` (L312) reads **ground-truth `Type`/`Size`/`Color`/`Angle` integers straight out of the dataset annotations** — there is no perception. The dominant signal is `cubemind.reasoning.rule_detectors.score_candidates` (called L677, L691), deterministic integer-domain logic. **VSA participates only as a 0.5-weighted tiebreaker** (`comp_scores[i] += dist_scores[i] * 0.5`, L709-712) inside a bare `try/except: pass` (L700, L713-714), so it can silently contribute nothing. The headline +14.5pp grid improvement (67.5% -> 82.0%) came from `_score_position_rules` (L932) extracting entity **bbox** fingerprints from the same XML — also not VSA. And `benchmarks/iravenx.py` (740 lines, the I-RAVEN-X 100% OOD result) **imports no VSA at all**; its imports are `argparse, json, logging, sys, time, pathlib`.

**Consequences, in order of importance.** (1) Any public or fundraising claim of the form "our VSA reasoning layer scores 86-90% on I-RAVEN" is **not supported by this code**. The defensible statement is: *a symbolic rule-detector achieves 90.3% on I-RAVEN given ground-truth attribute annotations.* That is a legitimate published-ablation configuration, but perception-based systems solve I-RAVEN from **pixels**, so quoting this number against theirs without the qualifier would misrepresent it, and `parse_panel_entities` is about ten minutes of reading away for any reviewer doing diligence. (2) **Fixing VSA bundling capacity will not move this benchmark much** — an earlier estimate in this project that hierarchical bundling plus a larger d would take 86% to 92-95% was wrong, because it assumed VSA drove the score. It does not. (3) What *would* make it a genuine VSA result is the differentiable perception frontend — CNN -> blockSoftmax -> bind/unbind. That code exists at `cubemind/_archive/perception/cnn_encoder.py` (real grilly Conv2d GEMM fwd/bwd, `_block_softmax` L105) but is **unreachable**: its only live consumer `benchmarks/iravenx_image.py` L32 imports `cubemind.perception.cnn_encoder`, which does not exist (verified `ModuleNotFoundError`), and cubemind's own `docs/papers/ablation-notes.md` L164-165 files it under **"Planned Future Ablations, A6."**

**Capacity context for whoever builds A6.** I-RAVEN runs at **k=8, l=64 (D=512)** (`iraven.py` L1298-1301), not the repo default k=80/l=128/D=10240 (`core/constants.py` L20-22). `encode_panel_nvsa` (L279) superposes **9 four-way-bound entities** into one D=512 code. cubemind's own capacity formula (`docs/architecture/02-vsa-foundations.md` §2.7: `N_max ~ 0.18*D/log(D)`) gives **N_max ~ 15 at D=512** — so a 3x3 grid runs at roughly 60% of a budget that is asserted in prose and **enforced nowhere in the codebase** (no assert, no counter, no warning). Hierarchical bundling *is* implemented for the distribution rule (`_score_distribution_rule` L1104-1153: rows bundled L1127-1128, then rows into a master L1129) but there is **no cleanup between the levels** — only a final `discretize()`, while `bundle(normalize=True)` renormalizes each block per level and compounds attenuation. That is exactly the failure H-B4 predicts, and the mitigation cubemind planned for it ("A5: NSA-adapted bundling", ablation-notes L161-163) is unimplemented — grep for `NSA`/`noise_strength` in Python returns zero hits.

## 5. Group C — Vocabulary, tokenization, and the softmax bottleneck (a core design requirement, not an optimization)

**Reframed 2026-07-23.** CubbyLLM's vocabulary will be significantly larger than cubby-lm's 32k — explicitly *because* the mechanisms below can remove the full-vocabulary softmax step that would otherwise make a large vocab expensive to train and serve. Everything in this group graduates from "worth checking if it helps" to "the output-head mechanism CubbyLLM's vocab strategy actually depends on." The open question is no longer whether to pursue this, it's which specific mechanism, and how large "much larger" ends up being — both still genuinely open (see H-C6).

**H-C1 — Dynamic hyper-encoder embeddings are the enabling mechanism for a much larger vocabulary, not an optional parameter-count optimization.** Source: ZeTT (hypernetwork predicts token embeddings for any target vocabulary on the fly) and zip2zip, motivated by the observation that static embedding/unembedding tables scale linearly with vocab size and can reach up to 93% of total model params in typical LLMs at 100k+ vocab — the exact regime CubbyLLM is now explicitly targeting, unlike cubby-lm's comparatively modest 32k. Status: SPECULATIVE mechanism (ZeTT/zip2zip are external research, not yet reproduced against CubbyLLM's actual target scale), but no longer optional to check — it's close to a load-bearing assumption behind the vocab requirement itself. **Validate** by prototyping a hyper-encoder generating embeddings on the fly for a synthetic vocabulary at the target order of magnitude, and confirming (a) generated-embedding quality is competitive with a static lookup table at a small/medium vocab size where both are still cheap enough to compare directly, and (b) the hyper-encoder's own compute cost per token doesn't reintroduce the cost problem the static table was supposed to avoid. **Kill criterion:** generated embeddings measurably underperform static ones at matched vocab size, or the hyper-encoder's forward cost scales badly enough to erode the "fast" half of the goal — either result means the large-vocab requirement needs a different enabling mechanism, or a smaller target vocab after all.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): kill criterion HALF-triggered at toy scale.** On the real spm32k token stream (cubby-lm valid.txt, V=8k kept, d=64, identical budgets): the **quality clause FAILS** for a pure surface-form generator — static table val loss 3.886 (ppl 48.7) vs char-CNN hyper-encoder 4.389 (ppl 80.6), +0.50 nats; surface form alone cannot recover what a table memorizes. The **cost clause PASSES decisively** — 1.26µs/token to regenerate the full table, cacheable at inference, embedding params V-independent (77k vs 578k). Read alongside zip2zip's actual design (static base vocab + composed hypertokens), this steers toward a **hybrid** (static core + generated/composed tail), not pure generation — see H-C6. Toy-scale caveat stated in the log; re-check at target scale before betting the vocab strategy. `validation/exp_c1_hyperencoder.py`.

**H-C2 — zip2zip-style LZW hypertoken compression reduces autoregressive step count 20–60%, directly improving latency.** Status: **VERIFIED (2026-07-23 campaign — measured on the real tokenizer, no model change).** grillcheese_spm32k_v2 over 8MB of cubby-lm's valid.txt (2.07M base tokens): **per-document (streaming-honest) reduction 22.2%** — the claimed range reproduced at its low end; a *persistent cross-document dictionary* reaches **53.4%–66.6%** (32k→992k hypertokens minted) — at/above the claimed top. The persistent-dictionary variant is the H-C6 dynamic-vocab question in disguise: ~22% is free, the rest is bought with exactly the machinery H-C6 would have to decide to build. `validation/exp_c2_lzw.py`.

**H-C3 — Removing the full-vocabulary softmax is the specific mechanism that makes a much larger vocabulary affordable at the output head.** The general pattern (retrieve top-K via an ANN index, softmax only over those, instead of a full-vocabulary softmax) is exactly what cubby-lm's `VSABindingHead` cosine-similarity-against-a-frozen-D=10240-codebook already did structurally, so it's not a foreign idea to graft on — it's an extension of something the predecessor already had the right shape for. GrillCheese's `gcheese-faiss/` module is a small-scale (sub-100k item) working existence proof this pattern is production-viable: a hand-written Vulkan GPU reimplementation of brute-force kNN (built because CUDA faiss-gpu doesn't run on their AMD hardware), running *side by side* with a separate softmax-attention memory-read path that they are actively optimizing — i.e., a real system where both approaches coexist and get compared. Status: PLAUSIBLE, and the clearest "this is already almost what we do" finding in the whole survey. Self-reported performance figures (sub-2ms @ 10k memories, 2-5x GPU speedup) are explicitly flagged by their own `CRITICAL_ISSUES.md` as unvalidated planning targets, not measurements. **This is no longer a formalize-what-already-exists nice-to-have — it's the specific mechanism the new, larger vocabulary is being designed around, so it needs validating at the target vocab scale, not at cubby-lm's old 32k.** **Validate** by building an explicit ANN-retrieval output head (or a Sylph-style sigmoid-decomposed alternative, per H0) at a representative large-vocab scale and benchmarking latency/accuracy against a full-softmax baseline at the same scale, not against cubby-lm's existing numbers.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): mechanism confirmed REQUIRED; bounds measured (CPU, batch=1).** Full-head cost is linear in V (measured 7.8× for 8× vocab at d=2560; 47.9ms/token at the real 32k×10240 shape). The retrieval floor (exact scoring over a K=1024 shortlist) is ~flat at 0.2–1.5ms — a **32× win at the real head shape**. Naive IVF over *random* codewords is the pessimistic bound and it's poor (37–80% top-1 agreement, falling with V) — index quality on *trained* embeddings is the real engineering question before committing to a specific ANN. The decisive number is memory: **V=1M fp32 codebook = 10–41GB** (d=2560/10240; the 1M row is explicit linear extrapolation, not measurement) — at large V the softmax bypass isn't an optimization, it's the only way the head exists. One correction to this hypothesis's framing: the current `VSABindingHead` does NOT avoid the O(V·D) cost — it's still a full-vocab matmul; it saves *parameters*, not compute. `validation/exp_c3_latency.py`.
