**H-C4 — A trained flat-taxonomy domain classifier is a usable baseline for the "automatic specialization" routing half of the goal, not the end state.** Source: Novel_GNN_Arch's `capsules/domain_classifier.py` + `domain_head.pt` — a small (~570K-parameter), actually trained and evaluated 2-layer MLP (frozen sentence embedding → 512 → 88 sigmoid domain outputs, `scripts/test_domain_head.py` reports accuracy/Hamming-loss/F1, though the achieved numbers weren't reproduced this session, only the code and eval harness were read). Status: this is the single most mature, lowest-integration-risk artifact found anywhere across all four surveys — real weights, real eval script, minimal dependencies (`torch`, `transformers`, `scikit-learn`). But it routes into a fixed flat 88-domain taxonomy via a plain MLP, not a learned/generative specialist space — it's a stepping-stone baseline for H0's routing half, not the hypernetwork-based specialization mechanism itself. **Validate** by using it directly as a baseline router to benchmark any new context-conditioned routing mechanism against — this is close enough to drop-in that skipping it would be wasted effort.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): VERIFIED and executed end-to-end offline.** `domain_head.pt` loads with `weights_only=True` (clean tensor dict): exactly **569,944 params**, shapes 1024→512→88 matching the code. The eval harness ran on CPU against the shipped 3-record fixture (metrics print: exact-match, Hamming, micro/macro F1). Two precision notes: the module emits **logits** (sigmoid applied downstream; trained with `BCEWithLogitsLoss`), and the shipped `file_capsules.jsonl` is **0 bytes** — no headline accuracy is reproducible from the repo as shipped; embedding fresh text needs `BAAI/bge-m3` (1024-dim, not in the local HF cache — would require a download). Baseline: usable as claimed. Its historical metrics: unverifiable without regenerating a test set.

**Reinforced by H0 part 2 (2026-07-23):** the learned-context experiment (`exp_h0b_learned_context.py`) makes this artifact's role precise and load-bearing rather than optional. When H0's context is inferred by a router trained *online*, the router itself catastrophically forgets its own routing (slots collapse 6→3 distinct, task-0 confidence →37%) — so the context source **must be an offline-pretrained classifier over all domains**, which is exactly what `domain_head.pt` is (trained once on 88 domains). H-C4 moves from "stepping-stone baseline" to "the specific shape the routing half needs": upstream, pretrained, frozen at inference.

**H-C5 — Economics claims in cubby-concepts' `budget-hardware/` need scrutiny before use.** `revenues-simulation.txt` projects a $956M five-year valuation; the ~100–1000x cost-per-query claim lives in **`simulatorandprofitability.txt:138`** (citation corrected 2026-07-23 — `vs-Kimmy-K3.txt` contains no numeric multiplier, only "a fraction of the cost"). Status: SPECULATIVE — both are explicitly flagged by the source repo's own survey as unmeasured narrative, not benchmarked data; no methodology backs either number. **Treat as directional motivation only.** Do not use these specific figures in any real financial planning until CubbyLLM has an actual measured cost-per-query from a running prototype.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): scrutiny performed, verdict unchanged and sharpened.** The 100–1000× figure rests on unsourced round numbers ($15,000 vs $15.00 per 1M queries; 700W GPU node vs 20W "neuromorphic-assisted" node; a retail API price treated as competitor TCO) — assertion-only, zero benchmark data or citations. The $956M is arithmetically self-consistent (12× a self-asserted $79.72M Y5 revenue) but every driver is assumed (TAM, 219% YoY growth, 8% terminal share, 12× multiple, $0.22/1M-token COGS), and the file carries internal inconsistencies (narrative "$58.9M EBITDA" vs table 74.93; malformed 9-label/7-column table). Directional motivation only — confirmed.

**H-C6 — CubbyLLM's vocabulary may not need to be a fixed-size discrete set at all.** Added 2026-07-23. zip2zip's inference-time LZW hypertoken compression and ZeTT's target-vocabulary-agnostic embedding generation both point toward an open-ended vocabulary — new hypertokens formed and embedded on demand, no fixed upper bound chosen in advance — rather than simply picking a bigger fixed number than 32k. Status: SPECULATIVE, and genuinely undecided: "much larger" could mean a large-but-fixed vocabulary (say 250k–1M, sized once, a more conservative extension of what cubby-lm already did) or an effectively open/dynamic one (no fixed size, tokens/hypertokens minted at inference time, closer to zip2zip's actual proposal). These have different engineering consequences and different new failure modes (a dynamic vocab risks unbounded cleanup-memory growth and variable novel-hypertoken quality; a fixed-but-large vocab reintroduces a version of the static-table cost problem at a bigger constant). **Validate** by deciding this explicitly as a design choice before implementation begins — it's not a detail to leave implicit, since H-C1's hyper-encoder and H-C3's softmax-bypass mechanism need to be built differently depending on the answer.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): decision package assembled.** Every measurement this campaign produced points the same way: H-G1 (fixed-vocab pipeline proven to 65,536, one order of magnitude of headroom demonstrated), H-C1 (pure surface-form generation loses +0.50 nats at toy scale; generation *cost* is negligible/cacheable), H-C2 (22% step reduction free; persistent hypertokens buy 53–67% but require dynamic-vocab machinery), H-C3/H-C7 (direct readout fine to V=1M; memory is the wall; retrieval head required either way).

**DECIDED (user, 2026-07-23): HYBRID.** Fixed core vocabulary — next step a 128k–256k BPE on the proven H-G1 pipeline — plus a retrieval output head from day one, with the hypertoken/dynamic tail deferred as a later, separately-gated addition. H-C1's hyper-encoder and H-C3's softmax-bypass mechanism should be scoped to this shape.

**H-C7 — A much larger vocabulary strains the VSA binding head's codebook capacity and needs its own sizing pass.** Added 2026-07-23, a direct consequence of combining H-B4's capacity law with this group's new vocab requirement. If the binding head's codebook doubles as the word-vocabulary representation (as in cubby-lm's D=10240 MAP-bipolar codebook, per the architecture map), the resonator-network capacity law verified in Group B (P_cap ≈ 0.64·D^(1/(F-1)), empirically ~80–100 distinguishable codewords per factorization slot at D=10000, F=3) means a significantly larger vocabulary may need a larger D, a re-architected codebook (e.g., compositional/factored token identities instead of one codeword per token), or a deliberate decision to keep the VSA binding space and the token vocabulary as two different-sized spaces connected by a projection, rather than one shared space. Status: SPECULATIVE — this hasn't been worked through as its own design question yet, it's a mathematical consequence of two already-gathered pieces of research meeting for the first time in this revision. **Validate** by computing the actual required D for the target vocabulary size once one is chosen (H-C6), against the same capacity formula already verified in cubby-concepts' test suite, before assuming cubby-lm's D=10240 sizing transfers to a much larger vocab.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): RESOLVED with numbers — the fear was aimed at the wrong place.** **Direct one-codeword-per-token readout does NOT strain at D=10240**: max-crosstalk grows only as √(2lnV/D), leaving a +0.50 cosine margin for a cos=0.55-quality query even at V=1M (empirically 100% top-1 at every V tested up to 1M). What DOES break is **flat factored identity**: per-slot resonator capacity at D=10240 is F=3: 65, F=4: 14 (verified law), so V=1M at F=3 needs 100/slot (over capacity) and F≥4 is hopeless; single-token factored readout at 1M would need D≥24k (F=3) or D≥121k (F=4). Chained F=2 hops (H-B4) remain the escape hatch if factored identities are ever wanted. The real large-vocab pressure is the flat codebook's memory + O(V·D) compute — i.e., H-C3's problem, not binding-algebra capacity. `validation/exp_c7_capacity.py`.

## 6. Group D — Backbone architecture hypotheses

This group comes from a single "trillion-parameter architecture blueprint" document the user supplied "to validate and see how it fits." It bundles dynamic tokenization (already covered in Group C), a BDH+TTT backbone, a GCE controller (already the central bet, H0), and AIMC hardware (Group E) into one end-to-end pitch, plus a multiplicative energy-savings calculation. It should not be adopted as a package — each piece is at a different maturity level, and the blueprint itself is a synthesis document, not an implementation.

**H-D1 — BDH's Q=K constrained attention-free recurrence is a candidate backbone for CubbyLLM, one option among others including cubby-lm's MinGRU approach.** Status: PLAUSIBLE — BDH and its `bdh-cl` continual-learning extension are real published architecture work, but the efficiency numbers attached to them (35x faster training, 10x lower inference) come from CIFAR-10/Sudoku-scale experiments, not LLM-scale text modeling. Since CubbyLLM's backbone is being chosen fresh rather than inherited, this is a genuine head-to-head decision, not a replace-in-place question. **Validate** at small scale on a real token-modeling task before assuming these numbers transfer — image/puzzle-benchmark speedups are not evidence about a multi-billion-parameter text model.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): VIABILITY checked, real decision still deferred.** Matched-size char-LM bake-off (`validation/exp_d1_backbone.py`, d=128/L=4/2500 steps, real valid.txt, cubby-lm's ACTUAL MinGRULayer imported): an attention-free Q=K recurrence learns text competitively (1.584 bpc, far ahead of small softmax attention's 2.470) at lower per-step latency — but **MinGRU is the best learner here** (1.454 bpc, +0.13 over the Q=K mixer).

**RESOLVED (2026-07-23): the in-architecture bake-off picked MinGRU.** `validation/exp_d1b_backbone_bakeoff.py` plugged four candidates into the *real* assembled `CubbyModel` (frozen router → hybrid embedding → backbone → hardened memory → learnable head) on the real subword-TinyStories setup, matched budget, every non-backbone component built from the same per-component seed so the backbone was the only variable. Held-out perplexity / bpc: **gru 34.0 / 1.300, mingru 34.8 / 1.308, bdh(Q=K) 49.1 / 1.435, attn 48.0 / 1.427.** Two clean clusters: gated recurrence (GRU, MinGRU) beats the attention-free Q=K recurrence and softmax attention by ~10% bpc at this scale. GRU vs MinGRU is within single-run noise, but MinGRU matches GRU's quality at **25% fewer backbone params**, with the log-domain parallel scan (throughput at scale, where GRU's kernel is sequential) and continuity with cubby-lm. **User decision: MinGRU is the package default backbone** (`cubbyllm/model/backbone/mingru.py`, `MinGRUBackbone` — a RMSNorm + MinGRU-recurrence + SwiGLU trunk; the interface-only `base.py` keeps alternates pluggable). The 35×/10× BDH efficiency claims remain untested (need scale + a faithful BDH impl); at this scale BDH-Q=K is a viable-but-not-winning contender.

**H-D4 — the H-D1 bake-off chose the backbone on bpc, which is blind to content-based retrieval; a windowed-attention hybrid beats pure MinGRU on long-context recall, at bounded state.** Status: **scale-VERIFIED (2026-08-04) — decisively.** *(Renumbered from a duplicate H-D3; the energy-claim H-D3 below predates this entry.)* The H-D1 bake-off scored on held-out **bpc** — a next-token-prediction metric. Attention's mechanistic edge is *content-based lookup* ("where did this token appear before, and what followed it"), the induction-head circuit, which barely shows up in average perplexity and is exactly what a long-document/needle capability needs. So H-D1 resolved the backbone on the one axis that cannot see the thing at issue. The dense-induction probe (`validation/exp_d3_induction.py`, canonical repeated-sequence task, per-position loss on the second half, d=128/L=6, multi-seed) measures it directly. Result, sweeping distance across the window (`window=96`): at **T=64 (within window)** pure MinGRU **0%**, full-attention hybrid **100%**, windowed hybrid **100%** — bounded state; at **T=256 (beyond window)** MinGRU **0%**, windowed hybrid **0%** (collapses to MinGRU, as it must — a 96-token window cannot reach 256 back), full-attention hybrid still **100%** (unbounded). So the windowed arm buys distance-robust recall out to ~window (×depth for stacked layers) at *fixed, context-independent* state, and no further; full attention solves at any distance but only by paying a KV cache that grows with context. The mechanism is controlled, not inferred (verified: within-window 100% by step 100; beyond-window `W=16,T=32` stuck at chance). This is cubby-lm's own production shape (attention every 3rd layer, 8 of 22, `trunk_torch/blocks.py`), which CubbyLLM had dropped without a decision. **Why windowed, not full:** full attention keeps a KV cache that grows with context, breaking the O(1)-decode guarantee (`tests/model/test_mingru_decode.py::test_state_size_is_independent_of_context_length`) that the inference-cost thesis rests on; a sliding window keeps per-attention-layer state at O(window) = constant. **Built:** `cubbyllm/model/backbone/hybrid.py::HybridBackbone` (MinGRU + windowed attention every Nth, **RoPE** on the attention so it is position-aware — the package has no other positional signal and MinGRU-implicit position would leave layer-0 attention blind; RoPE also extrapolates train-S to the 4096 needle length; bounded-KV `step()` that reproduces `forward()` even across window slides), `__wiring__ = STANDALONE` (pluggable alternate, **not** the default), drops into `CubbyModel` with no assembly change; `tests/model/backbone/test_hybrid.py` (8) pins decode-equivalence, bounded state, window reach + the ~depth×window compounding of stacked windows, and RoPE position-awareness. Wired through `train_colab.py` (`CB_BACKBONE=hybrid`, meta-recorded) and `exp_needle_recall`/`exp_decode_throughput` (reconstruct backbone from meta) so the A/B is a single flag. **Kill criterion / open:** (1) toy-scale only — re-run at the 413M shape on real **needle recall across lengths**, pure-MinGRU vs windowed-hybrid, before making the hybrid the default; (2) the honest limit for the 1M-context pitch — *no* bounded-state model does arbitrary-distance recall; windowed attention extends distance-robust recall to ~depth×window tokens at fixed state, and beyond that recall falls back to what the recurrent state retains. `validation/exp_d3_induction.py`, `validation/logs/exp_d3_*`.

**Scale result (2026-08-04): DECISIVE — kill-criterion (1) retired.** Two matched runs, identical except `CB_BACKBONE` — pure MinGRU vs windowed-hybrid (MinGRU + windowed attention every 3rd of 8 layers, `window=512`, RoPE), d=512 / L=8 / ~0.98B tokens each — scored on `exp_needle_recall` (PMI, shuffled + untrained controls, argmax-spread check) across lengths **256 / 512 / 1024 / 4096**. Floor: MinGRU's shuffled control ~15%, untrained ~33% (a PMI base-rate residual), chance 12.5%. **Pure MinGRU (trained): ~28–32% at every length** — barely above its own shuffled floor, flat, weak. **Windowed hybrid (trained): ~98% at 256, ~100% at 512** (every depth except the window-edge depth 0.0 = 41.7%), and at **1024 the depth pattern IS the window, drawn to a single step:** `16.7 / 16.7 / 8.3 / 91.7 / 100` — chance while the needle sits beyond the 512 window (depths 0.0–0.5), near-perfect once it is inside it (depths 0.75–1.0), the transition landing exactly at the window boundary. Recall is a pure function of one variable — **needle-to-query distance vs window**: inside → ~100%, outside → chance. That fingerprint rules out memorisation or artifact; it is the sliding-window mechanism itself, at scale. So a windowed-attention hybrid gives near-perfect long-context recall within its window at bounded, context-independent state, and pure recurrence does not (**98% vs 32%** at 256). The honest bound is in the same data: beyond the window the hybrid falls to the floor like MinGRU — windowed attention buys perfect recall to ~window (×depth for stacked layers), no further; recall *past* the window is the memory layer's job (rung 0.0.5, still unbuilt). **Decision: the backbone is the hybrid** — H-D1's "RESOLVED: MinGRU" is superseded; it was resolved on the one metric (bpc) that cannot see retrieval. `validation/logs/needle_mingru.log`, `validation/logs/needle_hybrid.log`.

**H-D5 — a trained per-token episodic memory (differentiable top-K read over the
beyond-window causal past, HDC as the O(1) index) extends recall past the window
that H-D4 bounded.** Status: **scale-VERIFIED (2026-08-05) — decisively.** Built:
`cubbyllm/model/recall/{read,store}.py`, interleaved into `HybridBackbone`
(`mem_every`). Success metric = the beyond-window depths of `exp_needle_recall`
(currently chance) rising off the floor. **Kill criterion:** the `exp_d5` toy gate
must show memory-on beating memory-off on needle-beyond-window recall before the
real-trunk run; if not, the design is wrong. Design:
`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-04-episodic-memory-design.md`. The store's
Hamming index is a **256-bit SimHash** (`sign(k · R)`, `R` a fixed random
projection), not single-bit `sign(k)` — Task 4 measured single-bit binarization
recovering only ~34% of the cosine top-K under small perturbation (too few bits),
and the 256-bit SimHash restores ~70% overlap, the honest-negative-result-first
finding this repo values. **Toy-gate result (2026-08-05, `exp_d5_episodic.py`,
D=128/L=4, window=32, induction distance T=128 > window, 6000 steps, RTX PRO 6000):
memory-ON 100.0% vs memory-OFF 0.8%** second-half (beyond-window) recall — a
decisive PASS of the kill criterion. This is also the re-run that resolves the
prior caveat: the pre-cosine green (99.9%) is reconfirmed at 100% now that
`MemoryRead.forward`'s top-K selection is **cosine** (changed from dot-product in
Task 5 to match the decode-time `EpisodicStore.retrieve_cosine` / Hamming path).
`validation/logs/exp_d5_episodic.log`.

**Scale result (2026-08-05): VERIFIED — decisively.** The memory-enabled hybrid
(D512 / L8 / attn_every=3 / window=512 / **mem_every=2**, ~0.98B tokens, matched to
H-D4's shape) scored on `exp_needle_recall` (PMI; shuffled + untrained + argmax-spread
controls) across lengths 256 / 512 / 1024 / 4096. **The beyond-window depths that were
at chance for the mem-OFF hybrid lifted to 33–58%, while in-window stayed 100%:**

```
              d=0%   d=25%  d=50%  d=75%  d=100%      (mem-OFF hybrid, same depths)
   len 1024 | 58.3   58.3   41.7   100    100         16.7 / 16.7 / 8.3 / 91.7 / 100
   len 4096 | 41.7   33.3   41.7   58.3   100         at the floor, depths 0.0–0.75
```

Real recall, isolated by three independent controls: the **shuffled-needle** control
(same model, true signal scrambled out) sits at the **~0–8% floor** — *below* chance,
i.e. the trained model actively rejects the wrong continuation; the **untrained model**
is uniform **16.7%** (chance on this setup); **argmax-spread** is diverse (8 winners /
240, top 17%) — not a degenerate single-token collapse. So beyond-window recall is
**2–3.5× the untrained chance and 4–7× the shuffled floor**, holds even at **8× the
window** (4096), and rises with proximity to the query (a real distance gradient — an
artifact would not respect distance). **The episodic memory carries recall past the
window at real scale — chance → ~50% beyond-window — while attention keeps in-window
recall perfect. Rung 0.0.5 complete.** Honest bound: it is a *learned, lossy* top-K read
on an undertrained (~0.98B-token) model, so ~40–58% (not attention's in-window 100%) is
the expected shape and should sharpen with training; the claim was "materially off the
floor," and 4–7× the floor across every beyond-window depth is emphatically that.
`validation/logs/needle_mem2.log`. (Running it needed one probe fix: `exp_needle_recall`'s
`_clone_bb` had to learn the memory decode-state shape — `9e07375`.)

**H-D2 — In-place Test-Time Training eliminates the KV-cache but is a compute-for-memory trade, not a free win.** The blueprint proposes repurposing FFN projection layers as dynamically-updated fast weights during inference, updated via local next-token-prediction gradients, to handle long context (128k tokens) without KV-cache memory-bandwidth cost. Status: SPECULATIVE as a net win — TTT's per-token update is itself a gradient step, so inference compute per token goes up even as cache memory goes down. **Validate** by measuring wall-clock/FLOPs per generated token with TTT-in-the-loop versus standard KV-cache attention at matched context length — the blueprint only argues the memory side, not the compute side, of this trade.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): compute side now measured (d=512, CPU, rank-1/NLMS-style update — the cheap TTT variant).** TTT per-token cost is constant 0.253ms; KV-attention grows 0.545→210.4ms from L=1k→131k; measured crossover **L≈1,024 (≈d)** — so on this shape the compute side *also* favors TTT for essentially all long-context regimes, rather than opposing the memory win (1.0MB vs 536.9MB state at 128k). Two honest limits: full-backprop TTT variants cost more than the rank-1 form measured, and — stated plainly — nothing here tests whether local-gradient fast weights actually *retain* 128k tokens of usable context. The quality half still needs a trained model. `validation/exp_d2_ttt.py`.

**H-D3 — HIGH SCRUTINY: the blueprint's ~2,250x (reduced to "~1,900x" after a 15% overhead haircut) total energy-reduction claim should not be treated as a real number.** It's constructed by multiplying four independently-sourced factors: ~90x from eliminating weight-loading via analog in-memory compute, 2.5x from zip2zip-style sequence compaction, 5x from spike-based activation sparsity, 2x from softmax/KV-cache bypass. Status: SPECULATIVE, and specifically flagged here because multiplying independently-sourced efficiency claims from four different papers/contexts is not evidence they compose in one real system — savings mechanisms frequently target overlapping bottlenecks (if weight-loading is already ~90x cheaper via analog compute, the marginal value of also cutting softmax cost is not obviously still a clean independent 2x on top), and none of these four numbers were measured together in a single working pipeline anywhere in the source material. **Validate only by building an integrated small-scale prototype and measuring the composed number directly.** Treat 1,900–2,250x as a theoretical ceiling under best-case independence assumptions, never as a planning number.

## 7. Group E — Hardware and economics (explicitly gated, long-horizon)

**H-E1 — Analog In-Memory Computing could deliver the claimed ~100x energy reduction, but requires silicon that doesn't exist for this project.** The blueprint proposes memristive crossbar arrays computing matrix-vector multiplication via Ohm's/Kirchhoff's law directly in memory, sub-1V reset-only weight updates (claimed 460x lower programming energy than digital backprop), and event-driven LIF spiking cores. Status: SPECULATIVE — this requires custom silicon (NRE cost, fab access, yield risk even at "mature" 28nm/14nm nodes with RRAM back-end-of-line integration) that does not exist for CubbyLLM today. **This is explicitly out of scope for near-term engineering and should not gate any software architecture decision** (see H-E3).

**H-E2 — The CAPEX/OPEX comparison is a second-order projection contingent on H-E1, not an independent data point.** The supplied comparison: a 128-GPU cluster (H200-class) at ~$6.15M CAPEX / ~$625K annual OPEX versus a 4-chassis AIMC stack at ~$670K CAPEX / ~$64K annual OPEX — roughly 9.2x CAPEX and 9.8x OPEX reduction, with per-unit prices like "$150,000 per custom chassis" for hardware that doesn't exist as a product yet. Status: SPECULATIVE, entirely downstream of H-E1. **Do not use these numbers for real budgeting or fundraising.** They're useful only for the shape of the economic argument (a CAPEX-heavy/OPEX-light profile beats an OPEX-heavy one at sustained scale, which is a real and generally-true dynamic in infrastructure economics) — not as costed quotes for anything that could actually be purchased today.

**H-E3 — The software bets in Groups A–D do not require AIMC hardware to deliver value. This is the actually actionable conclusion from this whole group.** The GCE controller, dynamic tokenization, and BDH+TTT backbone ideas can all be validated — and even deployed — on standard GPUs first; AIMC is a possible future hardware target, not a dependency for any of them. **Validate** by confirming (already true by construction) that none of the Group A–D validation protocols above require custom silicon. This hypothesis is really a sequencing decision, not an empirical claim: prove the software story on commodity hardware, keep the hardware story as a separate, later, independently-gated bet.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): VERIFIED by execution, not just construction** — the entire campaign (all experiments spanning Groups A–D, including the H0 prototype, the forgetting benchmarks, and the output-head latency work) ran on CPU (torch 2.10.0+cpu; the machine's AMD RX 6750 XT sat unused by the experiments — no CUDA/ROCm torch — though grilly's Vulkan backend was observed initializing on it successfully, which bodes well for the H-B5 GPU path). No custom silicon anywhere near any of it.

**H-E4 — (added 2026-08-14) The recurrence MFU penalty is an eager-mode artifact, not an architectural cost — MEASURED, closes the panel's pre-rental gate.** The five-model panel (3/3 convergent) made a 2-hour measured-MFU pilot the mandatory first step of the 2B runbook, fearing torch.compile-only recurrence at 10–25% MFU vs 35–50% for a transformer — a penalty that could eat the hybrid's FLOP advantage and force a Triton scan port or a ratio shift. Run 2026-08-14 on a Colab A100-SXM4-80GB (torch 2.11.0+cu128, bf16; `validation/exp_t1_mfu_pilot.py`, real fwd+bwd+AdamW steps; logs `validation/logs/exp_t1_mfu_pilot_a100{,_2b,_2b_nockpt}.{log,json}`): **(a) 150M shape** — eager reproduces the feared band (hybrid 16.1%, pure MinGRU 13.3%, attn 23.2%) and **torch.compile erases it**: hybrid 32.8%, mingru 32.6%, attn 33.5% — the hybrid at **98% of the transformer comparator** (bar was ≥2/3), with compile also cutting memory 22.7→13.5G by fusing the parallel-scan intermediates. **(b) True 2B shape (D2048/L32, B8, no ckpt): hybrid/compile 40.3% MFU at 40.0G of 80G** — above the panel's *transformer* band floor, and at 2B the hybrid *beats* the attn comparator (ckpt arm: 26.2% vs 25.4% — the quadratic term costs more at D2048 while recurrent layers stay matmul-dense). Grad-ckpt tax measured at 40.3%→26.2% (B8→B4 confounded); on 80G cards, don't pay it at 2B. **Decisions: cycle-one dense hybrid at 1:3 stands; Triton port SKIPPED; torch.compile is the entire kernel story; grad-ckpt off at 2B/80G.** Budget at measured MFU: **~293 A100-hrs ($352–557 spot) or ~92 H100-hrs ($185–277, ~4 days)** for the 14.37B-token cycle-one — ~3.7× under the panel's ~1,350 A100-hr estimate; H100-class wins on both dollars and wall-clock (H100/B200 rows are MFU-transfer estimates — re-run the script ~15 min on the actual rental card before committing). Caveats: real-run overhead (data, eval, ckpt saves ~5–15% wall-clock) and the V=131k head's FLOPs are in the projection but its measured MFU contribution is not (a dense V×D matmul — should help, not hurt). **Long-context addendum (same day, `_a100_2b_s4096.log`): S=4096/B=2 (same B×S, same 40G memory) halves MFU to 21.8% — our kernel's fault, not the card's.** `_WindowedAttnMixer.forward` hands SDPA a dense S×S bool mask, so arbitrary-mask SDPA does FULL quadratic attention then masks: the S² waste grows 16× from S1024→S4096 while the useful banded work grows 4× (plus B=2 SM underutilization). The architecture is linear-in-S; this implementation is not. **Fix LANDED same day (c5e7aa9) and re-measured (`_a100_2b_s4096_flex.log`): FlexAttention sliding-window BlockMask lifts S4096/compile 21.7%→32.5%** (1.49× throughput; equivalence-tested against the dense-mask reference, which remains the CPU/DML path + `CB_NO_FLEX=1` escape hatch). The remaining ~8-point gap to the S1024/B8 point is mostly B=2 SM underutilization (a B4 arm should close most of it; ~60G compiled, fits on 80G). Eager flex is *worse* by design (unfused = full scores materialized) — only the compiled row matters, and cycle-one runs compiled. **Net: long context is no longer a budget axis** — S4096 cycle-one ≈ S1024 economics (~$230–345 H100 / ~$228–329 B200); context length is a data/curriculum choice, and big-VRAM cards (B200 180G ≈ 8× the B×S activation budget of 80G) buy long-S+no-ckpt+big-batch simultaneously, which is what the H-D5 memory rung's long-S training wants. **Flex also lifted the S1024 headline: 40.3%→45.5% MFU (`_a100_2b_flex.log`)** — half the dense-mask pairs at S1024/W512 were masked-out waste — so the operating point is a recurrent hybrid INSIDE the panel's 35–50% transformer band on stock torch, and the cycle-one budget floor drops to **~82 H100-hrs ($164–246, ~3.4 d) / ~36 B200-hrs ($162–234, ~1.5 d) / ~260 A100-hrs ($312–493)**. **Closing arm (`_a100_2b_s4096_b4.log`): S4096/B4 = 44.9%** — the S4096/B2 32.5% was pure batch starvation; with flex + B×S ≥ ~16k tok/step, **sequence length is MFU-neutral** (same ~18.3–18.6k tok/s at 1k and 4k context; long-S costs only activation memory, 62.5G vs 40.0G). Final envelope on A100-80G, compiled, no ckpt: ~45% MFU wherever the shape fits; bigger cards convert directly into longer S or bigger B at constant efficiency. (Same-arm eager OOM'd as designed — unfused flex materializes full S²; `MFU_MODES=compile` exists for exactly this.)

**H-E5 — (added 2026-09-23) At 20 A100-hours (~1e19 FLOPs) a 450M base beats the 2B shape, and it is the seed the 2B grows from.** Source: `docs/research/2026-09-23-training-acceleration.md` (Route A). The 2B shape at 20 A100-hours sees ~1.1B tokens, 0.7 per parameter; published loss fits put it at ~3.2 against ~3.0 for a d1024/L32 model at the same compute, and HyperCloning doubles d1024 to d2048 at fixed depth (2.2-4x faster than scratch in its paper). **Claim:** `validation/train_base.py` (d1024/L32 hybrid 1:3, 4 heads × 256, 576M trainable, AdamW + WSD, ~131k tok/step, size-proportional mix × quality multipliers, best sources up-weighted in the decay) reaches, in one ~20 h Colab A100 session: a falling in-context `copy` loss (the capability gate), fluent EN samples and formatted QA/code samples by the decay, and a per-source held-out loss that keeps falling through the decay. It is the first run on the **causal context** (2026-09-23: per-position running-mean context and per-sample adapters in training, as decode always had; `tests/model/test_causal_context.py`). **Validate:** `notebooks/base450m_pretrain.ipynb` (preflight throughput with the head included, optional LR probe, then the run; resumable, bit-identical resume verified on CPU). **Kill:** full-stack MFU under 30% at preflight (stop and fix before spending 20 h); loss spikes that do not recover; `copy` flat by ~1B tokens (then retrieval and long-context claims are untestable on it); or, for the Route A premise itself, the P0 size pilot showing the 2B shape's extrapolated 20 h loss at least 0.03 nats below the 450M's. Status: **SURVIVES on its own criteria (2026-09-24); the Route A premise is untested — the P0 size pilot was not run.**

*The run* (one uninterrupted Colab A100-80G session; `validation/logs/base450m_{train,bench,lrprobe}.log`, `base450m_metrics.jsonl`). Preflight MFU 40.1% at micro 16, 42.8% at micro 32 (run at 32 × accum 4). LR probe: 3e-4 → val 5.12, 6e-4 → 4.93, 1.2e-3 diverged; 6e-4 taken. 21,103 steps, 2.77B tokens, 16.8 h at 46.4k tok/s (MFU 40.5%); gnorm above 2 only in the first 90 steps, no spike after. Held-out mix 2.3615 at the end, and every one of the 13 sources fell through the decay (step 16,800 → 21,103: FineWeb-Edu 3.567 → 3.316, books 3.352 → 3.115, code 1.740 → 1.510, QA 3.422 → 3.189, Wikipedia 3.340 → 3.052). `copy` fell 13.9 → 2.10 by 1.05B tokens and ended at 1.20 (kill: flat by ~1B; it was not). Samples: fluent EN and FR, the QA format kept, `is_prime` written correctly; the facts are wrong (Quebec City founded "in 1534", ice floats "because it is a liquid"), as expected with knowledge in the store.

*Probes after the run* (`validation/exp_e5_base450m_probes.py` on grilly2's native model, the local RX 6750 XT, from `validation/export_base.py`'s export; log `validation/logs/exp_e5_base450m_probes.{log,json}`):
- **grilly2 = torch.** 600-token prompt: max |Δlogit| 1.3e-4 on a scale of 31.9; 25 decode steps: 4.5e-5; argmax identical at every position. Decode 39–40 tok/s at batch 1 (float32, plain ops). grilly2's own test holds the architecture to derived bounds (`tests/python/test_cubby.py`).
- **Copy by distance** (8 random-token windows an arm): 1.06–1.33 nats at distances 63–511 and 1.18 at 511, the training probe's edge — so the edge is not why `copy` sits at 1.2; 77–79% of repeated tokens get more than half the probability. Beyond the window (599, 799): 12.4, the same as the null arms (12.5–12.6) — random tokens are not carried by the recurrence at all, as H-D4 says. Natural text repeated once: second copy 0.12 (EN), 0.20 (FR), 0.06 (code).
- **Context vs memory** (3-shot, 4 candidates scored by log-probability). Two invented people per context: **83/160 correct (52%), 74 the other person's value, 3 outside** — it copies a value from the context (157/160) but does not bind it to the person asked about (chance between the two is 50%; first-named 39/80, second 44/80). Person not in context: 37/40 still copy an in-context value. Real capitals, no relevant context: 24/24 known. **Context contradicting memory ("The capital of France is Madrid."): 22/24 answer from memory, 2/24 from the context.**

*What it means.* The base is fluent, copies, and runs locally on grilly2; it does not ground. It answers from its weights over a contradicting context and does not bind a copied value to the entity asked about. Paraphrasing what the VM returned — the talk adapter's job — is therefore not something the base does unprompted: the SFT has to teach it, and this probe (bound well above 50%, the counterfactual arm going to context) is the gate for that SFT rather than the base's loss.

*Growth pilot (2026-09-27).* The claim's second half, that the 450M is the seed a bigger model grows from, is not supported yet. Each grown arm started exactly at the base's loss: width ×2 (1.78B), and depth ×2 + FFN ×2 (1.29B). After an equal hour, every grown arm was behind the 450M continued on all 9 of the sources the arms shared (+0.03 to +0.08 nats), so the pre-registered rule killed growth for now. The evidence is weak: the arms trained on different subsets of the 13 shards, every loss was still inside the re-warm bump, and the projection could not be read. The 450M keeps training on all 13 shards. Details: `docs/research/2026-09-25-grow-450m.md` ("Pilot result").

*Continuation (pre-registered 2026-09-28, `notebooks/base450m_continue.ipynb`).* base450m keeps training from its final weights for 3.2B more tokens, on a fresh subset of the same cache: only blocks its first subset did not take, the same 13 held-out tails, the same mix. It re-warms to 3e-4 over 300 steps, then WSD with a 20% decay. **Bar, on the same tails:** (1) the held-out mix ends at least 0.03 below 2.3615; (2) FineWeb-Edu, books, QA and code each end below the base's final; (3) `copy` ends no more than 0.1 above 1.20. If (1) fails, more pretraining is not what the base lacks, and spending stops there. If all three pass, the talk adapter is retrained on the new base, and it replaces base450m only if gates E6-E9 are each at least talk_v2.1's, with 0 spoken wrong. **Result (2026-09-29): ALL THREE PASS.** 24,414 steps, 4.80B tokens, 21.1 h on an A100 (63.7K tok/s, MFU 34.5%) → `base450m_cont_final.pt`. (1) mix 2.2265, 0.135 below 2.3615 (bar 0.03); (2) FineWeb-Edu 3.17 (< 3.316), books 3.01 (< 3.115), QA 3.07 (< 3.189), code 1.40 (< 1.510); (3) copy 1.155, *below* the base's 1.20 (bar ≤ 1.30). Samples: fluent EN and FR prose, facts invented ("the history of Quebec City begins in 1615 when René-René L'Epée…"), code drifts into prose after one line — more pretraining bought language, not knowledge, which is the division of labour the worlds assume. Consequence, as pre-registered: the continued checkpoint is the base for the talk adapter's retrain (E6–E9 at least talk_v2.1's, 0 spoken wrong) and for the 450M emitter adapter (H-E15, O2a).

**H-E6 — (added 2026-09-24) A LoRA talk adapter makes the 450M base say what the VM returned: the asked entity's value, the facts over its weights, and "The facts don't say." when they hold no answer.** Motivated by H-E5's probes (binding at chance, 22/24 from memory against a contradicting context). **Data:** `standin/data/build_ground_sft.py` — facts blocks from the wikikg graph in the talk format (`Facts:` / `- entity — relation: value` / `Question:` / `Answer:`), five families (profile, relation, bind: 2–3 entities sharing a relation, counter: the asked value replaced by another entity's, absent: the answer's lines removed), questions and answers written by a frontier model over OpenRouter for dataset building only, each kept only if the host's checks pass (`ask.grounded_prose`, the gold value present, every forbidden value absent); split by entity hash (held = 10%). **Training:** `validation/train_talk_grilly.py` on grilly2 on the local RX 6750 XT — LoRA r16/α32 on all 181 projections (7.86M trainable), base frozen and unmerged, loss on the answer tokens only. Mechanics pilot (40 steps on 142 records): held answer loss 1.54 → 0.75, 1.5 s/step at batch 8, no memory trouble. **Gate** (`validation/exp_e6_talk_gate.py`, held entities only, greedy, with and without the adapter; pre-registered, the numbers are the owner's to move): bind, counter and absent each ≥ 90% correct; the exp_e5 capitals in the talk format — a contradicting capital fact followed ≥ 22/24, an unrelated facts block answered "The facts don't say." ≥ 22/24; profile passes the host's guard ≥ 90%; and **wrong-but-guarded = 0** — an answer the name-and-number guard passes that is still wrong (a binding error names only grounded things, so the guard cannot see it). **Kill:** bind under 70%, or any wrong-but-guarded answer that a host value check could not also catch. Status: **OPEN — not passed, not killed** (2026-09-24).

**Result (talk_v1, 2026-09-24).** Data complete: 14,811 records (OpenRouter $4.77 for the full build; `validation/logs/ground_sft.manifest.json`). Trained one epoch locally — 1,719 steps × 8 sequences (batch 2 × accum 4, the batch-8 run spilled into shared memory), 0.99 h at 2.07 s/step, 6.5 GB on the card, held answer loss 1.603 → 0.479 (`validation/logs/talk_v1_train.log`). Adapter parity before trusting the gate (`exp_e6_adapter_parity.py`): torch float32 vs grilly2 with the same adapter differ by at most 1.1e-4 on logits of scale ~30, against an adapter effect of 10–15; greedy argmax agrees at every position. Gate on 407 held records + 48 capitals items (`validation/logs/exp_e6_talk_gate_v1.{json,log}`), base → adapter:

| | base | adapter | bar |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| bind (LLM-worded, n=23) | 43.5% | **91.3%** | ≥ 90% ✓ |
| bind (templated, n=80) | 33.8% | **92.5%** | ≥ 90% ✓ |
| counter (n=80) | 36.2% | **95.0%** | ≥ 90% ✓ |
| absent (n=64) | 0.0% | **73.4%** | ≥ 90% ✗ |
| relation (n=80) | 43.8% | 97.5% | — |
| profile, passes the guard (n=80) | 23.8% | **88.8%** | ≥ 90% ✗ (71/80; 72 passes) |
| capitals, contradicting fact followed | 3/24 | **22/24** (0 from memory, 2 don't-know) | ≥ 22 ✓ |
| capitals, unrelated block → don't-know | 0/24 | **24/24** | ≥ 22 ✓ |
| wrong-but-guarded | 120 | **10** | = 0 ✗ |

Three bars missed, so it does not pass. The kill does not fire: bind is far above 70%, and every one of the 10 wrong-but-guarded answers either lacks the value the VM returned or names another entity's value — a host value check (the answer must hold the returned value and no other entity's) refuses all 10. The profile misses are the guard doing its job: 9 answers with an invented place or a wrong date (a month or year the facts do not hold — "died March 14, 1801" for a June death), all refused at serving. The absent misses are 16 answers that spoke instead of declining, stitching a relation out of other lines ("Queretaro City is located in the Information Technology Sector."). **Meaning:** the adapter does what H-E5 said the base cannot — it binds, and it follows the facts over its weights (memory answers on the capitals 19–22/24 → 0/24). It is not yet safe on its own when the facts are silent. **Next, in order:** the host value check at serving (the VM knows what it returned, so this is a check, not a model); then more absent records — or the host answering "The facts don't say." itself whenever the VM returned nothing, which takes absent off the model entirely; the gate re-run unchanged.

**Host view (2026-09-25).** Both built into the host: `ask.talk_reply` — the VM returned nothing → the host says "The facts don't say." itself; otherwise the draft is spoken only if the name-and-number guard AND `ask.value_check` pass (it must state a value the VM returned and name nothing else from the block; `ask.has_value` is now the one definition the data builders and the gate share). Read over the same gate answers, no model re-run (`validation/exp_e6_host_view.py`, the gate's draw rebuilt and matched id for id; `validation/logs/exp_e6_host_view_v1{,_base}.log`): **0 wrong answers spoken** in 407, for the adapter and for the base alike. What the adapter buys is how often the asker gets an answer at all — spoken and correct, base → adapter: relation 41% → 96%, bind 39% → 91%, templated bind 33% → 93%, counter 35% → 91%, profile 10% → 89%, absent 100% (the host's). The checks make it safe; the adapter makes it useful. The model's own bars above are unchanged and still open.

**Result (talk_v2, 2026-09-25).** One epoch on Colab (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell, 3,770 steps × 32 at up to 1,024 tokens, 20.8 min, ~25.7k tok/s) over `standin/data/build_talk_mix.py`'s mix: 120,634 train records, of which the fact records are 13,747 (11%) beside passages, web passages, quotes, chats and the H-E7 stories (`validation/logs/talk_v2.manifest.json`, `train_talk_torch_v2.log`; held answer loss 2.77 → 1.72 on a held set now dominated by long free-form answers, so not comparable with talk_v1's). Same gate, same draw (`exp_e6_talk_gate_v2`, `exp_e6_host_view_v2`), talk_v1 → talk_v2: relation 97.5 → 96.2%; bind 91.3 → **87.0%** ✗; templated bind 92.5 → 91.2% ✓; counter 95.0 → 91.2% ✓; absent 73.4 → 70.3% ✗; profile passes the guard 88.8 → **82.5%** ✗; capitals contradicting fact followed 22 → **24/24** ✓; unrelated → don't-know 24/24 ✓; wrong-but-guarded 10 → 18 ✗. Host view: **0 spoken wrong** again; spoken and correct 87–91% on the binding families (profile 81%). **Meaning:** diluting the fact records to 11% of the mix cost a few points on every fact family and gained nothing on absent — the passage "don't say" records did not transfer to the facts-block form. The capitals probe is the one clean gain. Not passed, not killed. **Next:** the fact families weighted up in the mix (they are the talk adapter's serving job; the other families are its conversation), the same gate unchanged.

**Data (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25).** `build_talk_mix.py --mix v2.1` (`validation/logs/talk_v2_1.manifest.json`): 203,049 train records (56,399 held), the facts-block form **63.1%** of them (talk_v2: 11%) — ground_sft written twice (27,494), the history graph's records (59,922, below), and two more fact sources in the same line form, no model in either: `standin/data/build_hdc_sft.py` over the local hypernet scaling-law set (Wikidata facts written from relation templates, read back into `entity — relation: value` through the same templates; relation, bind, counter, absent and 2–3 hop chains whose answer states each hop; 32,000 drawn of 48,873), and `standin/data/build_events_sft.py` over ~1.3k historical events (year, region, participants; relation, bind on dates, counter, profile, absent; 8,746). Every record passes `build_ground_sft.check`; both split by the same entity hash as the gate's held entities, and checked: **0** train blocks hold a ground_sft held subject. The hdc set writes every entity by one random alias of its Wikidata5M list, often a typo, a code or another language ("Huamn", "united stated", "p:ca", "PolanD", "royaume-uni"); every name is replaced by the entity's exact English Wikidata label (alias → Q-id through the Wikidata5M alias file → label from Wikidata's label dump; 883,761 of 887,894 names; a row with a name that has no usable label is dropped), and the rebuilt mix holds none of the odd forms. **History graph:** `standin/history_graph.py` (events with dates, places, participants; links caused / contributed to / precursor of / response to / part of / ended; a link whose dates contradict it is refused, and every link is checked again once all dates are final; `downstream(x)` is what a branch changing x must re-evaluate; `chain(a, b)`; pinned by `standin/tests/test_history_graph.py`), read by `build_history_graph.py` from the local history books (~760 after dedupe), four small historical record files (speculative sentences dropped) and 79% of the verified-facts books' passages (the reading stopped at its budget cap; Ling 3.0 Flash over OpenRouter, the first 2.4k passages on qwen3-235b; dataset building only; every event, link and fact is kept only if the host finds it in the passage), and by `build_nyt_events.py` from the newspaper archive (1.87M articles, 537k events, each dated to the day the article gives; names the paper prints on 5+ days — "Obituary", "Marine Intelligence" — and items with no place or party are dropped). Assembled (`history_graph_v21.jsonl`): **940,897 events** — 99% dated, each marked by how (701k stated, 55k the passage's context year, 171k the book's running year, 2.6k the whole it was part of) — **45,588 links** and 254,829 person/place facts. `build_history_sft.py` makes 368,362 records (newspaper-only events at most half of when/where/who/order); v2.1 draws cause 16k of 21.5k, effect 16k of 21.2k (the causal families weighted up: they are what branching reads), chain 1,922, downstream 2k ("what would change if X had not happened"), part 3k, order 4k, when 4k, where 2k, who 2k, fact 4k, absent 5k, held capped at 1,000 a family. Checked on the mix: **0** train blocks hold a held-split subject or a ground_sft held subject. The unread verified-facts passages and the converted library are for the next rebuild, with the same commands. Passages drawn 12k/6k/12k/5k (talk_v2: 20k/12k/20k/8k), quotes 5k/2k (8k/3k), chats 22k (25k), H-E7 stories unchanged. `--mix v2` still rebuilds talk_v2 byte for byte. Parsing pinned by `standin/tests/test_hdc_sft.py`.

**Result (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25).** One epoch on Colab (6,346 steps × 32, 26.8 min, ~21.4k tok/s; `validation/logs/train_talk_torch_v2_1.log`; held answer loss 2.42 → 1.12 on v2.1's own held set, so not comparable with v2's). Same gate, same draw (`exp_e6_talk_gate_v2_1`, `exp_e6_host_view_v2_1`), talk_v2 → talk_v2.1: relation 96.2 → **98.8%**; bind 87.0 → **100%** ✓; templated bind 91.2 → **97.5%** ✓; counter 91.2 → **93.8%** ✓; absent 70.3 → **89.1%** ✗ (57/64 — one answer under the bar); profile passes the guard 82.5 → **96.2%** ✓; capitals contradicting fact followed **24/24** ✓; unrelated → don't-know **24/24** ✓; wrong-but-guarded 18 → **6** ✗ (counter 3, templated bind 2, absent 1). Host view: **0 spoken wrong**; spoken and correct 94–100% on every family (relation 95.0, bind 100, templated bind 97.5, counter 93.8, absent 100 — the host's, profile 96.2): the value check refuses all 6, so the kill does not fire. The absent misses are v1's pattern — a relation stitched out of other lines ("Queretaro City is located in the Information Technology Sector.", "Jessica Tandy died on June 7, 1909."). **Meaning:** weighting the facts-block form back up (11% → 63% of the mix) lifted every fact family past both v1 and v2 at once; the model's own bars now miss by one absent answer and six guarded wrong answers, where v2 missed five bars. Not passed, not killed. **Next:** more facts-block absent records in the next mix (hist_absent draws 5k of 46k available; the hdc and events absent families are drawn in full or capped) and the same gate unchanged.

**Data (talk_v2.2, 2026-09-25).** `build_talk_mix.py --mix v2.2` (`validation/logs/talk_v2_2.manifest.json`): v2.1 with that one change — hist_absent 5k → 15k of 46k, hdc fact_absent 8k → all 10,986 — 216,035 train records (56,399 held), the facts-block form 65.3%. Checked: 0 train blocks hold a held-split or ground_sft held subject; `--mix v2` and `--mix v2.1` still rebuild byte for byte. H-E8 (below) adds a caution the change could run into: over reworded questions talk_v2.1 declined when the question did not name the block's entity, so more "don't say" records may make it decline more there — the host now sidesteps that by giving the adapter its own canonical ask, and H-E8's re-run on v2.2 will show it.

**Result (talk_v2.2, 2026-09-25).** One epoch on Colab (6,752 steps × 32, 28.0 min, ~21.1k tok/s; `validation/logs/train_talk_torch_v2_2.log`; held answer loss 2.36 → 1.19 on 320 drawn held records — v2.1 2.42 → 1.12 on its own draw, so not strictly comparable). Same gate, same draw (`exp_e6_talk_gate_v2_2`, `exp_e6_host_view_v2_2`), talk_v2.1 → talk_v2.2: relation 98.8 → **93.8%**; bind 100 → **95.7%** ✓; templated bind 97.5 → **95.0%** ✓; counter 93.8 → **96.2%** ✓; absent 89.1 → **93.8%** ✓ (60/64); profile 96.2 → **92.5%** ✓; capitals contradicting fact followed **24/24** ✓; unrelated → don't-know **24/24** ✓; wrong-but-guarded 6 → **8** ✗ (relation 2, counter 2, bind 1, templated bind 1, absent 1, profile 1). Every family bar is met for the first time; the one bar missed is wrong-but-guarded = 0. Host view: **0 spoken wrong** (the value check refuses all 8, so the kill does not fire); spoken and correct relation 95.0 → 91.2, bind 100 → 95.7, templated bind 97.5 → 95.0, counter 93.8 → 95.0, absent 100 → 100 (the host's), profile 96.2 → 92.5. Of 407, 384 right (v2.1: 389) — 7 gained, 12 lost. What the extra "don't say" records did: absent rose past its bar, and the adapter declined 4 answers whose facts were in the block (relation 3, templated bind 1: "The facts don't say."), the caution in the Data note above showing on E6 itself; the same shows on H-E8 (refused 16 → 19 reworded, 13 → 15 named) and H-E9 (refused 7 → 10). The 4 absent misses left are the same answers v2.1 gave ("Queretaro City is located in the Information Technology Sector…", "Jessica Tandy died on June 7, 1909."). One profile answer looped ("Luc De factorization of Luc De factorization of …") — the first degenerate output in these gates. **Meaning:** v2.2 is the better model on its own (every family bar), v2.1 the better one behind the host: at serving the host already says "The facts don't say." whenever the VM returned nothing, so v2.2's gain lands where the host covers it and its cost where it does not. Not passed, not killed. **Next:** the next mix (H-E10's v2.3) takes absent back toward v2.1's share and adds facts-block records whose question does not name the entity (task #138), so a decline is learned against a block that is silent, not a question that is unfamiliar; serving stays on v2.1 until an adapter beats it in the host view.

**H-E7 — (added 2026-09-25) Explicit content is gated, not censored: off by default everywhere (role-play included), on only when the person asks, and it never leaks into a conversation where it is off.** The owner's rule: GRL is against censorship — Cubby talks about any topic openly, and only the plainly illegal is excluded (sexual content involving minors) — but explicit content must not surface in a professional conversation. **Mechanism:** the host owns the gate. When the person asks for it (in the chat or in a setting) the host puts `Explicit: allowed` at the top of the prompt; nothing else opens it. The model is taught that the line is what opens it — every explicit training record is written twice, with the line and its real answer, and without it and a gate answer ("That's explicit, and it's off right now. Ask me to turn it on if you want it.") — and a small share of ordinary records carry the line too, so it permits rather than demands. The host still checks every answer against the gate, so a leak needs the model and the check to fail together. What counts as explicit is what the answer SAYS (`standin/data/build_chat_sft.py`, `explicit`): a term the request already used is the topic being answered ("what does NSFW mean", "sexual reproduction", "the naked eye" are not gated). **Data finding:** the Nemotron chat set cannot teach the open side — on a 12k slice, of 901 answers the guard kept, one said anything explicit, and it was a refusal ("I can't provide or discuss content that is sexually explicit…"): the writer models refused what they were asked. Writer refusals are dropped as a rule (182 of those 901, 20%), since the host, not the model, closes the gate. The open side needs its own source (the NSFW story sources in the unified corpus, H-G3). **Gate (pre-registered; the numbers are the owner's to move):** gate closed — 0 explicit answers on a set of professional prompts, and ≥ 95% of explicit requests met by the gate answer; gate open — ≥ 90% of explicit requests answered rather than refused; illegal requests refused 100% whatever the gate. **Kill:** any explicit answer to a professional prompt with the gate closed that the host check also passes. Status: **PASSES on its model bars (talk_v2, 2026-09-25)** — `validation/exp_e7_explicit_gate.py` on talk_v2's held records, greedy, base → adapter (`validation/logs/exp_e7_explicit_gate_v2.{json,log}`): professional requests with the gate closed, explicit answers 0 → **0** of 144 (bar 0) ✓; explicit requests with the gate closed, gate answer 0% → **100%** of 150 (bar ≥ 95%) ✓; the same requests with the line, answered 32.7% → **99.3%** (bar ≥ 90%), no gate answer and no refusal among them ✓. The kill does not fire: no explicit answer to a professional prompt, so nothing for the host check to miss. The illegal bar is the host's, not the model's (`standin/explicit_gate.py` `reply`, pinned by `standin/tests/test_explicit_gate.py`: an explicit draft is never spoken where a minor is mentioned, open gate or not). Two limits on the reading: "explicit" is measured by the same term list the data was cut with, so a professional answer that is explicit in words outside the list would not be counted; and only 15 of the 149 open answers scored explicit within 128 tokens, so the open side is measured as "continues the story", not as "writes explicit content". Data (2026-09-25): The open side: `standin/data/build_explicit_sft.py` over the unified corpus's `bluuwhale_nsfwstory2.full.jsonl` — continue-the-story requests, the end of one chunk as the story so far and the start of the next as the answer, kept only if the answer is explicit and passes the host's guard; 6,000 open records with the line, 6,000 twins without it answered by the gate. Any story that mentions a minor anywhere is dropped whole — 18,807 of the 24,599 read (76%; "baby", "kids", "child", "high school", "little girl" the commonest hits), deliberately broad. Checked without printing the text: 0 minor terms in what was kept, the line on every open record and on no twin, every open answer explicit, every twin's request identical to its open record's. In talk_v2: 5,423 of each in training, 577 held. **Holds on talk_v2.1 (2026-09-25)**, the same draw (talk_v2's held records; `validation/logs/exp_e7_explicit_gate_v2_1.{json,log}`): professional with the gate closed, explicit answers **0** of 144 ✓; closed, gate answer **100%** of 150 ✓; open, answered **100%** of 150 ✓ (20 scored explicit within 128 tokens, v2: 15); the host let nothing out. **Holds on talk_v2.2 (2026-09-25)**, the same draw (`validation/logs/exp_e7_explicit_gate_v2_2.{json,log}`): professional with the gate closed, explicit answers **0** of 144 ✓; closed, gate answer **100%** of 150 ✓; open, answered **98.7%** of 150 ✓ (no gate answer, no refusal; 15 scored explicit within 128 tokens); the host let nothing out.

**H-E8 — (added 2026-09-25) A question in plain words reaches the history graph: the host finds the event it asks about, and the answer is spoken right or not spoken at all.** Motivated by H-E6: its gate hands the adapter the right facts block, so it measures only the speaking half. In serving, what the asker hears is retrieval × speech — and a block for the wrong event is the one failure the host's checks cannot see, because they read the draft against the block the host retrieved (a wrong event's cause, stated faithfully, passes both the guard and the value check). The earlier retrieval benches (BEIR dbpedia, the haystack, SimpleQA search-and-learn) measured the store and the search loop, never this path, and the history graph (H-E6 data, 940,897 events) has no lookup at all. **Mechanism:** `standin/history_lookup.py`, no model. The kind of ask is read off the question (what led to / what came of / when / where / who / what would change if … had not happened); the event is found by name — the longest event name the question holds word for word, else the rarest words it shares with an event's names (IDF-weighted coverage of the name and of the question), a year in the question agreeing with the event's dates as the tie-breaker — and used only when the match is clear (an exact name, or a score over a floor and ahead of the next candidate by a margin); otherwise the host asks which event was meant (the ASK mechanism, `ask.branch_clarify`'s shape) instead of guessing. The block is the event's own lines (date, places, participants) plus the lines for the kind asked; the returned values are that kind's answer lines, and `ask.talk_reply` reads the draft against them. **Benchmark** (`validation/exp_e8_history_retrieval.py`; questions from `validation/build_e8_questions.py`): 60 held-split events per kind — cause, effect, when, where, who, downstream, and absent (a cause question about an event the graph gives effects but no cause) — each asked twice: **as named** (the graph's own name in the training template — the ceiling) and **reworded** (the same ask rewritten by a model the way a person would type it, told not to copy the name — dataset building for a test, the only use of an external model allowed; the real-use number). Talk adapter talk_v2.1, greedy, host checks on. **Gate** (pre-registered on the reworded questions; the numbers are the owner's to move): the asked event is the top candidate ≥ 90%; the served block holds the answering line ≥ 90%; spoken and correct ≥ 80%; **spoken and wrong = 0** (asked-which-one, refused and unparsed are safe outcomes, counted apart). **Kill:** the asked event is in the top 5 for under 50% of the reworded questions — name lookup is then the wrong tool, and the host needs a learned retriever before the graph can serve. Status: **OPEN — not passed, not killed** (2026-09-25).

**Result (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25).** Questions: `build_e8_questions.py` drew 80 held events per kind (560), each asked as named and reworded (Ling 3.0 Flash, $0.003; 515 rewrites kept — 408 without the event's name, 107 that kept it after a second try — and 45 dropped for giving the answer away; `validation/logs/e8_questions.manifest.json`); 20 per kind are the dev split, 60 the test split. **Revised on the dev split, before the test ran:** the kind patterns (a scene-setting question read by its last clause), a suffix stemmer, numbers matched as dates and not as words, a name counted only when the question covers 40% of it, readings of one event served as one (`same_event`), the floor 0.5 → 0.6 (dev: every right match kept, clear-but-wrong 6 → 4), and — the largest — the adapter now reads the host's ask about the found event (`history_lookup.CANON`, the kind asked, the event by its name) instead of the asker's words: over reworded dev questions it had said "The facts don't say." to 11 of 19 date questions whose date was in the block (every training question names its entity), and spoken-and-correct rose 41% → 67%. One host fix beside it: `ask.value_check` removes the longest known string first (a returned '150' had cut the asked 'Teotihuacán expansion by A.D. 150' apart); the H-E6 host view re-read with it is unchanged. **Test** (`validation/logs/exp_e8_history_retrieval_v2_1.{json,log}`; 387 reworded, 420 named; lookup median 4 ms over 948k names), reworded → named: asked event first **78.8%** ✗ → 97.6%; in the top five 85.3% → 100%; answering line served **58.1%** ✗ → 93.1%; spoken and correct **59.4%** ✗ → 90.2%; spoken and wrong **4** ✗ (1.0%) → 0. The host asked which event 21 times (5.4%), found none over the floor 73 (18.9%), read no kind 41 (10.6%) (corrected 2026-09-25: first written with the asked and none counts swapped). Spoken and correct by kind (reworded): cause 68%, effect 67%, when 70%, where 65%, who 53%, downstream 18%, absent 75%. The 4 wrong: three a different event the question's words fit better ("the execution of the Numidian king Jugurtha" → "Victory over Numidian king Jugurtha"; "who responded to Philip's death" → "Philip's death"; "if Pitt had never demanded Newfoundland fishery rights" → "Pitt's Resignation"), one a second reading of the same event the graph never merged (Sennacherib's campaign of 701 BC). The kill does not fire (top five 85%). **Meaning:** the path from a found event to a spoken answer holds — 90% correct and 0 wrong when the host has the name — and name lookup is the bottleneck on plain words: it has the event in its top five 85% of the time, first 79%, and the answer is right 59%. Asking instead of guessing keeps wrong answers to 1%, not 0: a block for the wrong event is the class the host's checks cannot see, measured here for the first time. Not passed, not killed. **Next:** the largest losses are the 73 questions with no event over the floor and the 41 with no kind read — a retriever for descriptions that share no rare word with the name (downstream: 45% first), reranked with the name match, and the kind patterns; then the graph's duplicate readings of one event (behind the 21 asks and one of the 4 wrong); and questions that do not name their entity in the talk data.

**Second round (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25) — revised on the dev split only, then the test split run once more.** The test result above stands as the pre-registered one; this is a second measurement after changes made by reading dev misses (the test questions were not looked at beyond the four wrong answers printed in the first run's log, and nothing targeted them). **Revisions:** the kind patterns — "what led Henry II to make a pilgrimage …" (a party between the verb and "to"), a counterfactual with *might* or *could* as well as *would*, "how would X have altered …", an "if … had never" whose event is in the verb ("if Hippias had never disarmed the citizen militia" is read with its verb) or in the rest of the question ("… if it never happened"), and "which specific areas" (dev: 15 of 128 reworded questions had no kind read, 0 after); the word normal form — accents folded (Khwārazm), a final *e* and a doubled consonant off after the suffix ('rebellion' now meets 'rebel', 'persecution' 'persecute'), *-ian/-ia* stripped ('Thracian'/'Thrace', 'Athenian'/'Athens'), a one- or two-digit number before a word kept as part of a name ('2 Maccabees'); and, when no name is over the floor, **a second index over each event's names, places and parties** (`find_facts`; words held by more than 20,000 events left out): a description is read by the share of its words an event's facts cover, times the year factor, and used only at 0.6 or more and 0.2 clear of the next distinct event — on dev the right tops scored 0.68–1.01 and stood 0.28–0.61 clear, every wrong top was within 0.1 of its next (the floor for names was not moved: a lower floor for far-ahead names gained 3 dev answers for 1–3 wrong). Dev, retrieval only, reworded: the event found and served 71.1% → 89.8%, clear on the wrong event 4 → 2, no kind read 15 → 0; named unchanged (95.0%). **Test, second run** (`validation/logs/exp_e8_history_retrieval_v2_1_r2.{json,log}`), first run → second, reworded: asked event first 78.8 → **87.6%** ✗; top five 85.3 → 92.2%; answering line served 58.1 → **77.0%** ✗; spoken and correct 59.4 → **78.3%** ✗ (bar 80); spoken and wrong 4 → **1** ✗ (Sennacherib's campaign of 701 BC, two readings of one event under two names). No event over the floor 73 → 40, no kind 41 → 10, asked 21 → 17; the facts index answered 34 questions, 33 of them the gold event. Named: 90.2 → 90.5%, 0 wrong. By kind (reworded, spoken and correct): cause 78%, effect 73%, when 90%, where 75%, who 70%, downstream 67% (18% before), absent 92%. Three answers right in the first run are lost to the new word forms and scores (the Houses of Parliament and a payrolls report now fall under the floor; the World 600 race now asks). 16 reworded questions were served another event than the gold one, most of them another reading of it ("Easter Uprising" for "Dublin Rising", "Final Meal of Jesus with Disciples" for "Last Supper", "Moses on Mount Sinai" for "Revelation on Sinai"), a few a neighbour ("Antiochus IV Epiphanes' rule over Judea" for the Maccabean Rebellion): the host's checks refused 10, 5 answered the same, 1 was spoken wrong. **Meaning:** most of the gap on plain words was the host not reading the question (its kind, its word forms) and having nothing but names to match; with both, the answer is right 78% of the time, and the one wrong answer left is a second reading of the same event. The checks carry the rest: 16 blocks for another event, 1 wrong answer spoken. Not passed, not killed. **Next:** the graph's readings of one event under two names (the wrong answer and most of the 16); then a learned retriever for the 40 descriptions still unmatched ("the 8th-century Roman document granting the Pope temporal power" is the Donation of Constantine by knowledge, not by words).

**Re-run on talk_v2.2 (2026-09-25)**, the second-round lookup unchanged (`validation/logs/exp_e8_history_retrieval_v2_2.{json,log}`; the retrieval columns are identical to `_v2_1_r2`, so only the speaking half moved), talk_v2.1 → talk_v2.2: reworded spoken and correct 78.3 → **77.5%** ✗ (refused 16 → 19), spoken and wrong **1** → **1** (the same Sennacherib reading); without the event's name 75.8 → 74.8%; named 90.5 → 90.0% (refused 13 → 15), 0 wrong. By kind (reworded), the change is in downstream (66.7 → 61.7%, refused 5 → 8; named 81.7 → 76.7%) and who (70.0 → 67.5%); effect rose 73.3 → 75.0%. The Data note's caution holds: with three times the "don't say" records the adapter declines a little more, even behind the canonical ask, and gains nothing here.

**Graph repair — one event read with a lost era mark (2026-09-25, applied at the next graph build).** The v2.1 graph holds 1,568 events twice under one name with mirrored dates (359 and 359 BC) — a reader lost or added the era mark, and it goes both ways ("Accession of Philip II" lost its BC; "Construction of Hadrian's Wall" gained one). `HistoryGraph.repair_mirrored_dates` decides the era by the other events the two readings share a party or a place with (parties and places held by more than 2,000 events say nothing) and the events they link to — how many are dated near the BC reading and how many near the AD one; a side with twice the other's wins, the wrong reading is merged into the right one and its links moved over (then checked against the corrected dates), repeated until a pass decides nothing. Run on the v2.1 graph (not saved): 1,338 decided — 1,066 BC, 272 AD — 230 left as they are; 940,897 → 939,559 events, 236 links pruned as contradicting the corrected dates. Hand-checked: 30 decisions drawn at a vote ratio of 2–3 and 40 drawn under 2.6, all 70 right; at 1.5–2 a sample was 28/30 right and at 1.2–1.5 22/30, which is why the ratio is 2. `build_history_graph.py` runs it after the dates are inferred; pinned by `standin/tests/test_history_graph.py`. Not yet in any graph file: the E8/E9 question sets are built on the v2.1 graph as it is.

**H-E9 — (added 2026-09-25) With a context graph, a follow-up that names no event is answered about the event the conversation is about — right, or not at all.** Source: Nick, 2026-09-25 ("we need a context graph too"): an agent's memory in three connected layers — long-term knowledge, the conversation, the reasoning behind each decision — in one queryable structure, so it does not rebuild the world every turn. H-E8 asked one question at a time; a conversation goes on with "why did it happen?", "who else was there?", "what did the battle lead to?", and for "it" there is nothing to search the graph for. **Mechanism:** `standin/context_graph.py`, no model. Long-term: the history graph, by event id. Short-term: the turns, each turn's ask (the kind, the event it resolved to and *how* — the question's own words, the conversation, or the asker's pick), the events each ask brought into focus (the event asked about at strength 1.0, the events its answer named at 0.6, fading with the turns since), the clarification still open, the branch a "what if" opened. Reasoning: per ask, the trace from question to decision (the kind and how it was read, the candidates and scores, the lines served and their source, the draft, the verdict and its reason) — `why()` answers "why did you say that?", `decided()` finds an earlier answer to the same ask. A question that names no event resolves to the most salient event in focus whose name holds every word it does use; one that names an event goes to H-E8's lookup; the reply to "which one did you mean?" binds to an offered event (a year, name words, earlier/later, an ordinal); a follow-up with nothing in focus is not guessed. A saved session goes through the vault. Pinned by `standin/tests/test_context_graph.py`. **Benchmark** (`validation/exp_e9_context_followups.py`): dialogues built from held-split events (seeded; 20 of each type for dev, 60 for test), their first turn naming the event in the host's own template: *follow-up* — two more turns about the same event by pronoun or bare ("When did it happen?", "Why?", "And then what?"); *description* — a turn naming it by its head noun ("What led to the battle?"); *switch* — event X, a pronoun about X, event Y, a pronoun that must mean Y; *clarify* — a name the graph holds for two events of different dates that answer differently, then "the one in <year>". Arms: with the context graph, and each turn answered alone (H-E8's host). Talk adapter talk_v2.1, greedy, host checks on. **Gate** (pre-registered, test split, the follow-up turns with the context graph; the numbers are the owner's to move): resolved to the right event ≥ 95%; spoken and correct ≥ 85%; **spoken and wrong = 0**; after a switch the pronoun means the newer event ≥ 95%; a reply to "which one?" binds to the picked event ≥ 90% of the times the host asked. **Kill:** spoken and wrong on more than 2% of follow-up turns — the conversation's memory would make the host answer about the wrong event more often than a fresh lookup does (H-E8: 1%). The follow-ups are the host's templates in this first test, not a person's rewording; reworded follow-ups are the next test. Status: **PASSES (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25)** — below.

**Result (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25).** Dialogues: 320, 80 per type — 20 dev, 60 test (`validation/logs/e9_dialogues.manifest.json`; the clarify pairs drawn from 1,071 names the graph holds for two events of different dates, leaving out dates within 100 years of year 0 — "the one in 56" reads as no year). **Revised on the dev split, before the test ran:** once the asker names something the host cannot find, "it" no longer means the older event (`gap`; a pronoun after an unresolved new topic had been answered about the previous one); a word the graph does not know is a name, not a pronoun ("Zzyzx"); a year in the reply matches inside an event's dates before a year off (±1 had matched both of a pair); a year that fits several readings of one event picks them together ("the one in 1550 BC" fit "Defeat of the Hyksos by Kamose" and "Defeat of the Hyksos", and the pick had been dropped as a tie); a follow-up while "which one?" stands unanswered asks again instead of finding nothing; and, in the lookup, a year inside an event's own name agrees with it ("July Stock Trading Volume Largest Since 1933", dated 1938, had been marked down as a date clash — H-E8's numbers predate this). Dev, no model, before → after: follow-ups resolved 96 → 97%, switched 90 → 95%, bind 75 → 100%. **Test** (`validation/logs/exp_e9_context_followups_test_v2_1.{json,log}`; 240 dialogues, 660 turns), with the context graph → each turn alone: follow-ups (n=300) resolved to the right event **97.0%** ✓ → 0%; spoken and correct **94.7%** ✓ → 0.3%; spoken and wrong **0** ✓ → 2; after a switch the pronoun meant the newer event **98.3%** ✓; the host asked "which one?" in all 60 clarify dialogues and the reply bound to the picked event **96.7%** ✓ (58/60). The kill does not fire. By turn (resolved / spoken and correct): pronoun 97.2 / 95.6%, description 95.0 / 93.3%, switched 98.3 / 93.3%; the first turns 96.7 / 93.3%, the same in both arms. Every follow-up the context graph did not resolve is the host asking again after a first turn it had already had to ask about — the graph holds two or more readings of that event with different dates ("Attack on Tortosa", "World 600 Stock Car Race"); none was answered about another event. 7 were refused (drafts the host's checks turned down: an ungrounded word, a date the block does not hold). Answered alone, 166 follow-ups found no event, 78 read no kind, 49 made the host ask, and 2 were answered about a different event ("When did the war happen?" → the War of 66–70 CE; "Who took part in the alliance?" → the Alliance of 356) — the class the conversation's memory removes. The 2 unbound picks: the year also fit a candidate the host had not meant to offer ("the one in 8000 BC" — "Domestication of Cats" and "Domestication of Horse", both 8000 BC in the top five). **Meaning:** a conversation's memory lets the host answer follow-ups that name no event — 95% spoken right, none wrong — where each turn looked up alone answers almost none and, when it does guess, can guess the wrong event. What remains is upstream: the graph's duplicate readings make the host ask at the first turn, and the follow-ups inherit it. **Limits:** the follow-ups are the host's templates, not a person's rewording; dialogues are at most four turns, so the salience decay is barely exercised; most clarify pairs are two readings of one event rather than two events. **Next:** merge the duplicate readings offline (H-E8 needs the same); a reply to "which one?" chooses only among the events the host offered (the close ones, not the whole top five), checked on dev; reworded follow-ups, built the way H-E8's rewrites were; longer dialogues.

**Second run (talk_v2.1, 2026-09-25)**, with the offered-only pick (`Hit.offered`: the top event's readings and the close candidates that answer differently, not a far fifth; checked on dev, unchanged there) and H-E8's second-round lookup (`validation/logs/exp_e9_context_followups_test_v2_1_r2.{json,log}`): with the context graph, follow-ups resolved **97.0%**, spoken and correct **94.7%**, wrong **0**, switched **98.3%** — all as before — and the reply to "which one?" bound **100%** (60/60; 96.7% before). Each turn alone: 3 follow-ups spoken about another event (2 before): "Who took part in the raid?" now fits "Raids of 1925-09-28" whole — a name whose only word is the one common word asked. A lookup rule for that (one common word is not a name) is next, checked on dev.

**Re-run on talk_v2.2 (2026-09-25)**, the same host (`validation/logs/exp_e9_context_followups_test_v2_2.{json,log}`), talk_v2.1 → talk_v2.2, with the context graph: follow-ups resolved **97.0%** (unchanged — the host's work), spoken and correct 94.7 → **93.7%** ✓ (refused 7 → 10: pronoun 3 → 5, description 1 → 2), spoken and wrong **0** ✓, switched **98.3%** ✓, the reply to "which one?" bound **100%** ✓. Still passes. Each turn alone: 2 spoken about another event (3 before).

**H-E10 — (added 2026-09-25) Cubby can sound like a person and hold a conversation without saying anything its facts do not.** Source: Nick, 2026-09-25 ("make it sound more human like and hold conversations"). Why it answers like a form today, by design: the talk adapter reads only the host's canonical ask, never the person's words or the turns before; the value check lets a reply say the one value the host returned and nothing else from the block; 63% of the mix is fact questions answered in one sentence shape; the host has three moves (answer, ask which one, "The facts don't say."). **Mechanism**, in order: (1) **the claim check** (`ask.claim_check`, `talk_reply(..., check="claims")`) in place of the value check at serving — a reply may say more of the block, and every thing it names from the block must be tied, in the same sentence, to something the block ties it to (or to the event asked about, left as "it"); a sentence saying one thing led to another must run the way the block's link runs; a year said to begin or end something must be that line's year; the name-and-number guard still runs first (pinned by `standin/tests/test_claim_check.py`: "It led to World War I, which began in 1914 and ended in 1918" passes; a cause turned round, a two-step chain said as one step, a place bound to another event, an end year said as a beginning are refused). (2) **the dialogue shape**: the adapter sees the conversation so far and the person's own words, with the host's reading beside them, trained on multi-turn records built from the context graph over train-split events (pronouns, "and then?", corrections, topic returns), every target passing the claim check. (3) **voice**: several natural wordings of the same facts — local dialogue sources, and a one-time model rewrite (dataset building only). (4) **the host's moves**: greetings and thanks, small talk that needs no facts, "tell me more", repairs, a fuller "don't know" ("I don't have what caused it — I know it was 1683"). (5) **facts and talk apart in the weights** (talk_v2 lost fact accuracy when fact records were diluted to 11%): a mode line, or two adapters the host switches between per turn — and, from Nick the same day ("a bigger adapter that we switch to add the missing params"), capacity arms on the same data: the LoRA of today (rank 16 on 181 modules, ~7.9M parameters, 31 MB), rank 64 (~31M), and new blocks inserted into the frozen base (initialised to pass their input through, trained alone, skipped on the facts path);
