and, from Nick again ("some kind of moe"), experts in two tiers — first per-turn experts the host picks by the turn's kind (facts, voice, French talk from the Claire pairs), each trained and gated alone and named in the ask's trace; only if a larger single adapter shows capacity is the limit, a learned mixture of LoRA experts inside the layers (a router per layer, two experts active per token) — routed hierarchically, the host as the coarse router and the learned one only among the experts of the group it picked (the two-stage shape of AT-MoE and HDMoLE, reported to keep routing readable and capacity balanced), rank-wise gating of one larger adapter (SMoRA, "each rank could be an expert") as the lightest version, and rank given by layer rather than uniformly (D-MoLE's gradient norms, AlphaLoRA's spectra) as a cheaper first step than more experts. **Benchmark:** held-split dialogues (E9's builder, test split, the person's turns also reworded), answered by talk_v2.2 and by the new adapter(s), host checks on. **Gate** (pre-registered; the numbers are the owner's to move): naturalness — a blind pairwise judge (an outside model: testing use; both orders, position drawn at random) prefers the new replies in ≥ 70% of pairs, and Nick's blind picks on 30 pairs agree with the judge ≥ 75%; ≤ 50% of the new replies are one of the fact records' fixed answer forms; conversation — E9 follow-ups spoken and correct ≥ talk_v2.2's, spoken and wrong 0; facts — E6 fact families spoken and correct within 2 points of talk_v2.2, E8 spoken-and-wrong no higher than talk_v2.2's; the claim check refuses 100% of a probe set of false statements made from real blocks (a value bound to another event, a cause turned round, a two-step chain as one step, an end year as a beginning). A bigger adapter counts only if it beats rank 16 on naturalness with the same data at equal facts numbers. **Kill:** any statement the claim check lets through on E6/E8/E9 that its block does not make — the value check goes back in front. Status: **OPEN** (2026-09-25); step 1 built and pinned, not yet run on a gate.

**H-E11 — (added 2026-09-25) What the host's scaffolding teaches the talk adapter can be trained into it, so it answers as well without the scaffolding — and nothing false comes in with it.** Source: Nick, 2026-09-25, from EMPO² (Liu, Kim et al., Microsoft Research, arXiv 2602.23008v2): an agent acts with tips it wrote itself and retrieves from memory, then its policy is updated as if the tips had not been there, weighted by the reward — the tips' benefit moves into the weights and the memory is not needed at test time (Qwen2.5-7B: ScienceWorld 33.2 → 75.9 over GRPO, WebShop 79.3 → 88.3, both measured with no memory). Kept apart from H-E10 so the two effects are measured on their own. **Why:** the talk adapter leans on scaffolds the host builds every turn — the host's canonical ask (H-E8 dev: over reworded questions, 41% spoken and correct without it, 67% with it; the adapter declined dates that were in its block when the question did not name the event), the host's reading of the conversation, and, after H-E10, lessons from earlier traces. Each costs host work and ties the adapter to one prompt shape. **Mechanism:** (1) *day* — for train-split turns the adapter drafts N answers with the scaffold: the person's words with the host's reading beside them, and lessons retrieved from past traces, written by the host from its verdicts ("asked by a description, refused: named another event's value") — never a fact, never a person's conversation; (2) *the reward is the host's, no model*: a draft that passes the name-and-number guard and the claim check and states the returned value scores, a refused one does not, and each draft is weighed against the others for the same turn; (3) *night* (the sleep cycle) — the adapter is updated on the same drafts with the scaffold taken out of the prompt, weighted by that advantage, the tokens the unscaffolded adapter finds very unlikely masked (EMPO²'s stabiliser). First version, with no RL trainer: supervised training on the passing drafts only, scaffold removed; the advantage-weighted update next. Privacy: only lessons from benchmark and train-split traces; nothing from a person's conversation enters training without their consent (the vault rule). **Benchmark:** E8 reworded (test split) and E9 follow-ups (test split), the adapter given the person's words only — no canonical ask, no reading beside them — before internalisation (the floor), after it, and today's adapter with the scaffold (the ceiling); host checks on. **Gate** (pre-registered; the numbers are the owner's to move): without the scaffold, the internalised adapter's spoken and correct is ≥ 15 points above the floor and within 5 points of the ceiling on E8 reworded and on E9 follow-ups; spoken and wrong 0 on both; E6 fact families within 2 points of talk_v2.2 (nothing forgotten). **Kill:** internalisation brings any spoken-and-wrong answer on E6/E8/E9, or lifts the unscaffolded answers by under 5 points — the scaffold stays and the training is not worth its cost. Status: **OPEN** (2026-09-25); needs H-E10's dialogue-shape records first.

**H-E12 — (added 2026-09-25) The host's procedures improve themselves from their own failed traces — changed only where a trace first went wrong, and adopted only if no earlier answer gets worse.** Source: Nick, 2026-09-25, from SkillPivot (Feng, Wang, Sun; arXiv 2609.29154v1): a failed agent trajectory is rarely wrong from the start — it gathers useful evidence, then deviates. SkillPivot finds the deviation point (a per-step score from tool-call validity, new progress toward the answer and repetition), has a stronger teacher continue from the same prefix, contrasts the student's failed suffix with the teacher's successful one, and writes a small, conditional change to the natural-language skill, merged only if an LLM reviewer judges that it keeps the old rules and generalises (ToolQA 53.18 → 59.36% over six task groups, with gains on groups it never evolved on; LogicBench 88.01 → 91.22%; WildClawBench 36.36 → 73.00% over five rounds; the evolved skills also lift four other models). The finding that matters most here: reflecting on the whole failed trajectory made the skills worse than none (52.52% against 53.18%); failure-only reflection reached 56.30%, teacher-only imitation 55.46%, same-prefix contrast 59.36% with the shortest edits. Kept apart from H-E11 (the scaffold moved into the weights) and H-A13 (composition rules learned from episodes) so each is measured alone. **Why:** the host's procedures — the kind patterns, the word normal form, the floors and margins, the context graph's resolution rules — have so far been revised by hand from dev misses (H-E8's second round: spoken and correct 59 → 78% on reworded questions). The context graph already keeps, per ask, the trace from question to decision (the kind read and how, the candidates and scores, the lines served, the draft, the verdict and its reason), and on dev every step can be checked against gold — so where a trace first went wrong is a lookup, not an estimate from embedding similarity. **Mechanism**, no model in the loop: (1) *deviation step* — for each failed dev trace, the first step whose output disagrees with what gold implies (no kind or the wrong kind read; the gold event not among the candidates, not first, or not clear; the answering line not served; the draft refused or wrong); (2) *teacher from the same prefix* — the alternatives at that step are tried with everything before it held fixed (the other kind readings, each candidate in the top five, the gold event's lines), and a continuation counts only if the rest of the pipeline then reaches a spoken, correct answer through the host's own checks — search plus verification as the teacher, the branching walk's shape (task #82); (3) *suffix contrast → edit* — the difference between the failed and the successful continuation, written as one edit from a fixed set (a kind pattern, a word-form rule, a word that is not a name, a floor or margin for one class of match), conditional on what the deviating step saw; (4) *regression gate* — H-A13's whole-record rule: an edit is adopted only if it fixes at least one trace and re-running every dev trace on record turns no right answer into a wrong or refused one; the ledger keeps every edit, adopted or not, retired never deleted. A model proposing edits would be a training-time use and is left as the owner's call. **Second use, for the talk adapter** (feeds H-E11): the name-and-number guard and the claim check know the first part of a draft that fails, so a refused draft has a deviation point too; the adapter is re-run from the same prefix under the host's constraint (the returned value, no other entity's), and the pair — same prefix, failed suffix, passing suffix — trains only where the two differ, instead of on the whole draft. **Benchmark:** the edits are evolved on E8 and E9 dev traces only and measured on a fresh E8 test set (new held-split events, reworded the same way — the current test split has now been read three times) and on E9 test. Arms: today's hand-revised host, the evolved host, and whole-trace edits (the same edit set, chosen from the whole failed trace with no deviation step — SkillPivot's losing control). For the adapter: same-prefix pairs against whole-draft training on the same number of examples. **Gate** (pre-registered; the numbers are the owner's to move): on the fresh E8 reworded test, the evolved host's spoken and correct ≥ the hand-revised host's + 3 points, spoken and wrong not higher; E9 follow-ups still pass H-E9's gate; every adopted edit has 0 regressions on the dev record (by construction, and checked on test: no answer the hand-revised host gets right is lost to an edit); the whole-trace arm below the deviation arm. Adapter: same-prefix pairs lift E6/E8/E9 spoken and correct over whole-draft training by ≥ 2 points, spoken and wrong 0. **Kill:** no adopted edit set beats the hand-revised host by ≥ 1 point on the fresh test, or an adopted edit brings a spoken-wrong answer on any test — the procedures stay hand-revised. **Advantage over conventional agents:** SkillPivot's skills are prose an LLM reviewer accepts at a score of 0.5; here an edit is a rule the host runs, adopted only when every past trace comes out the same or better — a regression is counted, not judged. Status: **OPEN** (2026-09-25).

**H-E13 — (added 2026-09-25) The graph knows when it holds one event twice under two names — and does not join two events because their names share words.** Source: Nick, 2026-09-25, from R-DEIM Net (Pushp, Prajapati, Sarma; arXiv 2609.30100v1), a 76M-parameter paraphrase judge: a token-by-token similarity matrix between the two sentences (MiniLM-L12 encoder) read by 3/5/7 2D convolutions and attention pooling, beside a small decoder (Flan-T5-small) trained to write a rationale for the pair, whose hidden states are pooled as a second feature; the loss charges a false "same" twice a false "different". Quora Question Pairs 90.07% accuracy / 90.16 F1 (10-fold, pair-level, so one question can sit in train and test; the rationale branch adds +1.5 F1 over the interaction branch alone). Two limits the paper states or shows: on PAWS, pairs built to share their words and differ in meaning, its zero-shot F1 is 53.5% (chance); and its rationales were written by an LLM and are not claimed faithful to the decision. **Why:** H-E8's one wrong answer is Sennacherib's campaign of 701 BC held as two events under two names, and most of the 16 reworded questions served another event's block were served another reading of the gold one ("Easter Uprising"/"Dublin Rising", "Final Meal of Jesus with Disciples"/"Last Supper"); H-E9's unresolved follow-ups all inherit a first turn the host had to ask about because two readings disagree on the date. The era repair (H-E8, above) joins only one name with mirrored dates; readings under different names are untouched. Our hard pairs are PAWS's kind — "Domestication of Cats"/"Domestication of Horse", "Death of Jugurtha"/"Victory over Numidian king Jugurtha" — so the words cannot carry the decision; the graph's facts can. **Mechanism:** (1) *candidates, no model*: pairs of events dated within a year of each other (or with overlapping spans) that share a place or a party, or a rare name word; (2) *the judge*: the evidence the graph holds — dates, places, parties, the events each links to — and the two names aligned word by word in the host's normal form (R-DEIM's interaction matrix, over names); first as a scored rule (the era repair's votes), then as a small learned judge in R-DEIM's shape over the same evidence, trained with the false-join penalty; (3) *the rationale is the evidence*: "same year (1916), same place (Dublin), 3 shared parties, the names share 'Rising'" is built from the graph, not generated, and every agreement it states is re-checked against the graph before the join — faithful by construction, where R-DEIM's is not claimed to be; (4) *the join*: above an operating point chosen for precision, a `same_as` link carrying the rationale (both names kept, read by the lookup's `same_event`, reversible — unlike the era repair's merge); below it, `maybe_same`, and the host offers both readings together when asked rather than answering from one; (5) *second arm*: the same judge between a question and a candidate event reranks H-E8's top five (the asked event is in the top five for 92.2% of reworded questions, first for 87.6%). **Labels** (no model at serving): Wikidata5M's alias lists for the names — names of one Q-id as the same, different Q-ids sharing words as hard negatives (how many events they cover to be counted first); for whole events, a dev and a test set drawn from the candidate pairs, stratified by score and labelled by hand, plus the known E8 cases; a model may label a training set (dataset building only), checked by hand on a sample. **Gate** (pre-registered; the numbers are the owner's to move): on held labelled pairs, join precision ≥ 99% at recall ≥ 50% of the true same-event pairs among the candidates, and ≥ 95% of hard negatives (at least half the name words shared, different events) not joined; 100% of the rationales' stated agreements hold in the graph; on the repaired graph (gold ids mapped through the joins), E8 reworded spoken and wrong 0, spoken and correct not below the same adapter's on the unrepaired graph, and blocks served for another reading 16 → ≤ 8; E9 follow-ups spoken and wrong 0 and fewer first-turn asks. Rerank arm: asked event first ≥ 90% on reworded questions, clear on the wrong event not up. **Kill:** precision under 95% at every threshold that reaches 30% recall — pair judging cannot join events here, and the host keeps offering both readings; or any E8/E9 answer spoken wrong because of a join. **Advantage over conventional models:** a model that folds two names into one entity does it silently in its weights, where it can be neither seen nor undone; here each join is a graph edge carrying its evidence, re-checkable and reversible, and a wrong join is counted on a labelled set before it serves. Status: **OPEN** (2026-09-25); tasks #141 (same event under two names) and #137 (learned retriever) fold into it.

**Step 1 — what the local alias table covers (2026-09-25).** The only Wikidata alias list on disk is the hdc label table (`hdc_names.tsv.gz`: 1.25M alias and label keys → Q-id, built for the hdc entities; no full Wikidata5M alias file or local Wikidata dump was found). Matching every event name in the v2.1 graph exactly (case-folded; scratch `e13_alias_cover.py`):
- **Coverage:** 2,924 of 940,897 events (0.3%) have a name in the table. They are mostly battles, wars, sieges, laws and treaties.
- **Shared Q-ids:** 442 Q-ids are held by two or more graph events (1,322 events), and 120 of those carry two or more different names.

A sample of 25 of the 120 shows three things:
- **Real second readings under another name:** "Ninth Crusade"/"Lord Edward's Crusade" (1271), "Invasion of Poland"/"Nazi invasion of Poland", "Polish-Soviet War"/"Polish-Bolshevik War", "Sand Creek massacre"/"Battle of Sand Creek", "Battle of Capo Colonna"/"Battle of Stilo" (982), "Battle of Hürtgen Forest"/"Battle of the Hurtgen Forest".
- **Different events under one name that the alias table maps to one Q-id:** "Battle of Cynoscephalae" in 364 BC and in 197 BC (two real battles); "Battle of Hama" in 605 BC, 1175 and 1204; four laws called "Lex Julia". The alias route labels names, not events, which is this hypothesis's premise seen in the labels themselves.
- **Readings of one long event dated at different points:** "Thirty Years' War" at 1600, 1618, 1630 and 1648; "Second French Empire" at 1848, 1852 and 1870. The candidate rule must be kind-aware: a war, reign, empire or era needs a span tolerance, while a battle or death needs about a year.

A period and its part ("Bronze Age"/"Middle Bronze Age") is a fourth relation — part of, not the same — and the join must not take it. Date errors show up too ("Battle of Solferino" at 1850 and 1860; the battle was 1859), so the dates are evidence, not ground truth.

**Meaning:** the local table gives a seed of labelled pairs (the 442 groups) but not a training set. Wikidata's full alias lists, or a hand- or model-labelled set, are needed for scale. **Next:**
- the kind-aware candidate rule and a count of the pairs it produces;
- labels for the 442 groups plus a stratified draw of candidates. Who labels them — Nick, me with Nick's spot-check, or a model at dataset time — is the owner's call.

**H-E14 — (added 2026-09-28) One process that owns boot, state, the turn and the night makes the modules one agent without changing an answer: every reply replays from a signed ledger, a boot that fails its gates does not serve, and no tool runs that its turn kind did not grant.** Source: Nick, 2026-09-28 ("we need some kind of bootstrap and an orchestrator, in fact the full system should act as an agent harness"); the panel's two rounds (`docs/panel_fluent_harness.md`, `docs/panel_compiler.md`: six reviewers, one design, four of six said build it first). Spec: `docs/research/2026-09-28-harness-v0.md`. **Mechanism:** (1) boot reads a signed config (thresholds, store and adapter hashes, the gate pack), checks every hash, runs the VM self-test and a preflight gate pack, and refuses to serve on any failed check, with a ledger row naming it; thresholds are never recalibrated at boot. (2) The turn is a TaskFrame with a Budget (tool calls, VM hops, fetches, forge attempts, depth, wall); every tool call goes through one registry that denies by default, enforces effect classes (read / propose / write) at call time, spends the budget, and writes a ledger row, refused or not; exhaustion is a declared outcome, never a silent truncation. (3) The reply's hash, the frame, its verdicts and its calls are one ledger row per turn; the person's words go through the vault. (4) The night runs under the store lock; a turn that arrives meanwhile is refused, never served from a store mid-write. (5) The model proposes and phrases; the harness never lets it decide truth, permissions, writes, content or whether to speak. **Built (v0):** `standin/harness.py`, pinned by `standin/tests/test_harness.py` (12 tests, fakes only): boot serves and ledgers; refuses on each of five injected faults (a store hash, an adapter hash, an edited threshold, the VM self-test, a failed preflight family) without moving a threshold; deny-by-default and never-live hold and are ledgered; budgets end a frame as `exhausted`; a turn replays from the ledger with its calls and verdicts; the night holds the lock. **Gates (pre-registered; the owner's to move):** same answers as the modules on E6/E8/E9 (byte-identical replies on the same draw); 100% of spoken replies have a ledger row; refuse-to-serve 5 of 5 on injected faults; replay ≥ 99% of 200 turns; 0 of 50 forbidden calls executed, 50 ledgered; kill-and-restart 100 times with 0 double-spoken and 0 lost turns; p95 latency ≤ the modules' + 20%. **Kill:** an answer changed by the harness (gate 1), or gate 2 or 3 missed: then it is plumbing, and the report says so. Status: **OPEN**; v0 built on fakes, not yet wired to the serving brain. *Panel round 3 (2026-09-28, eight frontier models, `docs/research/2026-09-28-panel-round3-synthesis.md`)* amends the spec before it is built further: store generations instead of the lock, ledger-first at-most-once speaking, the hormone state and generation id in every turn row, audit replay at 100% split from regeneration replay, `gen_tokens` in the budget, a quarantine store and one `promote` tool, typed postconditions, child permissions as an intersection, world namespaces; and it adds the finding the panels missed: a VM-verified chain is not a correct answer when the emitter binds at chance, so programs and replies carry slots the host fills (H-E15, next). **v0.1 built and wired (2026-09-29; `docs/research/2026-09-28-harness-v0.md`, "v0.1"):** all nine round-3 changes in `standin/harness.py` — store generations with two keys (the night writes g+1 beside g, the boot pack runs on the candidate, a harness-signed manifest is swapped atomically, turns pin their generation; no lock), speaking as a ledger-first at-most-once effect (intent row → bytes → spoken row; orphans resolved at boot, never re-spoken), everything that bends a turn in its row with audit replay split from regeneration (20/20 on fakes, unchanged when the engine drifts), typed postconditions declared at creation and child grants as intersections, `gen_tokens` and the frame → VM `Budget` mapping, quarantine + `promote` and `fetch` as an external effect with VM modules under effect classes and the harness setting a program's budget and knowledge path, worlds as namespaces (`Fact` with status; counterfactuals only in `branch:` worlds), rotating gate packs that must carry the three real defects (`standin/data/gate_pack.json`), the live-emitter size cap. `standin/harness_wiring.py` puts the registry in front of `CubbyBrain`'s doors (gated worlds, `run_program_proto`, `emitter.emit`; the harness senses and routes once outside the frame) — on a CubbyBrain-shaped fake, seven turns byte-identical to the unwired brain with the same hormone trajectory, every read, program and write a row under the frame's budget. Gates on fakes: refuse-to-serve 8/8, deny probes 500/500 refused and ledgered, audit replay 20/20. `serve_api --harness` boots the real brain through it (a dev config signed here, the boot pack derived from the store's own facts with the three defects; a failed check exits 3). Still the owner's on the real brain: gate 1 on E6/E8/E9, latency, "no budget binds".

**H-E15 — (added 2026-09-29) The emitter never copies a name or a number: with the host placing every entity and number through slots, a 450M emitter answers reworded questions better than a copying one, and a program about the wrong entity cannot run.** Source: panel round 3 (`docs/research/2026-09-28-panel-round3-synthesis.md`; five of eight converged on it independently) and the owner's decision the same day: **the 450M is the program emitter, the LFM2.5-2.6B stand-in keeps the talk seat** (the verified role on the small model, the guarded-but-unverifiable one on the fluent one; both fit the 12 GB card, Q4 ≈ 1.7 GB + fp32 ≈ 2.3 GB). **Why:** a VM-verified chain is not a correct answer: a program about the wrong entity carries real facts, clears every hop and passes the value and claim checks; the "0 spoken wrong" numbers were measured with the host handing lookups to the talk adapter, never with a 450M writing programs, and the base binds a copied value to the asked entity at chance. **Mechanism** (`cubbyllm/reasoning/slots.py`, pinned by `tests/reasoning/test_slots.py`, 6 tests): (1) the host extracts the question's spans into a slot table — `$E1..` entities by longest match over a name index (the history graph's names, a store's keys), `$N1..` numbers by position, `$R1..` referents the context graph resolved — and the emitter sees the question annotated (`... of [E1: iridomyrmex bigi]?`); (2) the emitter writes `bind frame, SEED, "$E1"`, never the name; (3) the host checks the program before the disposer — an unknown slot or a copied literal (a span written out instead of its slot) is a refusal that never runs, and the unused spans are reported — then fills the slots by string substitution and hands the filled program to the same disposer, walk and VM as today (`SlotEmitter` wraps any `Emitter`; the loop is untouched, so the copying path stays as the control). Relations stay words: they are a closed vocabulary the emitter must learn, not a copy from context. (4) The harvest converts without a name index (`to_slots`: the program's own entity literals found in the prompt are the spans; fill is the exact inverse), which measures the extractor's coverage of 1.2M records for free. **Benchmark:** E8 reworded (test split; 59% spoken and correct today) and the emitter families, slot emitter vs copy emitter at equal training data, with the extractor miss rate, the wrong-slot rate and the grounded-but-wrong rate before the host logged apart. **Gate** (pre-registered; the owner's to move): reworded spoken-and-correct ≥ 70%, spoken-wrong ≤ 1%, grounded-but-wrong before the host halved, 0 copied literals run; extractor misses the dominant residual. **Kill:** wrong-slot errors dominate the residual — the model mis-assigns slots, a binding failure in another costume, and the fix is the trunk's binding (H-E5's continuation, the synthetic binding arm), not the format. Status: **OPEN**; the layer and the harvest converter built. **The 450M emitter adapter is built, not yet run (2026-09-29):** `notebooks/emitter_450m.ipynb` (`_build_emitter.py`; logic `validation/train_emitter_torch.py`, data `validation/emitter_data.py`) trains LoRA r16 over the frozen base on v12e in slot form — chain hop objects and the plan seed → `$E` (the index = every name in the fact lines + the seed, one id per distinct name), the prompt's numbers → `$N` by position in arithmetic and kernel programs and their step comments, role-binding left literal as the copy control, the echoed `# question` comment dropped; 11,831 of 11,845 records convert (14 uncovered) — then generates the val split greedy, checks each program against its slot table, fills, and writes `val_generations.json` for `standin/eval_emitter_vm.py --val-generations` on the local card (or live through grilly2: `standin/emitter.py: Cubby450mEmitter`, `--cubby`). The smoke (tiny random base, CPU, VM read included) runs end to end. To run after the continuation finishes: on the continued base if its readout passes, else on base450m_final.

**H-E15, live in both seats (2026-10-17; `serve_api --cubby450m <base450m_cont export> --emitter-adapter cubby450m_v12e_w_step_cont --talk-adapter talk_v2_2 --talk-export <base450m export> --ask --web off`, port 8766 beside the stand-in on 8765; nothing third-party in the loop).** The programs seat is `SlotEmitter(Cubby450mEmitter)` over the worlds' 373,689 names plus a question's own capitalised runs (`NameIndex`); the talk seat is `Cubby450mTalk`, the host rendering the adapter's own formats (a facts block, the conversation's turns) from the structured context the callers now pass. Two trunks for now: the emitter adapter was trained on base450m_cont, talk_v2_2 on base450m. **Eight live questions, the 450M vs the stand-in on the same loop:** verified by both — the mother of Justin Trudeau (Margaret Trudeau, through a fetch), the country of Oak Park (United States), the father of Bill Haslam (Jim Haslam); the profile "who is marie curie?" spoken by the 450M talk adapter and kept by the guard ("…unites the professions of physicist and chemist, and holds a position at the professor level. She was born on November 7, 1867, in Warsaw…"). Refused by the 450M, verified by the stand-in: "Where was Marie Curie born?" and "Where was Ernest Hemingway born?" (plan `birth` — a relation word the question does not hold, so coverage refused it; lever 6 finds no label for it because the store says `birthplace` / `birth date` and the table maps `born` to the canonical labels, never to `birth`), the two-hop capital-of-citizenship question (plan `country | citizens | E2`, seed `citizenship`: the chain shape is not held) and "who is the prime minister of canada?" (plan `prime minister | sue`). ~3 s a question on the RX 6750 XT once loaded (the stand-in 1-6 s). **Reading:** 0 wrong spoken on either arm; the 450M's gap is the emitter's relation words and its multi-hop shape, which is exactly what the `program` shard in H-E20's decay and the next emitter adapter are for — the host, the slots, the walk and the VM did not have to change to swap the seat. The copying path (the stand-in, in the bank's shadow when `--gguf` is given beside `--cubby450m`) stays the control.

**H-E16 — (added 2026-09-29) The branch runner is the graph-of-thought walk generalised: every alternative — an ambiguous hop, a counterfactual, a timeline — is a branch run in its own VM, and the decision among branch reports is a certified CubeLang decision program, not a host heuristic.** Source: the owner, 2026-09-29 ("do not forget GoT and decision trees"), on top of the 2026-09-24 branching walk (`docs/research/2026-09-24-graph-of-thought.md`: every candidate at an ambiguous hop becomes a branch, bounded at 12, each certified by the greedy bar, spoken only when every branch finished, was certified and names one answer; exp_r31: 32 of 53 blind refusals were only apparent, 32 right, 0 wrong) and the QC decision pattern (`cubelang/examples/qc_decision.cube`: the host injects registers, a compiled `.cube` rule returns PASS / REJECT / REVIEW, the audit carries the rule's hash). **Mechanism:** (1) the GoT walk's branches, the counterfactual's `exclude X` and the era's `T^t` role are one thing at the wire — a `Branch` on the RunRequest (built 2026-09-28: `exclude`, `assume`, `max_jumps`; the `BranchReport` on every result) — so the thought graph the panel already draws is the branch tree, and the branch runner inherits the walk's bound and its agreement rule; (2) the **decision among branches is a decision tree in CubeLang**: the host injects each branch's report as registers (verdict, symbol, margin, `assumption_used`, jumps) and runs a certified `Decide` program whose `if` ladder returns SPEAK-ONE / ASK-WHICH / REFUSE / CONTINGENT — the thresholds live in the program's constructor (the one thing signed), the program's hash rides on the ledger row, and `qc_decision.cube`'s safe subset (create, assign, add, sub, compare, if/else) is all it needs; the GoT rule "speak only when all branches agree" is its first arm, written down and certified rather than living in `pipeline.py`; (3) the emitter may propose a Decide program for a new branch kind, but it serves only after the VM certifies it and the ledger signs it — a decision rule is a tool, and the forge's promotion path is its path. **Benchmark:** the E9 follow-ups that branch (ambiguous "it"), CLUTRR's fork records (exp_r31's 53), the reworded counterfactual set (18%). **Gate** (pre-registered; the owner's to move): the certified Decide program reproduces the branching walk's verdicts on exp_r31's 53 records exactly (32 spoken, 21 refused, 0 wrong) — the compatibility kill, like H-E14's gate 1; reworded counterfactual ≥ 40% spoken and correct with spoken-wrong ≤ 1% and 100% of spoken counterfactuals with `assumption_used`; every branch decision's ledger row names the Decide program's hash. **Kill:** a Decide program that lifts spoken-correct only by relaxing the agreement arm, or by a threshold not in its constructor. Status: **OPEN**; the wire-level branch built, the Decide program and the closure-aware overlay next. **VM step 0 built (2026-09-29)** — the panel's prerequisites before the runner trusts a program (`cubelang/docs/STEP0.md`; synthesis addendum): an absent role is Null structurally (reason `absent_role`; probe 500/500 Null, present-role 500/500, the genuine 3-role similarity unchanged), `@hook/@before/@after/@cron` are strict errors and `@internal/@system/@once` are enforced, the request carries `Budget{ops, queries, wall, jumps}` refused by name, every recover is reported with its runner-up, margin and reason (the Decide program's `margin` register now exists), and the emitter SFT builder gates chains on correctness and literal coverage (v3cf: 517 verified → 513, 4 verified-but-wrong out).

**H-E17 — (added 2026-09-29; H-F2 M4) One question in front of every write and every route — "which world is this, and is it possible there?" — answered by MoWM's worlds with symbols and scores only, makes the store's anti-poisoning gate auditable and gives the store a third answer besides keep and drop: hold it as latent until a source attests it.** Source: the owner, 2026-09-29 ("MoWM as the real world model behind the bridge: which worlds, what a possibility branch returns, how the capsule stores read it"); the H-F2 M3 screens above; the latent tier learn.py already holds back from speech. **Which worlds (one interface, three kinds):** fact worlds (a FactStore/CapsuleStore per domain, era or source — covers by best hit, supports by members, contradicts by a stored (subject, relation) with a different object, `serve.contradiction`'s rule factored into `bridges.possibility.same_subject_clash` so every writer runs the same test), law worlds (a MoWM `World` with axioms, or a `Knows` — covers by centroid, supports by `best_member`, predicts by its transition model cleaned up against members; knowledge, never state), time worlds (a fact world sliced by when the fact held; the Branch's `T^t` role). **What a possibility branch returns:** `Possibility(world_id, verdict ∈ {POSSIBLE, IMPOSSIBLE, UNKNOWN}, score, margin, support, predicted, latent, why)` — POSSIBLE when the routed world holds it (`member`) or a member clears `tau_answer` (`resembles`) and nothing contradicts it; IMPOSSIBLE when a member contradicts it; UNKNOWN when no world covers it or the routed world neither holds nor contradicts it, with `latent` naming the world it waits in. `possible` means "in a world's domain and not ruled out", never "true". **How the capsule stores read it:** `CapsuleStore.add` with an oracle refuses IMPOSSIBLE into `ledger` (serve says which world and which member), keeps UNKNOWN with `meta.latent` and provenance `(latent)` (attested by `attest`), keeps POSSIBLE with its support, then tells the world model (`oracle.learn`) so a latent world fills with what waits in it and the next such fact routes there — spawn a latent world, verify later; `route_world` asks the oracle first; `PossibleRelations` is the `KnownRelations` whose UNKNOWN is the unknown_relations branch. Built: `cubbyllm/bridges/possibility.py` (the contract, `StorePossibility` — the oracle without MoWM), `mowm/bridges/possibility.py` (`MoWMPossibility`, mowm → cubbyllm only; fact worlds from texts, seeded domain worlds mounted, latent spawn through `maybe_spawn`, a `Knows` surface), 20 + 17 tests, `docs/research/2026-09-29-mowm-worlds.md`. **Benchmark:** `validation/exp_f2m4_possibility.py` — the chain-QA pool as 8 relation-family fact worlds, 400 true facts held out, 400 contradictions planted, the same worlds for every oracle. **Gate** (pre-registered): MoWM routing ≥ the store rule − 2 pts; planted caught ≥ 90%; true facts refused ≤ 2%; members asked back possible ≥ 98%; store `possible()` ≤ 5 ms. **Kill:** MoWM routing loses > 2 pts or refuses > 2% of true facts → the bridge stays the stores' own rule and MoWM keeps the law worlds only. **Result (2026-09-29, n=1500; `validation/logs/exp_f2m4_possibility.json`): all gates PASS.** Store rule: routing 0.937 (parity 1.00 with `route_world`), planted caught 0.990, true refused 0.0125, members 1.00, 4.0 ms. MoWM under **member routing** (best member per world — `query_worlds` in its exhaustive form): the same verdicts verdict for verdict (parity by construction; the gain is one router holding fact, law and latent worlds), routing 0.937 / 0.972 when routed, 11.7% abstain, 4.6 ms, 8 production-width worlds built in 0.24 s. **Two findings from the run.** (a) Checking the contradiction in the routed world only caught 0.918 (`*_run1_routed_only.json`): 29 of the 33 escapes had routed away from the fact's home world — the swapped-in object pulls the best member into the 840-fact 'misc' world, where nothing holds the subject — so both oracles now ask every other non-latent world that holds the subject (an index lookup each, `TripleIndex.about`; `cross_world`, off for time worlds, where the same subject legitimately has another object in another year): 0.990, the 4 left being a template-split mismatch ('X is the country Y is in' vs the 'of' form). (b) **The recorded negative:** the open-set **centroid** rule at its calibrated taus does not transfer to fact worlds — a member sits ~1/√n from a centroid of n quasi-orthogonal facts, so 68% of questions and 61% of the worlds' own members fall below the floor (routing 0.830, planted caught 0.443); it stays the rule for worlds of few coherent axioms, and `routing='member'` is the oracle's default. The 5 refused true facts (1.25%) are second values of multi-valued relations — the cost of the existing gate's every-relation-is-functional choice, now measured. Status: **PASSED at this screen**; not on the serve path until `CubbyServe.mount_oracle` is called; temporal `exclude` through a year-world and the law worlds' first ask are next.

**H-E18 — (added 2026-09-29) Program first, words second: emitter data made by sampling the PROGRAM, running it in the VM, then having a dataset LLM write the question and keeping it only when a round trip lands on the VM's value, widens the emitter's coverage past what reverse-engineering GSM text gives, at no cost in correctness.** Source: Caco (arXiv 2510.04081: code-first CoT synthesis, execution filter, instruction reversal, answer-consistency check); the owner, 2026-09-29 ("yes"). **Our change to Caco:** the judge is the VM, not an LLM — a question is kept when a blind re-solve (answer mode) or the v12e emitter's own program run in the VM (emitter mode) returns the sampled program's value; nothing an LLM says is ever the label. **Mechanism:** (1) `ArithSampler` draws GSM-shaped step DAGs (2–10 steps, + × − ÷, integer values, every step consumed, no trivial step) in v12e's exact dialect (`arith_program`, byte-identical to v12e's Natalia/Julie); the VM computes the value; (2) the writer sees the steps in words with results named, never a computed value, and writes one problem in a random situation; (3) host checks: a question mark, every input number present (digits, number words, or GSM's implicit 2/3/4/12), no intermediate value stated; (4) the round trip; (5) records are v12e's schema (`source program_first/…`, `slot_record` converts them unchanged). Chains: `PathSampler` walks a fact store over reused relations only, never a path whose question is in the dataset; the writer rewords the canonical question; a plan record (CotPlan) and a chain record (CotChain, VM-verified) per kept wording. Bucket 0 of the shape / seed hash is the held-out eval, written by a different writer. Built: `standin/data/build_program_first.py` (`selfcheck`, `arith`, `chain`, `mix`), 15 tests. **Gate** (pre-registered; the owner's to move): v12e + PF vs v12e alone at equal `--steps`; on the reworded held-out eval, VM-correct +10 pts; no v12e val family drops > 2 pts; 0 copied literals and 0 unknown slots. **Kill:** < +3 pts → binding, not wording, is the bottleneck, and program-first data stays out of the mix. **Selfcheck (2026-09-29, no LLM):** 1000/1000 programs VM value == sampler value, 569 distinct shapes, 458/1000 with a shape v12e has none of (2,741 v12e shapes), 22 ms/program. **Smokes with the local writer (LFM2.5-2.6B, answer mode):** the pipeline works and the filters do their job, but this writer is too weak for either family — arith 1 kept of 20 (13 dropped an input number, 6 wrote a different computation; the round trip caught every one), chains 0 of 23 (near-verbatim rewordings; as blind planner it never emits the JSON plan). Fixed on the way: think tags, echoed computation and appended solutions are stripped (`_clean_question`), steps are shown in words with a one-shot example (the symbol table was echoed back), `--max-steps`, rejects sampled into the manifest. Status: **OPEN; builder built, the data run waits on a writer** — a stronger dataset LLM (OpenRouter, the owner's model and budget; ~6M tokens for 2,000 programs × 2 + 1,000 paths × 3) or the emitter-mode check with a better local writer. **Writer screen on OpenRouter's free tier (2026-09-29; the paid account was at its credit limit; solver fixed at Nemotron-3-Super, reasoning off for the writers):** Nemotron-3-Ultra arith 5/12 kept, chains 8/12; Qwen3.8-27B arith 5/12, chains 10/12 (vs the local 2.6B's 1/20 and 0/23) — every arith reject was a question that encodes a different computation, caught by the blind re-solve. Free-tier limits (1,000 requests a day; Gemma-4 rate-limited upstream, Inkling refused outside agent apps) make the full run a paid one. `openrouter:<model>@off|low|…` sets the reasoning effort; 429/5xx are retried, 402 stops the run with a clear message. **Paid screen (same solver, 16 arith / 12 chains each):** Gemini 3.8 Flash arith 12/16, chains 9/12; GPT-6 Luna 9/16, 11/12; Nemotron-3-Ultra 8/16, 10/12; DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash 4/16, 10/12; Qwen3.8-27B 4/16, 11/12. **Data run (2026-09-29, $1.57; writers crossed so the eval never shares the training writer; solver Nemotron-3-Super):** arith train (Gemini) 1,000 programs → 2,000 questions → **1,367 kept (68%)**, 443 programs with a shape v12e lacks; chain train (Luna) 500 paths → 1,500 wordings → **1,104 kept (74%)**; held-out eval (bucket 0, the other writer) 236 arith + 297 plan + 297 chain = 830 records (`standin/data/out/pf_heldout_eval.jsonl`), 0 prompts, 0 arith shapes and 0 chain seeds shared with train; 60 sampled records re-run in the VM, 0 mismatches. Every arith reject but 22 was the round trip (611): the question encodes another computation. **The kept set is skewed short** — kept rate 95% at 2 steps, 81% at 3, 61% at 4, 44% at 5, 28% at 6, 17–19% at 7–8, 1/4 at 9, 0/10 at 10 — so the round trip, as built, buys correctness with length; a long-program arm needs the emitter-mode check or a stronger solver, and the gate should report accuracy by step count. The mix (`build_program_first.py mix`): `standin/data/out/emitter_sft_v12e_pf.jsonl`, 15,388 records = v12e's 11,845 + 1,341 arith + 1,101 plan + 1,101 chain (duplicates across shards dropped). Status: **data built; the A/B is wired and waits on one Colab session.** `notebooks/emitter_450m.ipynb` trains both arms on `base450m_cont_final.pt` at the control's two epochs (1,843 steps of 16, same seed and settings; slot form: control 14,744 train rows, program-first 18,265 — 35 plan wordings whose seed is not spelled as in the path drop out of slot conversion), generates the v12e val split and the held-out eval (`pf_heldout_eval_slots.jsonl`: 236 arithmetic, 285 plan, 294 chain) for each arm (`train_emitter_torch.py --gen-extra`), and `validation/exp_he18_ab.py` reads the four files through the real VM, by task and by arithmetic step count, and applies this gate and kill (pinned by `standin/tests/test_exp_he18_ab.py`; on the reference programs themselves it reads 1.000 on all 815 held-out and 614 v12e val records). **Control arm, first read (2026-09-30; v12e val, 318 records, the real VM):** held program loss 0.0894 at step 1,843 (6.2 min on the A100); plan 0.968, chain 0.873, kernel 0.844 (4 copied literals refused), arithmetic 0.031 — the programs run (0.969) but compute the wrong thing (operands reversed, extra steps): the 450M's real weakness, and the family this A/B targets — and role-binding 0.000, which was the read, not the model: the role names ACTION / AGENT / OBJECT are special tokens in bbpe128k and the trainer decoded with the tokenizer's default (skip special tokens), so every `bind evt, ACTION, "…"` came back `bind evt, , "…"`. Round trip of the val references through the old decode: role-binding 0/64, chain 62/63, everything else and all 815 held-out records exact; through the fix (`emitter_data.decode_program`, also used by the live `Cubby450mEmitter`) 100%. The held-out files are unaffected; the notebook's regenerate cell reruns each arm's v12e val from its saved adapter (`train_emitter_torch.py --gen-only`, the old file kept as `.prev.json`), and `exp_he18_ab.py` refuses a val file decoded before the fix. **Open for the owner:** the gate's "0 copied literals" is stricter than the control itself (arithmetic 2/64, kernel 4/64 copied, every one refused by the host) — absolute as written, or "no more than the control"; to settle before the program-first numbers are read. **Result (2026-09-30, `validation/logs/exp_he18_ab.json`; both val files regenerated with the decode fix, role-binding now 1.000 in both arms): VERDICT NEITHER** — two of three gates pass, the slot gate fails under either reading. Held-out eval (the other writer, 815 records): **0.415 → 0.548 (+13.4 pts)** — plan 0.225 → 0.495 (+27.0), chain 0.915 → 0.993 (+7.8), arithmetic 0.021 → 0.059 (+3.8; by step count +5.9 at 3 steps, +3.1 at 4, nothing at 5+). v12e val (318): no family down (arithmetic +1.6, chain +3.2, plan +1.6, kernel and role-binding level; _all 0.742 → 0.755). Slots: the program-first arm copied 13 literals and named 10 unknown slots (all arithmetic and kernel; 9 of the unknown slots on held-out arithmetic) against the control's 10 and 0 — so it fails "0 copied, 0 unknown" and also "no more than the control"; every one was refused by the host and none ran. Reading: rewording by a second writer is what the plan and chain emitters lacked (a 27-point plan gain on shapes and seeds never trained on), and program-first chain and plan data carry no slot cost (0 copied, 0 unknown in both families); for arithmetic the data is too small and too short to matter (+3.8 pts) and it raised unknown slots — arithmetic moves to H-E19 (TinyGSM through the VM, the school). Next: keep the program-first chain and plan records in the emitter mix; hold its arithmetic records out until H-E19's gate A says what the number-slot changes do to unknown slots.

**H-E19 — (added 2026-09-30) Arithmetic is a world and a school: with every number placed by the host (the question's, in digits or words, and the world's conversion facts), 50× more verified plans (TinyGSM through the VM), and a curriculum that starts at one operation, lets the emitter try until the VM says it is right, halves its tries as it masters a level, opens harder levels after, and rewards each verified solve with dopamine (the striatum's prediction error), the 450M's arithmetic climbs from 3% and keeps improving — learning from its own mistakes, not from a teacher model.** Source: the owner, 2026-09-30 ("how do we fix arithmetics?"; "arithmetic should improve over time, the model needs to learn from its mistakes"; "like a human does not start with rocket science algebra... try until it gets it right, then the amount of tryouts are reduced over time... each time it solves one right dopamine gets up as a reward"; "arithmetic is a world... it can go into the world and query it to get the right tools / data"; and `cubelang/examples/gsm8k.cube`, his earlier ISolverLearn solver whose `verify` / `learn(expected, actual)` / `classify_error` / `patterns` loop this runs for real — that file only parses, cubelang docs/DRIFT.md "Bugs found" #1). Prior art: TinyGSM (arXiv 2312.09241: 12.3M synthetic GSM problems with Python; a 125M generator 63.1% on GSM8K, 68.9% with a verifier), rejection-sampling fine-tuning (arXiv 2308.01825: LLaMA-7B 35.9 → 49.3, helps weaker models most, more distinct correct paths help more). **Why:** the control arm's 64 v12e val arithmetic programs (2026-09-30): 2 correct; 30% have the right number of steps, 1/64 the right operation sequence, 67% use the right numbers — the model picks numbers but cannot plan — and 62% of v12e's arithmetic programs carried a constant no slot covered. **Mechanism, built (2026-09-30):** (1) *every number placed by the host* — `slots.extract` slots numbers written in words ("four", "twice", "half" = the harvest's `div 2`, "a dozen"; one id per value), matches literals by value ("$3.50" is the program's 3.5 — a hole in both the converter and the copied-literal guard), and takes a world's constants as `$K` slots (`[K1 = 60 minutes per hour]`); on v12e, arithmetic records still carrying a constant 62% → 37% (words) → 28% (world), leftover constants −64%, 500/500 re-filled programs VM-correct; (2) *the arithmetic world* (`cubbyllm/reasoning/arith_world.py`): 36 conversion facts, each offered when the question names both units, and solved **procedures** — the question's content words with numbers masked, SimHash-coded like the hippocampus, and the slotted program the VM verified — recalled as (id, similarity) and proposed rebound to the new question's slots, latent until attested (the VM hit a known answer); a mistake solved once is recallable on the next similar question before any retrain; (3) `emitter_data --drop-step-values`: `# step 0: $N1 - $N2` without the `= 48` the model had to compute in its head (and the step index no longer mistaken for a number); (4) `standin/data/import_tinygsm.py`: TinyGSM's Python → v12e's dialect by `ast` only (never executed; any call, loop, branch, list is a counted reject), steps the result does not use pruned, our evaluator's values re-checked in the real VM, a program using a number the question never states dropped, questions sharing a 10-word run with an eval question dropped — first 3,000 rows: **1,481 kept (49%)**, 400/400 VM-agree, 359 one-step … 1 ten-step, 4% still carrying a constant after slotting; (5) *the school* (`cubbyllm/reasoning/school.py`): levels one operation → two steps → three-four → words and units → distractors → five-six → seven-ten, each batch the current level + 20% review of mastered ones; a problem is tried up to the level's budget (16 → 8 → 4 → 2 → 1 as the solve rate over 64 problems clears 0.8; doubled back below 0.3; at full budget a struggling level also practises the one before); dopamine = reward − expected per level (the striatum's rule: +1 solved by the VM against the gold, 0 not; a refusal is never punished), so a first solve bursts, a routine one is ~0 (boredom: move on) and a failure where success was expected dips; training rows carry it as their weight — the solve reinforced by its surprise, each wrong attempt paired with the solve (or, unsolved, with the dataset's verified program *shown* like a worked answer) and located at its **first wrong step** by the VM's step values and named by kind (stopped early, reversed, unit conversion, one number off — gsm8k.cube's `classify_error`, counted per level as its `patterns`). The school's map is also the serve-time **competence boundary** (`speak_policy`: a mastered level speaks on one verified program, a level in practice only on a VM majority of k, a level not reached is refused, never guessed — the owner: a model made to be good at everything at every level at once answers past what it knows). Pinned by `tests/reasoning/test_slots.py` (8), `test_arith_world.py` (4), `test_school.py` (9), `standin/tests/test_import_tinygsm.py` (11), `test_emitter_decode.py` (3). **The runner, built (smoke only):** `validation/train_school_torch.py` — k programs sampled at temperature in one batch per problem, slot check → fill → the VM (a persistent `CubelangSession`) against the gold, the curriculum's record → dopamine → rows, the update (dopamine-weighted cross-entropy on solves and shown solutions, a length-normalized preference on (wrong, right) pairs; on a tiny model the right plan's log-prob gained on the wrong one's in 20 updates), a fixed held-out slice read at one try each round and the round **kept only if it does not drop** (else the adapter goes back to the best: improvement over time, never a regression shipped), every solve written to the arithmetic world; `--no-curriculum` is gate B′'s control. *Against the L3 "evolver" failure modes* of "Agentic World Modeling" (arXiv 2604.22748, §5: a system that revises its own model from evidence must show improvement on held-out probes and regression suites, and guard against confirmation bias, catastrophic forgetting, reward hacking and Duhem–Quine blame assignment): the held-out slice is fixed and never chosen by the learner, and levels the learner is weak at are practised more, not less (confirmation bias); a round is kept only if the held-out does not drop **and** a regression suite of the other families (chain, plan, kernel, role-binding; `--regress`, scored by H-E18's rule) loses at most one record (forgetting); a program that writes the gold as a literal, or uses no number from the question, is refused and earns nothing (`answer_written_in`; reward hacking); a mistake is blamed on its component — the slot check (binding), the first wrong step (the plan), a world constant, or the VM (blame assignment). Its two modes of growth are both here: parameters (the school's LoRA rounds) and structure (the world's attested procedures and conversion facts). The CPU smoke (a random 4-layer model, the TinyGSM slice as the pool: levels 0–6 hold 246 / 249 / 241 / 332 / 206 / 147 / 60 problems) runs end to end. On Colab the notebook builds cubelang from its repo (or uses a Linux binary on Drive). **Gates** (pre-registered; the owner's to move): *A, data and format* — the 450M adapter on v12e (words, world, no step values) + TinyGSM shard 0, VM-correct at one try on the v12e val arithmetic (329) and the program-first held-out arithmetic (236) ≥ 20% (from 3.1%), copied literals ≤ the control's; *B, the school* — after its rounds, one-try accuracy ≥ +10 pts over A on the same held-out, no mastered level down > 2 pts on review, and each accepted round ≥ the last (improvement over time, not one jump); *B′, the ordering* — the same loop with every level open from the start (`--no-curriculum`: same tries, budgets, dopamine and mistake rows, levels drawn uniformly) at the same number of problems: the curriculum arm ≥ +3 pts over it. The literature finds curricula add little (e.g. Wu, Dyer & Neyshabur, ICLR 2021: marginal on standard benchmarks, helpful mainly under a limited budget or noisy labels) — measured on transformers trained in one pass; the owner expects a recurrent pupil in a try-until-right loop to differ, and B′ is the test of that, reported whichever way it falls; *C, memory* — a problem the school solved and wrote to the world is solved on a reworded variant through recall alone ≥ 50%, before any retrain; *D, honesty* — at serve, k samples run in the VM and speak only on a majority: spoken-wrong ≤ 1%. **Kill:** A under 10% — the 450M cannot plan GSM arithmetic even with 50× the plans, and arithmetic stays with the stand-in; B under +3 pts — the curriculum adds nothing over plain SFT on the same data, and the school is dropped for RFT alone. **Gate A result (2026-09-30; `validation/exp_he19_gate_a.py --stops` → `validation/logs/exp_he19_gate_a.json`, pinned by `standin/tests/test_exp_he19_gate_a.py`; adapter `cubby450m_v12e_w_tg_cont` = v12e_w + 100K TinyGSM, one epoch, 7,172 steps, held program loss 0.079; every program run in the real VM): KILL, read on the held-out eval.** v12e val arithmetic (329): **0.146** (control 0.031 on its 64; the new adapter 0.125 on the same 64) — by step count 2: 0.36, 3: 0.12, 4: 0.13, 5: 0.05, 6+: 0. Program-first held-out arithmetic (236, the other writer): **0.055** (control 0.021) — 3: 0.08, 4: 0.06, 5+: 0. Slots are clean (val 0 copied / 0 unknown; held-out 1 / 1 against the control's 4 / 0), so the number-slot changes did what they were for. Gate A (≥ 20% on both) fails on both, and the held-out sits under the 10% kill line. **Why:** the adapter learned the look of GSM, not planning — it writes too many steps (held-out: 79% of programs longer than the reference, control 62%) and half its held-out programs loop (a step body repeated 3+ times: 51% against the control's 14%; 40% never close before the 640-token cap, which is also why generation took ~20 s a record). The loop is a symptom, not the cause: cut after every step and run in the VM, a perfect stop reaches only 0.085 on the held-out (0.216 on val) and a stop at the reference's length 0.055 (0.134) — the plans are already wrong before the repetition starts. Reading, as pre-registered: the 450M does not plan GSM arithmetic from 100K verified plans; arithmetic stays with the stand-in, and the school's gates B–D are not run on this pupil. Not tested, and the owner's to reopen: data scale (100K is 0.8% of TinyGSM's 12.3M, one epoch) and a larger pupil. The school, the arithmetic world and the competence boundary are built and do not depend on the pupil. **The stand-in on the same problems (2026-09-30, `[stand-in]`; `standin/eval_emitter_vm.py --gguf emitter_v12e.Q4_K_M.gguf`, the LFM2.5-2.6B LoRA trained on the same v12e data, raw prompts, every program run in the VM → `standin/data/out/eval_emitter_vm_he19_standin_v12e_{pfheld,val}.json`):** program-first held-out arithmetic **0.852** (3 steps 0.94, 4: 0.83, 5: 0.79, 6: 0.75, 7: 0.50, 8: 0.25) against the 450M's 0.021 / 0.055; v12e val arithmetic **0.745** (0.703 on the control's 64; 2 steps 0.92, 3: 0.86, 4: 0.70, 5: 0.69, 6: 0.47) against 0.031 / 0.146. On the held-out it never loops, always closes, and 90% of its programs have the reference's step count (the 450M: 22% / 15%). Same data, same VM, same questions: the data was not the bottleneck, the 450M base is — the kill's reading holds. The held-out questions were kept by Nemotron-3-Super's blind re-solve (all 236 `passed: answer`), never by this emitter, so the stand-in is not selected for. What the stand-in lacks: it writes every number itself, so the host has no slot check on it (2% of its held-out programs use a number the question never states), and it is not the trunk. Its practice zone for the school is 6+ steps. **Two credit-free reads (2026-09-30, the owner: "something we can test without credits"), and a bug.** *(B) Units, no model* (`validation/exp_he19_units.py` → `logs/exp_he19_units.json`; Roy & Roth 2017's unit dependency idea as a floor: every placed number tagged by rule from the words around it, the asked unit from the question, every expression tree over the numbers enumerated by subset DP): typing prunes the held-out search from a median 951 candidates to 61–94 (~15×), but the gold value is **never the only survivor** (0 of 493 problems, under any regime, even with elided numbers free to take any unit) — units fix which numbers combine, never plus-vs-minus or times-vs-divide, and those multiply over steps; rule tags are weak besides (gold survives 25–36%: `sells 36` has no noun, `36 leave` reads as one). Units are a pruner for a search, not a planner; the choice stays local, per step, from sentence cues. *(A) Teacher-forced next step* (`validation/exp_he19_next_step.py`, the Gate A adapter on grilly2 locally, the GOLD program up to step k−1 as the prefix, only the next block generated, scored by the VALUE the block computes over the gold registers; held-out 236 = 925 steps + 236 stops): **step 0 right 80%** (op 82%, operands 91%), **every later step ~22%** (k=1 0.22, k=2 0.24, k=3 0.20), stop right 82% (97% on 3-step plans, 64% on 4-step: a length prior, not a plan). 182 of the 236 first steps are literally `$N1 op $N2` in text order — a pattern, not planning — and 0.8 × 0.22 × 0.22 ≈ 4% is the one-pass 5.5%: the one-pass loops were state loss, but the local decisions are wrong regardless. The mechanism: 87% of later gold steps chain from a register (`add s1, s0`); the model chains 34% of the time and starts 65% of later steps from a fresh question number — a register name is a pointer with no content the trunk can bind to, where a number with its words in the question it binds at 91%. Not the window (the question and prefix sit inside the 512), not the recurrence alone (H-P3): a binding failure of the state's representation. *The bug:* `emitter_data.slot_record` replaced the first occurrence of a literal's text in its op line, so `div s2, 2` became `div s$N5, 2` — ~9% of arithmetic slot rows in every slot file since H-E15 (v12e 532 records, v12e_w 847, TinyGSM 27,931), invisible to the VM because the fill turned `s$N5` back into `s2`, but a garbage token the adapters learned (`mul s$N4, 1;` in their generations). Fixed (the literal's own span; pinned in `test_emitter_decode.py`), the w-slot files regenerated (`*.regbug.jsonl` kept beside them; the teacher-forced run above read the old files, its cliff is far larger than the 9%). **The write-back arm, built (`cubbyllm/reasoning/step_loop.py`, 15 tests across `test_step_loop.py` / `test_exp_he19_memory.py`):** the state leaves the program and comes back as slots the host places — one emission per step (`create s1 : quantity;   # step 1: $S1 * $N3 / assign s1 = $S1; / mul s1, $N3;`), the host fills it, wraps it into a whole program (`wrap_step`), runs it in the real VM, and writes the value back under the question as `So far: [S1: 154 = N1 / N2]`; `return $S3;` is the stop; a copied value or an unknown $S refuses the step (H-E15's rule, `$S` is a slot kind now). `recut` cuts every gold program into its step rows (the `assign 0; add s0` idiom collapsed to `assign $S1`), 0 dropped once the bug was fixed; the gold rows replayed through the loop and cubelang solve **236/236** held-out at 4.9 emissions a problem. Data on Drive: `emitter_sft_v12e_w_tg30_step.jsonl` (146,557 train rows: v12e_w's arithmetic as 27K step rows + 30K TinyGSM records as 113K step rows + the other families as they were; ~14M tokens, ~20 min on the A100) and `pf_heldout_eval_w_step.jsonl` (1,161 next-step rows); the notebook's `v12e_w_step` cell continues the Gate A adapter on it; the read is `validation/exp_he19_step_loop.py` (the loop around the adapter and the real VM, locally). **Gate (pre-registered):** arithmetic correct through the loop ≥ 20% on the held-out (Gate A's line, for the loop), and the per-step read at k ≥ 1 well above 22%. Kill: under 10% through the loop — the 450M cannot chain even from content it can bind, and arithmetic is the stand-in's. What it brings: no intermediate value is ever the model's, every step is inspectable and blamed at its line, and the memory the plan needs is the VM's registers written back in the trunk's currency, which is the owner's shape — everything an adapter on the frozen trunk, the state outside it. Pending from the same day, no credits: the val-set read, the 2.6B stand-in under the same teacher forcing (does a bigger pupil chain from register names?), and a zero-training probe (`--inject-state`: the computed values placed in the question as extra number slots for the Gate A adapter as it is). **Landed the same evening.** *Val (329, v12e's own wording):* per-step value 0.44 at step 0, 0.38, 0.32, ~0.30 after (all steps 0.355), stop right 80% — flatter and lower at the start than the held-out: the held-out's 80% first step was the sampler's regular `$N1 op $N2` shape, not skill; on its own wording the model chains from a register 66% of the time when it should (39% on the other writer's), so the register failure is partly wording familiarity, and the steady read is one local decision in three. *The control adapter (v12e alone) on the held-out:* step 0 0.67, later steps 0.20–0.26, stop 51% — Gate A's 100K TinyGSM plans raised the first step (0.67 → 0.80) and the stop (0.51 → 0.82) and left every later step where it was (0.20 → 0.22): more plans in the same format teach nothing about chaining. *The 2.6B stand-in under the same teacher forcing (raw prompts, literals):* per-step value **0.92, flat in k** (0.95, 0.90, 0.92, 0.96, 0.93, 0.80 at k = 0..5), stop right 98%. So register names are bindable by a pupil that can bind them; the step-2 wall is the 450M's, not the format's — which is exactly why the write-back is the right question for the 450M and not for the stand-in. *The zero-training write-back probe* (`--inject-state`: every value the gold prefix computed placed in the question as a further number slot, `So far: [N6: 154] [N7: 924]`, the Gate A adapter as it is): later-step value 0.221 against 0.210 without it — no lift; k=1 rose (0.21 → 0.28), k=2 and k ≥ 4 fell, and the stop collapsed (82% → 33% right, 67% ran on: an untrained `So far:` line reads as "keep going"). The adapter does not use a value it was never trained to look for; this probe could only have shown a free win, and there is none — it says nothing against the trained format. *The fixed slot files* (the register-digit bug) change the held-out read by nothing (later steps 0.21 → 0.21): the bug was in training programs, not eval prompts. Decision for the retrain (the owner's): the notebook's `v12e_w_step` cell trains only (`--no-gen`, ~20–30 min on the A100, ~3–5 credits); the read is `exp_he19_step_loop.py` locally. If the per-step value stays near 0.2 under a format whose every choice is a slot the trunk binds, the 450M is not the arithmetic pupil and the stand-in keeps the seat, as Gate A's kill said. **The write-back arm, RESULT (2026-10-01; `cubby450m_v12e_w_step_cont`, the Gate A adapter continued one epoch on `emitter_sft_v12e_w_tg30_step.jsonl`, 4,691 steps of 32 on an A100, 34.6 min, held program loss 0.897 → 0.040; read locally on grilly2 with the real VM): GATE PASSES.** *Teacher-forced, the step format* (`exp_he19_step_loop.py --teacher-forced`, all 1,161 held-out step rows, scored by the value each emitted step computes): step 0 **0.758**, later steps **0.549** (689; k = 1..7: 0.49, 0.60, 0.53, 0.69, 0.44, 0.54, 0.60 — no cliff), stop **0.932** — against the one-pass read's 0.80 / 0.22 / 0.82: the later-step wall is gone, the first step costs four points. *The loop, alone* (`exp_he19_step_loop.py`, the 236 held-out questions, every step filled and run in the VM and its value written back, no gold anywhere): **0.229 correct** (54/236; Gate A's one-pass 0.055, the control's 0.021), answered 0.970, refused 7 (5 steps the VM could not run, 2 past the 12-step cap), 4.7 emissions a question, same step count as the reference 57%; by length 3 steps 0.34, 4: 0.19, 5: 0.07, 6+: 0. Of the 165 wrong answers, the first wrong step is step 1 in 51, step 2 in 81, step 3 in 29, step 4 in 4; 8 had a right prefix and stopped early, 2 ran on. **Reading:** the pre-registered line (≥ 20% through the loop, kill < 10%) is met — the 450M's arithmetic was a state-representation failure, not only a capacity one: with the VM holding the state and writing it back as slots, one 35-minute adapter run took held-out from 5.5% to 22.9%, and the per-step chaining from 22% to 55%. It is still far below the 2.6B stand-in (0.852 one-pass) and decays with length (0.55 a step compounds), so the stand-in keeps the arithmetic seat for the demo; what this buys is the measured mechanism, on CubbyLLM's own trunk, and the next lever. **Honesty is the open problem:** the loop answers 97% and is wrong on 74% — the VM certifies that every step RAN, not that the plan is right, so gate D (k samples, speak only on a VM majority) and the competence boundary (`school.speak_policy`) are now the thing that makes this servable; and the school (gate B) now has a pupil inside its range: per-step 0.55 is where try-until-right with the step as the unit of reward has room. **Gate D, RESULT (2026-10-01; `exp_he19_step_loop.py --vote 5 --temperature 0.7`, same adapter, same 236 questions; each step is one greedy + four sampled emissions in one batched grilly generate, every candidate filled and run in the VM, the step's winner is the most-voted VM value, a chain's confidence is its lowest step agreement): FAILS the honesty line.** Majority 0.237 (greedy 0.229), answered 0.970, refused 7 (6 no candidate ran, 1 no stop), 4.6 emissions a question; speaking only when every step's winner had at least this share of the votes - 0.2: spoken 0.970 / right 0.237 / wrong 0.733 / precision 0.245; 0.4: 0.869 / 0.229 / 0.640 / 0.263; 0.6: 0.475 / 0.174 / 0.301 / 0.366; 0.8: 0.161 / 0.081 / 0.081 / 0.500; 1.0 (unanimous): 0.055 / 0.034 / 0.021 / 0.615. No threshold reaches spoken-wrong ≤ 1%: the 450M is confidently wrong - its samples agree with each other on the wrong step, so self-agreement is not evidence, and voting adds 0.8 points for five times the emissions. **Cross-model speak gate (`exp_he19_cross_check.py`, no model run):** speak only when the 450M loop's answer equals the 2.6B stand-in's one-pass VM result - greedy loop: spoken 0.233 (55), right 0.225, wrong 0.008 (2), precision 0.964; vote loop: 0.242 / 0.233 / 0.008 / 0.965. The stand-in alone is right 0.852 and wrong on 35; the loop disagrees on 33 of those 35. **Reading:** two independent routes through the VM (different model, different program shape) agreeing is the first gate that meets ≤ 1% spoken-wrong. As a servable policy the stand-in answers and the 450M loop is its cross-check: spoken-wrong falls from 14.8% to 0.8%, at the price of speaking on a quarter of the questions - coverage is bounded by the 450M loop, so the school (gate B, the step as the unit) is now what raises it, and the cross-check is the speak policy to carry into `school.speak_policy`. **Gate B, the step school, RESULT (2026-10-02; `train_school_torch.py --step` on Colab, 8 rounds of 192 problems from the write-back adapter, try-until-right per step, dopamine and mistake pairs per step, kept only when the 64-question loop gate does not drop and the other families hold): FAILS - no transfer.** A first run measured nothing (protobuf runtime older than `reasoning_pb2`'s gencode; every VM call failed inside the bridge and read as "does not run": gate 0.000, regression 0/32); fixed with a fail-fast VM self-check and a pinned runtime, and a judge bug that refused steps built only on earlier values ($S) was fixed before it. The valid run: practice climbed the curriculum (level 0 mastered, level 1 mastered, level 2 at 75-90% of steps solved, budgets 16 -> 1), the regression suite held 32/32 every round, the preference margin stayed ~0; the 64-question gate read 0.281 at round 0 and 0.234-0.297 after (round 2 kept at 0.297, +1 question; every later round reverted). Read locally on the full 236 through the loop with the kept adapter: **0.229** - identical to the write-back baseline (0.229; 51 right in both, 3 gained, 3 lost), by length 3: 0.35, 4: 0.20, 5+: 0; the pre-registered bar (>= 0.329) is not met. Cross-check with the 2.6B unchanged (spoken 0.237, wrong 0.008, precision 0.964). **Reading:** practice-set step accuracy is not the bottleneck; the loop fails on choosing the next step of a longer plan (5+ steps: 0 of 54), which per-step reward on the training distribution does not teach. The speak policy stands as the cross-check; the next lever is the plan (which step next), not the step. **Next-step ranking, RESULT (2026-10-02; `exp_he19_rank.py`, the write-back adapter, 400 random held-out step rows, local grilly2, no credits): the right step is on the model's short list, not at its top.** Every step is one op on two values the host holds, or a stop, so the host lists the menu (149 choices on average, 0 rows off it) and the adapter scores each by the teacher-forced log-prob of the whole emission (prompt read once, the recurrent state copied to every candidate; exact parity with the uncached read); candidates that compute the same value are one choice. Top-1 / top-3 / top-5 of the right choice: step 0 0.80 / 0.93 / 0.95; step 1 0.58 / 0.85 / 0.94; step 2 0.58 / 0.81 / 0.85; step 3+ 0.52 / 0.86 / 0.90; stop rows 0.99 / 1.00 / 1.00; all steps 0.62 / 0.86 / 0.91. The menu's argmax matches greedy writing (0.758 / 0.549 / 0.932), so writing loses nothing the ranking would not; but on later steps the right step is second or third in about 0.29 of rows. The gap between the first and second choice is a calibrated confidence: top-1 0.41 / 0.59 / 0.79 / 0.98 by quartile of the gap (Q4 gap > 2.8 nats). **Reading:** the knowledge is mostly there and the pick is the failure, which (a) makes per-step speak gates cheap (the gap, no sampling), (b) makes a VM-checked beam over the top 3 a real candidate for the loop (needs a chain-level chooser, since the model's own score is what put the right step second), and (c) points the training lever at a reranker over the menu (a pointer head trained contrastively on the 146k step rows, the right choice against the menu) rather than at more writing practice. **Next-step pointer head, RESULT (2026-10-03; `exp_he19_pointer.py`, frozen trunk + write-back adapter read once per step, a 0.86M-parameter head scoring the menu from the last prompt state and each held value's marker state, trained on 21.8k step rows, CPU): WORSE than the adapter's own ranking.** Dev (training distribution) top-1 0.63 at its best epoch, overfitting after epoch 6. Held-out, all 1,161 rows: step rows top-1 0.43 / top-3 0.68 / top-5 0.76 (step 0 0.56, 1 0.40, 2 0.47, 3+ 0.29), stops 0.97. On the 400 rows the ranking read scored: head step rows 0.43 / 0.65 / 0.77 against the adapter's 0.62 / 0.86 / 0.91. **Reading:** a head over pooled final-layer states loses what the adapter uses when it writes the step token by token (the `# step k: $A op $B` comment conditioned on each choice); a frozen-feature pointer is not the cheap win. The pick has to be trained where the knowledge is: a listwise loss on the adapter itself (the right emission's log-prob above the menu's), which is a GPU run, or the head as a prior combined with the adapter's candidate scores (needs the ranking read re-run with per-candidate scores kept). **Listwise fine-tune of the adapter, RESULT (2026-10-04; `train_rank_torch.py` on Colab: 12,000 step rows, the right emission against <= 11 wrong ones from the same menu (half hard: the gold's values under another op, the stop when a step is due), list cross-entropy + 0.5 x writing NLL, lr 3e-4, 3,000 steps, 28 min; read locally on the rebuilt export after the 4 TB drive failed): WORSE everywhere it counts.** Its own sampled-list read rose (held 12-way lists top-1 0.86 -> 0.92) while the full menu ranking on the same 400 rows fell: step rows top-1 0.43 / top-3 0.66 / top-5 0.76 (was 0.62 / 0.86 / 0.91), stops 0.90 (was 0.99), and the loop on the 236 questions fell to **0.059** (was 0.229), with 21 runs that never stop and 20 steps the VM could not run. **Reading:** two faults. The lr (3e-4, six times the cell's default) pulled the adapter off its writing, and the sampled list is not the menu: training beat the 11 sampled wrong entries while the other ~120 menu entries rose, so the 12-way read moved opposite to the menu and the loop. A rerun is set (lr 5e-5, writing weight 1.0, a fresh out dir); its pass bar is the full-menu ranking and the loop, never the sampled lists, and more negatives per list (or the whole menu, cheap on the shared prompt) are the fix if it still diverges. **Rerun, RESULT (2026-10-05; lr 5e-5, writing weight 1.0, 3,000 steps; `cubby450m_v12e_w_step_rank_lr5e5`):** the sampled held lists went 0.860 -> 0.905 with the writing loss flat (~0.05), the adapter moved modestly (median lora_B change 11%, against 64% at 3e-4; same keys, shapes and config -- not a save/load fault), and it still lost on both real reads: loop 0.119 (236; 3-step 0.12, 4-step 0.20; answered 0.970; seen templates 0.087, unseen 0.163) against 0.229; full-menu step top-1 0.478 against 0.619 (step 0 0.671, 1 0.352, 2 0.459, 3+ 0.451), top-3 0.753, stops 0.938 against 0.988. **Reading:** the lower rate halves the damage, it does not remove it -- training against sampled negatives teaches the wrong thing at any rate; listwise is parked until it can be trained against the whole menu. **Side reads the same day (CPU, no model):** (1) generalisation split (`exp_he19_seen_split.py`): 138 of the 236 questions reword a GSM8K problem whose template is in the training data, 98 do not; baseline loop 0.246 seen / 0.204 unseen, 5-vote 0.254 / 0.214 -- the score is mostly not memorised (n = 98: within noise), and every loop read is reported split this way from now on. (2) Model-free pruning (`exp_he19_prune.py`, rules judged on 27.6k GSM8K-train step rows, held-out only confirms): only 'negative result' (0.43% gold cut, removes 10.8% of wrong menu entries) and 'zero result' (0.19%, 4.1%) pass the 1% bar; 'fractional from whole' cuts 3.1% of train gold (0% on held-out -- the trap a held-out-tuned rule would fall into), 'x op x' 1.7%, 'no progress' 10.5%; 'use every number' fails 29% of gold traces. On the model's ACTUAL wrong top-1 picks the two safe rules catch 3% -- the model's mistakes are plausible numbers, so pruning is no fix by itself. (3) Error types (`exp_he19_errtype.py`, baseline 400-row ranking): at step 1 top-1 is flat across menu size (0.56 / 0.58 / 0.61), so menu size is not the step-1 cause; wrong picks are wrong op 27%, wrong operands 35%, both 38%; 43% are the reuse decision (19% ignore an $S they needed, 24% use one they should not); from step 3, 38% of wrong picks are early stops. Margins: right picks median gap 1.17 (56% under 1.4), $S-reuse errors 0.39 (85% under 1.4), other errors 0.58 (80%). Next: a margin-triggered top-3 beam over the menu (cutoff set on TRAIN rows, not the held-out quartiles) and step-row DPO on mined train mistakes (shared tokens cancel, unlike unlikelihood). **Beam over the menu, RESULT (2026-10-05; `exp_he19_beam.py`, baseline adapter, no training):** each step is picked FROM THE MENU (every legal op on the held values, plus the stops; negative and zero results pruned) by the adapter's own score, computed exactly over the candidates' token trie (parity with flat scoring 9e-5) and through the step's plan line only (the ops lines copy it; same top-1 as whole-step scoring on 8/8 dev rows) -- 5-7 s a menu instead of ~30. The branching cutoff was set before the loop on a DEV set (the emitter's own 80 GSM8K val problems, never trained on, disjoint from the 236): tau = 1.405, the 75th percentile of the wrong picks' margins (dev top-1 0.557, top-3 0.765). Max 10 steps. On the 236 (all / seen / unseen templates): free generation 0.229 / 0.246 / 0.204; **width 1, the menu's top pick: 0.275 / 0.290 / 0.255**; top-3 beam below tau (3 paths kept, 11.5 menus a question), finished paths ranked by summed log-prob 0.263 / 0.268 / 0.255, by the weakest margin 0.246 / 0.232 / 0.265, **by mean log-prob per step 0.309 / 0.326 / 0.286**; ORACLE (any finished path right) **0.496 / 0.536 / 0.439**. **Reading:** (1) picking the step from the menu instead of writing it is worth +4.6 points with no training, and holds on unseen templates (+5.1); (2) branching where the model is unsure puts the right answer among the finished paths for half the questions -- the search is no longer the bottleneck, choosing between finished paths is: summed log-prob prefers short wrong paths, the mean per step recovers part of the gap (0.309) and the oracle is 0.496. **Ranker chosen on dev (2026-10-06; `exp_he19_beam.py --dev 80:200`, the same beam and tau on 120 further emitter val problems, disjoint from the calibration 80 and the 236, all unseen templates):** sum 0.242, **mean 0.250**, min-margin 0.217, oracle 0.450 (13.0 menus a question).
