Mean per step wins on dev as on the 236, so the 236's 0.309 stands as the beam's result -- but its lead over sum on dev is one problem (30 against 29 of 120), so sum and mean are near-equivalent, and no ranker built from the model's own scores closes the gap: the oracle sits 0.20 above the best ranker on dev and 0.19 on the 236. Next: a path verifier (a PRM) trained on TRAIN-set beams with VM-checked labels (a partial path is on track when its values are among the gold program's), chosen on these dev problems and scored against the oracle. **The maths world's path scorer, PRE-REGISTERED (2026-10-06; the owner: the trunk is language only, one specialty per specialist -- cubby-lm's mixture of trunks -- and MoWM's HYLA picture: state and action as block codes, a future per world scored by plausibility x value, top-k kept):** in arithmetic the VM computes the future exactly, so the world model's only job is the score. `validation/exp_he19_cvl.py`: a state (the question's words around each number, SimHash codes from the fastword table, order kept by rotation; each $S an OP-bound fold of its operands; the question's ask) and an action (op, a, b) -> logit P(the future is on track), on track = the step's value is one the gold program still has to compute (a stop: the gold answer); trained with binary cross-entropy + a listwise term over the whole menu (cubemind's contrastive value rule, the VM supplying the future), on GSM8K-train/TinyGSM step rows only, plus one off-track state per problem; early stopping on val 200:329. The beam (tau 1.405) is rerun with every finished path kept (`--dump-paths`) on dev 80:200 and on the 236; one rule is chosen on the dev paths from a declared family (the mean log-prob; the scorer's sum or weakest step; mean log-prob + lambda x the scorer's mean, lambda in 0.5/1/2/4/8; HD-GoT consensus over the paths, cubemind `hd_got`) and read once on the 236, split seen / unseen. **Gate:** the chosen rule >= 0.339 on the 236 (+3 points over the mean ranker's 0.309; ceiling the oracle 0.496). **Kill:** at or under 0.309 -- the explicit structure the scorer sees does not separate right paths from wrong ones, and the shortlist (a maths specialist trunk, trunk B through the arena) is the next lever. **RESULT (2026-10-06/07; `exp_he19_cvl.py`, scorer v1: fastword v4 SimHash codes at D=2048, a QNet MLP over state + action codes, BCE + listwise InfoNCE over the whole menu, 12k train problems + one off-track state each, best epoch 1 by early stopping on val 200:329 -- val top-1 0.392, AUC 0.839; the beam rerun with `--dump-paths` on dev 80:200 (120 problems) and on the 236): KILL.** *Dev, the rule choice (all unseen templates):* lm_mean 0.250, q_sum 0.150, q_min 0.175, mix_0.5 0.258, mix_1 0.267, mix_2 0.258, mix_4 0.250, **mix_8 0.275**, got 0.125, oracle 0.450 -- mix_8 chosen, as declared, by the dev maximum. *The 236, read once (all / seen 138 / unseen 98):* **mix_8 0.267 / 0.290 / 0.235** against lm_mean 0.309 / 0.326 / 0.286 and the oracle 0.496 / 0.536 / 0.439 -- under the kill line (<= 0.309) by 4.2 points, and under the LM's own mean on both splits. Diagnostics only (never the result): q_sum 0.178, q_min 0.216, mix_0.5 0.318, mix_1 0.305, mix_2 0.301, mix_4 0.275, got 0.174. **Reading:** the scorer separates on-track from off-track STATES moderately (AUC 0.84 on val) but does not separate right finished PATHS from wrong ones: used alone (q_sum, q_min, got) it is far below the LM's mean, and mixed in it is a wash at small lambda and a loss at large -- the dev lead of mix_8 (+3 problems of 120) was noise, exactly the kind the one-read protocol exists to catch. The explicit structure the scorer sees (which numbers, which op, the ask) is not what distinguishes a right plan from a plausible wrong one on these problems; the model's own log-prob already carries that, and the oracle gap (0.19) stays open. As pre-registered, the shortlist moves to the maths specialist: trunk B through the cross-trunk arena. The README beam row stays at the mean ranker's 0.309.

**H-E20 — (added 2026-10-17) Grown and given a memory, on data that teaches following and acting: base450m_cont, grown depth ×2 + FFN ×2 with zero exits and the H-D5 episodic read inserted on every fourth layer (exits zeroed, so step 0 is the 450M exactly), trained at S = 2048 on fresh tokens with instruction-following chats, agentic trajectories and the emitter's own programs in the decay, ends at or below the 450M trained the same way on the same hours — and its beyond-window recall comes off the floor.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-17 ("see if we add memory every 2 layers if it will help"; "we need to grow the model too"; "add instruction following, that's a weak spot"; "we also need to do something for agentic"; "make generations every 500"). **What is measured before anything is trained** (`runs/probes`, local): the trunk's serving prompts never reach the window — emitter prompts max 337 bbpe tokens, live `/ask` questions ≤ 20, talk prompts 7.1% past 512 (p99 694, max 810) — so a beyond-window read changes nothing on 100% of program emissions and 93% of talk prompts today; what it can change is the long facts block, a long conversation, and the agent trajectories (3-5k tokens each), which is why the run trains at S = 2048 and why the agentic data goes in. **Mechanism, built:** (1) `cubbyllm/model/grow.py`: `grown_meta(..., mem_every=)` and `grow_into` insert fresh `MemoryRead`s with their output projection zeroed when the source has none (exact at step 0; the gradient reaches the read's exit first; a source that already carries a memory must keep its layout) — `validation/grow_base.py --mem-every`, `train_base.py` `CB_MEM_EVERY` / `CB_MEM_TOPK` / `CB_MEM_KEY` in the meta (absent at 0, so every earlier checkpoint's meta still matches); pinned by `tests/model/test_grow.py` (forward, decode and gradient). Local step 0 on `base450m_final.pt`, depth ×2 + FFN ×2 + memory every 2nd of 64 layers: 576.3M → 1,363.9M parameters, **EXACT** (`validation/logs/grow_step0__smoke_d2f2m2.log`: loss diff 0, max |logit diff| 6e-4). (2) `validation/make_decay_shards.py`: three shards added to the token cache — `instruct` (61,677 of the host's guarded Nemotron chats in the talk format, 15.5M tokens), `agent` (10,102 open_thoughts_agent_trove trajectories — observe → analysis / plan / commands JSON → observe, 79.5M tokens; our tools are not shell commands, the loop's shape and the action format are what the base takes), `program` (25,955 slotted emitter records, 7.3M tokens), each with a document-aligned held-out tail in `_holdout.json` (`validation/logs/make_decay_shards_2026-10-17.json`). (3) `notebooks/grow_mem_continue.ipynb` (`_build_grow_mem.py`): two arms from `base450m_cont_stable_end.pt` (no re-warm), equal hours on the same card, S = 2048, samples every 500 steps; **as run (2026-10-17/18, a 96 GB RTX 6000):** the grown arm fit micro 2 only — 64 layers of saved activations at S = 2048 plus the 128k-vocab logits in float32, not the 22 GB of weights and Adam; activation recompute (`CB_GRAD_CKPT=1`) was added after it started and is on for every later grown session — and Nick set the optimizer step to 80,000 tokens (19 forwards a step instead of 32): 77,824 tokens a step, not the pre-registered 131,072, so the control arm runs at the same step and hours (the notebook carries `TOKENS_PER_STEP` and its readout checks both arms' step size and hours from the metrics before any gate is read); the grown arm's memory on every 4th of 64 layers = 16 reads, the count "every 2 layers" gives on the 32-layer base, because each read keeps a (B, S, S) similarity and a (B, S, K, d) gathered value set for the backward pass; the new shards at ~5 / 10 / 2% of the decay (0.8 / 0.3 / 0.7 epochs at 1.2B tokens) and a trace in the stable phase. **Gates (pre-registered; the owner's to move):** (1) growth — the grown arm's held-out mix at or below the control's at equal hours (+0.02 tolerance; above → the 450M still wants data before parameters, growth stays killed); (2) memory — `exp_needle_recall` on the grown final at 1024 / 2048 / 4096: beyond-window depths > 25% where the control sits at chance, in-window unchanged (flat → the read did not train at this scale; serve without it); (3) the decay data — each arm ends below its own first reading on `instruct`, `agent` and `program`, and no checked source (FineWeb-Edu, books, QA, code) ends more than 0.02 above the control; (4) serving — the emitter and talk LoRAs retrained on the winner, gates E6-E9 each at least talk_v2_2's with 0 spoken wrong, exp_r17 and the step loop at least the 450M's own, an instruction-following gate (`validation/exp_if_follow.py`, to build: rule-checked constraints over held chats) above the 450M's; the gap to the stand-in on the same benches reported as measured. **Kill:** gate 1 fails by more than 0.05, or gate 3's checked sources regress on both arms (the decay data hurt the base: cut its share before re-asking). **Serving path, ported (2026-10-17):** grilly2's cubby model carries the read (`grilly/infer/models/cubby.py`, `CubbyMemory` + per-stream stores in `CubbyState`, batched — torch's step is one stream at a time), `validation/export_base.py` writes the memory layers (`memory_norm`, `memory.{q,k,v,o}`) and the `mem_*` config fields, and the store is sized by the stream's length (`max_length - window` rows, 16,384 at most), so a grown-with-memory base serves through the same `--cubby450m` path as the 450M. Pinned: grilly2 `tests/python/test_cubby.py` (every test twice, with the memory on every second layer: prompt parity, the step across the store's wrap, greedy identical, row selection) and CubbyLLM `tests/model/test_export_grilly.py` (a tiny base with memory, exported, gives `CubbyModel.forward`'s and `CubbyModel.step`'s logits on grilly2). **First read, the grown arm (2026-10-18; `runs/grow_mem_grown`, a 96 GB RTX 6000, 20.0 h, micro 2, 77,824-token steps, 5,426 steps = 0.42B tokens at 6.2k tok/s — one eleventh of the continuation's 4.8B in 21 h, the price of 64 layers at S = 2048 without activation recompute):** held-out mix 2.340 at the start (the continuation's stable_end, S = 1024) → 2.451 at step 450 (the growth's transient once the zeroed exits move) → flat at 2.44 for the whole stable phase (3,900 steps, 0.3B tokens: no recovery) → 2.315 after the decay, 0.024 below its start and 0.089 above the continuation's own final (2.227). Every checked text source ends 0.1–0.2 above the continuation's final (books 3.22 vs 3.01, wikipedia 3.09 vs 2.87, fineweb_edu 3.22 vs 3.17, code 1.39 vs 1.40). The new shards learned (gate 3's first clause): instruct 2.66 → 2.11, agent 1.85 → 1.46, program 0.66 → 0.20. **The memory's own probe:** `_copy` (a half-sequence repeated; at S = 2048 the repeat is 1,024 tokens back, beyond the window, so only the read can lower it) sat at 12.28 at step 450 and 12.07 at the end — the floor (ln 128k = 11.8) — against 1.15 in-window at S = 1024: the read did not come off the floor in 0.42B tokens. Gate 2 (`exp_needle_recall`) is still to run on the final, but this is its answer in advance; gate 1 waits on the control arm at the same step and hours (which, at 32 layers and micro 8–16, will see three to four times the tokens). Reading: the experiment as run is underpowered on the grown side — the growth's transient was never paid back and the read never trained — and the cause is throughput, not the design; a second grown session needs `CB_GRAD_CKPT=1` at micro 8–16 (built after this arm started) and, to cut the read's cost, fewer memory layers (`--mem-every 8`: eight reads) or a shorter stable at S = 2048 after a longer one at S = 1024. **Gate 2, read (2026-10-18, an A100; `validation/exp_needle_forward.py` — `exp_needle_recall`'s probe by batched forwards, built because the step path's 4,096 matvecs a prefix would not run here; `validation/logs/needle_grow_mem_grown_2026-10-18.json`, `needle_base450m_cont_2026-10-18.json`): 1,024 / 2,048 / 4,096 tokens, the needle at depths 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 %, 12 trials a cell against 7 distractors (chance 12.5 %), PMI scoring, trained vs the shuffled-needle control vs an untrained model.** In-window cells (the needle within 512 tokens of the probe) are 100 % for both models at every length, and the window's edge is exactly where the design says: 256 back at 1,024 → 100 %, 512 back at 2,048 → chance. Beyond the window, averaged over the eleven such cells: the grown final **15.9 % trained against 16.8 % for its own shuffled control**; the 450M continuation 9.1 % against 11.4 % — both at chance, neither distinguishable from its no-signal control; per cell the grown arm's best is 33 % (4 of 12) at 1,024 / depth 0, inside the noise of 12 trials. **Gate 2 fails at 0.42B tokens**: the read did not come off the floor, as `_copy` said. The pre-registered consequence is "flat → the read did not train at this scale; serve without it" — read here as: not at this scale; the arm never left the growth's transient, and the throughput fixes (`CB_GRAD_CKPT`, micro 8–16, fewer reads) come before any judgement of the mechanism. Status: **OPEN — grown arm run: gate 3a passes, gate 2 fails at 0.42B tokens, gate 1 waits on the control; a second grown round continues from `grow_mem_grown_stable_end.pt` (`CONTINUE_FROM`, `RUN_SUFFIX='_2'`).**

**H-M1 - (added 2026-10-04) An n-gram memory in host RAM, addressed by bound qFHRR word codes.** Qwen4's preview (Qwen3.8-Flash-Next) carries a 51B-parameter bigram/trigram embedding table read at layer 2 by direct address, so it lives in host RAM and is prefetched; MiMo-V3's HySparse2 shows the related trick of an indexer choosing what sparse layers read. The CubbyLLM form: the key of a trigram is built from the fastword table's compact codes (80 integer phases per word), (rot2(a) + rot1(b) + c) mod 128 per block -- qFHRR binding with position by block rotation -- and a memory row is addressed by the phases of g blocks (g = 3: 2.1M buckets; several block groups = several tables). Hoped: an address that is exact like a hash for different facts but shared for paraphrases, in the same block space as MoWM, the hippocampus and the VM frames. **Screen, RESULT (2026-10-04; `exp_hm1_ngram_hv.py`, the rebuilt v4 table: 60,147 words, potion-retrieval-32M teacher, axiom routing macro 0.315; 597k distinct corpus trigrams; per-block phase agreement p and same-bucket rates by pair class, the blend rebuilt exactly as (1-b) proj + b signal):** at the shipped blend 0.5 (and already at 0.25: 86% of blocks) the per-block argmax IS the word's signal slot, so the key is a pure hash: no meaning in the address (every pair class at chance, p = 0.008 = 1/128) and a perfectly uniform load (24.85% of trigrams share a g=3 bucket against 24.78% for a uniform hash; max load 6; no full-key duplicates). With the teacher part alone (b = 0): semantic nearest neighbours agree on 24% of blocks (30x chance), hand paraphrase pairs on 9%, same-type entities (france/germany, a different fact) on only 1.8%, random 0.8%; load stays uniform. But exact g = 3 buckets keep little of that: neighbours share one 3.9% of the time, 12% across 4 tables. **Reading:** (1) the shipped table already gives a free, balanced, deterministic n-gram hash from 80-byte codes -- the Engram/Qwen address, decodable back to its words; (2) meaning survives in the phases and separates different facts well (entities 1.8% vs neighbours 24%), but exact bucket matching throws most of it away. Next: soft addressing on the teacher part -- multi-probe (the top 2-3 phases per block) or a product-key read with the dense n-gram code -- and a two-part key (identity blocks for separation, semantic blocks for sharing); then an LM test (the paper's open problem #6).

**H-M1 soft addressing, RESULT (2026-10-05; `exp_hm1b_soft_address.py`, same v4 table and 597k trigrams; query = a corpus trigram with ONE word swapped, 4,000 per class; the writer stores at the exact top-1 key, the reader combines each word's top-3 slots per block into the key's top-8 phases and ranks the m^g buckets of each table by summed score -- a product-key read):** at blend 0, g = 3, top-16 probes per table: across 4 tables the original row is found for 23.7% of semantic-neighbour swaps (exact buckets: 12%) and 6.2% of paraphrase swaps (exact: 0), against 0.18% for entity siblings (a different fact) and 0 for random words; across all 26 three-block tables, 50.1% neighbours, 20.4% paraphrases, 0.73% entities, 0.03% random. Self queries hit at K = 1 every time. A wider reader (top-8 slots, 16 phases) changes almost nothing (neighbours 9.4 -> 9.7% per table at K = 16), and blend 0.1 is worse in every class. **Reading:** a soft read doubles what the address keeps and still keeps different facts apart (neighbour/entity ~70x at 26 tables); the ceiling is the teacher's per-block agreement (neighbours 24% top-1, hand paraphrases 12%: potion puts big/large far apart), not the reader. The cost is reads: 26 tables x 16 probes = 416 row reads per n-gram against one per head for a hash. What it brings over a hashed n-gram table (Engram, Qwen4): an unseen paraphrased n-gram still reaches a related row instead of nothing. Next: a two-part row (an identity tag from the signal blend -- 0 collisions in v4 -- stored with each row, so a read knows exact from similar) and a teacher with stronger paraphrase agreement, then the LM test.

**H-M1 teacher swap, RESULT (2026-10-05; the v4 build with `paraphrase-MiniLM-L6-v2` as the teacher -- fastword_table_v5p, same 60,147 words, CPU, 2 min; the same two screens, blend 0):** per block, the hand paraphrase pairs agree on 24% of blocks (potion 9%), entity siblings on 5.3% (potion 1.8%), random at chance; load stays uniform. Soft read, g = 3, top-16 probes: across 4 tables paraphrase swaps reach the original row 35.3% of the time (potion 6.2%), entity swaps 1.65% (potion 0.18%), random 0.03%; across 26 tables paraphrases 74.7% (potion 20.4%), entities 7.6% (potion 0.73%), random 0.08%. (The neighbour class is each teacher's own nearest word, so it does not compare across teachers.) MoWM routing macro on this table is 0.246 against potion's 0.315. **Reading:** the teacher was the ceiling -- a paraphrase-trained teacher lifts paraphrase recall 3.7x, but it also pulls same-type entities together (france/germany), so the leak of a DIFFERENT fact rises 10x (selectivity paraphrase/entity ~28x -> ~10x at 26 tables). Two consequences: (1) the n-gram memory and MoWM want different tables (MoWM routing keeps potion); (2) the identity tag is now required, not optional -- a read must say 'exact' or 'similar' so a france-row reached from a germany-trigram is used as a prior, never as the fact. Caveat: 35 hand paraphrase pairs only. Next: the two-part row (tag = the signal-blend phases, exact by construction) and the LM test.

**H-M1 Attract-Repel specialisation, RESULT (2026-10-05; `hm1_attract_repel.py`):** a GLOBAL linear map on the teacher vectors, trained on WordNet constraints -- attract synonyms (17.4k pairs), repel antonyms (0.8k) and co-hyponyms (29.7k, siblings under one hypernym, e.g. two European countries) -- with every word of the screen's test classes (the 35 paraphrase pairs, the entity sets: 143 words) BANNED from every constraint, so any gain on them is generalisation, and a 10% word split as dev. The textbook Attract-Repel loss (hardest in-batch negatives) barely moved the map (train synonyms 0.148 -> 0.150: it spreads every word apart); the working objective pulls synonyms to 1, pushes repel pairs to the random-pair level and keeps random-pair cosines (keep 2, repel weight 1 or 4, chosen on the dev pairs). Mean cosine, unseen dev synonyms / co-hyponyms: potion 0.15 / 0.06 -> 0.23 / 0.08; paraphrase-MiniLM 0.38 / 0.26 -> 0.53 / 0.29 (repel 1) or 0.35 / 0.14 (repel 4). Tables rebuilt through the same vocabulary and projection (builder `--teacher npz:`), same screens, soft read g = 3, top-16, any of 4 / any of 26 tables -- paraphrase, entity, random: potion 6.2 / 20.4%, 0.18 / 0.73%, 0 / 0.03%; potion+AR 14.5 / 20.7%, 0.08 / 0.53%, 0.03 / 0.03%; paraphrase-MiniLM 35.3 / 74.7%, 1.65 / 7.6%, 0.03 / 0.08%; +AR (repel 1) 16.1 / 55.1%, 0.83 / 3.8%, 0.03 / 0.13%; **+AR (repel 4) 31.5 / 64.8%, 0.47 / 2.2%, 0.03 / 0.08%** -- the repel-4 table keeps potion's selectivity (paraphrase / entity ~30x at 26 tables) with ~3x potion's paraphrase recall. MoWM routing on it 0.228 (potion 0.315: MoWM keeps potion). **Caveat that decides the next step:** repel 1 vs repel 4 was chosen on these screens, i.e. on the test lists -- the pick must be confirmed on a FRESH, larger paraphrase/entity list before it is called. A Qwen3-Embedding 0.6B teacher was started and stopped (CPU embedding of 60k words ~95 min; not screened).

**H-M1c, the Rubik's-cube encoding, RESULT (2026-10-05; `exp_hm1c_hamming.py`):** grilly v1's `cubemind/cube.h` encodes a cube state as sign(sum role_i * filler_i) (role = facelet position, filler = colour, both random) and recalls by a nearest-neighbour Hamming scan over every stored key (`cache.h`, XOR + popcount). Here: key(a, b, c) = bitwise majority of (role_p XOR filler_w), 10,240 bits, filler = the sign of the word's table vector (a SimHash of the teacher); all 597k corpus trigrams stored (764 MB bitpacked); 300 queries per class; a match is ACCEPTED when the original trigram is the nearest key and its distance < tau. With one of three words swapped the original is the nearest key for most queries in every class (random words too: 52%) -- two shared words carry it -- so the decision is the distance, which separates the classes: mean d/D paraphrase 0.12-0.17, entity 0.20-0.22, random 0.25. Accepted at tau = 0.15 (0.10), paraphrase / entity / random: potion 22.3% (14.0) / 0 (0) / 0; paraphrase-MiniLM 74.7% (28.3) / 10.7% (0.7) / 0; +AR repel 1 71.3% (33.7) / 7.3% (0) / 0; +AR repel 4 74.3% (31.3) / 4.0% (0.3) / 0. The shipped blend 0.5 matches blend 0 in this form (the signal is 80 of 10,240 bits), so the production table already works as a cube-style memory. CPU numpy scan: ~330 ms per query over 597k keys (the GPU scan is next). **Reading:** the cube encoding is the better read for our scale -- one exact scan instead of multi-probe buckets, no bucket boundaries to miss, and one threshold that sets the paraphrase / different-fact trade-off (the bucket read needed 26 tables x 16 probes for 64.8% / 2.2%; the scan gets 74.3% / 4.0% at 0.15 or 31.3% / 0.3% at 0.10). Hashing (Qwen4, Engram) is what a 51B-row table needs; ~1M rows do not. Same caveats: the 35 hand pairs, and the AR pick needs the fresh list. Potion+AR (re-run after a host-memory failure): 30.3% (14.3) / 0 (0) / 0 -- the specialisation lifts potion's paraphrase recall by a third with no entity leak at either cutoff.


## 8. Group F — Structural and organizational hypotheses

**H-F1 — CubbyLLM's layout should mirror cubemind's intended structure, not its actual sprawl.** Both `cubby-lm` and `cubemind` carry known structural debt (recorded during the earlier architecture-mapping phase): `trunk`/`trunk_torch` duplication, a ~35GB tracked `sandbox/` directory in cubemind, a `cloned/` vendor directory, an `_archive/` that's still importable despite being gitignored elsewhere, triplicated routing/kernels/registry code, and a `model.py`/`models/`/`model` naming collision. "Mirror cubemind's structure" should mean the clean package layout the docs describe, not a copy of the accumulated cruft. **Validate** by writing CubbyLLM's target package layout as its own short spec before porting any code, explicitly checking it against each known sprawl pattern above.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): spec written — `PACKAGE_LAYOUT.md` (status: PROPOSAL).** Every anti-sprawl rule in it is mapped one-to-one to a named observed failure, including two new rules from this campaign: any number quoted in docs must link the `validation/logs/` file that produced it, and corpora are referenced by pinned manifest (H-G3's drift lesson). Remains a draft until the user signs off.

**H-F2 — Both existing Cubby↔CubeMind bridges are too narrow for "automatic specialization" and likely need to become first-class interfaces.** The `NoveltyToWorldBridge` (Hebbian novelty → `WorldManager.register_specialist`) is a one-way, duck-typed data bridge. The `CubeLangHead` → `cubelang_bridge.py` path is a subprocess call to a compiled `cubelang.exe` — a text/file bridge, not a tensor bridge, and `CubeLangHead` is self-labeled "SCAFFOLD." Status: this is a probable-but-unconfirmed scope item, not yet a validated need. **Validate** by first defining concretely what "automatic specialization" requires from the environment (what needs to flow from model to world-model and back, and how often) before committing to redesigning either bridge — it's possible a narrower fix suffices.

**Bridge addendum (2026-08-30): ASK crosses the wire.** The reasoning bridge (`cubbyllm/bridges/cubelang_client.py` ↔ cubelang `run-proto`) now carries the VM's third outcome: a program that grounds several candidates and `ask`s is reported as `suspended{question, candidates}` (JSON Values) instead of an error, and is resumed **by re-execution** (`RunRequest.answers`, one per ASK; `VM::resume` still rejects any answer that is not an offered candidate — the trunk selects, never supplies). cubelang `d969a76` also seeds the SPEC's `IAgent` (think/act/observe) as a checked interface and fixes the strict verifier, which had classified `ask` as "trace-only" although it executes — the reason no asking program could pass verify-before-execute. Live tests: `tests/bridges/test_cubelang_ask.py`. This is the mechanism the stand-in's VM-mediated chat (and the "which one did you mean?" grounding contract, `examples/ground_min.cube`) needs; the chat program itself is unbuilt.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): the *need* is now demonstrable rather than assumed, definition still open.** H0's validated mechanism implies the bridges must carry (a) a context/task embedding in both directions, (b) novelty events (exists today, one-way only), and (c) generated-parameter or specialist handles (exists nowhere). Neither current bridge carries (a) or (c). Concrete interface definition belongs after the H-C6 and backbone decisions. **Added 2026-08-02 — DisARM is the estimator this bridge would need.** If the trunk must *emit* discrete hypervectors for the VM to consume, and that path is to stay end-to-end trainable, something has to carry gradient through the discretization. H-B5 measured the naive route and it is poor: the hard-sign STE path trains at **19.8%**, which is what turned block_ops' "zero-gradient problem of bipolar sign()" from assertion into measurement. grilly ships **DisARM** (`grilly.nn.disarm.disarm_gradient`, antithetic coupling, ~7.5x lower variance than REINFORCE), which targets exactly that. **Verified 2026-08-02: `nn/disarm.py` is real.** But note `docs/advanced/hdc-ops.md` is stale — the `disarm-*.glsl` shaders it lists do not exist in grilly's `shaders/`, along with three other `hdc-*.glsl` files it names. So DisARM is a CPU/Python path today, not GPU-backed; do not plan around shader performance. Scope it carefully after H-B6: this is NOT for the binding head, which is parameter-free and needs no gradients, and NOT for training the trunk to be bindable, which H-B6 falsified. It applies only to a differentiable model->VM emission path, if the bridge turns out to require one.

## 9. Group G — Training data (`$CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA`)

Surveyed 2026-07-23, after the vocab/from-scratch correction above — this folder wasn't part of the original research corpus; the user pointed to it directly as material for testing and for building CubbyLLM's own training set. It's substantial: roughly 210GB across 20 top-level files and 16 subdirectories, almost entirely real (not synthetic) data — logged conversations, instruction data, Gutenberg/Wikipedia/news text, and an already-trained tokenizer history. One finding that came out of this survey is covered above instead of here, since it's substantively about the binding head: the `grilly.experimental.vsa.ops` discovery is H-B5. Everything else training-data-specific is below.

**H-G1 — Real tokenizer-scaling history should ground the H-C6 vocab-size decision, not replace it.** `tokenizer/` (15GB) holds three actually-trained, actually-validated fixed vocabularies, each with a real `report.json`: `grillcheese_spm20k_v3` (19,947, SentencePiece unigram), `grillcheese_spm32k_v2` (32,000, SentencePiece unigram — this is the exact tokenizer cubby-lm's frozen trunk uses, trained on a rebalanced corpus with French boosted to 27%, 15.16GB corpus), and `grillcheese_bbpe65k_v3` (65,536, byte-level BPE, the largest ever built here, trained on a 6.32GB subsample of a 115GB pool). No open-ended/dynamic tokenizer has ever been built in this pipeline — the only precedent anywhere in this stack for a very large *discrete* symbol space is the SVC subject space in `svc_targets/` (208,538 distinct subjects), a different subsystem entirely (VSA role-filler targets, not an LM tokenizer). Status: VERIFIED (real files, real stats, independently sampled this session). This doesn't answer H-C6, but it grounds it: "much larger than 32k" has a proven, low-risk next step one order of magnitude away (65,536, using tooling that already works), against the higher-risk, unprecedented open/dynamic path (H-C1, still SPECULATIVE in this project). **Validate** by costing out a straightforward next fixed-vocab step (e.g. 128k–256k BPE, same pipeline as the 65.5k build) before committing to the harder dynamic-embedding path — the fixed path has a working reference here; the dynamic path doesn't, in this folder or anywhere else surveyed.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): the next fixed-vocab step is COSTED and it's trivially cheap.** Byte-level BPE (the same HF `tokenizers` machinery the 65k build used) trained on a 200MB slice of real corpus takes ~9.9s at **both** 65,536 and 131,072 vocab — the **65k→131k cost ratio is 1.00×** (vocab size barely touches BPE training time). Extrapolated to the production-size ~6.3GB subsample: **~5 minutes single-machine CPU**. So the vocabulary-size lever was never the expensive part — the real cost/risk is choosing and pinning the corpus mix (H-G3; note the 65k build's own report shows NSFW-labeled sources contributed ~136k lines to *tokenizer training* itself, not just pretraining). `validation/exp_g1_bpe_cost.py`.

**H-G2 — `temporal/nyt_data/` removes the biggest practical barrier to building the Zero-Forgetting Stability Benchmark.** 294 files literally named `<year>_<month>.json`, spanning 1851–2024, each a real NYT Archive-API record set with explicit ISO `pub_date` timestamps, headlines, sections, and keywords — already bucketed by year and month. A smaller, coarser `historical/` subdirectory (382MB) has date-*range*-tagged historical events and conflicts with causal-link annotations, a secondary option. Status: VERIFIED the data exists and is dated as described (independently sampled this session); PLAUSIBLE, not yet proven, that it's sufficient for the benchmark's actual needs — coverage is sparse (294 files, not every month present), so density across whatever time range gets chosen needs checking. Note: the three `temporal_corpus*.txt.meta.json` files at the parent folder's top level describe a further-processed corpus (assembled from this data plus Gutenberg books via `book_subj_map.json`, up to 3.59GB in the v3 build) that **no longer exists on disk anywhere in this folder** — only the raw source material survives; whatever consumed those files will need the corpus regenerated, not just located. **Validate** by building the actual benchmark harness (still the missing piece — the data existing isn't the same as the test existing) against `nyt_data/`, sorted and sliced by `pub_date`.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): DONE — the dated harness now exists and ran** (`validation/exp_g2_nyt_forgetting.py`: 8 year-spread eras 1852→2024, 128 headlines each, keys = sign-bundled word hypervectors of real headlines, written chronologically through the same four rules as the synthetic benchmark). Findings: real headline keys are **3.1× more correlated** than random bipolar (mean |cos| 0.0547 vs 0.0176), and that correlation costs every candidate ~2–3× recall at N=1024 — the honest-regime penalty, now measured. **The synthetic ranking SURVIVES**: NLMS best overall (18%, clean chronological forgetting curve 0%→61% oldest→newest era), SDM still the only order-agnostic method (~9–12% flat across 150 years), flat-decay keeps only the newest era (24%), Hopfield totally collapsed (0%). Whichever ordering survived both regimes is the one to trust — and it's the same ordering. `validation/logs/exp_g2_nyt_forgetting.log`.

**H-G3 — `unified/` (120GB, the corpus this pipeline currently treats as its best pretraining mix) needs a deliberate cleanup pass before it becomes CubbyLLM's training set.** Specific, independently-confirmed issues: it mixes explicitly NSFW-labeled story sources (`bluuwhale_nsfwstory2`, `mickume_alt_nsfw`, ~3.1GB combined) into the general pool with no visible separation or filter; its composition has visibly drifted since its own tokenizer-training report was generated (files that report references are no longer present in the current listing) — a reproducibility gap; there's meaningful cross-directory duplication elsewhere in the corpus (`factual/` and `gbooks/` are both Gutenberg pulls with likely overlapping IDs; `jsonl/` and `knowledgetxt/` share a byte-identical 4.8GB `wikibooks_corpus.jsonl`; `kbtxt/` has confirmed duplicate files under different names); and at least one alternative build (`v3_3/`) has a broken 0-byte file where 500k QA records were expected (`stackexchange_qa.500k.jsonl`). Status: VERIFIED — every issue listed was independently sampled and confirmed this session, not inferred. This is exactly what the user meant by "create a better dataset": the raw material for a strong corpus is here, but assembling it as-is would inherit real, fixable problems. **Validate/apply directly:** before any real training run, decide explicitly whether NSFW-labeled sources are in scope at all and if so how they're tagged and gated rather than silently blended in; run an ID-overlap check between `factual/` and `gbooks/`; and pin/snapshot whatever mixture actually gets used, since `unified/`'s current drift makes past results hard to reproduce.

**Validation result (2026-07-23 campaign): the named ID-overlap check is DONE and the fear was mostly unfounded on this axis** — `factual/` (1,976 book txts, 0.90GB) vs `gbooks/` (3,612, 1.87GB) share only **9 Gutenberg IDs (0.5% of factual/, ~0.00GB)**; the two pulls are effectively disjoint (`validation/logs/exp_g3_overlap.log`, name+size comparison — no content hashing of the 120GB). The OTHER confirmed issues (byte-identical 4.8GB `wikibooks_corpus.jsonl` in two places, `kbtxt/` dups, NSFW blending, report drift, the 0-byte `stackexchange_qa.500k.jsonl`) stand as found by the survey and still need the cleanup/pinning pass before training.

**Validation result (2026-08-14): H-G3 CLOSED — the 2026-07-30 cache build already was the cleanup pass; verified against disk, with one stale pin fixed and one accepted overlap documented.** Every gate checked against the real artifacts (`$CUBBY_DATASETS\_pipeline\corpus_sources.json` + `$CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA\token_cache`): **(1) NSFW scope: excluded structurally** — the `unified` source is an explicit 11-file allowlist (C4-realnewslike, twteval, infini-news, orca-agentinstruct, nemotron-CC math/HQ/personas, factnet, ultradata-math ×3); `bluuwhale_nsfwstory2`/`mickume_alt_nsfw` are absent from the config, not merely filtered. **(2) Dedup gates: resolved** — wikibooks consumed as ONE cleaned copy (`wikibooks_clean.jsonl`, neither raw duplicate path); `v3_3/` used only for `fineweb_edu.2m.jsonl` (the 0-byte stackexchange file unreferenced); `gbooks/`+`factual/` deferred out of pretrain entirely; open_thoughts reserved for SFT. **(3) Reproducibility: all 13 stored `.sig` signatures byte-identical to a live recompute** — nothing has moved or changed since build. **(4) The pin was stale and is fixed**: the doc's `5e7f8b73…` predated the same-day quarantine re-tokenization; the live `WeightedCorpusPipeline.manifest_hash()` over the real config is **`e03c991e2728759d25c67d285943baf44042dbebb4cdc12d72a7b39e76d2835e`**, now re-pinned in `corpus_manifest.md`, whose §0 table also carried four pre-quarantine token counts (books/verified_facts/kbtxt/wikibooks, +239M total) — corrected to disk-measured values. **(5) The true cache size is 17,744,604,983 tokens (17.74B), not the "14.37B" this project's session memory carried** — every budget derived from 14.37e9 scales ×1.23 at one epoch (H100 ~101 hrs $203–304) and the Chinchilla-2B target is ~40B tokens ≈ 2.25 epochs (H100 ~228 hrs $457–685; B200 ~100 hrs $451–651; A100 ~724 hrs) — still well under the panel's estimate, which itself assumed only ~14B tokens. **(6) One accepted overlap documented, not hidden**: `wiki_full` (3.04B) remains in the cache despite the manifest's own "DROP — superseded" dedup note; the routed `wikipedia` (125M) overlaps it by ≤0.7% of the cache — accepted for this build, revisit only on a rebuild. **The pretraining gate is OPEN**: config-pinned, hash-verified, scope-explicit.

**H-G4 — `socratic_test_text.jsonl` is a ready-made general-capability regression check, not a forgetting test itself, and can be used immediately.** Confirmed to be the GSM8K test split (1,319 grade-school math word problems, chain-of-thought format, `#### <answer>`-terminated) sitting directly in the training-data folder despite being an eval set. Status: VERIFIED. Useful run alongside the Zero-Forgetting benchmark (H-A3/H-A4/H-G2) specifically to distinguish "forgot a learned fact" from "general reasoning capability regressed" — a narrow forgetting probe alone wouldn't catch the second failure mode. Nothing to validate here — this is the one finding in the whole survey that's already exactly the shape it needs to be.

**Also confirmed:** `labeling/svc_domain_labels.json` uses the identical 88-category flat domain taxonomy already found independently in AURA_GENESIS and Novel_GNN_Arch (H-C4's context) — a third independent sighting, reinforcing that this is a real, deliberately shared convention across this user's projects rather than coincidence. One hygiene issue worth fixing if this taxonomy gets reused: 16 categories are listed twice, once capitalized (e.g. `History`, index 38) and once lowercase (`history`, index 82), fragmenting what should be one category into two valid-but-separate codes.

**H-G5 — Synthetic domain-tagged Q&A distilled from the domain-sorted corpus is the SFT-phase instruction data, and its domain tags feed the router — a later phase, not a pretraining input. Added 2026-07-25.** Once the corpus is cleaned and domain-partitioned (the book pipeline → `$CUBBY_DATASETS\domains\<domain>\`, and the facts pipeline → `domains/verified_facts/<era>/<region>/<topic>/`, both built this session), a frontier model reached via OpenRouter can generate question/answer (and instruction/conversation) pairs *from* that content — distillation from a stronger model, not the model training on its own output. The CubbyLLM-specific reason to do it deliberately rather than grab a generic instruction set: **tag every generated pair with its source domain.** That both populates the functional domains the taxonomy already reserves (`QA`, `instruction`, `conversation`) *and* gives the θ=f(c) router (H-C4 / H0) exactly the per-domain instruction data that trains specialization — generic untagged Q&A would not. The tooling exists (Novel_GNN_Arch `capsules/openrouter_encoder.py`, `file_topics_openrouter2.csv`). Status: **SPECULATIVE** — nothing built or measured, and explicitly a *later-phase* item, for two sequencing reasons: (a) instruction-tuning data is consumed *after* a base model is pretrained on raw text, so this neither blocks nor is blocked by pretraining; (b) at TB scale (the facts corpus alone is headed there per H-G3 / the facts pass) exhaustively Q&A-ing every source is a large, real OpenRouter spend — worth doing once, on a *pinned* corpus (H-G3), not on a still-converting one. It augments SFT; it does not replace the raw domain text pretraining actually learns from. **Validate** by prototyping the generator on a small per-domain sample (~5 sources/domain): lock the output schema and a quality bar (answer grounded in the source passage; valid format; domain-appropriate), and measure per-domain generation cost — cheap, and it de-risks the bulk run without committing the spend. Defer bulk generation to the SFT phase. **Kill criterion:** if the generated pairs don't measurably beat the instruction data already in hand (`instruct_55k_clean.jsonl` etc.) at SFT or at improving domain routing, or if per-domain cost at corpus scale is prohibitive relative to that gain, don't do the bulk spend — fall back to existing instruction sets plus targeted generation only for under-covered domains.

**H-G6 — `AutomatedScientist/wikikg-trajectories` (MIT) is the real fact store AND the chain/tool-calling corpus: Wikipedia already in the template-fact shape the planner, the triple index and the VM consume. Added 2026-09-04 (owner: "gold, Wikipedia already formatted for the VM").** 365,923 (subject, relation, object) triples from 45,416 Wikipedia articles, 1.5M two-hop random-walk paths with directions, and 2M tool-calling trajectories whose two tools — `query_relations(subject, rel_type)` and `get_neighbors(entity, direction)` — are exactly `TripleIndex.hop` / `by_subject` / `by_object`, i.e. the store's own operations. Claim: verbalized as "O is the R of S" template facts it becomes the serve store (the live store today is the harvest sample's fact pool — no "capital of France"), the chain family regenerated on real entities (the harvest corpus is typo'd Wikidata: "Huamn"), and the tool-calling family grounded in the store. Kill criterion: fewer than 80% of triples verbalize into facts `parse_fact` reads back, or the index at full size costs more than the serve host can hold, or VM-verified chains over it fall below the harvest's precision.

**Validation result (2026-09-04 screen, `validation/exp_g4_wikikg_screen.py`, log `validation/logs/exp_g4_wikikg_screen.log`): the store half is VERIFIED, the question half needs two maps.** 359,385 template facts from 365,923 triples (dropped 3,967 self-loops/empties), `parse_fact` round-trip 100% on 20k; **`TripleIndex` over all of them builds in 16.5 s at 284 MB peak** — a cosine `FactStore` would need 14.7 GB of float32 rows, so the wiki world is lookup + a lexical fallback (the pure-python IDF overlap re-tokenizes the store per query: an inverted index is a prerequisite). Relations: **2,902 types**, not the card's 180+ — X_OF forms 27.7% of triples (direct), a 41-entry noun map 45.0%, a generic lowercase phrase 31.2% (HAS_PART 32k, IS_A 13.7k, CREATED_BY 7.4k…) → a noun map for the top ~100 relations first. Paths → grammar questions: **13.6%** of two-hop paths are direct without an inverse-relation map (17,272 of 25,194 sampled hops need one; every direct path parses). VM sample 300 (lookup walk, no search, real cubelang): 130 verified = the walk's end, **162 verified with a different valid end** — the graph is many-valued (TIMELINE_EVENT alone is 21% of triples), so a random-walk end is not a gold answer: chain questions come from functional hops (unique lookups) or are set-valued; 8 exhausted (the one-candidate walk's frontier case). No precision problem: every verified chain is two real triples. Build order in `TODO.md`.

**Validation result (2026-09-04, same day, `validation/exp_g4b_wiki_world.py`, log `validation/logs/exp_g4b_wiki_world.log`): the two maps built and the world SERVES.** `standin/data/wikikg.py`: a 163-entry relation map (noun, inverse noun, reading direction) covering 96.6% of triples, inverse readings for 56.3% → **552,297 template facts** (356,902 forward + 195,395 inverse), `TripleIndex` build 46 s / 613 MB peak; `FactStore`'s lexical fallback is now an inverted index (6 ms/query at this size, near-universal tokens skipped past 10k facts); `serve.py --wiki` / `serve_api.py --wiki` mount it, and an exact hop-0 hit in any world's index routes the question there. Chain questions from FUNCTIONAL hops: **12.5% of random-walk paths** (24,965 of 200k), **VM sample 300/300 verified to the one gold answer** (87 ms/question) — the chain family's real-entity corpus exists. The honest bound: **SimpleQA reachability 19.9% entity-known, 0.1% lookup-reachable** (3 of 4,326) — the graph is 45k articles; SimpleQA is the search-and-learn loop's number, not the store's. Status: store VERIFIED at serve scale; questions VERIFIED (VM); coverage BOUNDED.

## 9b. Group P — Prospection: branching futures, timescales, keyframe compression (added 2026-08-25)

Origin: a design conversation (2026-08-25) framing a "5th dimension" — the landscape of alternate futures and pasts indexed by the choices made along the 4D trajectory — as a capability target: counterfactual timelines ("what if Alexander the Great was never born?") and goal-directed choice selection by predicted short/medium/long-term outcomes. Operationally the object is path space with a measure over it, and an LM already contains it implicitly (every softmax is a one-step fan of branches); what it lacks is an explicit handle. The five hypotheses below are the parts that are *about this architecture* and cheaply falsifiable now. The parts that are not — a causal graph over history with a stated held-fixed set, multi-γ successor-feature heads, eligibility traces plus episodic write-back for delayed credit, goal vectors with generalized policy improvement, Dyna-style dreaming behind a grounding filter, provenance-tagging of imagined episodes in the store — are a roadmap, not claims, and are deliberately NOT entered here until each has a cheap experiment. Scripts: `validation/prospection/` (its README has the run matrix and the checkpoint env vars); `validation/prospection/test_prospection.py` runs all five quick. Toy-scale numbers are d=32, L=2, CPU — mechanism demonstrations; the checkpoint arms ran the same day on `$CUBBY_DRIVE\cubbyllm\ab_hybrid.pt` (d=512/L=8, the H-D4 winner) and `hd5_mem2.pt`, and where the two disagree the checkpoint is the one that counts.

**H-P1 — The decode state is a forkable world snapshot: branching from it is exact, O(1) in context length, and isolated.** Source: `HybridBackbone.step` / `MinGRUBackbone.step` carry fixed-size state (the H-D4 bounded-decode guard), and `exp_needle_recall.py` already branches candidates from one prefix state. Validate: k branches from one cloned state vs k independent full passes; state floats vs prefix length; origin/store isolation. Kill: any fp drift, any growth with prefix, any leak. **Validation result (2026-08-25): VERIFIED at toy scale** — max |forked − independent| = 0.00e+00 on mingru, hybrid and hybrid+episodic-memory; state floats 96 / 577 / 1345 constant across prefixes 8/32/64; no origin mutation, no store leak; 96 vs 240 `step()`s for 4 branches (`validation/logs/exp_p1_fork_equivalence.log`). **Checkpoint arm (same day): VERIFIED at scale** on `ab_hybrid.pt` (d=512/L=8, the H-D4 winner) and `hd5_mem2.pt` (+ episodic memory every 2nd layer): exact at 0.00e+00 on both; state bounded past the window at 1,575,427 floats (≈6.3 MB — 1,572,864 of them the three attention KV caches, 2,560 the five recurrent layers) and 2,755,075 with the memory FIFOs; store isolation holds at scale (`validation/logs/exp_p1_fork_equivalence_ab_hybrid.log`, `_hd5_mem2.log`). **Honest cost accounting:** the hybrid's fork/keyframe cost is the attention window, not the recurrence — a snapshot re-enterable without the preceding 512 tokens is ~6 MB, not ~10 KB; with tokens stored, a recurrent-only keyframe at t−window plus a 512-step recompute is exact and small. This is the structural argument for building counterfactual machinery on this backbone rather than on a KV cache that grows with context, and it is the primitive H-P4/H-P5 rely on.

**H-P2 — Choice points are sparse and the model's own next-token entropy localizes them, so "open branches only at high-entropy steps" is a budget rule with a real signal behind it (vicarious trial and error happens only at decision points).** Source: the design conversation; Redish (2016) for the biological shape. Validate: on the branch grammar (exactly one free choice per 8-token segment, everything else deterministic) AUROC of entropy vs the labeled choice positions on the parallel and the decode path; flagged fraction vs the true 1/8. Kill: AUROC well under 0.9 or the fraction far off. **Validation result (2026-08-25): VERIFIED at toy scale** — loss 0.198 nats/token vs the grammar floor 0.173; entropy 1.375 (≈ ln 4) at choice points vs 0.002 elsewhere; AUROC 1.000 on both paths; flagged fraction 0.125 (`validation/logs/exp_p2_choice_points.log`). **Checkpoint arm (same day): the toy result does NOT transfer as-is.** On `ab_hybrid.pt` over 4,095 positions of real Wikipedia text (80 articles across the 20 domain files next to the checkpoints, `grillcheese_bbpe128k.json`): entropy median 4.32 nats, p90 6.14; fraction above 1 / 2 / 3 nats = **0.914 / 0.830 / 0.701** (`validation/logs/exp_p2_choice_points_ab_hybrid.log`). At this scale choice points are not sparse — an absolute threshold would open branches at 70–90% of steps. Status of the *budget rule*: **PLAUSIBLE, needs reformulation** — a relative criterion (entropy above the model's running median, or the top decile within a window) or a stronger model; the detector claim (entropy localizes the choices that exist) stands, the sparsity claim is a property of the model's confidence, not of text.

**H-P3 — The MinGRU retention gate is uniformly short-memory at init, cannot represent time constants above ~1000 steps, and only lengthens as far as the data forces it; a chrono-style init spreads the spectrum over decades without touching the scan or the decode path.** Source: `mingru.py` (`a = 0.001 + 0.998·σ(proj_d x)`, bias init 1.0); Tallec & Ollivier 2018 (chrono init); Murray et al. 2014 (cortical hierarchy of intrinsic timescales). Validate: per-unit τ = −1/ln a from the geometric-mean retention over real inputs at init / after toy training / after chrono init; pin the formula against `_MinGRUMixer.step`; delayed copy default-vs-chrono at lags 8/32/128. Kill: any of the three substrate facts wrong. **Validation result (2026-08-25): VERIFIED at toy scale** — init τ p50 ≈ 2.8 steps for every unit; **τ ceiling 999.5 steps** (a ≤ 0.999 by construction); toy training moved no unit past τ ≈ 4 (its longest dependency is 3 steps — gates lengthen only as far as the data demands); chrono init τ p10/p50/p90 ≈ 2.4 / 15 / 260 with 22% of units slower than 100 steps; delayed copy (300 steps, informational): default init sits at chance beyond lag 8 (2.71 vs ln 16 = 2.77) while chrono reaches 2.52 at lag 32 and 2.60 at lag 128 (`validation/logs/exp_p3_gate_spectrum.log`). **Checkpoint arm (same day) — the load-bearing finding of the group:** on `ab_hybrid.pt` over 2,048 real tokens the five recurrent layers have median τ = 0.5 / 0.7 / 1.1 / 1.5 / 1.8 steps (layers 1/2/4/5/7) — **shorter than init (2.8)**; 0.5% of recurrent units have τ ≥ 8, none ≥ 100, slowest single unit 111 against a cap of 999; conditional latching (instantaneous τ ≥ 100) does not hide long memory either — 1.2% of units latch on ≥10% of steps, the p90 unit on 0.4% (`validation/logs/exp_p3_gate_spectrum_ab_hybrid.log`). **After ~1B tokens the MinGRU layers of the hybrid are 1–2-step mixers; every long-range dependency lives in the three windowed-attention layers.** That is consistent with H-D4 (pure MinGRU ~32% recall vs ~98% for the hybrid inside its window) and it reframes the whole timescales thread: there is no slow subspace in the trained substrate to hang a multi-timescale scheme on — long time constants have to be *made* (chrono init, an explicit long-horizon objective, or an LRU-style rotation-block recurrence), or the timescale machinery lives in attention and the episodic store, where the H-D5 memory rung already is. **Decision pending, not made:** whether the 2B run uses chrono init on its recurrent layers. It is free and decode-neutral, but the long-lag gain is small at d=32 and unmeasured at scale — one pilot arm, not a default, and the checkpoint spectrum above is the arm's baseline. The ceiling is a constraint to carry into any "learn across timescales" design: anything slower than ~1000 steps has to live in the episodic store or the attention window, not in a gate.

**H-P4 — Keyframe compression of the state trajectory should be recompute-from-keyframe (exact, cheap by H-P1), not interpolation; interpolation earns its place only on slow units, by the sampling theorem.** Source: the AURA note `$CUBBY_DATA_ROOT/AURA_MIND\papers\temporal_memory_interpolation.md` (memory compression as keyframes + regenerated in-betweens via Hilbert / Hamiltonian / Fourier interpolation); Delétang et al. 2023 for the wider prediction-is-compression principle. Validate: linear-interpolation error vs hold-last per gap; per-unit error vs τ; recompute error; bytes/step; numerical pins on the note's operators. Kill: interpolation not under hold-last at gap 4; recompute not exact. **Validation result (2026-08-25): VERIFIED at toy scale, and the note's operators are FALSIFIED as written** — with two keyframes and no time axis, the Hilbert and Fourier strategies equal plain linear interpolation to 4e-16 (Re and F are linear maps), and the rank-one Hamiltonian flow conserves the analytic-signal norm (drift 4e-10) and misses keyframe 1 by 0.715 relative — it rotates one component's phase, it does not travel; on the toy (τ ≈ 3) linear interpolation rel-RMS 0.59 / 0.83 / 1.16 at gaps 2 / 4 / 8 vs hold-last 0.80 / 1.03 / 1.26 — barely better than doing nothing, worse than the unit mean from gap 8; Spearman(log τ, error) = −0.38, so slow units do interpolate better and the multi-resolution rule has signal; recompute exact at 0.0e+00; 256 B/step of states vs 32.5 B/step for keyframes + tokens at gap 8 (`validation/logs/exp_p4_keyframe_interp.log`). **Checkpoint arm (same day): VERIFIED at scale, same verdict, stronger** — on `ab_hybrid.pt` over a 1,024-token real trajectory (5 recurrent layers × 512): linear interpolation rel-RMS 0.74 / 0.87 / 1.00 / 1.10 / 1.17 at gaps 2 / 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 vs hold-last 0.92 / 1.10 / 1.21 / 1.30 / 1.34 — worse than the unit mean from gap 8; Spearman(log τ, per-unit error) = **−0.88**; recompute exact at 0.0e+00; recurrent state 10,240 B/step vs 1,282 B/step for keyframes + tokens at gap 8, with the attention KV cache on top per H-P1 (`validation/logs/exp_p4_keyframe_interp_ab_hybrid.log`). Interpolation-based compression is dead on this model. **Design consequence for the store:** keep tokens plus sparse keyframes and recompute — reconstructed states are generated, so if they are ever written back they carry the same provenance tag an imagined episode must. If a phase-aware slow subspace is wanted, the rigorous form is a complex-diagonal (rotation-block) recurrence à la LRU (Orvieto et al. 2023), not interpolation between two real vectors — SPECULATIVE, unbuilt, no experiment yet.

**H-P5 — Counterfactual re-entry works mechanically on a world the model fully knows: snapshot at a choice point, substitute the choice, re-run, and the continuation switches to the ground-truth alternative while overdetermined outcomes stay put.** Source: Pearl's ladder (abduction → intervention → prediction) with the recurrent state as the abduced latent; the design conversation's fragile-vs-overdetermined distinction. Validate: on the branch grammar, teacher-forced counterfactual accuracy vs template_j; agreement on shared-prefix positions; flip on the first divergent position; next-choice washout KL; state-distance profile. Kill: counterfactual at chance, overdetermined predictions changing, no washout. **Validation result (2026-08-25): VERIFIED at toy scale** — factual control 1.000; counterfactual 1.000 (chance 0.125); overdetermined unchanged 1.000; fragile flipped / correct 1.000 / 1.000; washout KL 0.050 nats at the next SEP while |h_f − h_cf| is still 4.6 — washout is a readout property, not a state one, so an intervention's residue persists in state past the boundary and its horizon is a number to measure, never assumed zero (`validation/logs/exp_p5_counterfactual_probe.log`). **What this does NOT show:** rung three on a world the model does not fully know — history — where the dependency structure has to come from the DBpedia/Wikidata graph and the held-fixed set has to be stated explicitly ("never born" is entangled with Philip's court before 336 BC; "dies at the Granicus in 334" is clean). That is a bridge/graph item in H-F2's territory, not a backbone item, and no experiment exists for it yet.

**H-P6 — A multi-horizon (successor-feature) prediction head on the trunk is free at matched tokens and makes the medium-term future (8–500 tokens) linearly readable from h_t — without any change to the backbone. Added 2026-08-25.** Source: the design conversation's short/medium/long-horizon mapping plus the H-P3 checkpoint finding that the trained recurrence carries no long time constants, so the medium horizon must live in the trunk representation and the attention window, not in a gate; Dayan 1993 / Barreto et al. 2017 (successor features: predict discounted future feature occupancy, then re-target goals by re-weighting features). Construction: per horizon γ ∈ {0, 0.875, 0.98, 0.998} (mean horizons 1 / 8 / 50 / 500 tokens) one linear map h_t → the discounted average of the future tokens' centered unit embeddings, targets computed inside the training window by the package's own log-domain scan run time-reversed, targets DETACHED (the H-B6 lesson: an aux loss that can reach its own target Goodharts), loss 1−cos, weight 0.1 next to next-token CE. **Validate** (`validation/prospection/exp_p6_multihorizon_pilot.py`, `notebooks/multihorizon_pilot.ipynb`, ~15 min/arm on an A100 at d=512/L=8/2000 steps): two matched arms, then held-out CE; per-horizon cosine skill vs the trivial mean-direction predictor; a linear probe fitted post hoc on frozen features of BOTH arms (how much future the trunk carries anyway vs how much the objective adds); the H-P3 gate spectrum of both arms (does an explicit long-horizon objective lengthen any recurrent time constant?); the representation-health and copy-floor gates. **Kill:** held-out CE worse than baseline beyond run-to-run noise (~0.01 nats at 65M tokens); or skill only at horizon 1 — then the trunk cannot express the medium-term future at this scale and the multi-γ design is dead. Status: ~~SPECULATIVE / UNRUN~~ **RUN 2026-08-26 on an A100-80G (`notebooks/multihorizon_pilot.ipynb`, d=512/L=8 hybrid 1:3, B=32/S=1024, 2000 steps = 65.5M tokens per arm, 16.4 / 16.6 min, 62.6 GB peak; the completed head arm ran at weight 0.03 — a 0.1 attempt was stopped at ~500 steps, where its head cosines matched 0.03's within 0.01). Logs: `validation/logs/exp_p6_{baseline,multihorizon}.{json,log}`.** Three findings:
(1) **"Free" — VERIFIED.** Held-out CE 5.0025 (baseline) vs 5.0044 (+head): Δ = +0.0019 nats, +0.003 bits/token, inside eval noise; the train curves match to three decimals once the aux term is subtracted (step 2000: 4.864 vs 4.948 − 0.081 = 4.867). Health gates identical in kind: retrieval 95.3% vs 92.2% (both fluctuated 90.6–93.8% during training), ff 0.415 vs 0.418, copy-freq 12% in both (the copying circuit emerged by step 500 in both arms).
(2) **"Medium-term future linearly readable" — VERIFIED, but the head is not what makes it so.** With centered unit-embedding targets the trivial mean-direction predictor scores 0.004 / 0.014 / 0.031 / 0.079 at horizons 1 / 8 / 50 / 500. A linear probe fitted post hoc on the frozen features of the *baseline* arm scores **0.188 / 0.232 / 0.331 / 0.414** — the trunk already carries the 50- and 500-token future without ever being asked to; the probe on the head arm scores 0.191 / 0.240 / 0.348 / 0.437 (+0.003 / +0.008 / +0.017 / +0.023) and the trained head itself 0.194 / 0.237 / 0.340 / 0.426. Longer horizons are *easier* (averaging over more future tokens), the next token is the hardest. So the successor-feature quantity the design wants is a **post-hoc readout on the trunk, not a pretraining objective**: the explicit loss buys ~0.02 cosine at 500 tokens over a probe that costs nothing and risks nothing.
(3) **"An explicit long-horizon objective lengthens the recurrence" — FALSIFIED at this scale/weight.** The gate spectra of the two arms are identical to two decimals: recurrent-layer τ p50 = 1.18 / 1.40 / 1.86 / 2.01 / 2.09 (layers 1/2/4/5/7) in both, no unit ≥ 8 steps in either. The horizon information lives in the attention layers and the trunk's composition, exactly as H-P3's checkpoint arm predicted. Bonus trajectory point from the same run: τ p50 ≈ 2.0 flat at step 500 (`_mh_baseline_step500` logs), 1.2–2.1 at step 2000, 0.5–1.8 at ~1B tokens (`ab_hybrid`) — the recurrence shortens monotonically with training from its init of 2.8.
**Decision:** the head does NOT go into the 2B runbook, not even as an arm — there is nothing for it to buy that a 300-step linear probe on the finished trunk doesn't already deliver. The multi-γ successor readouts become a post-training component of the prospection design (fit on frozen features, like the FastWord table is model-free), which is a strictly cheaper and safer place for them. The remaining open route to long *recurrent* time constants is chrono init (H-P3's pending pilot); the objective route is closed. **Caveat on the checkpoint files, not the numbers:** the `mh_baseline.pt` that reached Drive is the step-500 periodic save (Drive sync race across five 1.8 GB writes; `mh_multihorizon.pt` is step 2000), so the optional P2/P3 cell measured a step-500 baseline — logged as `*_mh_baseline_step500.log` and valid as a step-500 point only; every number above comes from the in-script evaluation of the in-memory step-2000 models in the JSON. The script now saves once, staged locally, and verifies the step.

**H-P7 — Chrono init on the recurrent gates survives training on the hybrid and gives the recurrence a usable memory beyond the attention window, at no cost in held-out CE. Added 2026-08-26 — the last open route to long *recurrent* time constants.** Source: Tallec & Ollivier 2018 ("Can recurrent neural networks warp time?" — gate biases set so the time constants are log-uniform over the range the task needs); H-P3's measured trajectory (τ shortens 2.8 → 2.0 → 1.2–2.1 → 0.5–1.8 from init through ~1B tokens, cap ~1000) and H-P6's closure of the objective route (a 500-token prediction loss moved no time constant). The specific doubt this tests: whether gradient descent keeps a long-τ spectrum when windowed attention is available to do the long-range work more cheaply — the same pressure that eroded the default init. Construction: `_common.chrono_init_` on every recurrent layer's `proj_d` bias, τ log-uniform in [1, 500] (below the gate's 999 ceiling), bias-only (weight scale 1.0 — the paper's form; P3's CPU measurement used 0.1 only to make the init spectrum legible). **Validate** (`validation/prospection/exp_p7_chrono_pilot.py` — a thin wrapper over the P6 pilot runner — and `notebooks/chrono_pilot.ipynb`; ~16 min/arm on an A100 at d=512/L=8/2000 steps plus needle eval): two matched arms, CB_CHRONO=0/1, then in this order (1) held-out CE at matched tokens; (2) **survival** — the gate spectrum at init vs after training, per layer (the runner now records both); (3) **beyond-window memory** — the impulse response of the stacked recurrent state to one substituted token, read at lags past the 512 window, where only the recurrence can carry it (baseline reads 0.000 there; pinned on a tiny hybrid in `test_prospection.py`: default init < 1e-3 at lag 128 past a 16-window, chrono > 10× that); (4) **capability** — `exp_needle_recall.py` on both checkpoints at 256/512/1024/4096, TRAINED vs its own shuffled control. **Kill:** CE worse beyond ~0.01 nats; or the trained spectrum collapsing onto the baseline's (p50 1–2, no unit ≥ 8) — then chrono erodes like the default init and the route is closed for good, leaving the episodic store as the only beyond-window mechanism (H-P3's conclusion becomes final). Survival with a non-zero beyond-window impulse response but no needle gain is a recorded fact, not a pass: the gate *can* hold a long time constant but has nothing useful to put in it at this scale. CPU smoke (2026-08-26): chrono arm init τ p50 16–23 with 20–30% of units ≥ 100, preserved after 30 steps; impulse response 0.115 at lag 64 past a 32-window vs 0.000 for the default init. **Status: RAN 2026-08-26 (Colab A100, two matched arms, d=512/L=8/window 512, 2000 steps × 32 × 1024 = 65.5M tokens/arm, ~15–16 min/arm; logs `validation/logs/exp_p7_{baseline,chrono}.{log,json}`, copied from Drive 2026-08-28; checkpoints `$CUBBY_DRIVE\cubbyllm\chrono_{baseline,arm}.pt`) — KILLED on clause (1), verdict recorded 2026-08-28.** (1) **Held-out CE 5.442 vs 4.998 nats — +0.444, 44× the 0.01-nat kill threshold**, at matched tokens with the LR already decayed to 3e-5; the gap shrinks monotonically (51 at step 1 — the paper-form bias-only init with weight scale 1.0 starts the recurrence saturated, train loss 63 vs 12 — 4.1 at step 100, 0.73 at 500, 0.53 at 1000, 0.44 at 2000) but is decelerating, not closing; bits/token 7.85 vs 7.21. (2) **Survival: partial — eroded ~4×, did NOT collapse onto the baseline**, so the second kill trigger did not fire: trained τ p50 4.4–9.4 per layer (init 18–28; baseline 1.2–2.2), units ≥ 8 steps 40–54% (init 62–70%; baseline 0), units ≥ 100 steps 5–10% (init 23–27%; baseline 0). (3) **Beyond-window impulse response: non-zero but ~1e-3** — at lags 768 / 1000 past the 512 window, 0.0015 / 0.0010 vs the baseline's 0.00016 / 0.00002 (9× / 45×), while *inside* the window the chrono arm's response is about half the baseline's (0.25 vs 0.53 at lag 1: slow gates damp the immediate signal). (4) The needle-recall capability read was not run by the Colab session (no log exists) and was deliberately not run afterwards: a CPU smoke on 2026-08-28 (`exp_needle_recall.py` on `chrono_baseline.pt`, 256 tokens, 2 trials, local `$CUBBY_TRAINING_DATA\token_cache`) confirmed the probe runs at ~5.5 s/trial at 256 tokens, which prices the full pre-registered read (4 lengths × 5 depths × 12 trials × 3 arms × 2 checkpoints) at ~13 CPU-hours — for a read that cannot inform anything: (1) already kills, and at this pilot's 65.5M-token budget the *baseline* is far below the ~1B tokens at which H-D4's hybrid first showed in-window needle recall (copy-frequency health 12% / 9% says neither arm copies from context yet), so both arms would read near chance regardless of the gates. The smoke's own 0/2 is n=2 noise, not a number. Side readings: frozen-trunk probe at 500 tokens 0.447 vs 0.416 (slightly better on the worse model — not comparable at a 0.44-nat CE gap); binding health retrieval 92.2% vs 95.3%, ff-cos 0.43 vs 0.41; copy-frequency 9% vs 12%. **Reading: this is the entry's pre-registered "recorded fact, not a pass" case at full strength — the gate *can* hold a long time constant through 65M tokens of training, and it has nothing useful to put in it at this scale, at a cost of 0.44 nats.** Decision: chrono is **not** a cycle-one arm at the 2B pilot; H-P3's conclusion becomes final — *beyond-window memory lives in the attention window and the episodic store, not in the recurrence*. If anyone re-opens this, the only arm worth running is the un-pre-registered one — weight scale 0.1 (P3's smoke form) and τ_max ≈ 64 to avoid the saturated start, at 4× tokens — and it would need to close the whole 0.44 before survival even matters; not queued.

## 9c. Group S — Senses: a sensory cortex learned by play (added 2026-10-10)

Origin: Nick, 2026-10-10 — Cubby (the BlockWorld agent) should navigate and manipulate from what he actually sees and feels, so the same neurons work in other applications and the outside world; blocks stand for external objects; the game is the safe sandbox where the trial and error happens; it must be a benchmark *and* a component of the full model / agentic harness. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-sensory-cortex-design.md` (forks approved: headless eyes, a sighted→blind ladder, a CubbyLLM plugin built on cubemind perception, CubeLang modding only after vision and in a sandbox). The benchmark rungs S0–S6 live in the private sandbox repo. All entries below are **SPECULATIVE / UNRUN**.

**H-S1 — Salience is physical: a retina built from cubemind's bio-vision channels plus a centre–surround contrast channel, read out as GIF spike rates, ranks emissive, bright, high-contrast and moving things above the background without any rule saying they matter.** Source: Nick ("a glowing block will activate the neurons more as it brings attention to it; same for bright vs dark colours or contrast"); `cubemind/perception/bio_vision.py` (Weber–Fechner luminance, opponent colour, motion); Itti & Koch 1998 (bottom-up salience). Validate: S0 — over rendered frames with known block ids, the salience rank of emissive / high-contrast / moving pixels vs a matched background sample. Kill: those classes not ranked above background (AUROC near 0.5), or the ranking explained by luminance alone with contrast and motion adding nothing. **First read (2026-10-10, `validation/logs/s0_salience_2026-10-10.json`; `standin/cortex/`, 12x16 tiles, 64x48 frames, 40 seeded scenes per class, each with four plain distractor blocks of the ground's kind; top-1 = the target's tile is the most salient):** raw responses (the default) — glow 0.75, bright colour 0.50, contrast 0.38, moving body 0.90, control (a fifth plain block) 0.31. Itti–Koch map normalisation is worse on every class (glow 0.59, contrast 0.03, motion 0.41) and is off. **Luminance alone** (the ablation the kill clause asks for) — glow 0.81, bright colour 0.53, contrast 0.56, moving 0.69, control 0.28: motion adds (0.90 vs 0.69), but for static targets the colour and centre–surround channels add nothing over luminance and the saturation channel costs the white target (0.38 vs 0.56) against colourful distractors. Reading: glow passes; the kill clause holds for the colour/contrast channels as weighted today. Next: weight them by learning (which tiles predicted something worth looking at), not by hand, and re-read. Status: **OPEN — partly killed (colour/contrast weighting).**

**H-S2 — Depth plus proprio-driven path integration rebuilds the map the sighted agent gets for free, well enough to plan on.** Source: cubemind's place / grid cells; the sandbox's ground-truth window. Validate: S1 per-frame occupancy precision/recall, S2 map IoU after N ticks blind, S3 goal-reach success and path length vs the sighted agent at matched ticks. Kill: S3 far below sighted while S1 occupancy is good — path integration, not vision, is the bottleneck, and the fix belongs there before anything else is built on it. **First read (2026-10-10, `validation/logs/s1_s2_where_2026-10-10.json`; `standin/cortex/where.py`; a sighted agent plays 2400 ticks in a new procedurally generated world, the cortex maps from 64x48 depth frames every 4 ticks):** pose given — S1 solid precision 0.96, free precision 1.00, solid recall 0.99; S2 map IoU 0.77. Pose integrated from an odometer with a 3% gain error per axis and per-tick noise — drift 0.91 blocks, S1 solid precision 0.37, recall 0.62, S2 IoU 0.36. A first re-localiser (shift the pose within ±0.3 to agree with the map at 1-block resolution) diverges (drift 50.6, IoU 0.17) and is off. Reading: vision is not the bottleneck (S1 high when the pose is right); path integration is, as the kill clause foresaw — under a block of drift halves the map. Next: sub-cell surface matching of depth points (ICP-style) and place recognition from what is seen, then re-read before anything blind is built on it. Second re-localiser (same day, `validation/logs/s1_s2_where_2026-10-10b.json`): point-to-plane matching (translation only, only along directions the surfaces constrain, capped per frame) against a 0.25-block surface map corrects a displaced frame in isolation (0.2 off -> within 0.01) but in motion it is still worse than the odometer alone (drift 1.27 vs 0.90, IoU 0.06 vs 0.39): matching a map built from the same drifting poses only re-confirms the drift, and the residual errors walk. It stays off. What would work is the textbook remedy: landmarks seen again (loop closure) and online calibration of the odometer's gain against frame-to-frame visual odometry. Status: **OPEN — the bottleneck located.** **S3 first read (2026-10-10, `validation/logs/s3_blind_2026-10-10.json`; 6 seeded fields with walls, a step, a hazard and four food blocks, 3000 ticks, starting hungry, moves known but no kind of block): blind Cubby — no window, no item list, his eyes and touch through this cortex into the map his mind plans on, position from his body — gets around and stays whole (cells stood on 119 vs sighted 212; harms 0.0, deaths 0.0) but finds food in 2 of 6 fields against the sighted 6 of 6 (first meal 2162 vs 769 ticks, capped). The gap is kinds, not ways: his eyes split one material into several kinds (each a new thing to try) and merge look-alikes, so foraging by trying kinds is slow. Next: stabler kinds (cell votes are in; prototype merging next), then re-read.** **S3 second read (same day): the gap was where he looked, not what he made of it.** Merging prototypes that drift together (`KindCodebook.consolidate`) changed nothing (`validation/logs/s3_blind_2026-10-10b.json`: still 2 of 6, first meal 2162). Tracing one field showed why: eating is done looking straight up (so the use goes nowhere), and no walk ever brought the eyes back down — after his first meal blind Cubby walked on staring at the sky, saw no new solid cell, and circled frontier cells that could never resolve. Sighted Cubby never noticed, because the block window does not care where he looks. With the eyes settling back to ~20° down on every move and sweeping while he waits (a gaze, not a new sense; `validation/logs/s3_blind_2026-10-10c.json`): blind Cubby eats in **5 of 6** fields (was 2), first meal 1311 ticks (sighted 769), meals 1.17 (sighted 2.67), cells stood on **215 vs the sighted 212**, kinds known 9.5, deaths 0, harms 0.33 (he found the hazard by touching it twice, where DNA told the sighted one). Reading: with eyes that look where he goes, the blind map is as good as the window for getting around; the remaining gap is slower food (trying kinds one by one), which is what a learned guess from looks to use is for. The lesson for the cortex: an embodied eye needs a gaze policy, and the next one should be the cortex's own fixation (salience) rather than a fixed rest angle.** **Re-run with events delivered (2026-10-15; the local driver dropped every event when the agent was driven one tick at a time (found under H-S10); postmortem off, so only the fix differs; `validation/logs/s3_blind_2026-10-15_events.json`, `validation/logs/s1_s2_where_2026-10-15_events.json`):** S3 unchanged in substance — blind Cubby eats in 5 of 6 fields, first meal 1316 ticks, meals 1.33, cells stood on 214.5 against the sighted 212, harms 0.17; the sighted run is identical. S1/S2 took a different walk (600 frames instead of 500), so its numbers move — pose given: map IoU 0.93 (was 0.77); pose integrated: 0.36 (0.39), drift 0.17 blocks (0.90); re-localised against its own map: 0.07 (0.06) — and the reading holds: integration is the weak tier and re-localisation as built does not help.

**H-S3 — A label-free kind codebook over fixated patches (spike→VSA block codes, prototype recall in the cortex's own memory) separates the world's materials better than colour statistics, and binding the seen code with the felt code makes "looks like this" predict "feels like this".** Source: the design's "what" stage; the sandbox knows each pixel's true kind. Validate: S1 cluster purity vs true kinds against a colour-histogram k-means baseline; after touch, held-out prediction of the tactile code from the visual code. Kill: purity not above the colour baseline; cross-modal prediction at chance. **First read (2026-10-10, `validation/logs/s1p_s6_what_2026-10-10.json`; `standin/cortex/what.py`: chromaticity + log brightness per surface pixel, online prototypes, no labels; a sighted agent plays 2000 ticks, pose given):** purity 0.97 and completeness 0.91 (16 kinds learned for 13 true kinds) against the colour-histogram baseline's 0.89 / 0.76 — shading no longer splits a material (chromaticity is shading-invariant), so the codebook beats colour statistics. Seen ⊗ felt: once a kind has been felt, sight predicts its feel almost exactly (error 0.002 per channel vs 0.037 for predicting the average feel), but for a kind touched for the first time, borrowing the feel of look-alikes is WORSE than the average (0.175): in this world looks are only weakly tied to feel across materials. **Re-run with events delivered (2026-10-15; the local driver dropped every event when the agent was driven one tick at a time (found under H-S10); postmortem off, so only the fix differs; `validation/logs/s1p_s6_what_2026-10-15_events.json`):** a different walk (206k pixels instead of 309k, 865 touches instead of 478); separation still passes — purity / completeness 0.94 / 0.83 against the colour baseline's 0.74 / 0.49 (a wider margin); a kind felt before is predicted almost exactly (0.0002 per channel against 0.032 for the average feel); a first touch by look-alikes is still worse than the average (0.088 against 0.032). Status: **OPEN — separation passes; look-alike transfer of feel fails as built** (next: a learned map from appearance features to feel instead of nearest-prototype borrowing, and more first touches per run).

**H-S4 — Touch is learnable as its own channel: slip, roughness, softness and steadiness are told apart from contact signals alone, and an unsteady feel predicts a fall before vision does.** Source: Nick (blocks that feel rough, slimy, slippery; blocks that fall or disappear soon after being touched should feel like moving sand); the world's grip and support physics. Validate: S6 — discrimination from touch only; lead time between an unsteady contact and the observed fall. Kill: discrimination at chance, or no lead time (the channel carries nothing proprio does not). **First read (same log):** over 478 floor contacts on 3 materials, a nearest-centroid read of the touch vector alone tells them apart 1.00 of the time — a check that the channel carries the difference, not yet a test (three materials, and the fall-prediction half is not measured). Status: **OPEN.**

**H-S5 — Learning progress beats raw prediction error as the curiosity signal: with a noisy distractor in view (a flickering light), an agent rewarded by the drop in its forward model's error keeps exploring the learnable world, while one rewarded by the error itself gets stuck on the noise.** Source: Nick ("let it play with blocks for a while, then it tries new things until it discovers what can be useful"); Schmidhuber 1991, Oudeyer et al. 2007 (learning progress); Burda et al. 2018 (the noisy-TV failure). Validate: matched blind runs with the two intrinsic rewards and a flickering emissive block placed in view; map coverage and affordances discovered at matched ticks.
