Kill: no difference, or prediction error winning. **First read (2026-10-10, `validation/logs/p1_noisy_tv_2026-10-10.json`; the forward model is `standin/cortex/forward.py`: per face of each cell seen (and per distance band, so haze is not mistaken for change) a running prediction of its look, a never-seen face predicted from its region's usual look; its error per cell is surprise, the older half of the cell's last 32 errors minus the newer half is learning progress, a region's value the mean over its cells. Blind Cubby, fed, in the six S3 fields with a 3 x 2 "TV" of fixture blocks in the middle whose look changes at random every tick; three arms differing only in what sends him back to a place he has seen — "stale" (how long ago he looked, the reference agent), "error" (surprise) and "progress" (learning progress), each ranking places on its own scale, the most interesting worth 20 steps; 3000 ticks):** the noise half **passes** — the error-driven Cubby spends 0.43 of his time at the TV and sends 0.74 of his look-again choices to it (0.50–0.84 in every field), the progress-driven one 0.085 of his time (less than the reference's 0.16: the TV is surprising and teaches nothing, so he leaves it alone) and 0.05 of his choices. The coverage half **fails as built**: cells stood on 155 (progress) vs 149 (error) vs 239 (stale), map cells known 3445 vs 3290 vs 4134, kinds known 9.0 vs 9.8 vs 9.7 — progress does not out-explore error, and both curiosity arms explore less than the reference, because looking again (about 42 choices a run, each a walk and a look around) takes time from the frontier in a small field where, once it is mapped, there is little left to learn but the noise. Reading: learning progress is the right signal to ignore a noisy TV (the pre-registered contrast in attention is large and holds in every field), but as a pull on behaviour it is not yet worth what it costs; by the kill clause on coverage this read is a **no-difference** between the two intrinsic rewards. Next (a new read, not a re-tune of this one): progress only when it is more than its own noise (a per-cell significance test instead of the raw drop), the pull scaled by how much is left unexplored, and a field where something learnable changes (crops growing, water spreading) so there is real progress to find. **Re-run with events delivered (2026-10-15; the local driver dropped every event when the agent was driven one tick at a time (found under H-S10); postmortem off, so only the fix differs; `validation/logs/p1_noisy_tv_2026-10-15_events.json`):** the same reading — the error-driven Cubby spends 0.37 of his time at the TV and sends 0.65 of his look-again choices to it, the progress-driven one 0.05 and 0.01, the reference 0.11; cells stood on 159 (progress) against 158 (error) and 257 (stale), map cells 3514 against 3421 and 4269. Status: **OPEN — attention contrast passes, exploration benefit not shown.**

**H-S6 — What the cortex learns in the sandbox transfers as machinery, not as appearance: on a new seed and recoloured looks (and on Unity frames) the retina, salience and where stages hold their S0–S3 numbers without retraining, while the kind codebook has to relearn — and relearns faster than from scratch.** Source: the design's transfer table (§6); the honest limit that rendered blocks are flat-coloured cubes. Validate: S5. Kill: S0–S3 collapsing under the shift (the stages overfit the palette), or the codebook relearning no faster than from scratch.

**H-S7 — Skills learned in the sandbox stay useful outside it when they are stored as programs over affordance roles and abstract intents: given only a sense adapter and a motor adapter for a new environment, an agent carrying the sandbox library reaches competence in fewer trials than a fresh agent with the same adapters, and a transferred skill that does not hold there is retired for that environment alone.** Source: Nick ("the complicated part is to make the learned skills something permanently useful outside the world"); the CubbyLLM rule that a new skill is a program, not a new model; the fortune-cookie testimony mechanism (Beta trust over told facts) reused as trust in transferred skills. Validate: sandbox-trained library vs fresh, same adapters, in at least two non-sandbox environments (Cubby-Man's maze; a GUI task), trials-to-criterion and verdict precision of transferred skills. Kill: no sample-efficiency gain in either, or transferred skills that keep firing after their verdicts fail (trust not doing its job).


**H-S8 — The brain learns as a whole: sensory cortices (eyes, skin, body, needs; later ears) that write percept tokens into the language trunk's residual stream, routed to per-sense experts and trained together with it on one objective (every part predicts what comes next, its own input and the others'), ground language in what is perceived better than the same cortices trained apart and glued to a frozen trunk, without costing the language the trunk has.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-15 ("our brain does not train one cortex at a time; it is the sum of all the senses / cortex information that drives learning"); design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-whole-brain-design.md`; multimodal competition (arXiv 1905.12681, 2203.12221), Mixture-of-Transformers (arXiv 2411.04996), uncertainty-weighted losses (arXiv 1705.07115); zero-exit admission from `cubbyllm/model/grow.py`. Validate: arms at equal compute — J (joint from step 0), S (cortices trained alone, then attached to a frozen trunk), T (text only) — on held-out episodes and seeds of the private sandbox: G1 grounded questions answered from percepts (J ≥ S + 5 points; T can only guess), G2 kinds recognised from fewer views and first-touch feel predicted better than the cortex alone (J better than S), G3 held-out text loss within +0.02 nats of T. Kill: J no better than S on both G1 and G2, or G3 failed — the cortices then stay separate and are joined the cheaper way (S). Status: **OPEN — designed, not built.**

**H-S9 — Event → action → consequence: with Cubby's own acts as tokens between what he senses and what follows, and counterfactual forks from the sandbox (the same state, the other acts tried), the whole brain of H-S8 learns what an act causes, not only what follows what.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-15 (the ARC loop, after a sketch from Gemini, modified: the action is Cubby's own act, not the stimulus); design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-whole-brain-design.md` §4.7; interventional vs observational data. Validate: arm J against J-shuffled (action tokens shuffled across ticks in training), held-out forks and seeds: G4 the forward head's error on the consequence of each tried act ≥ 20% below J-shuffled's; G5 the inverse head picks the act from (event, consequence) ≥ 2× chance and above J-shuffled. Kill: no gain over J-shuffled on G4. Status: **OPEN — designed, not built.**

**H-S10 — Critical thinking in two modes: a postmortem after a surprise (locate the wrong channel, blame the feature the prediction leaned on by counterfactual re-entry, correct a fast exception memory and the replay priority, widen the spread instead when similar events disagree) and conditional branching before uncertain or irreversible acts (roll the forward model per act and per likely outcome from a fork of the state, score by value + learning progress − risk) make Cubby correct a mistake on its second occurrence and avoid harm, where habit and plain replay do not.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-15 ("if it got its prediction wrong it would adjust it for future similar events (in postmortem mode) and / or evaluate all the possibilities in branches (when in conditional mode)"); design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-whole-brain-design.md` §4.8; H-P1/H-P2/H-P5 (forkable state, branching where uncertain, counterfactual re-entry), H-E19 (blame at the first wrong step), prioritized replay (arXiv 1511.05952), uncertainty arbitration between habit and deliberation (Daw, Niv & Dayan 2005). Validate, first in Cubby's current mind, then in the brain: G6 second-encounter error on held-out surprise pairs ≥ 50% lower than replay alone at equal compute, and on pure-noise events the spread widens instead of the prediction flipping (calibration no worse); G7 at risky decisions, harms plus deaths ≥ 30% lower than acting on the best prediction, within the same decision-time budget, with the chosen branch's predicted value correlated with what it delivered. Kill: no G6 gain over replay alone, or predictions flipped on noise; or no G7 gain over habit. **First read, G6 at the level of the agent's affordance memory (2026-10-15, `validation/logs/g6_postmortem_2026-10-15.json`; the prototype lives in the private sandbox agent's memory: a prediction off by 0.6 or more that came from blocks like this one is blamed on the property bits that set the block apart from the ones that misled it — any bit a block that went the usual way also had is cleared (re-entry from memory), bits it has are preferred to bits it lacks, and failing any single bit two bits together are tried — and each blamed bit becomes a lesson that every later block it bears on adds to (each block counted once, however often met) and that is consulted before likeness for a block never tried; a wrong prediction from experience of the very block is not blamed on a bit: the block now goes both ways, and after that neither way is a surprise. Protocol: 100 trials for each of six kinds of prediction, five decided by one bit and one by two bits together — a dozen blocks behaving the usual way, met twice each; a surprise block; then a never-met block sharing only the cause, and a never-met control block on the usual side):** second-encounter error **0.36 against 0.79 for plain replay (−55%; gate ≥ 50%: passes)** and 0.72 with the surprise replayed three times (−50%). Per one-bit rule 0.30–0.33 against 0.65–0.94, the cause blamed in every trial with about one bit blamed per surprise; the two-bit rule 0.54 against 0.68 (the right pair is among the blamed in 90% of trials, but credit is split over about six pairs). No spill: control-block error 0.16 against 0.15. Noise (one bit makes a block go either way at random; after a surprise on one block, six more): the next block is predicted at 0.55 (unsure, not flipped), the prediction ends at 0.51 (replay 0.19, which never learns the bit), and calibration is better (expected Brier 0.31 against 0.41) — but 14% of the predictions made during a run of the same outcome reach 0.8 or more, against 0% for replay, so the no-flip clause **fails** as set in the bench (flips at most replay's + 0.05). In the live agent (2500 ticks, one world) the postmortem fires six times, each with a blame its voice can say ("a sharp block never hurts to touch — it did; it is sharp"). Reading: one surprise now teaches the cause, not only the block, and the lesson stays on the blocks it bears on; the price is that a run of chance outcomes on a noisy bit looks like a rule for a while — the trade-off of learning from one example, not yet solved. Next: G7 (conditional branching before risky acts) in the agent, then both modes in the brain's heads (H-S8). **G7 in the agent (2026-10-15, `validation/logs/g7_conditional_2026-10-15.json`): habit judges every step by its best prediction (a floor holds and does not hurt when that is more likely than not); conditional weighs a step over its outcomes — its cost plus the chance of a hurt (from the floor, or from brushing a block beside it believed to hurt) times what a hurt is worth (15 steps) — and gives a floor it has never stood on no less risk than its own record with the unknown (how often a new floor hurt it the first time, Laplace-smoothed: the forward head's own predicted error). Field: walls, deep water, four food blocks and four burning floor patches that look like the ground around them, each a block never met; a hungry start, moves known, blocks not; 3000 ticks.** Harms plus deaths 5.29 → 1.19 per run over 48 seeds (**−78%, 95% CI 59–91%; gate ≥ 30%: passes**), fewer in 38 runs and more in 3 (sign test p ≈ 1e-8), the first 16 seeds and a 32-seed replication agreeing (−76%, −78%); meals 2.08 → 2.33 (difference CI −0.13 to +0.63: safety not bought with hunger), cells stood on 198 → 196, decision time unchanged (2.32 → 2.26 ms per tick: the same search with different step costs). Ablations on the first 16 seeds: weighing by likeness alone, without the record of the unknown, loses the gain (5.38 against 5.81, −8%); dropping the weight on what is beside a step keeps it (1.44 against 1.38) — the whole effect is treating a never-tried floor as risky as the unknown has turned out to be. The calibration clause **fails**: the risk given a never-tried floor does not tell the floors that hurt from those that do not (AUC 0.46; one base rate for every unknown) and it runs high (0.44 predicted against 0.17 delivered). Reading: G7's harm half passes by a wide margin from one ingredient — arbitrating by the agent's own track record with novelty (Daw, Niv & Dayan's uncertainty arbitration) — not from branching over likeness predictions; a chosen branch's predicted value is not yet informative. Harness fix found on the way: the local driver dropped every event when the agent was driven one tick at a time, as all per-tick benches did (fixed; the reads it touched are re-run under H-S2, H-S3 and H-S5). **Baselines and controls (2026-10-15, `validation/logs/g6_baselines_2026-10-15.json`, `validation/logs/g7_controls_2026-10-15.json`).** G6 against learners that see the same blocks as bit vectors: second-encounter error 0.36 for the postmortem against 0.55 for naive Bayes (the strongest; the postmortem 35% lower, paired-bootstrap 95% CI 32–39%), 0.73 for class-balanced logistic regression (51% lower, CI 49–53%) and 0.88–0.93 for plain logistic regression at any of three ridge strengths; on noise the learners that can generalise from one example flip at least as often as the postmortem (naive Bayes 0.20, balanced logistic regression 0.21, against 0.14) — the no-flip clause fails for every fast learner here, and the postmortem fails it least. G7 controls (16 seeds): **a correction to the reading above** — fearing every never-tried floor at a fixed 0.5 gives the same harms as fearing it at the agent's learned record (1.38 against 1.38), so the active ingredient is treating never-tried floors as uncertain, not learning how uncertain; within 3000 ticks the record stays near its prior. The price of a hurt matters, with a plateau: 5 steps 2.44, 15 steps 1.38, 45 steps 1.31 harms plus deaths (habit 5.81). In a control field whose new-looking floors do not hurt, caution costs little that can be measured: meals 2.63 against 2.75 (difference CI −0.50 to +0.25), cells stood on 197 against 198, but the cautious mind stands on a never-tried floor 35 times against 95 (it learns new floors more slowly). **A frontier LLM, and held-out worlds (2026-10-15, `validation/logs/g6_llm_2026-10-15.json`, `validation/logs/g7_heldout_2026-10-15.json`).** G6 with a frontier language model as the learner, told the same trials as text, features anonymised and renamed per trial: Gemini 3.8 Flash's second-encounter error is 0.45 — the postmortem's is 21% lower on the same 600 trials (paired CI 19–24%) and lower on every rule — its control error 0.05 (the postmortem's 0.16 is plain replay's likeness, 0.15), and on noise it flips as often as the other fast learners (0.21); GPT-5.5 at low reasoning effort, on 120 trials, 0.74. G7 on 48 held-out worlds (fresh seeds, the paper's own chemistry key, moves learned from scratch in a 2500-tick nursery instead of a saved mind): harms plus deaths 3.85 → 0.58 (−85%, CI 66–96%), fewer in 41 worlds and more in 2 (sign test p ≈ 2e-10), meals 1.67 → 1.92 (CI +0.08 to +0.44), decision time 2.71 → 2.59 ms per tick; in the control with nothing that hurts (16 held-out worlds) a small cost: meals 1.94 → 1.88 (CI −0.19 to 0.00), cells stood on 195 → 191, never-tried floors stood on 92 → 40. Status: **OPEN — G6 second-encounter passes and G7 harm passes (−78%); G6's noise clause and G7's calibration clause fail; both modes still to move into the brain's heads.**

**H-S11 — The advisor loop: given a goal G, past trials P and a history of choices H, the whole brain (H-S8 to H-S10) lists the options that really reach G with true pros and cons, picks the best under the user's criteria or asks the one question that settles the pick, because each option is a branch its forward model played out, P is its postmortem memory, and H reveals the user's criteria.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-15 ("I want to do G, I tried P, given that in the past I did H, what are my options? And which choice you think would be best for me?"); design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-whole-brain-design.md` §1 (the advisor loop). Validate: held-out sandbox scenarios (G, P, H), every option and claim run in a fork, against the trunk answering without branching and a general language model answering from the same description, at equal compute: A1 ≥ 90% of listed options reach G; A2 ≥ 80% of the distinct ways the forks find are listed; A3 ≥ 90% of stated pros and cons hold; A4 the best option under given criteria picked ≥ 80%, and without them a question asked when the top options swap on an unweighted criterion (≥ 80%) and not otherwise (≤ 10%); A5 a failed trial from P re-proposed without saying what changes in ≤ 5%, and the pick agrees with H's revealed preference ≥ 70%. Kill: no better than the no-branching trunk on A1 and A3, or no gain from P and H over answers given without them. **Benchmark built, baselines read (2026-10-15, `validation/logs/advisor_bench_2026-10-15.json`; the benchmark itself lives in the private sandbox): 72 held-out scenarios — three kinds of goal × three modes (criteria given; H only; neither), 24 per mode — every listed option played out in a fork, plans in a six-step language, the user's criteria weighing time, harms, blocks used up, staying dry and leaving the world as it was; three reference arms, each also run with P and H hidden:** *forks* (every way played out on the truth — the ceiling Cubby's branching aims at) passes every gate (A1 = A2 = A3 = 1.00; right pick 1.00 with criteria given; asks 1.00 when the options trade off, 0.00 when one dominates; no failed trial repeated; pick from H 0.96), so the gates are reachable — with H hidden its pick falls to 0.58 (it has to ask), the measured worth of H. *prior* (the same decision rule without forks: costs averaged over other worlds) is close on validity and truth (A1 0.99, A3 0.97) and loses mostly on the pick (0.96 with criteria given; asks 0.91 when it should, 0.08 when it should not), where it weighs a cost known from P against an estimated one; the facts the words leave out (whether the blocks in hand stack here, whether a block can be broken) are where it lists options that do not work. *LFM2.5 1.2B* (local, all layers on the GPU, JSON-constrained, the same words) fails every gate: A1 0.17 (47 of 274 listed options reach G), A3 0.79 over its 135 claims about options that work, right pick with criteria given 0.00, asks 0.09 when it should, pick from H 0.00; with P and H hidden A1 falls to 0.02 (5 of 254) — P teaches it the form of a plan more than its content (15 of its 47 working options copy a trial that worked). Reading: the benchmark separates the arms and every number the kill clause needs is measurable, but in these worlds branching on the truth beats a good no-fork prior by only 1–3 points on A1 and A3 — too little room for Cubby's arm to show it beats the trunk without branching. Before that arm runs, the worlds need more that only playing out shows (costs hidden from the words, plans that fail halfway, longer chains of steps). **Level 2 (2026-10-15, `validation/logs/advisor_bench_l2_2026-10-15.json`): the same worlds plus a fence between the player and the goal whose one gap mostly forces a detour (the median way gets 18% longer), and by one goal a pile of the building material to quarry (a third way: break, carry, build — none of your own blocks used up, more of the world changed).** *forks* still passes every gate (pick from H 0.88; 0.71 with H hidden). *prior* falls to A3 0.92 (0.90 blind) and asks 0.86 when it should (0.71 blind): the detours are what an estimate made without playing out gets wrong; A1 barely moves (0.985). *LFM2.5 1.2B*: A1 0.18 (46 of 259), A3 0.79, right pick with criteria given 0.04; blind A1 0.00 (0 of 245). Reading: level 2 widens the forks–prior gap on true claims from 3 to 8 points and on asking from 9 to 14, not on validity — these worlds still rarely make an option fail halfway. Status: **OPEN — benchmark built and baselines measured; Cubby's arm waits on the whole brain (H-S8 to H-S10).**
**H-S12 — Space at the input, provably: a place is a unit vector in a Hilbert space (fractional power encoding, `S(p) = IFFT(exp(iΘp/scale))`), bound into the token's embedding at the input and read back from the trunk's state through a gated route the trunk itself drives, so a village is a scene the model can be shown without a word of text and asked about with answers guaranteed by the algebra rather than hoped for from training.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-17 ("implement spatial understanding provably at the input level; to build a village geo spatial understanding is important; Hilbert spaces could be useful for the spatial features; it also needs to have a link route from the trunk so it knows when to use it"); Plate 1995 (fractional powers of unitary vectors), Komer & Eliasmith 2019 (spatial semantic pointers), Frady, Kleyko & Sommer 2021 (the kernel reading: `⟨S(p), S(q)⟩ = k(p − q)`), Skilling 2004 (the Hilbert curve); RoPE is the same encoding in one dimension, inside attention — `S(p)` is its 3-D sibling at the input, for every mixer. **What is a theorem and what is a capacity.** Theorems, each checked to float precision (`tests/spatial/test_ssp.py`, `test_hilbert.py`, `tests/model/test_spatial.py`): moving is binding, `S(p + d) = S(p) ⊛ S(d)`; the origin is the identity, so a token with no place is unchanged and every text golden is bit-identical with the link on (checked with `torch.equal`); similarity is a kernel of the displacement alone, 1 at 0, ~0.6 one `scale` apart, ~0 two apart; a box's vector is a closed form (a geometric series per axis, a 1,000-cell box costing one cell); a place reads back exactly on the lattice; on the Hilbert curve consecutive cells share a face and every `8^j` consecutive cells fill an aligned `2^j` cube — 512 tokens are an 8×8×8 block of the world where row-major order gives a 2×16×16 slab. Capacities, measured (`tests/spatial/test_scene.py`): a scene vector `V = Σ code(name) ⊛ S(cell)` at the trunk's width d = 1024, 16 things in a 32×32×4 box, reads back what-is-at-p and where-is-X at 1.00/1.00 and a box's contents at 1.00 (d = 512, 32 things: 0.95 / 0.66 — width buys capacity). **Mechanism, built (2026-10-17).** `cubbyllm/spatial/`: `ssp.py` (the encoding, numpy), `hilbert.py` (the curve, vectorised, any dimension), `scene.py` (`Scene`: exact geometry — where / at / adjacent / nearest / within / offset / path — and the vector reading of the same scene, plus `facts(entity)` in the loop's `OBJ is the REL of SUBJ` template: `position`, `<direction> neighbour`, `nearest <kind>`, `region`, derived on every call and never stored, so the walk and the VM verify them like any other fact), `route.py` (the host's route: the scene is the source when a relation word is the scene's or the seed is a thing in it). `cubbyllm/model/spatial.py` (`SpatialLink`): **bind** at the input, `e ⊛ S(p)`; **scene**, `V_t = Σ_{i ≤ t, placed} e_i ⊛ S(p_i)` — the running sum of the placed tokens' bound embeddings, causal like the context's mean, one accumulator per stream at decode, no codes but the model's own embeddings (so `V ⊛ S(p)^-1` is the embedding of what is at `p`, already in the trunk's vocabulary); **read** mid-trunk (layer L/2, through `HybridBackbone(read=)`, so the layers after it can decode what comes back), `g · o(V ⊛ unitary(q)^-1)` with `g = sigmoid(w·rms(h) + b)` the *whether* (bias −4 at start), `q = W rms(h)` made unitary the *what place* (the trunk's magnitudes cannot change the place it names), `o` zero at start (growth's rule: exact at step 0), zero read with nothing placed — the gate and the query are what trains, the lookup they route to is algebra. Wired flag-gated: `CubbyModel(spatial=)`, `forward(tokens, positions, placed)` / `step(token, state, position, placed)` with `state["scene"]`, `train_base.py` `CB_SPATIAL=1` (`meta["spatial"] = {scale, seed, at}`, absent at 0, every earlier checkpoint's meta unchanged), `move_model` carries it; **the data**: `validation/make_spatial_shard.py` — villages (8–32 things of six kinds, one or two named regions, a 24×24×3 box) listed along the Hilbert curve with every name token carrying its cell in a `.pos.i16` side file, then 6–12 questions in the loop's shapes answered from `Scene`'s exact reading (position, `<direction> neighbour`, nearest `<kind>`, `n <direction> of`, region, and `What is at here?` with `here` a placed token: the question itself is a place), documents padded to `--doc` tokens and listed in `_align.json` so `train_base.Corpus` never cuts a village (it reads the side file, starts every window on a document, and hands `forward_batch` a `Tokens` batch — ids plus places — that the link trains on; a corpus without places behaves exactly as before); `--mode text` writes the control arm's shard, the same villages with cells as words and nothing placed; `standin/ask.py` `AskLoop(scene=)` routes a spatial question to the scene's exact reading (the loop then fetches, gates, stores with provenance `scene`, walks and verifies: "What is the position of the east neighbour of well?" → `11 7 0`, verified, fetching only the hop that was missing; pinned in `test_scene.py`). The decode step binds, accumulates and reads what the forward does, and the scene is causal (`test_spatial.py`); the shard's answers are the scene's, exactly the name tokens and the asked `here` carry places, and the corpus/link path runs end to end with gradient at the gate (`tests/spatial/test_shard.py`). **BlockWorld's surface, read for this** (`cubby-blockworld`): the world is `VoxelGrid` cells `(x, y, z)` with z = 0 the floor and `FACES` the six neighbours — the scene's lattice and compass; `orders.py` gives villagers tasks `at` a cell (`order(world, vid, task, at=)`), which is where a placed plan lands; Cubby v0 already encodes cells compositionally for its own map (`agents/cubby_v0/vsa.py`, one operator Δ per move) and keeps its hut as placements relative to where he stood (`hut.py`) — the same algebra, so his map and the trunk's scene can share a space. **Gates (pre-registered):** (1) input — a base continued with `CB_SPATIAL=1` on the `spatial` shard (`make_spatial_shard.py --villages 20000`, ~10M tokens; `CB_MIX_JSON {"spatial": w}` beside the text) answers the held-out villages' questions at ≥ 90% where the same base continued on the `spatial_text` control (cells as words, no link) sits below 60%; (2) route — the gate `g` opens (> 0.5) on ≥ 90% of spatial questions and stays shut (< 0.1) on ≥ 95% of the held text, with the text held-out loss within 0.01 of the control's; (3) serving — through `AskLoop(scene=)`, a village of 32 things: every spatial question the loop's grammar parses verified by the VM with 0 wrong spoken, and a plan "place X n east of Y" executed through `orders.py` landing on the cell the scene computed; (4) the curve — the 450M's beyond-window recall (H-E20 gate 2) on scenes serialized along the curve at least its recall on the same scenes in row-major order. **Kill:** gate 1 below 75%, or gate 2's text loss worse by more than 0.02 (the link costs the trunk on text; keep the host side only). **Cost, stated:** the link has no grilly2 form yet (`export_base.py` refuses a `spatial` meta; serve through torch until ported); FFTs on DirectML are untested — the link trains on CUDA. Status: **OPEN — built and pinned (the encoding, the curve, the scene, the host route, the trunk's route mid-trunk, the shard and its control, the corpus path); nothing trained yet — gates 1–2 run on the next base continuation (`CB_SPATIAL=1`, both shards built); the Unity placement path (gate 3) is the next piece.** **First read at the 450M (2026-10-20, `notebooks/spatial_facts_450m.ipynb` run 1: `base450m_cont_stable_end` continued 2,808 steps = 0.37B tokens an arm on an RTX PRO 6000, the two loci-token shards at ~4.6% each; `link` = `CB_SPATIAL=1 CB_SPATIAL_QUERY=placed CB_ROUTE_LOSS=1`, the link added to the trained trunk with `o = 0`; `text` = the same villages with cells as words, at the link arm's step count; `validation/exp_hypertoken_recall.py`, every held-out question teacher-forced):** villages -- `link` **0.793** exact with the address on the question, **0.700** with the questions' places stripped (the trunk's own query, taught by the route loss, finds the address), against `text` **0.995**: **gate 1 fails** -- in-window, cells written as numbers are answered almost perfectly by attention, and the link is worse there at this budget (it is not killed by the letter: 0.793 is above 0.75; it is beaten). **Gate 2 passes** on the plain-text sources: held-out loss 2.2318 (`link`) against 2.2286 (`text`), +0.003, every source within 0.017 (`_mix`, which weights each arm's own loci-token shards in, reads 2.1662 / 2.1442); `instruct` and `program` read 0.10-0.13 lower on `link` because that arm still drew Drive's duplicate copies of them, held-out tails included (fixed since: the Corpus skips such copies). What it says: the places channel costs the trunk nothing on text and its route is learnable at scale, but where attention can see the words, the words win; the case for places is what attention cannot see -- beyond the window, or more things than a window holds -- which run 2 tests (H-K10). Throughput: 54.6k tok/s, 29.5% MFU (`link`, micro 16); run 2 at micro 32: 59.6k tok/s, 32.2%. Where a step goes (run 2's `link`, its trace re-read from the device kernels alone -- the first `profile_base.py` counted every kernel twice, once as itself and once in the op that launched it, and filed the attention kernels under matmul; fixed): GEMMs **42%** of device time, running at ~377 TFLOPS, **75% of the card's peak**; attention 15% (dense-masked memory-efficient kernels: no flex on this card, the full 1024 x 1024 computed for a 512 band); the MinGRU scan and its backward 13%; other elementwise 22%; the vocabulary's softmax and loss 3%, optimizer 2%, the link's FFTs 1%; the GPU 99% busy. MFU is GEMM speed x GEMM share: the GEMMs are fast, there are too few of them; the speed is in the 58% that is not GEMM. The 128k vocabulary is not 2.8% (as first written here) but ~15% of the step: its head is 29% of the GEMM FLOPs. **Run 3 on the G4 (`validation/speed_check.py`, under the profiler):** torch 57.1k tok/s, + the fused MinGRU kernel 63.7k (+11.5%; the scan 72 -> 18 ms a step), + fused MinGRU + flex attention at 99 KB tiles 71.8k (+25.7%; attention 84 -> 26 ms); the banded flash kernel failed there on its compile-time stride (fixed since, `e1185e6`). In the run: ~75.0k tok/s, **40.6% MFU** (run 2: 59.6k, 32.2%). **Gate 3, real villages (order 0034, 2026-10-23; counts only in `validation/logs/exp_s12_scene_files_2026-10-22.json`, the villages and their logs kept in the private BlockWorld repo):** 60 villages (20 seeds × 3 days) exported from BlockWorld's own village sim, 3,600 questions fixed before any village existed. **Host** (`AskLoop(scene=)` and the VM): **0 wrong spoken in 3,600**; it routes 4 of 7 shapes (position, one-step neighbour, nearest of a kind, region: every answer right, every "nothing" refused), none of "n steps away", "what is at x y z", "where is X". **Act:** 20 plans placed through the world's own order, **20 of 20 standing on the computed cell**, 22 of 22 unplaceable targets refused before any order. **Trunk** (run 5, link + copy, on the placed shapes it was trained on): things **0.006**, villager names **0.000**, "nothing" 0.972 -- **below the pre-registered kill line (0.75): the synthetic villages did not transfer.** The same villages with every thing renamed to a shard word read 0.581 (`diag_s12_names_2026-10-22.json`): mostly the words (the trunk opens an unseen village thing with "nothing", and the copy does not supply the first token), partly the places (0.58, not the shard's 0.95). So: the host's sense of place works on a real world and acts on it exactly; the trunk's does not yet, and the next step is a private shard of exported villages (order 0035).

## 9d. Group K — Knowledge, the map, the state: the pause of 2026-10-20 (added 2026-10-20)

**Why this group exists.** Nick, 2026-10-20: "lets pause and do a thorough research … we need to find something that will really challenge everyone … plan / test some new hypothesis that are or seem to be way ahead … lets not be afraid of complex problems"; then: "the status is only for inspiration we wont take their research we will create our own"; "also search github for inspiration … some hidden gems are in small repos sometimes"; and two questions that turned into hypotheses of their own: "décupler [tenfold] the information without trillion of tokens and still have the same power … 10 fold could be 1,000,000 fold too … 40B token to train the model to be par to a 1T token model" (H-K7, data efficiency) and "kv cache is one of the problem when its massive its energy consuming a lot" (H-K8); a repo to read, `google-research/swirl-dynamics` (H-K9); and "if tokens are the problem why not simply replace them by something more efficient? like hypertokens" (H-K10). **The rule for this group:** the field's results are inspiration, named once per entry so the difference is explicit; every hypothesis is ours, stated in the algebra we already have (H-S12's Hilbert space: a unit vector per place, binding as motion, similarity as a kernel), with a pre-registered gate and a kill, and the cheap half of each run the same day where it could be. **The thread through all ten:** one space for place (H-S12), fact (H-K1), memory (H-K8) and time (H-K4), so what, where and when compose by binding; the trunk learns *when* to read (a gate, a query), the algebra guarantees *what* comes back, and the VM verifies before anything is spoken. A conventional model has none of these as objects — it has weights that may or may not have memorized them.

**What the field is doing, read for inspiration only (2026-10-20).** Test-time memory in the trunk: Titans (a neural memory updated by its own surprise), Titan-Llama (memory modules on a frozen Llama-3.2-1B, ~1% of the parameters trained), Nested Learning / HOPE (layers as learners at different update frequencies, a continuum memory), Gated DeltaNet-2 (erase and write decoupled, channel-wise, 1.3B/100B tokens), Kimi Linear and the Qwen hybrids (3:1 linear:attention), "Language Models Need Sleep" (offline passes that consolidate evicted context into fast weights), HeLa-Mem (Hebbian associative memory for agents); representation: "Attention as Binding" (attention is approximate VSA; explicit binding would be exact), Haga's disentangled successor information (grid cells and word embeddings from one factorization), grid-cell path integration as a group representation (`ruiqigao/grid-cell-path`), the capacity analysis of vector-symbolic superpositions (the d/(2 ln M) law); knowledge: "Physics of Language Models 3.3" (2 bits per parameter, ~1,000 exposures to store a fact), Memory Layers at Scale (product-key memory, 128B memory parameters), Hrrformer (holographic attention everywhere — linear time, lost accuracy); the bench the field now scores on: ARC-AGI-3 (interactive, hidden rules, Oct 2026 leaderboard: all closed models, the best in the sixties); and the small repos: `swirl-dynamics` (DySLIM — regularize a learned dynamics to keep the true system's invariant measure, so long rollouts stay on the attractor; flow-map matching and MeanFlow — one-step jumps over a time interval with the semigroup consistency Φ_{s→u} = Φ_{t→u} ∘ Φ_{s→t}; "Evolve Smoothly" — latent dynamics in the parameter space of a small network, kept low-dimensional by a consistency term; debiasing by optimal transport / rectified flow between a cheap model's distribution and the expensive one's; WENO-NN — a learned weight inside a numerical scheme that keeps the scheme's guarantees). Links in the appendix row.

**H-K1 — The fact store as a map: entities are places, relations are displacements, composition is path integration, relation discovery is unbinding — in the same Hilbert space the trunk binds places into.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-20; H-S12's encoding (`cubbyllm/spatial/ssp.py`). Inspiration: rotation-based knowledge-graph embeddings (RotatE) score a triple by a rotation per relation; ours uses the spatial link's phases (one space for the village and the graph), inverses are free (φ → −φ, so a backward hop is the same operator), composition is exact algebra (S(e0) ⊛ D1 ⊛ D2, never touching the store), and every proposal is verified by the VM before the loop speaks it — the map proposes, the walk checks. **What it brings:** "how is o related to s" by unbinding (D = S(o) ⊛ S(s)^-1, ranked over the 2,902 relations) — a question a conventional model answers from memorized text if at all; two hops in one read; the store's capacity in bytes, not tokens (H-K7). **Mechanism, built:** `validation/exp_k1_map.py` — entity phases (E, F) learned, relation phases (R, F) learned, score `mean_f cos(θ_s + φ_r − θ_o)`, softmax over the true answer and 8,191 shared negatives in both directions (SparseAdam on the 375k-row table: a touch is a step of `lr` radians), against TransE at the same width (2F real dims) and budget; the wiki world (`standin/data/out/wikikg`: 374,975 entities, 2,902 relations, 365,923 triples, 5% held out, 2,000 two-hop paths whose both hops are training triples). **Gates (pre-registered):** (1) composition — two-hop Hits@10 by path integration ≥ 0.6 × one-hop Hits@10; (2) relation discovery — unbinding Hits@3 ≥ 0.5 over 2,902 relations; (3) one hop — MRR within 10% of TransE's at the same width and budget. **Kill:** gate 3 worse than TransE by more than 30%, or gate 1 below 0.3. **First read (2026-10-20; F = 256, 10 epochs, 8,191 shared negatives, 3,000 held-out triples, 2,000 two-hop paths; `validation/logs/exp_k1_map_{map,transe}_2026-10-20.json`):** the map — one hop MRR 0.383 / Hits@1 0.290 / Hits@10 0.542, relation discovery by unbinding MRR 0.180 / Hits@3 0.188, two-hop by path integration Hits@10 0.980; TransE at the same width and budget — one hop MRR 0.185 / Hits@1 0.091 / Hits@10 0.328, relation MRR 0.037 / Hits@3 0.041, two-hop Hits@10 0.956. Gate 1 passes (two-hop / one-hop Hits@10 = 1.81, the paths being over training hops); gate 3 passes at 2.1× TransE instead of within 10%; **gate 2 fails** at Hits@3 0.188 — 4.6× the baseline but far from 0.5, on a graph of mean degree 1.9 where most entities carry one or two facts and the displacement between a held-out pair is generalisation, not fit. Caveats, stated: the map's learning rate was probed at 2 epochs (0.05 > 0.2 > 0.5 > 1.0; SparseAdam's first touch steps ~3× lr on a row touched twice an epoch) and its relations start uniform on the circle (a relation is a displacement, distinct from the start — it cost one hop at 2 epochs and bought relation discovery); TransE's rate was probed the same way (0.01 of 0.003 / 0.01 / 0.03) and it memorised the training set (loss 0.13) — a stronger baseline would be regularised, so the 2× is a first read, not a claim. Status: **OPEN — gates 1 and 3 passed, gate 2 failed as pre-registered; next: the map's phases as the fact link's codes (H-K10 gate 4), and relation discovery re-read on the dense core of the graph (entities with ≥ 5 facts).**

**H-K2 — Surprise-gated episodic writing: the store keeps what the trunk did not predict, and erases what it later confirmed.** Source: Nick's "find something that will really challenge everyone" and H-E20's gate 2 (beyond-window recall at chance with a store that holds every aged token: 15.9% vs 16.8% shuffled). Inspiration: Titans (surprise drives the memory update), Gated DeltaNet-2 (erase and write as separate gates); ours is a *write mask on the row store we already have* — the token's own loss at training (its surprisal at serving) decides whether its (k, v) enters the store, the gate's later read error decides erasure, and the VM's verification is the confirmation signal on the host side. **What it brings:** a store of news — a needle is surprising by construction, filler is not; capacity scales with what was unexpected, not with length. **Advantage over conventional models:** a KV cache keeps everything and eviction heuristics score rows after the fact by attention mass; ours decides at write time by prediction error, which is the definition of information. **Mechanism (to build, flag-gated):** `CubbyMemory` with `CB_MEM_WRITE=surprise` — rows ranked by the token's loss within each window, the top `mem_write_k` kept, the rest never written; erase on a confirmed read (the read's output matched the next token's logit). **Gates:** (1) the needle probe (`exp_needle_forward.py`, 4,096, three depths): beyond-window recall ≥ 50% with a store of 256 rows chosen by surprise where the same 256 rows chosen by recency sit at chance (< 20%); (2) held-out text loss within 0.01 of the ungated store; (3) the store's row count at 4,096 ≤ 1/8 of the ungated one. **Kill:** surprise-chosen rows no better than recency-chosen by 10 points. Status: **OPEN — not built; runs on the grown model's second round.**

**H-K3 — Continual learning by construction: a frozen trunk, and a sleep pass that distills the day's VM-verified facts into the context adapter's bases, per context.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-20; H0 (the context channel), H-A7 (the learning gate). Inspiration: Nested Learning (levels at different update frequencies), "Language Models Need Sleep" (offline consolidation of evicted context); ours consolidates *only what the VM verified*, into the adapter the architecture already has, with the learning gate (H-A7) deciding whether the update is CE-neutral before it is kept, and the trunk's goldens unchanged by construction (the trunk is frozen; features off = bit-identical). **What it brings:** a model that is better tomorrow at what it checked today, for an adapter-sized update, with forgetting measured as a number rather than hoped away. **Advantage:** conventional models retrain, fine-tune with drift, or retrieve; ours learns facts by writing rows and consolidates them by sleeping, with the trunk's language untouched. **Gates:** (1) after one sleep pass over 1,000 verified facts, recall of those facts through the adapter alone (store removed) ≥ 80%; (2) text held-out loss within 0.01; (3) the previous day's 1,000 facts recalled ≥ 70% after the next day's pass (forgetting bounded). **Kill:** gate 1 below 50% or gate 3 below 40%. Status: **OPEN — not built; the sleep pass is `exp_if_follow.py`'s sibling (H-E20 gate 4).**

**H-K4 — A clock in the state: a one-dimensional fractional-power time code bound into memory keys and the scene, so before / after / when are displacement queries.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-20; H-S12 (the same encoding, dims = 1); Group P (keyframes need a coordinate). Inspiration: RoPE is this code inside attention only; ours is a readable coordinate the trunk, the host loop and the VM share, and a what–where–when triple is one bound vector `e ⊛ S(p) ⊛ T(t)`. **What it brings:** temporal questions by algebra — "what happened between t1 and t2" is a box kernel read (H-S12's closed form), "which came first" is a sign. **Gates:** (1) a synthetic diary (events with times): between-t1-and-t2 recall ≥ 90% through the box read; (2) in the trunk, with `T(t)` bound into the episodic keys, the needle probe's beyond-window recall at 4,096 at least the untimed store's, and "which came first" questions ≥ 80% where the untimed store sits at chance. **Kill:** gate 2's needle recall drops by more than 5 points (the clock costs the read). Status: **OPEN — the host side is a `Scene` with dims = 1 (cheap, next); the trunk side follows H-K2.**

**H-K5 — Interactive hidden-rule learning in BlockWorld: a private ARC-AGI-3-like bench where the rule is a chemistry the agent must act to discover, propose as a program, and verify with the VM.** Source: Nick ("something that will really challenge everyone"); ARC-AGI-3 (interactive, hidden rules; the field's current yardstick). Inspiration: the bench's shape; ours is in our own world (BlockWorld's `VoxelGrid`, chemistry, `orders.py`), scored by the VM, never by a model. **What it brings:** a measurable test of learning-by-doing — propose → act → verify — where verification is in the loop, not in the weights, and the transcript of actions is the evidence. **Advantage:** a frontier model given the same observations must guess the rule from text; the loop runs the experiment. **Gates:** (1) 20 hidden rules; the loop recovers ≥ 60% with ≤ 50 actions each; (2) a frontier model (when OpenRouter credit exists), given the same observation transcripts and the same action budget, scores ≤ the loop + 10 points; (3) every rule the loop states is VM-verified before it is spoken (0 wrong spoken). **Kill:** the loop below 30%. Status: **OPEN — not built; the rule generator is the first piece (`cubby-blockworld`, private).**

**H-K6 — Provable binding heads: an explicit bind / unbind head with unitary role vectors in place of some attention heads, so role-filler structure is a theorem where attention has a tendency.** Source: "Attention as Binding" (attention ≈ VSA, approximately); H-B6 (the trunk is already shaped to be bindable). Ours is the exact version: roles are unitary (`SpatialLink.unitary`), binding is the circular convolution the spatial link uses, unbinding is its conjugate, and a role-swap is a different vector by construction. **What it brings:** who-did-what-to-whom that cannot be confused by swapping the names; composition that survives nesting. **Gates:** (1) on a role-filler task with swapped roles, a bind head ≥ 95% where an attention head of the same width ≤ 80% at the same training budget; (2) text loss within 0.02 with 1 of 4 heads replaced. **Kill:** gate 2 fails. Status: **OPEN — not built.**

**H-K7 — Décupler: the same power from twenty-five times fewer training tokens (40B for what a 1T-token model knows and does), and on facts from a million times fewer, because a fact does not have to be learned from sightings and a textbook does not have to be scraped.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-20 ("décupler [tenfold] the information without trillion of tokens and still have the same power … 10 fold could be 1,000,000 fold too, the higher the better … per example we need 40B token to train the model to be par to a 1T token model"). Inspiration: the exposure law ("Physics of Language Models 3.3": ~2 bits per parameter, ~1,000 sightings to store a fact, wording diversity needed to extract it); the data-quality result (phi: a 1.3B model on ~7B tokens of textbook-quality data matching models trained on far more web); the data-constrained scaling law (Muennighoff et al. 2023: repeating data up to ~4 epochs costs almost nothing against fresh data); RETRO and kNN-LM, Memory Layers at Scale (retrieval as knowledge). Ours: the store is H-K1's map plus the exact store, algebraic and VM-verified, not a dense index; the textbooks are written by our own loop (the VM, the walks, the scene, paraphrased by rule — no external model in Cubby's loop, OpenRouter only to build datasets); and the trunk's part is a *route* (a gate and a query, as in H-S12), not the facts. **Where the factor comes from, honestly split.** (a) **Facts — unbounded.** Most of a trillion-token corpus is its long tail: names, numbers, dates, who-did-what, each seen many times so the weights keep it. **Measured on our own trunk** (`validation/exp_k7_exposure.py`, 2026-10-20: a 1.06M-parameter `train_base` trunk, 1,000 synthetic facts in a Markov language, one pass, 10 min on the CPU): exact recall 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.50 / 0.99 at 1 / 10 / 100 / 1,000 exposures with paraphrased wordings (first object token 0.00 / 0.01 / 0.74 / 1.00), 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.30 / 0.86 verbatim, language held-out loss 6.96 → 2.67 — **gate 1 passes**: the law holds here, and wording diversity helps extraction as in its paper. A fact in the store costs one write (the exact store recalls it at 1.00 by construction, the vector map at H-K1's Hits@1): a fact scraped a million times is written once. (b) **Language — bounded by quality, not by the store.** On the same data, 25× fewer tokens costs ~2.5× the reducible loss (the Chinchilla exponent, D^−0.28); what beats the law is what the tokens are. Ours write their own textbooks: verified facts in many wordings, verified reasoning traces (BlockWorld chemistry, wiki-world walks that the VM checked), the scene's questions with answers guaranteed by the algebra — repeated up to ~4 epochs under the learning gate (H-A7 decides whether an update is CE-neutral). (c) **Structure at the input — paid in algebra, not tokens.** A village bound as a scene is learned from zero text about space (H-S12 gate 1: ≥ 90% against < 60% for the text control at the same tokens); the same mechanism carries a fact link (H-K1's phases bound into entity tokens, read mid-trunk) and a clock (H-K4). **What it brings:** the fact power of a trillion-token model from a store the size of a disk, the language of one from textbooks the loop wrote, and facts that update without a gradient step. **Advantage over conventional models:** they buy every fact with ~1,000 sightings of web text and cannot change one without training again; ours writes it once, checks it with the VM, and spends its tokens on language. **Gates:** (1) ✓ the exposure curve (above); (2) tokens-to-recall — the trunk + store pair (the trunk routes, the store answers) ≥ 90% of held-out facts at one exposure where the trunk alone needs ≥ 100 (≥ 100× fewer fact tokens for the same recall); (3) the corpus accounting — the share of fact tokens in our cache (decode a sample, count entity / number / date tokens a store could hold), reported either way, ≥ 30% expected; (4) **the 40B-vs-1T test, scaled down** — two trunks of the same size (20M-class, a Colab job): one on the full text budget, one on 1/25 of it plus the store, the loop's textbooks and the input channels; the pair ≥ the full trunk on fact QA (through the loop), within 0.15 nats on the text held-out loss, ≥ 90% of it on BlockWorld tasks; (5) at size — the 450M continued with the fact link answers wiki-world questions at ≥ 2× the text control at the same tokens. **Kill:** gate 2 below 50% (the route does not learn; the host loop stays the only path), or gate 4's text loss worse by more than 0.4 nats (the textbooks do not close the law's gap; then the claim is facts only). Status: **OPEN — gate 1 passed 2026-10-20 (`validation/logs/exp_k7_exposure_{paraphrase,verbatim}_2026-10-20.json`); gates 2–5 to build, gate 5 on H-S12's mechanism (places → entities).**

**H-K8 — A holographic state in place of the KV cache: beyond the window, one vector per chunk of C tokens, the superposition of the chunk's bound (key, value) pairs with unitary keys, read by unbinding; bytes per token flat in context length.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-20 ("kv cache is one of the problem when its massive its energy consuming a lot … if we are able to do something like that it would be immense power in the end"). Inspiration: Hrrformer (holographic attention everywhere; linear time, lost accuracy on language), linear-attention states (Gated DeltaNet-2, Kimi Linear: a d×d matrix per layer), the VSA capacity analyses; ours keeps exact attention inside the window (512), goes holographic only beyond it, in chunks with a summary key for selection, in the same algebra as place and fact (`SpatialLink.unitary`, `bind`, `unbind` are the operators), and — from `swirl-dynamics`' DySLIM — asks that the state's statistics stay stationary over long rollouts. **The law, derived and measured (`validation/exp_k8_holo.py`, 2026-10-20):** with random unitary keys and unit values every decoy's dot with a read is N(0, C/d), its maximum over N candidates ~ √(2 C ln N / d), so a nearest-value decode over N holds while C ln N < d/2 (one vector alone: M ln M < d/2). Measured: the law's crossing is where the decode sits at 0.68–0.70 at every d (512 / 1024 / 2048) — gate 1 as written (90% within 25% of the law) **fails**, and the corrected rule is that 90% holds to about half the law (M = 31 / 55 / 97). The chunk store at N = 4,096: at d = 1024 (the grown model's width) chunks of 16 decode 1.00 / 1.00 / 0.97 over query noise σ = 0 / 0.5 / 1.0, chunks of 32 0.97 / 0.90 / 0.53 (**gate 2, C = 32, σ = 0.5: 0.90, at the line**), chunks of 64 0.61 / 0.51 / 0.14; at d = 2048 chunks of 32 decode 1.00 / 1.00 / 0.98 and 64 0.97 / 0.95 / 0.64; summing all chunks (no selection) decodes nothing (0.00) and top-4 summed is worse than top-1 — selection is the whole mechanism; the read is attenuated (cos 0.16–0.25 to the true value) though decodable. **Bytes per generated token at the grown geometry (D = 1024, L = 64, window 512, 16 memory layers), bf16:** full attention at N = 1M touches 262 GB per token; the window + row store we have, 2.2 GB (resident 35 GB); window + chunks (C = 32), 1.15 GB (resident 2.2 GB); at N = 4,096 all three sit on the 64-layer window's floor of 128 MB — the energy argument is a long-context argument, and the resident saving (16×) is the larger one. **What it brings:** a context of a million tokens held in 2 GB and read at a cost flat in its length. **Advantage:** a KV cache's per-token bytes grow with N and so does its energy; ours is bounded by construction, and the beyond-window read is one Fourier-domain product, no softmax over rows. **Gates:** (1) **failed as written** (the law marks the 0.68 point; recorded, not re-registered); (2) **passed at the line** (0.90); (3) model level, to build — a tiny trunk with the holographic store (`mem_kind=holo`, flag-gated) on a synthetic recall task matches the row store within 5 points and beats window-only by 30; (4) the grown model's variant at 4,096: needle recall ≥ the row store's with ≤ 1/16 of its resident bytes; (5) stationarity — the state's norm and the read's SNR drift < 10% over 100k tokens of rollout (DySLIM's question, asked of our state). **Kill:** gate 3 fails (the trunk cannot use a 0.16-cos read; then rows stay, chunks go). Status: **OPEN — gates 1–2 run (`validation/logs/exp_k8_holo_2026-10-20.json`); gate 3 is the next build (a `CubbyMemory` sibling, < 300 lines, off by default).**

**H-K9 — Jumps in time with a composition law: a learned flow map Φ_k on the trunk's state that gives the state k steps ahead in one application, trained with the semigroup consistency Φ_j ∘ Φ_k = Φ_{j+k}, so prospection's keyframes cost a read, not a rollout.** Source: `google-research/swirl-dynamics` (flow-map matching, MeanFlow: one-step jumps over a time interval with the consistency of the flow; DySLIM; "Evolve Smoothly"), read at Nick's request 2026-10-20; Group P (keyframes, branching futures); H-S12 (composition is binding — the displacement algebra already composes exactly, the learned part is the trunk's dynamics). **What it brings:** a 64-token lookahead for one application instead of 64 forwards; a planner that can afford to look; the energy argument applied to thinking ahead. **Advantage:** conventional models plan by generating; ours would jump in state space and verify the landing with the VM. **Gates:** (1) on BlockWorld traces, Φ_k's state after k = 8 / 32 actions decodes the right world facts ≥ 80% (the rollout's own decode = 100%); (2) consistency error ‖Φ_j(Φ_k(s)) − Φ_{j+k}(s)‖ / ‖s‖ < 0.1 on held-out traces; (3) planner success with keyframes ≥ 90% of token-by-token rollout at ≤ 1/8 the forwards. **Kill:** gate 1 below 50%. Status: **OPEN — not built; traces come from `cubby-blockworld` (private).**

**H-K10 — Loci tokens (named *hypertokens* until 2026-10-22): the unit the trunk reads is a bound vector, not a text piece — identity ⊛ place ⊛ time ⊛ role ⊛ store address — so a verified fact is one input unit that the trunk reads back by algebra, and a span of language is one unit of a length chosen by information rather than by a merge table.** Source: Nick, 2026-10-20 ("if tokens are the problem why not simply replace them by something more efficient? like hypertokens (tentative name)"; "and for language are we condemned to use tokens?"). **Name (2026-10-22, Nick).** Called *hypertokens* until 2026-10-22, renamed because the word is taken twice. zip2zip (Geng et al. 2025, arXiv 2506.01084) uses it for tokens merged by LZW compression at inference, the sense H-C1, H-C2 and H-C6 use. Augeri 2025 ("Hypertokens: Holographic Associative Memory in Tokenized LLMs", arXiv 2507.00002) uses it for symbolic codes built from error-correcting codes, written into the *prompt* of an unmodified model in rare or private-use tokens, and retrieved by the model's own attention and an inner-product argmax; v1 names no model, dataset or baseline. A **loci token** is different in kind: it is the trunk's own token embedding bound to a place at the input (`e ⊛ S(p)`, a fractional power encoding). A scene per document sums the tokens that write. A token that asks reads its place back inside the trunk by unbinding, exact by the algebra rather than found by attention, and a copy path cleans the read up against the document's written ids into the output (order 0001). The name is from the method of loci: things put at places, recalled by going back to the place. File and log names keep the old word (`exp_k10_hypertoken.py`, `exp_hypertoken_recall.py`, `hypertoken_recall_*`), so every earlier link still resolves. H-S12 is the first loci token (a token bound with its place), the scene `V = Σ e_i ⊛ S(p_i)` is the first loci-token store, and the read mid-trunk (`SpatialLink.read`) is how the trunk uses one. Inspiration, named for the difference: byte patches sized by entropy, sentence-level concept units, text diffusion over spans, multi-token prediction — none keeps an exact decode; ours does (the lattice theorem). **Three problems one unit causes, and what the loci token does to each.** A subword carries ~1 byte of identity and no structure, so the trunk rebuilds structure from statistics — that is the 100–1,000 sightings of H-K7; with the structure bound in at the input, a fact given *once* is read back exactly (the read is algebra, the gate learns *when*). Every piece is a forward pass, so the cost is paid per piece, not per meaning — a span of n pieces as one unit runs the trunk once (H-K8's energy argument compounds). A fact of ~20 pieces is 20 places to be wrong — a fact as one unit is decoded whole from the store. **Two halves, honestly separated.** *Input (cheap, ours to choose):* a span is `Σ_i e(piece_i) ⊛ S(i)`, a bag with order, exactly decodable by unbinding at `S(i)`, length bounded by the capacity law (M ln M < d/2: ~55 pieces at d = 1024, ~35 at d = 256); the boundary comes from the host (a word, a name, a fact, a sentence — the ask loop's grammar and `route()` are the linker's start) or from an entropy rule; language words stay words — loci tokens replace the fact-bearing spans and add structure to the rest. *Output (the hard half):* predicting the next span is predicting a vector, not a distribution over 128k symbols — a vector cannot say "70% Paris, 30% Lyon"; ambiguity becomes a superposition, and a superposition decoded blindly is a blend. Routes: keep piece-level output for free text and use loci tokens at the input only (this entry's gate), or emit a point in the Hilbert space that the *store* decodes — nearest entity, unbind for relation and object — exact for the structured part of what the model says and free of the vocabulary softmax (Group C's bottleneck), silent on the rest. **What it brings:** a fact seen once and answered; the trunk run once per meaning; the fact half of the corpus at one unit per fact (H-K7's factor before any sightings argument). **Advantage over conventional models:** their token is a text piece and structure is a hope; ours is a bound vector and structure is a theorem. **Mechanism, to build (flag-gated, < 300 lines):** `validation/exp_k10_hypertoken.py` — a tiny `train_base` trunk with `SpatialLink` (the existing channel, unchanged): documents of 12 facts `s r o` with the object's token placed at the fact's address `cell(s) + offset(r)` (an entity's cell a hash into a 1,024³ lattice, a relation's offset a lattice step the kernel separates), ~150 tokens of filler language, then questions `Q s r HERE → o` with `HERE` placed at the same address — the questions fall beyond the attention window (64), the facts are random per document (never seen in training), so the answer can come only from the scene's algebraic read; the control is the same documents with nothing placed (text; the plain path, bit-identical). **Gates (pre-registered):** (1) one sighting — held-out documents' questions answered ≥ 90% through the channel where the text control sits at chance beyond the window (and at ≥ 80% *inside* the window, to show attention can do it only within reach); (2) the text goldens bit-identical with the channel off (SPEC) and the filler language's held-out loss within 0.02 of the control's; (3) spans — a word of up to 8 pieces presented as one bound unit at the input, the trunk's next-piece loss on the following text within 0.05 of the piece-by-piece baseline at ≤ 1/4 the forward passes; (4) output — on the wiki world, the trunk's emitted vector decoded by the store (nearest entity) answers ≥ the text head's exact-match on the same questions. **Kill:** gate 1 below 60% (the trunk cannot learn to query the scene for a fact it was shown once; then loci tokens stay a host-side object), or gate 2's loss worse by 0.05. **First read (2026-10-20, `validation/exp_k10_hypertoken.py`, a 3.4M-parameter `train_base` trunk, D = 256, window 64, 8 random facts per document shown once, questions 120+ tokens later, 1,000 steps × 16 documents, 25 min on the CPU per arm; `validation/logs/exp_k10_hypertoken_*_2026-10-20.json`):** before any training the scene decodes the object at the fact's address at 0.84 by nearest entity (cos 0.17–0.35 to the true embedding: the attenuated read of H-K8, with `HERE`'s own bound embedding at the same address). *The loci-token arm* — the question's `HERE` token carries its address and the read's query **is** that address (the gate and the decode `o` learned, the gate started open at 0 for a trunk trained from scratch): held-out answers 0.00 → 0.21 (step 200) → 0.53 → 0.81 → 0.86 → 0.92 → **0.94** at 1,000 — **gate 1's first clause passes** (≥ 90%); *the text control*, the same documents with nothing placed: **0.00** throughout, the answer beyond attention's reach; the filler language's held-out loss 2.075 in both arms — **gate 2 passes**; the in-window control (4 facts, no filler): **0.22** at 1,000 steps — attention learns the in-window copy slowly, the second clause not shown at this budget (the algebraic read reached 0.94 *beyond* the window in the same steps). *The learned-query arm* — H-S12's route, the trunk asked to recover the address from its own state through `q = W rms(h)`: **0.00** for all 1,000 steps, the gate drifting shut (−4 → −4.08 from the design's start; 0 → −0.36 when started open). Read plainly: the algebra delivers, the trunk learns to decode an algebraic read in a few hundred steps when the address arrives with the token, and *recovering* an address from the hidden state is the hard part at this scale — which is exactly H-S12's gate 2 risk, now measured small. **Built the same day, flag-gated:** `SpatialLink(query="placed")` (`CB_SPATIAL_QUERY=placed`, meta `spatial.query`, absent at the default so every earlier meta still matches): a placed token's query is its own place `S(p)` from the input, exact; the unplaced keep the learned query; the default path is bit-identical (`tests/model/test_spatial.py`, decode = forward with places asked). **Second read — the route (2026-10-20, same trunk and budget; `validation/logs/exp_k10_hypertoken_*_2026-10-20.json`).** The learned route is teachable: a *route loss*, `1 − cos(unitary(q), S(p))` at the placed tokens — the side file knows every question's address, so the trunk's own query is taught where to look, for free at training time — takes `--query learned` from 0.00 to **0.82** (route cosine 0.54). What did not work, recorded: the gate pinned open for 250 steps then released (an outside suggestion, freeze-and-release) 0.00 alone and 0.77 with the route loss; a per-frequency complex query head (the shape of the deconvolution, d parameters instead of d²) 0.69 with the route loss — the dense head can also project the mixing layers' noise out, the diagonal one cannot; a *matched* read (the query's conjugate at its own magnitudes instead of unitary(), to stop a noise-dominated frequency being stretched to modulus 1) 0.19 — unitary() is right for this read; the dense head started at the identity 0.66. Controls asked for by the outside review and run: attention over the *whole* document (window 256) **0.03** at 1,000 steps — the algebraic read is the sample-efficient path, not a stand-in for a longer window; a *mismatched address* (every `HERE` carrying another fact's valid address) **0.00** on every arm — the channel relies on the address alone, no other path carries the answer; and the trunk's own gated recurrence (MinGRU, a dense state past the window) is the text control that sat at 0.00. Built: `CB_ROUTE_LOSS=λ` in `train_base` (the query tapped in the forward; needs `CB_GRAD_CKPT=0`), `validation/make_fact_shard.py` (the wiki world's facts as loci tokens: a fact's address is the subject's hashed cell plus the relation's step, a name a span up the z axis read piece by piece; `facts` / `facts_text` built, 20,000 documents, 240,208 facts, 119,839 questions, on the cache and the Drive), and `make_spatial_shard.py --ask placed` (`spatial_here` / `spatial_text_here`: every village question carries its address). Status: **OPEN — gates 1–2 passed with the address given, the learned route at 0.82 when taught; next: the 450M continued on `spatial_here` + `facts` with `CB_SPATIAL=1 CB_SPATIAL_QUERY=placed CB_ROUTE_LOSS=1` against the text controls at the same tokens (H-S12 gates 1–2 and this entry's gate 4 in one run), then spans (gate 3).** **Run 1 at the 450M (2026-10-20, the same runs as H-S12's first read):** facts at addresses in-window (`facts`, 512-token documents) -- `link` **0.016** exact (first token 0.27), `text` **0.987**: the read did not learn, and the comparison was the wrong one. Causes, in the shard and the scene: every placed token wrote into ONE scene per 1,024-token window -- two documents, every name piece, and the questions' own `here` and answer pieces at the very addresses they asked about -- ~150-250 items in d = 1024, past the capacity law's ~55 for 90% (H-K8); and the facts sat inside the attention window, so `text` copied them. **Built for run 2 (`cad8da2`):** a thing WRITES into the scene and a question only ASKS there (`.role.u8`; `Tokens.flags`), the scene restarts at every document (`SpatialLink.scene(starts=)`), and the facts move beyond the window -- `facts_far1k` (6-10 facts, ~800 tokens of real text, then the questions; ~24 written items, the first question 809 tokens past the last fact) against `facts_far1k_text`. Run 1's link final read with the roles: villages 0.836 (12 held-out documents), `facts_far1k` 0.000 (never trained on it). **Run 2 (2026-10-20, `spatial_link_2`: 3,058 steps = 0.40B tokens; the roles, a scene per document, `facts_far1k`):** villages **0.776** exact (route alone 0.729); far facts **0.005** exact (first token 0.22, route alone 0.010) -- the read learned nothing on the facts. `validation/diag_fact_read.py` (24 held-out documents, 120 questions, 446 later answer tokens, CPU) splits it: first piece 0.242 with the places and 0.242 without (the read at `here` reaches nothing); the later pieces **0.096 with the places, 0.213 without, 0.229** with only the writing tokens bound -- every placed token was bound at the input, so each answer piece, asking for the next, entered the trunk as `e (*) S(p_next)` and the trunk lost which token it had just read; the read itself added ~0.02. Two causes: the ASK tokens' input binding (a question scrambled its own words) and the first piece's address on `here`, three tokens before the answer. The text arm was not run (the link's gates fail on its own numbers; gate 2 stands from run 1). **Built for run 3:** `SpatialLink(bind="writes")` / `CB_SPATIAL_BIND=writes` (with roles, only a thing at a place is bound; an asking token enters as itself and asks with `S(p)`; the default keeps every earlier run exact) and `facts_far1k_lead` (`make_fact_shard.py --lead`: run 2's documents, ids identical by md5, the token right before each answer asking for its first piece -- every token that predicts a piece asks for it). **Run 3 (2026-10-21, `spatial_link_3`, G4: 3,418 steps = 0.45B tokens; `CB_SPATIAL_BIND=writes`, `facts_far1k_lead`; trained on the fused MinGRU kernel + flex attention at 99 KB tiles from step 353, ~75.0k tok/s, 40.6% MFU):** villages **0.953** exact (first token 0.960; route alone 0.748) -- up from 0.776 / 0.793, past gate 1's link bar of 0.90 (the gate itself still wants `text` below 0.60, and in-window `text` read 0.995 in run 1); far facts **0.021** exact (first token 0.229; route alone 0.014). `diag_fact_read.py` on 24 held-out documents: first piece 0.250 with the places, 0.233 without; later pieces **0.309 with, 0.300 without** -- the scrambling is gone (run 2: 0.096 against 0.213), the trunk's own guess at the rest of a name rose, and the read adds ~0.01-0.02. So with every token that predicts a piece asking for it, and the asking tokens themselves, the read still does not carry an open-vocabulary token at 0.45B: the villages' answers come from a few dozen names, the facts' from the whole 128k vocabulary, and the read's vector (the input embedding of the token at the address, plus crosstalk) has to be decoded through the 16 layers after the link into the untied head -- a copy the trunk learns for a closed set and has not for all tokens, on ~1% of its tokens. Next (not run): a copy path -- the read scored against the embedding table straight into the logits, gated (the vector already IS a token's embedding) -- or the read nearer the head. Text arm not run (gate 4 fails on the link's number alone). **Order 0001 (2026-10-21, CPU; `docs/orders/0001-far-facts-local.md`): the read carries the facts; the trunk never learned to copy them out.** Run 3's checkpoint, nothing trained (`validation/diag_fact_cleanup.py`, 400 held-out documents, 9,451 piece reads; `validation/logs/diag_fact_cleanup_run3_2026-10-21.json`): the scene unbound at each asking token's address and cleaned up by cosine is the stored piece 0.758 of the time over the 128k vocabulary (0.457 whole answers) and **0.992 among the document's own writers (0.965 whole answers)**. Cos to the target is 0.165, against 1/sqrt(37.5) = 0.163: the learned table behaves like random codes, and the 128k-way decode sits on the random-code ceiling (`validation/sim_hrr_ceiling.py`: 0.765 per piece at 37 items in d = 1024, `validation/logs/sim_hrr_ceiling_2026-10-21.json`). The scene split by a hash of the address into K = 2 / 4 gives 0.859 / 0.996 whole answers even over 128k (`diag_fact_cleanup_grid_run3_2026-10-21.json`). **Correction to run 3's reading:** the villages never tested copying. A stored `pond` is answered ` pond` (another token), and 76% of village questions answer "nothing": their 0.953 is empty-read detection plus a closed-set map. *The toy* (`validation/exp_k10_far_facts.py`, D 256, 1,000 steps x 16 documents, teacher-forced): the 450M-like combination (vocabulary 8,192, 4 facts of 1-5 pieces, 1 question a document, L 8 read at 4) reads **0.000 on 3 seeds** (`exp_k10_far_facts_combo_s*`); the vocabulary step alone 0.000, the pieces step 0.221, an 800-token gap 0.941 (distance is not the cause). *The fix:* a copy path (`cubbyllm/model/spatial_copy.py`, `CB_SPATIAL_COPY=ring`, absent = nothing built). At asking tokens, `p = (1 - lam) p_LM + lam p_copy`, where `p_copy` is the read's cosine softmax over a fixed ring of the last 256 ids the document wrote (the decode state stays fixed-size) and lam is read-aware (`CB_SPATIAL_COPY_BETA`: the gate also sees the best match against a threshold). It reads **0.930 / 0.908 / 0.895** exact on 3 seeds, the language within 0.005 (`exp_k10_far_facts_combo_ringb_s*`). The gate from the stream alone reads 0.922 but ends the name only 0.469 of the time (it cannot see an empty address); the copy over the whole vocabulary reads 0.357. Added to run 3 with nothing trained: far facts 0.000 -> **0.258** exact (first piece 0.250 -> 0.800), villages unchanged at 0.982 (`diag_copy_step0_run3_ringb_2026-10-21.json`, 24 documents). **What it brings:** a fact seen once, 800+ tokens back, answered by its address, with a decode state of the scene plus 256 ids a stream. **Advantage over conventional models:** they recall that far only through a KV cache that grows with every token. Next: run 4 (`spatial_link_4`: the copy added to run 3) -- far facts >= 0.50 exact, villages >= 0.90, text mix within 0.02 of run 3's. **Run 4 (2026-10-21, `spatial_link_4`, G4: 846 steps = 0.11B tokens, 0.5 h; run 3 continued with the copy added, `CB_SPATIAL_COPY=ring`, tau 3.04, beta 0.115; 68k tok/s, 36.9% MFU against run 3's 40.6%, compiled, no per-step recompiles):** far facts **0.761** exact (first token 0.975; 2,002 held-out questions; run 3: 0.021) and villages **0.953** (run 3: 0.953) (`validation/logs/hypertoken_recall_spatial_run4_link_2026-10-21.json`). Pass on both. The text mix **fails**: 2.2596 against run 3's 2.2224 (+0.037; the line is 0.02). That is the schedule, not the copy. Every pure-text source rose +0.07-0.10 when the learning rate re-warmed from 3e-6 to 3e-4 with a fresh optimizer, peaked at +0.13-0.17 by step 500, and only partly came back in the decay, ending +0.05-0.06. Those sources have no asking token, so the copy never touches them (`spatial_run4_link_metrics_2026-10-21.jsonl`). **The gain lives in the copy alone** (`validation/diag_swap_copy.py`, 64 held-out documents a shard, CPU; `diag_swap_copy_run4_2026-10-21.json`): run 3's trunk with run 4's trained copy (1,027 parameters: the gate's d + 1, tau, beta) reads far facts **0.844** exact (first 0.988) against run 4 as trained at 0.738, villages 0.958 against 0.960. The trunk's drift cost the facts 0.11, and its text is run 3's bit for bit, since the copy leaves every non-asking position unchanged. **What it brings:** the far facts go from 0.021 to 0.84 on a trained 450M by training ~1,000 parameters, with its language untouched. **Advantage over conventional models:** they add knowledge by fine-tuning, which moves every weight and the language with it, or by context, which grows the KV cache. Next: train the copy alone on run 3's frozen trunk (order 0005), and report where the rest of the gap to the read's 0.965 goes. **Run 5 (2026-10-21, `spatial_link_5`, G4: 674 steps, 0.17 h, 183k tok/s; run 3 with the copy added and `CB_TRAIN_ONLY=copy`: only the copy's 1,027 parameters train, at 3e-3, on the two asking shards):** far facts **0.946** exact (first token 0.994; 2,002 held-out questions; run 3: 0.021, run 4: 0.761) and villages **0.953** (run 3: 0.953) (`validation/logs/hypertoken_recall_spatial_run5_link_2026-10-22.json`). Every one of the 21 text sources stands within 0.005 of run 3's (worst 0.0000), and `validation/diag_frozen_check.py` finds 0 of the 326 trunk and 5 router tensors moved (`diag_frozen_check_run5_2026-10-22.json`). The copy ends at tau 3.04 → 4.40, beta 0.115 → 0.009, and gate weight norm 2.62. The probe's split (64 documents; `spatial_run5_link_metrics_2026-10-22.jsonl`): first piece 0.997, later pieces 0.978, the name's end 0.938; the gate's λ is 0.80 where the asked address holds a piece and 0.056 where it is empty. The read's own ceiling among the writers is 0.965 whole answers, and run 5 reads 0.946. **What it brings:** a trained 450M answers facts it was shown once, 800+ tokens back, at 0.95 exact, by training 1,027 parameters, with its language provably unchanged. **Advantage over conventional models:** fine-tuning would move every weight to add this, and a long context would pay a growing KV cache; here neither. **In plain words:** it remembers what it was told once, far back, and nothing else about how it talks changes. **Order 0006 (2026-10-22; CPU toy: d 256, 8 layers, 1,000 steps; `validation/logs/exp_k10_free_text_<arm>_s<seed>_2026-10-21.json`, copy-alone reruns `..._alone_2026-10-22.json`):** text keys `u(W e(s)) ⊛ u(W e(r))` and a pointer that reads a free-text question's subject and relation words. On subjects never seen in training, asked in seen phrasings, the pointer reads 0.854 / 0.899 / 0.870 exact (3 seeds); hashed addresses with the learned route read 0.000 and a learned query on text keys 0.006, so the pointer is what makes text keys reachable. With the address given, text keys read 0.848 and hashed places 0.857: no cost as addresses. The pre-registered lines missed (U 0.848 < 0.90; P on held-out phrasings 0.816 / 0.694 / 0.785, 1 of 3 >= 0.80; absent facts answered 5.3-9.9% > 5%) for one cause, the mix: the copy alone picks the right answer on 0.955-0.974 of present facts, mixed 0.846-0.857, and a hard switch at λ >= 0.5 recovers only to 0.875; about 12% of present facts are answered wrong. Next: the switch trained alone (order 0021). **Order 0011 (2026-10-21):** readable fact names; run 5, nothing retrained, reads 0.931 on the new spelling against 0.941 on the same 64 documents of the old (`hypertoken_recall_spatial_run5_on_far1k_v2_2026-10-21.json`): the copy carries over. **Order 0021 (2026-10-22, the same toy; `validation/logs/exp_k10_switch_<arm>_s<seed>_2026-10-22.json`, `k10_policies_<arm>_s<seed>_2026-10-22.json`):** training the switch alone on the frozen toy lifts U from 0.848 to 0.879 but leaves present facts answered wrong at 7.3-9.2% and absent ones answered at 6.9-10.2%. The read is not the limit: one threshold on the best match separates present from absent at a balanced 0.947-0.963. The fallback is: when the gate closes, the trunk speaks, and at an asking position its pick is never the fact. **Copy or refuse** (the copy's whole answer when every piece clears the threshold, NONE otherwise) on the same models: present wrong 0.6-1.3%, absent answered 2.9-4.9%, refused 12-16%, exact 0.830-0.872 (threshold 0.17-0.18, picked on the evaluation documents). Facts come from the copy or not at all. **Order 0022 (2026-10-22; `exp_refuse_450m_end_2026-10-22.json`, `k10_refuse_<arm>_s<seed>_2026-10-22.json`):** copy or refuse as the contract, with the contract saying the answer's end (`end_id`). On the 450M (run 5, nothing retrained; theta 0.117 fit on 200 calibration documents, measured once on 200 test documents): present wrong **1.7%**, absent answered **0.0%**, refused **32.9%**, exact 0.654, against the mix's 12.4% wrong and 100% absent answered. Both wrong-answer lines hold; the kill line (refusals at most 25%) fires. Absent questions are already all refused at this theta, so the threshold is spent on present facts answered wrong: one scalar separates present from absent, not right from wrong among present facts.
